### [1] [For whom the Bell tolls: joint measurements and state updates in quantum field theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30468)
*Christopher J. Fewster,Christiane K. M. Klein*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives joint probability distributions for smeared field observables in quantum field theory using the Fewster-Verch measurement framework, and constructs a QFT-based Bell test (using a coupled Proca-scalar model) that violates the CHSH inequality—approaching Tsirelson’s bound when vacuum effects are suppressed—demonstrating compatibility of Fewster-Verch state updates with strong quantum correlations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how state update rules in the Fewster-Verch measurement framework for quantum field theory relate to observable statistics and quantum nonlocality, particularly in Bell tests where measurement-induced state changes must be rigorously defined in relativistic settings.

Method: Derives the joint moment generating functional and unique joint probability distribution for commuting normal observables $A(f_i)^*A(f_i)$ on a bosonic Fock space, assuming a Green hyperbolic field theory; applies this to construct a concrete Bell test model using a recently introduced coupled Proca-scalar field theory for polarization measurements.

Result: Obtains explicit joint probability distribution and moment generating functional for photon-number-type observables; constructs a relativistic, QFT-based Bell experiment violating CHSH up to Tsirelson’s bound (in the vacuum-rejection limit); confirms consistency of Fewster-Verch state updates with maximal quantum correlations.

Conclusion: The Fewster-Verch state update rules are compatible with strongly nonclassical, Bell-violating correlations in quantum field theory; this supports an informational interpretation of state updates—i.e., they encode conditioning on measurement outcomes rather than objective physical collapse.

Abstract: State update rules and their behaviour in Bell tests play an important role in the study of measurement schemes and quantum information. We consider the state updates of the Fewster-Verch measurement framework for quantum field theory using their connection to the probability distribution of measurement outcomes. We focus on a set of observables of the form $A(f_i)^*A(f_i)$, where $A(f)$ is the (complex) smeared field operator for any theory induced by a formally hermitian, Green hyperbolic differential operator, and where the $(A(f_i))_{i=1, \dots, m}$ are assumed to be normal and mutually commuting. Under the assumption that these operators have a sufficiently nice representation on a bosonic Fock space, we derive the joint moment generating functional and the unique joint probability distribution for the observables $(A(f_i)^*A(f_i))_{i=1,\dots,m}$. As an application of these results, we construct a model of a Bell inequality test within quantum field theory using a recently developed coupled Proca-scalar model for polarization measurements. We show that our Bell test model can violate the CHSH inequality, approaching Tsirelson's bound for maximally entangled states in the limit where vacuum effects are perfectly rejected. These results show that the state update rules derived in the Fewster-Verch framework are compatible with strongly quantum correlations. They also support a viewpoint in which state updates should be understood in relation to available information.

</details>


### [2] [Frobenius $2$-algebras and $R$-matrices](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29898)
*Hibiki Shibuya,Yuji Terashima*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces Frobenius 2-algebras—categorical generalizations of Frobenius algebras—and shows they canonically produce R-matrices satisfying the Yang–Baxter equation in the monoidal category of endomorphisms; it illustrates the construction via three examples, including a categorical recovery of Kashaev’s universal R-matrix for the Heisenberg double.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a unifying categorical framework for constructing solutions to the Yang–Baxter equation, generalizing known constructions (e.g., Kashaev’s) and linking Frobenius structure to braiding in higher algebra.

Method: Definition and study of Frobenius 2-algebras in monoidal 2-categories; derivation of an R-matrix from the Frobenius 2-algebra structure via duality and cyclicity; verification that it satisfies the Yang–Baxter equation in End(X); explicit instantiation in three settings: braided monoidal categories, Hopf algebras with invertible antipode, and representations of Heisenberg doubles.

Result: Every Frobenius 2-algebra yields a canonical Yang–Baxter operator (R-matrix) in End(X); the construction recovers Kashaev’s R-matrix for the Heisenberg double, thereby categorically generalizing it.

Conclusion: Frobenius 2-algebras serve as a natural higher-categorical source of Yang–Baxter solutions, unifying and extending classical algebraic constructions—including those from Hopf theory and quantum groups—within a coherent 2-categorical framework.

Abstract: In this paper, introducing Frobenius $2$-algebras, which are categorical generalizations of Frobenius algebras, we show that every Frobenius $2$-algebra determines an $R$-matrix satisfying the Yang-Baxter equation in the monoidal category of endomorphisms of its underlying object. Moreover, we illustrate this construction through three examples: a pair of objects in a braided monoidal category, a Hopf algebra with invertible antipode, and representations of the Heisenberg double of a finite-dimensional Hopf algebra. In particular, in the third example, we recover Kashaev's construction of the universal $R$-matrix. Thus, our construction provides a categorical generalization of Kashaev's construction.

</details>


### [3] [Reproducing kernel and matrix representation of the deformed quantum optical density operator of $\mathcal{R}(p,q)-$super-coherent states on $\mathcal{B}_0\oplus\mathcal{B}_1$ fermionic quantum algebra](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30576)
*Kawèyim Lankpetre,Isiaka Aremua,Joseph Désiré Bukweli Kyemba,Bienvenu Gnim Adewi*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs and analyzes R(p,q)-super-coherent states with two Grassmann variables on a graded superalgebra, derives the diagonal representation of the density operator across four Fock space sectors using R(p,q)-calculus and Berezin integration, establishes sectorial reproducing kernels with key analytic properties, and demonstrates intelligent state behavior under Berezin quantization in the bosonic sector.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize coherent states to the R(p,q)-deformed supersymmetric setting and provide a rigorous functional-analytic framework—particularly diagonal representations, reproducing kernels, and quantum statistical properties—for quantum systems with both bosonic and fermionic degrees of freedom governed by two-parameter deformations.

Method: Constructs R(p,q)-super-coherent states on a Z2-graded superalgebra B0⊕B1; decomposes the graded super-Fock space into four sectors (bosonic, φ1-fermionic, φ2-fermionic, bi-fermionic); employs R(p,q)-derivative, angular integration, and Berezin integration to obtain diagonal representations of the density operator per sector; defines and analyzes sectorial reproducing kernels via resolution of identity; performs Berezin quantization in the bosonic sector to compute observables and dispersions.

Result: Explicit total density operator as sum of four sectorial contributions (interpreted as a mixture); unified compact expressions for diagonal density representations using sectorial parametric functions; rigorous derivation of sectorial reproducing kernels satisfying hermiticity, positivity, matrix multiplication, and series expansion properties; integral relation linking density operator, density function, and kernel; Berezin quantization yields explicit quantized operators and confirms intelligent state property via minimal uncertainty in super-position/momentum operators.

Conclusion: The R(p,q)-super-coherent states form a consistent, analytically tractable framework for deformed supersymmetric quantum mechanics: they support a resolution of identity, admit well-behaved reproducing kernels across all grading sectors, and exhibit optimal quantum uncertainty properties—thus extending standard coherent state theory to multi-parameter deformed superalgebras.

Abstract: We study the $\mathcal{R}(p,q)-$super-coherent states with two Grassmann variables $(\zeta_1,\zeta_2)$ built on the superalgebra $\mathcal{B}_0\oplus\mathcal{B}_1$ associated with the $\mathcal{R}(p,q)-$deformed quantum algebras of Hounkonnou and Bukweli-Kyemba, the two dilation parameters $\phi_1(p,q)$ and $\phi_2(p,q)$ being meromorphic functions of two variables evaluated at $(p,q)$. The graded super-Fock space splits into four sectors: bosonic, $\phi_1-$fermionic, $\phi_2-$fermionic and bi-fermionic. In each of them we establish the diagonal representation of the density operator by means of the $\mathcal{R}(p,q)-$derivative, angular integration and Berezin integration, and we give a unified compact form in terms of sectorial parametric functions. The total density operator is obtained in explicit form as the sum of the four sectorial contributions, and can be read as a mixture of the four sectors. We define the sectorial reproducing kernels, obtained from the resolution of the identity, we compute the matrix elements of the density operator in the super-coherent state basis, and we prove the hermiticity, positivity and matrix multiplication properties of the kernels, together with the series expansion of their diagonal values. An integral formulation relates the density operator, its density function and the reproducing kernel, the latter being the function associated with the identity operator. Berezin quantization in the bosonic sector yields the quantized operators associated with $z$, $\bar z$, $|z|^2$, $z^2$ and $\bar z^2$ and their mean values; the dispersions of the super-position and super-momentum operators show that the super-coherent states are intelligent states.

</details>


### [4] [A simple counterexample to the Hohenberg-Kohn theorem for excited states](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28296)
*Dominic Shillingford*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs explicit counterexamples showing that the excited-state Hohenberg–Kohn theorem fails for non-interacting fermions on a 1D torus: multiple distinct external potentials yield the same first excited-state particle density, violating injectivity of the potential-to-density map.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To challenge the validity of an excited-state extension of the Hohenberg–Kohn theorem—specifically, whether the external potential is uniquely determined by the particle density of an excited state—in the non-interacting fermionic setting.

Method: Constructs semi-explicit solvable models using the rescaled Lamé potential on the 1D unit torus; exploits translational invariance and spectral properties (simplicity of first excited energy) to generate a continuous family of potentials sharing the same constant first excited-state density; extends to odd particle numbers under a spectral nonresonance assumption; draws analogies to planar elastica for n=2.

Result: For every even n = 2l, there exists a one-parameter family of distinct external potentials yielding identical constant first excited-state densities—hence, the potential-to-density map is not injective. Under a nonresonance condition, analogous nonuniqueness holds for odd n ≥ 3. The n = 2 case links to classical elastica classification.

Conclusion: The excited-state Hohenberg–Kohn theorem does not hold in general for non-interacting fermions on the 1D torus, as demonstrated by explicit, analytically tractable counterexamples; uniqueness fails even for simple excited energies.

Abstract: For an even number of non-interacting fermions on the one-dimensional unit torus, a semi-explicit counterexample to the Hohenberg--Kohn theorem for excited states is exhibited. Indeed, for each $n = 2l$, it is shown that there exist constants $m \in (0, 1)$ and $\ell > 0$ such that an $n$-body system with the rescaled Lamé potential $v_*(x) = 2m \ell^2 \operatorname{sn}^2(\ell x; m)$ has a simple first excited energy and a first excited-state particle density which is constant on the torus. By translating this potential, we exhibit a non-trivial one-parameter family of potentials, all of which have a constant first excited-state density. As such, the potential-to-density map is not one-to-one in this setting. Under a spectral nonresonance assumption, we obtain the same nonuniqueness at a simple excited energy for odd $n \geq 3$. We also explore connections to the classification problem of elastica in the plane when $n = 2$, and outline some related questions of interest.

</details>


### [5] [Effective Hamiltonians and asymptotic band coupling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28328)
*Guillaume Bal,Paul Cazeaux,Daniel Massatt,Solomon Quinn*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: Derives effective macroscopic models for tight-binding Schrödinger equations with slow spatial variations using multiple-scale asymptotics, valid for spectrally localized wave packets—even at degenerate band points—and yields arbitrarily accurate long-time approximations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop rigorous, systematically accurate effective models for tight-binding Schrödinger dynamics in the presence of slow spatial variations, especially when spectral localization occurs at potentially degenerate points in the dispersion relation.

Method: Multiple-scale asymptotic expansion applied to tight-binding Schrödinger equations; construction of an effective Hamiltonian acting on the degenerate subspace, capturing inter-band coupling.

Result: Long-time approximations accurate to any prescribed order in the asymptotic parameter; effective Hamiltonian explicitly quantifying coupling between degenerate and non-degenerate bands; validated via applications and numerical simulations.

Conclusion: The framework generalizes prior adiabatic and semi-classical approaches by rigorously handling band degeneracies and slow modulations, enabling accurate macroscopic modeling of quantum transport in periodic media with perturbations.

Abstract: By a multiple-scale asymptotic expansion, we derive effective macroscopic models for a large class of tight-binding Schrödinger equations with slow spatial variations. For wave packets that are spectrally localized to some point $(K;E) \in \mathbb{R}^d \times \mathbb{R}$ in the unperturbed tight-binding dispersion relation, our effective models produce long-time approximations of the tight-binding dynamics, accurate to any prescribed order in the asymptotic parameter. Our framework allows the point $(K;E)$ to be degenerate, meaning that multiple (but not necessarily all) energy bands of the tight-binding Hamiltonian can attain the value $E$ at the frequency $K$. We construct an effective Hamiltonian on the relevant degenerate subspace, which quantifies the coupling between the degenerate and other bands. Several applications and numerical simulations illustrate the theory.

</details>


### [6] [On a class of affine Temperley-Lieb modules](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28389)
*Yacine Ikhlef*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes that affine Temperley-Lieb modules with the diagrammatic tensor product form a quasitensor (braided monoidal) category; when q is not a root of unity, a semi-simple ribbon subcategory is identified and linked to U_q(sl_2) highest-weight modules via fusion and associativity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the categorical structure of affine Temperley-Lieb modules—particularly their monoidal and braided properties—and to relate them to well-studied quantum group representations (U_q(sl_2)) via fusion and ribbon structures.

Method: Rigorous categorical analysis using diagrammatic tensor products; classification of modules (standard, simple quotients); verification of axioms for quasitensor, semi-simple, and ribbon categories; comparison with U_q(sl_2) representation theory via fusion rules and associativity constraints.

Result: Affine Temperley-Lieb modules form a quasitensor category; for non-root-of-unity q, the subcategory generated by simple quotients of reducible standard modules is semi-simple ribbon; evidence is provided that each such simple module corresponds bijectively (via fusion and associativity) to a pair of U_q(sl_2) highest-weight modules.

Conclusion: The affine Temperley-Lieb category admits rich monoidal and braided structure, and its representation theory is deeply intertwined with that of U_q(sl_2), suggesting a categorical equivalence or correspondence in the non-root-of-unity regime.

Abstract: We prove that the class of all affine Temperley-Lieb modules, equipped with the recently introduced "diagrammatic" tensor product, forms a quasitensor (i.e. monoidal braided) category. When the Temperley-Lieb parameter $q$ is not a root of unity, we consider the infinite subcategory generated by the simple quotients of reducible standard modules, and we argue that it is a semi-simple ribbon category. We present some evidence that, under the fusion rules and the associativity maps, each simple module in this subcategory is equivalent to a pair of $U_q(sl_2)$ heighest-weight modules.

</details>


### [7] [Integrable Orbital Exchange between Ellipsoidally Deformed $p$, $d$, and $f$ Electron States: Two-Angle Sklyanin Representations and Fused Elliptic $R$-Matrices](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28444)
*Vladimir Mikhalev*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs integrable orbital exchange models using ellipsoidally deformed p, d, and f multiplets, leveraging a two-angle Sklyanin algebra realization to obtain explicit Jacobi difference operators, spectra, eigenfunctions, and a positive inner product; it derives elliptic R-matrices via fusion, yields commuting charges and exchange Hamiltonians for various chain types, preserves local Casimirs, and recovers known models (Fateev, Takhtajan–Babujian) in appropriate limits.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop integrable orbital exchange models beyond standard spin systems by incorporating realistic orbital anisotropy—specifically ellipsoidal deformations of atomic multiplets—and to unify them within a rigorous algebraic framework (Sklyanin algebra) that ensures exact solvability, spectral completeness, and physical consistency (e.g., positive norm, conservation laws).

Method: Constructs models from ellipsoidally deformed p, d, f multiplets; employs a two-angle realization of the Sklyanin algebra to obtain explicit Jacobi difference operators D and L_i; uses Sklyanin's integral to define a positive inner product; applies elliptic fusion to derive R-matrices in angular generators; verifies Yang–Baxter relations to generate commuting charges and exchange Hamiltonians for homogeneous, alternating, and mixed-pair interactions; enforces preservation of local Casimirs (total angular momentum squared and distortion-plus-rotation kinetic-energy invariants).

Result: Explicit integrable orbital exchange Hamiltonians with ellipsoidal anisotropy; complete distortion spectra and orthogonal eigenfunctions; explicit elliptic R-matrices satisfying Yang–Baxter equation; families of commuting conserved charges; recovery of Fateev’s elliptic spin-one model in the p–p sector and Takhtajan–Babujian Hamiltonian in the rational isotropic limit; demonstration that exchange preserves local Casimirs while altering angular projections and correlations.

Conclusion: Ellipsoidal deformation provides a physically motivated and mathematically tractable route to integrable orbital exchange models; the Sklyanin-algebraic framework unifies geometric (ellipsoidal), algebraic (R-matrix, Yang–Baxter), and physical (Casimir-preserving dynamics) structures, enabling exact treatment of anisotropic orbital interactions beyond conventional spin-only paradigms.

Abstract: We construct integrable orbital exchange models from ellipsoidally deformed $p$, $d$, and $f$ multiplets. A two-angle realization of the Sklyanin algebra gives explicit Jacobi difference operators $D,L_i$, complete distortion spectra and eigenfunctions, and a positive inner product transported from Sklyanin's integral. In the $p$ sector the coordinate functions carry an exact spin-one action, and applying $D$ componentwise maps the confocal ellipsoid to a sphere. Elliptic fusion gives explicit $R$-matrices in the angular generators on both factors. Their Yang--Baxter relations yield commuting charges and exchange Hamiltonians for homogeneous and alternating chains, as well as mixed-pair interactions that can act between angular degrees of freedom on the same center. Exchange changes angular projections and correlations while preserving the two local Casimirs of each constituent, which fix its angular-momentum-squared and distortion-plus-rotation kinetic-energy invariants. The $p$--$p$ model recovers Fateev's elliptic spin-one interaction and reduces to the Takhtajan--Babujian Hamiltonian in the rational isotropic limit.

</details>


### [8] [An Elasticity Complex for Kirchhoff--Love Shells](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28480)
*Hang Yin,Albert Chern*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs an elasticity complex for Kirchhoff--Love shell theory using linearized Gauss--Codazzi equations, yielding a fine resolution of infinitesimal rigid motions, revealing an Airy potential for equilibrium stress, identifying natural boundary conditions, and establishing a Hodge–Morrey–Friedrichs decomposition for strain and stress on shells with boundary.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a geometric and cohomological foundation for linear shell elasticity that unifies compatibility, equilibrium, boundary conditions, and orthogonal decompositions in a single differential complex framework.

Method: Construction of a differential complex based on the linearized Gauss--Codazzi equations as compatibility conditions; use of sheaf cohomology to characterize rigidity; derivation of Airy potential representation from complex structure; identification of natural boundary conditions via adjoint operators; proof of Hodge--Morrey--Friedrichs decomposition using functional-analytic tools on manifolds with boundary.

Result: A well-defined elasticity complex for Kirchhoff--Love shells; characterization of its cohomology via infinitesimal rigid motions; existence and uniqueness of Airy potential for equilibrated stress; precise natural boundary conditions for displacement and traction; orthogonal decomposition of strain and stress fields satisfying tangential/normal constraints on the boundary.

Conclusion: The shell elasticity complex provides a unified, coordinate-invariant, and topologically informed framework that generalizes classical 2D elasticity results to curved surfaces, enabling rigorous analysis of compatibility, equilibrium, and boundary-value problems in thin-shell mechanics.

Abstract: We develop an elasticity complex for Kirchhoff--Love shell theory, based on the linearized Gauss--Codazzi equations as compatibility conditions for shell strains. The resulting shell complex forms a fine resolution of the sheaf of infinitesimal rigid motions, so its cohomology groups are determined by the corresponding sheaf cohomology. Remarkably, the complex also reveals an Airy potential representation of equilibrium stress fields. The framework further identifies natural boundary conditions for both kinematic and dynamic variables. Finally, we establish a Hodge--Morrey--Friedrichs decomposition for strain and stress fields on shells with boundary.

</details>


### [9] [Feshbach resonances of the second order in a Friedrichs model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28743)
*Dmitry S. Grudko,Alexander K. Motovilov*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies an extended matrix Friedrichs model capable of generating second-order Feshbach resonances; it identifies conditions under which two first-order resonances merge into a single second-order resonance, and characterizes the associated operator-root structure of the transfer function.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the emergence and merging of Feshbach resonances—particularly the transition from first- to second-order resonances—in an extended matrix Friedrichs model, motivated by spectral phenomena in quantum scattering and open quantum systems.

Method: Analytical spectral analysis of an extended matrix Friedrichs model; operator-theoretic interpretation of resonances via transfer-function zeros; study of eigenvalue trajectories and bifurcation behavior under variation of the coupling constant.

Result: Derivation of explicit conditions for the coalescence of two first-order resonances into a single second-order resonance; characterization of the nontrivial Jordan-block-like structure of operator roots of the transfer function at the merging point.

Conclusion: Second-order Feshbach resonances arise from a bifurcation of resonance trajectories governed by analytic properties of the transfer function; their operator roots exhibit degenerate, non-semisimple structure, reflecting the underlying spectral singularity.

Abstract: We consider an extended version of the matrix Friedrichs model that may produce Feshbach resonances of the second order. In this model, under enlarging of the coupling constant, a pair of eigenvalues of the unperturbed Hamiltonian embedded into the continuous spectrum first transforms in resonances of the first order. At some moment later on, these two resonances merge into a single resonance but of the second order, and we fix conditions for this event. Being based on the operator interpretation of resonances, we found that, in the case under consideration, the operator roots of the transfer function have a very particular structure.

</details>


### [10] [Pauli-consistent ensemble Monte Carlo method for bipolar charge transport in graphene](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28773)
*Tigran Zalinyan,Giovanni Nastasi*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a Pauli-consistent ensemble Monte Carlo method for simulating bipolar charge transport in graphene, incorporating phonon-assisted interband scattering and using a valence-vacancy representation to efficiently model electron–hole dynamics while preserving quantum statistics and charge conservation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable accurate, scalable, and physically faithful simulation of bipolar (electron–hole) transport in graphene—including interband processes like generation and recombination—while respecting Pauli exclusion and charge conservation, which standard Monte Carlo methods often violate or approximate poorly.

Method: A novel ensemble Monte Carlo method formulated in a valence-vacancy picture; explicitly includes phonon-assisted interband scattering; enforces discrete occupation bounds (0 or 1) during free flight and collisions; conserves net charge; validated via sensitivity/refinement studies and benchmarking against a deterministic discontinuous Galerkin solver of the same kinetic model.

Result: Excellent quantitative agreement between Monte Carlo and discontinuous Galerkin solutions across time evolution, carrier distribution functions, and steady-state transport observables; demonstrated computational feasibility for microscopic bipolar transport simulations in graphene.

Conclusion: The proposed method is both physically rigorous—respecting Pauli principle and charge conservation—and numerically robust and practical for large-scale kinetic simulation of bipolar quantum transport in graphene.

Abstract: We develop a Pauli-consistent ensemble Monte Carlo method for bipolar charge transport in graphene, with an explicit treatment of phonon-assisted interband scattering, including electron-hole generation and recombination. The method employs a valence-vacancy representation to describe coupled electron and hole populations without explicitly simulating the highly populated valence band. It preserves the discrete occupation bounds during both free flight and scattering while conserving net charge. The numerical properties of the method are assessed through sensitivity and refinement studies and comparison with an independent deterministic discontinuous Galerkin solution of the same kinetic model. The two approaches show very good agreement for the time evolution, carrier distributions, and steady-state transport observables across the investigated transport regimes. The results also demonstrate the computational practicality of the method for microscopic simulations of bipolar graphene transport.

</details>


### [11] [Trigonal Elliptic Coordinates on the Sphere and Some Applications](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28992)
*Vladimir Mikhalev*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs a theta-function-based parametrization of the unit sphere aligned with eight trigonal directions, derived from a cubic theta identity and expressible in Jacobi elliptic functions; it introduces both nonorthogonal and orthogonal angular coordinate systems linked to the Volterra chain, and applies them to simplify real spherical harmonics up to degree three in O'Brien's reduced Jahn–Teller model.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop geometrically adapted spherical parametrizations for trigonal symmetry—particularly relevant to Jahn–Teller physics—and to obtain compact, symmetry-aware representations of low-degree real spherical harmonics.

Method: Derives a theta-function parametrization of S² using a cubic theta identity (Bobek); reformulates it via Jacobi elliptic functions; constructs nonorthogonal coordinates (angular parameter + theta nome) whose squared Cartesian components satisfy the three-component Volterra chain; performs an algebraic angle transformation preserving the nome to obtain orthogonal coordinates with explicit metric and diagonal Laplace–Beltrami operator; applies both to O'Brien's reduced Jahn–Teller Hamiltonian.

Result: Explicit orthogonal spherical coordinates with diagonal Laplace–Beltrami operator; compact closed-form expressions for real spherical harmonics of degrees 2 and 3; identification of the orthogonal angle as the polar angle of an Eg quadrupolar doublet; discovery that one octupolar harmonic depends solely on the theta nome within each octant.

Conclusion: The theta-parametrization provides a natural, symmetry-compatible framework for trigonal problems in molecular and solid-state physics, enabling analytic simplification of angular dependence in Jahn–Teller models and spherical harmonics.

Abstract: We construct a theta-function parametrization of the unit sphere adapted to the eight trigonal directions. The construction follows from a cubic theta identity discussed by Bobek and admits an equivalent representation in Jacobi elliptic functions. The angular parameter and theta nome give a nonorthogonal coordinate system whose squared Cartesian components are related to the three-component Volterra chain. An algebraic change of angle, retaining the nome, yields orthogonal coordinates with explicit metric coefficients and a diagonal Laplace--Beltrami operator. We apply both parametrizations to the geometry of O'Brien's reduced Jahn--Teller model and give compact expressions for real spherical harmonics of degrees two and three. The orthogonal angle is the polar angle of an $E_g$ quadrupolar doublet, while one octupolar harmonic depends only on the nome within each octant.

</details>


### [12] [Spin-geodesic systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29005)
*Renato Feres,Zijie Hu*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: Introduces spin-geodesic systems—geometric dynamical systems on Riemannian manifolds coupling point motion to internal classical spin via a symmetric endomorphism field B; shows equivalence to abstract no-slip rolling and proves Liouville volume preservation despite non-symplecticity (except when B ≡ 0).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify and generalize geometric mechanical systems—including geodesic flows, magnetic flows, frame flows, and nonholonomic rolling—by introducing a spin-coupled deformation of geodesics governed by a symmetric endomorphism field B.

Method: Differential geometric and Hamiltonian mechanical analysis: constructs spin-geodesic systems via coupling a tangent vector flow with spin evolution governed by B; establishes equivalence to no-slip rolling in an ambient geometry defined by a B-dependent metric connection; computes phase-space volume form and verifies its invariance under the flow using Lie derivative and divergence-free properties of the Hamiltonian vector field.

Result: 1) Every spin-geodesic system is equivalent to a no-slip rolling system in an abstract ambient geometry; flatness of the B-induced connection recovers Euclidean rolling under integrability. 2) The spin-geodesic flow preserves the canonical Liouville volume on the mechanical phase space for arbitrary symmetric B—even though the flow is symplectic only if B ≡ 0.

Conclusion: Spin-geodesic systems provide a natural geometric framework unifying several classical mechanical models; their volume-preservation (Liouville property) holds broadly despite loss of symplectic structure, extending ergodic and statistical mechanical applicability beyond Hamiltonian settings.

Abstract: We introduce and study a class of geometric dynamical systems on Riemannian manifolds called spin-geodesic systems, in which the motion of a point is coupled to an internal classical spin through a symmetric endomorphism field $B$, the spin-coupling field. These systems may be viewed as $B$-dependent deformations of geodesic motion and have connections with frame flows, magnetic geodesic flows, and nonholonomic rolling systems. We show that every spin-geodesic system admits an equivalent interpretation as a no-slip rolling system in an abstract ambient geometry; when the associated metric connection, determined by $B$, is flat, this construction reduces, under the usual integrability hypotheses, to ordinary rolling on a Euclidean hypersurface. Our second main result is a Liouville theorem: for an arbitrary symmetric spin-coupling field B, the spin-geodesic flow preserves the canonical Liouville volume on its mechanical phase space. This holds even though the flow is not symplectic unless $B$ is identically zero.

</details>


### [13] [The marginal sine-Gordon field theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29024)
*Dyuman Bhattacharya*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: Constructs the 2D sine-Gordon vacuum at the critical coupling β²=8π using a lattice field with Dirichlet boundary conditions; establishes mass bounds, asymptotic freedom, uniqueness and relativistic invariance of the continuum vacuum, non-Gaussianity of field derivatives, nontrivial higher-order correlations, and universality for key observables and identities.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously construct the two-dimensional sine-Gordon model at the critical (Berezinskii–Kosterlitz–Thouless) coupling β²=8π, where standard perturbative and Gaussian approximations fail, and to establish its non-perturbative quantum field-theoretic properties including mass gap, universality, and operator structure in the continuum limit.

Method: Nonperturbative constructive QFT approach: (i) defines the model on a real noncompact lattice with nearest-neighbor kinetic term, positive cosine activity, and zero Dirichlet boundary data; (ii) uses renormalization group flow to determine bare parameters from prescribed low-energy response; (iii) proves spectral lower bound (mass gap) via energy spectrum analysis; (iv) verifies relativistic dilation symmetry, Hilbert space reconstruction via field derivatives, and non-Gaussianity via exponential moments and variance smearing.

Result: Rigorous construction of the 2D sine-Gordon vacuum at β²=8π; proof of uniform mass gap M satisfying c₋ ≤ M a_exit ≤ c₊; uniqueness and relativistic invariance of the continuum vacuum; non-Gaussian distribution of field derivatives; nonzero connected correlation of some finite even order >2 on separated supports; universality for higher harmonics, field equation, topological current, geometric stress tensor, and associated Ward/trace/contact identities; validated source-transfer theorem with explicit finite-scale conditions.

Conclusion: The critical 2D sine-Gordon model admits a well-defined, physically meaningful, and universal quantum field-theoretic realization with a mass gap, nontrivial operator content, and exact symmetries—resolving long-standing issues about its constructive status and infrared behavior at β²=8π.

Abstract: At $\beta_*^2=8\pi$ we construct the two-dimensional sine--Gordon vacuum from a noncompact real lattice field with nearest-neighbor kinetic energy, positive cosine activity and zero Dirichlet data. Its mass $M$ is the lower edge of the energy spectrum above the vacuum. In units of the length $a_{\rm exit}$ at which the flow leaves weak coupling it obeys $0<c_-\le M a_{\rm exit}\le c_+<\infty$ on compact positive endpoint intervals, with uniform constants. A prescribed response to a smooth background in a fixed square determines the nonlinear asymptotically free bare parameters at each sufficiently fine cutoff. In the local relativistic continuum theory the vacuum is unique, physical dilation realizes every positive mass, and literal derivatives generate the reflected Hilbert space even for finite composite and contact families. Variance and exponential moments of a derivative smear rule out a Gaussian law. A connected correlation of some finite even order above two is nonzero on separated supports. The construction gives universality for the specified finite nonnegative higher harmonics, the field equation, topological current and geometric stress tensor with their Ward, trace and contact identities, and a normalized source-transfer theorem with explicit finite-scale hypotheses.

</details>


### [14] [Path Integrals on Functional Lattices](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29045)
*Alexey A. Kryukov*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: Introduces 'functional lattices'—a continuum-space discretization scheme for QFT path integrals using localized basis expansions; proves convergence and regulator independence for free scalar fields via Galerkin analysis, extends to Minkowski space with iε-prescription, and validates perturbative convergence and renormalization behavior in φ⁴ theory.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a mathematically rigorous, regulator-independent continuum limit for path integrals without resorting to spacetime lattice regularization (e.g., Wilsonian lattices), preserving differential structure and enabling direct access to continuum operators and distributions.

Method: Expands quantum fields in discrete, complete, localized bases ('functional lattices') while keeping spacetime continuous; employs Galerkin approximation theory to prove strong operator convergence of finite-dimensional covariance operators and normalized generating functionals; handles interacting theories via pointwise and dominated convergence of perturbative graph integrands; implements numerical tests in 1D and 4D φ⁴ theory.

Result: Rigorous proof of convergence and regulator independence of the continuum limit for the massive free Euclidean scalar field; existence of a direct Minkowski construction with distributional iε → 0 limit; validated perturbative convergence up to second order in φ⁴; numerical recovery of known mass-gap coefficients (1D) and correct UV divergence/renormalization scaling (4D).

Conclusion: Functional lattices provide a viable, mathematically controlled framework for defining path integrals in continuous spacetime, bridging finite-dimensional approximations and continuum QFT without breaking differential or Lorentz structure, and supporting both analytic proofs and numerical implementation.

Abstract: Path integrals in quantum field theory are commonly approached through finite-dimensional approximations, but existence and regulator independence of the infinite-dimensional limit require separate analysis. We introduce a scheme in which spacetime remains continuous and the field is expanded in a discrete complete family of localized functions, called a functional lattice. Truncation to finite-dimensional functional subspaces yields ordinary finite-dimensional integrals while leaving the continuum differential operator unchanged. For the massive free Euclidean scalar field, we prove convergence of the finite-dimensional covariance operators and normalized generating functionals by a Galerkin argument. The limit is independent of the compatible functional lattice: any sequence of finite-dimensional subspaces dense in the quadratic-form domain converges to the same continuum covariance and generating functional. A direct Minkowski construction is obtained at fixed Feynman regulator $i\epsilon$, followed by the distributional limit $\epsilon\downarrow0$. For interacting theories, each truncation gives an ordinary non-Gaussian integral. At fixed perturbative order, convergence follows when the finite-resolution graph integrands converge pointwise and admit an integrable majorant independent of the functional resolution. Numerical results for one-dimensional $\phi^4$ theory approach the known first- and second-order mass-gap coefficients, while four-dimensional calculations exhibit the expected mass divergence and logarithmic wave-function renormalization. Thus functional lattices provide a continuum-space approach to path integrals with mathematically controlled limits in the cases considered here.

</details>


### [15] [A Novel Algebraic Approach to Medium-Range Solvable Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29351)
*Yuan Miao,Kohei Fukai*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper generalizes Jones' Baxterisation to construct solvable quantum spin chains with medium-range interactions using an extended Yang-Baxter equation and the chromatic algebra of 3-colouring; it yields four explicit models—including known ones (folded XXZ, hard-rod XXZ) and two new ones (three-state antiferromagnet, Rydberg-blockade ladder)—two of which exhibit non-invertible symmetries.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend integrability constructions beyond nearest-neighbor interactions by generalizing Jones' Baxterisation to medium-range quantum spin chains, leveraging algebraic structures (chromatic algebra, Rep(S₃)) that support richer interaction ranges and novel symmetries.

Method: Introduces an extended Yang-Baxter equation for R-matrices built from generators acting on two neighboring sites; constructs R and Lax operators from the chromatic algebra of 3-colouring; derives transfer matrices in involution up to boundary terms; uses Rep(S₃) equivalence to obtain four concrete representations yielding Hamiltonians.

Result: A non-difference-form solution to the extended Yang-Baxter equation; two families of medium-range solvable lattice Hamiltonians; four explicit models—two previously known (folded XXZ, hard-rod deformed XXZ) and two new (three-state antiferromagnet, Rydberg-blockade ladder); identification of non-invertible symmetries in two models.

Conclusion: Medium-range integrable spin chains can be systematically generated via algebraic Baxterisation using the chromatic algebra and its representation theory; this framework unifies known models and produces new solvable systems with exotic symmetries, broadening the scope of quantum integrability beyond nearest-neighbor interactions.

Abstract: We extend Jones' Baxterisation, which produces solutions to the Yang-Baxter equation directly from the generators of an algebra, to quantum spin chains with medium-range interactions. The key ingredient is an extended Yang-Baxter equation for R matrices that are built from the generators attached to two neighbouring sites. Together with the accompanying Lax operator, it gives rise to transfer matrices that are in involution up to boundary terms, exactly as in Jones' construction. We find a solution to the extended Yang-Baxter equation, of non-difference form, in terms of the generators of the chromatic algebra of 3-colouring, and obtain from it two families of medium-range lattice Hamiltonians. Thus, each representation of the algebra provides a medium-range solvable model. Using the relation between the chromatic algebra of 3-colouring and the fusion category $\mathrm{Rep}(S_3)$, we construct four such representations. They reproduce the folded XXZ model and the hard-rod deformed XXZ model, and give new medium-range models of a three-state antiferromagnet and of a Rydberg-blockade ladder. Two of the four models possess non-invertible symmetries.

</details>


### [16] [A thermodynamically consistent variational formulation and structure-preserving finite element methods for non-isothermal liquid--vapor flows with moving contact lines](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29390)
*Junkai Wang,Ting Xiao,Qiaolin He*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops thermodynamically consistent, structure-preserving finite element methods for non-isothermal liquid–vapor flows with moving contact lines, based on a variational formulation derived from nonequilibrium thermodynamics; it proves key physical properties (mass conservation, energy balance, entropy inequality, positivity/bound preservation) and validates the schemes in 2D/3D applications including droplet wetting and boiling.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct numerical methods for non-isothermal liquid–vapor flows with moving contact lines that rigorously respect fundamental thermodynamic principles—especially energy conservation and entropy production—while accurately capturing interfacial dynamics and thermal effects.

Method: Derives governing equations and boundary conditions via generalized Hamilton’s principle for nonequilibrium thermodynamics, based on dynamic van der Waals theory with gradient contributions and surface thermodynamics; develops two equivalent variational formulations (internal energy and Helmholtz free energy-based), each using distinct thermal variables (entropy density or temperature); constructs fully discrete finite element schemes using compatible spatial operators and discrete thermodynamic chain rules.

Result: Two thermodynamically consistent fully discrete finite element schemes are obtained; both provably satisfy: mass conservation, bound preservation for number density, strict positivity of temperature, exact total energy balance (including external work and heat exchange), and a temperature-weighted weak entropy inequality with nonnegative bulk and boundary entropy production; verified numerically in 2D/3D for droplet wetting, pool boiling, and droplet ejection.

Conclusion: The proposed variational framework and finite element schemes provide a robust, physically faithful foundation for simulating complex non-isothermal multiphase flows with moving contact lines, unifying thermodynamic consistency, structure preservation, and practical computability.

Abstract: In this paper, we present a variational formulation and structure-preserving finite element methods for non-isothermal liquid--vapor flows with moving contact lines. Based on the dynamic van der Waals theory with gradient contributions and surface thermodynamics, the governing equations and hydrodynamic boundary conditions are derived from a generalized Hamilton's principle for nonequilibrium thermodynamics. Two equivalent variational formulations based on internal energy and Helmholtz free energy are developed, using entropy density and temperature, respectively, as the primary thermal variable. Using the resulting weak forms, compatible spatial operators and discrete thermodynamic chain rules, we construct two thermodynamically consistent fully discrete finite element schemes. We rigorously prove the following properties for both fully discrete schemes: mass conservation, bound preservation for the number density, positivity of the temperature, an exact total energy balance incorporating external work and heat exchange, and a temperature-weighted weak entropy inequality with nonnegative bulk and boundary entropy production. Numerical experiments in two and three dimensions assess accuracy and structure preservation, with applications to non-isothermal droplet wetting, pool boiling and droplet ejection.

</details>


### [17] [Reconstruction and geometry of $4\times4$ quantum Sudoku](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29420)
*Fabio Deelan Cunden,Paolo Facchi,Roberto La Scala,Marilena Ligabò,Karol Życzkowski*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes 4×4 quantum Sudoku grids—arrays of rank-one projections satisfying orthogonality constraints across rows, columns, and blocks—and establishes necessary/sufficient conditions for block completion, uniqueness, reconstruction formulas (in quantum logic), unitary classification via overlap data, geometric parametrization by five glued squares, symmetry reduction to a disc with two classical points, and a measurement-induced embedding into a 7D polytope with classical Sudoku as vertices.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend classical combinatorial Sudoku to the quantum setting—specifically, understanding how quantum constraints (rank-one projections summing to identity in rows/columns/blocks) govern existence, uniqueness, geometry, and classification of 4×4 quantum Sudoku—and to bridge quantum information concepts (e.g., mutually unbiased bases, quantum logic) with discrete geometry and convex structure.

Method: Uses operator-theoretic analysis of rank-one projections in ℂ⁴; derives algebraic conditions for block completion via orthogonality and trace constraints; employs quantum logic to express reconstruction; classifies grids up to unitary equivalence using pairwise overlaps (inner products squared); constructs a five-square parameter space with side-diagonal identifications; computes quotient under classical Sudoku symmetries (permutations of rows/columns/blocks and relabeling); analyzes projective measurement onto first block’s basis to obtain fractional Sudoku and convex decompositions; embeds into a 7D polytope via moment-map-like coordinates.

Result: Necessary and sufficient conditions for completing two opposite 2×2 blocks into a full 4×4 quantum Sudoku; uniqueness of such completion; explicit reconstruction formulas in both matrix and quantum-logical forms; unitary equivalence fully determined by the 16 overlaps (mod phases), with nonzero count equaling grid cardinality; geometric model: five closed unit squares with one square’s sides identified to diagonals of the others; symmetry quotient is a closed disc containing exactly two classical Sudoku equivalence classes; measurement yields a fractional Sudoku that preserves the full unitary class and lies in the convex hull of ≤4 classical grids; natural embedding into a 7D polytope whose 12 vertices are the classical 4×4 Sudoku solutions.

Conclusion: The 4×4 quantum Sudoku forms a highly structured noncommutative generalization of classical Sudoku, governed by precise algebraic–geometric constraints. Its space admits an elegant low-dimensional parametrization, rich symmetry reduction, and faithful convex representation via classical instances—revealing deep connections between quantum measurement, convex geometry, and combinatorial design.

Abstract: We study the reconstruction and geometry of $4 \times 4$ quantum Sudoku, viewed as arrays of rank-one projections whose rows, columns and blocks decompose the identity. We give necessary and sufficient conditions for two prescribed opposite blocks to admit a completion, prove its uniqueness, and obtain explicit reconstruction formulas, also expressed in the language of quantum logic. The sixteen overlaps between these blocks determine the unitary equivalence class of the grid, while the number of nonzero overlaps equals its cardinality. Using a matrix representation, we parametrise the equivalence classes by five closed squares, with the sides of one square identified with the diagonals of the other four. The quotient by classical cell symmetries is a closed disc containing exactly two classical classes. Finally, measurement in the basis of the first block gives a fractional Sudoku that retains the full unitary class and is a convex combination of at most four classical grids. This measurement map embeds the five-square model into a seven-dimensional polytope whose vertices are the twelve classical Sudoku.

</details>


### [18] [Analytic continuation of the T- and S-matrices to unphysical sheets in a multichannel extension of the Friedrichs-Faddeev model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29441)
*Mikhail V. Kremenetsky,Alexander K. Motovilov*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper extends the Friedrichs-Faddeev model to matrix multichannel scattering, derives analytic continuation conditions for the T- and S-matrices onto unphysical energy sheets, and characterizes resonances as zeros of truncated physical-sheet scattering matrix eigenvalues.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize the Friedrichs-Faddeev model to multichannel matrix potentials and rigorously establish analytic continuation of scattering matrices onto unphysical Riemann sheets for resonance analysis.

Method: Analytic continuation theory applied to integral equations defining the T-matrix; derivation of explicit formulas linking unphysical-sheet T- and S-matrices to their physical-sheet counterparts via kernel constraints on the potential matrix.

Result: Sufficient conditions on potential matrix kernels ensuring analytic continuation of T- and S-matrices; explicit representation formulas for these matrices on unphysical sheets in terms of physical-sheet values; characterization of resonances as eigenvalue-zero points of truncated physical-sheet S-matrix.

Conclusion: Resonances in this matrix multichannel Friedrichs-Faddeev framework are precisely determined by spectral degeneracies (zero eigenvalues) of truncated scattering matrices evaluated on the physical sheet, enabling a purely physical-sheet computational route to resonance identification.

Abstract: We consider a matrix multichannel extension of the Friedrichs-Faddeev model. First, we formulate conditions on the kernels of the potential matrix that guarantee for the T-matrix (and then for the S-matrix) the existence of analytical continuation to unphysical energy sheets. We also obtain explicit representations for the analytically continued T- and S-matrices on unphysical sheets in terms of these matrices themselves, but only taken in the physical sheet. From the explicit representations obtained, it follows that the resonances on a certain unphysical sheet are nothing but the points at which the corresponding truncation of the scattering matrix in the physical sheet has an eigenvalue of 0.

</details>


### [19] [$μ$- and $q$-extension of special functions in quantum field theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29543)
*J. Blümlein,A.M. Gavrilik,U.Y. Lunha,O. Mykhailiv,C. Schneider*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs μ- and q-extensions of special functions (e.g., polylogarithms, harmonic sums, cyclotomic and root-valued iterative integrals) arising in perturbative QFT, showing μ-extension preserves function class (polynomially in μ), whereas q-extension yields genuinely higher transcendental functions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize special functions appearing in analytic calculations of single-scale QFT quantities—such as polylogarithms and nested sums—to settings with modified (anti)commutation relations, as expected in μ- and q-deformed quantum field theories.

Method: Systematic construction of μ- and q-extensions for iterative integrals (polylogarithms, Nielsen integrals, cyclotomic/root-valued letters) and their inverse-Mellin counterparts (harmonic sums, generalized/cyclotomic/nested binomial sums), analyzing structural preservation vs. transcendental escalation.

Result: μ-extensions preserve the algebraic class of all special functions except for square-root-valued letters; q-extensions generate new higher transcendental functions beyond standard iterated integral hierarchies.

Conclusion: The μ- and q-extensions provide distinct mathematical frameworks for deformed QFTs: μ-deformation maintains structural control via polynomial parameterization, while q-deformation inherently enriches transcendental structure—suggesting different physical implications for loop calculations in each setting.

Abstract: Analytic calculations up to single-scale quantities in perturbative quantum field theory result into special numbers and functions, which are generalizations as iterative integrals over special alphabets. In this way one obtains polylogarithms, Nielsen integrals, the iterated integrals over linear denominator terms, cyclotomic letters, letters induced by quadratic forms, and square-root valued letters. The inverse Mellin transform relates special nested sums to these quantities, which are harmonic sums, generalized harmonic sums, nested sums implied by quadratic forms, cyclotomic harmonic sums, and nested sums containing central binomials. We construct the $\mu$- and $q$-extensions of all these special functions. They are expected to occur in loop calculations within $\mu$- and $q$-extended quantum field theories via modified (anti)commutation relations. The $\mu$-extended special functions are quite different form the corresponding $q$-extended functions. While in the former case one observes class-preservation characterized by the polynomial $\mu$-parameter, except for the case of root-valued letters. In the latter case new higher transcendental functions are obtained.

</details>


### [20] [Interface currents of magnetized differential systems: quantization and bulk-interface correspondence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29719)
*Guillaume Bal*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes a bulk-interface correspondence for interface currents in magnetic insulators, extending the quantum Hall effect to non-elliptic systems with flat bands; it expresses the quantized interface current as a difference of bulk topological invariants (generalized Chern numbers or Hall conductivities) plus a boundary correction term, and analyzes stability and singularities near flat-band energies.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize the bulk-boundary correspondence beyond elliptic Hamiltonians to systems with flat bands (e.g., rotating shallow water, cold plasmas, pseudospin-one materials), where standard ellipticity fails but a Douglis–Nirenberg structure and spectral gap condition on an effective flat symbol hold.

Method: Uses functional analytic methods for self-adjoint differential operators with magnetic fields (Peierls substitution or minimal coupling in factorized form L*L+W); defines interface current via trace-class perturbation theory (Tr i[H,P]φ'(H)); employs Fredholm index theory and topological invariants—Kubo-Chern conductivity for nonzero field, Chern integral of field-free symbol for zero field, and a momentum-space Chern integral C across the wall; imposes freezing condition for non-elliptic case.

Result: Proves that the interface current σ_I is well-defined and quantized (2πσ_I = Fredholm index); derives bulk-interface formula 2πσ_I = J(a₊,B₊;α) − J(a₋,B₋;α) + C; computes J as either Kubo-Chern Hall conductivity (via generalized Landau level counting with extension correction) or Chern integral; shows stability under large perturbations; identifies divergence of current near flat-band energies due to Landau level accumulation.

Conclusion: A robust bulk-interface correspondence holds for broad classes of magnetic insulators—including non-elliptic systems with flat bands—where interface transport is governed by topological invariants of bulk symbols and a boundary correction; quantization persists under strong perturbations, but breaks down at flat-band energies where spectral accumulation occurs.

Abstract: This paper concerns transport along an interface between two insulators modeled by self-adjoint differential systems $H$ of arbitrary order, coupled to a magnetic field either by Peierls substitution or, for operators of the form $L^*L+W$, in the factor $L$. Each bulk carries a constant field, which may vanish. The principal symbol of $H$ is either elliptic or has a kernel of constant rank, in which case finitely many bands remain bounded at large momentum, as in rotating shallow water, cold plasmas, Maxwell's equations and pseudospin-one materials. Ellipticity is then replaced by a Douglis-Nirenberg structure and by a gap condition on an effective flat symbol. We show that, for every energy $\alpha$ away from the flat-band set and in a spectral gap of both bulks, the interface current $\sigma_I=\mathrm{Tr}\,i[H,P]\varphi'(H)$ is well defined, with $2\pi\sigma_I$ a Fredholm index, and that it is given, for all walls in the elliptic case and under a freezing condition otherwise, by a bulk-interface correspondence of the form $2\pi\sigma_I=J(a_+,B_+;\alpha)-J(a_-,B_-;\alpha)+\mathsf{C}$. For a nonzero bulk field, $J=2\pi\sigma_H$ is the Kubo-Chern Hall conductivity, which we compute by an extension-corrected count of generalized Landau levels. For a vanishing field, $J$ is the Chern integral of the field-free symbol, which is also, under a weak-field condition, the weak-field limit of the Hall conductivity. The term $\mathsf{C}$ is a Chern integral of the occupied projector at infinite momentum across the wall. The current is stable under large perturbations, and boundaries of the applied field inside a homogeneous material carry quantized currents that change at field-induced transitions. Near flat-band energies, where generalized Landau levels may accumulate, the current may diverge.

</details>


### [21] [Characterizing Ultrafast Processes via Work](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29778)
*Bernard Derrida,Michael Entov,Leonid Polterovich,Lenya Ryzhik*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies work fluctuations in mean-field systems undergoing a thermodynamic cycle (instantaneous parameter jump, relaxation, reverse jump), identifying a 'matching regime' where initial and final equilibrium macrostates coincide; there, average work per particle vanishes and total work fluctuations are non-Gaussian, whereas away from matching, average work is positive and fluctuations are Gaussian; the phenomenon is linked to Reeb chords in contact-geometric thermodynamics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To characterize the 'matching phenomenon'—where forward and reverse equilibrium macrostates coincide in the thermodynamic limit—in terms of work statistics, and to connect it to geometric thermodynamics via Reeb chords on Legendrian submanifolds.

Method: Analysis of scalar many-particle mean-field systems under suitable regularity and convexity assumptions; asymptotic analysis of work distribution in the thermodynamic limit; application of large deviations and central limit theorem frameworks; interpretation within contact geometry, mapping equilibrium states to Legendrian submanifolds and matching to Reeb chords.

Result: In the matching regime: (i) total work has a non-Gaussian limiting distribution; (ii) average work per particle → 0. Away from matching: (i) properly rescaled work fluctuations converge weakly to a Gaussian; (ii) average work per particle converges to a strictly positive constant. Matching corresponds geometrically to existence of a Reeb chord between two equilibrium Legendrian submanifolds.

Conclusion: The matching phenomenon induces a qualitative phase transition in work statistics—non-Gaussian vs. Gaussian fluctuations and vanishing vs. positive average work—revealing a deep link between statistical mechanics of mean-field systems and contact-geometric structure of thermodynamic state space.

Abstract: Consider the following cycle for a mean-field system initially in thermodynamic equilibrium: an instantaneous parameter jump, relaxation to the new equilibrium, and a reverse jump. For certain systems and parameter values, the two equilibrium macrostates coincide in the thermodynamic limit. We characterize this matching phenomenon through the work required to perform the cycle. Our main result concerns scalar many-particle mean-field systems under suitable assumptions. In the matching regime, the total work has a non-Gaussian limiting distribution, and the average work per particle tends to zero. Away from matching, the properly rescaled fluctuations of the total work have a Gaussian limiting distribution, and the average work per particle converges to a strictly positive constant. Further motivation arises from a contact-geometric model of thermodynamics, in which matching corresponds to a Reeb chord between two equilibrium Legendrian submanifolds.

</details>


### [22] [Modulation equations and coherence dynamics in a qubit chain coupled to a circulating fluxon](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29827)
*O.M.Kiselev*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives modulation equations for quantum amplitudes of a fluxon interacting with an exchange-coupled qubit chain, using a unitary passage operator and resonant normal form to separate slow dynamics from fast corrections; it quantifies coherence errors from ordered emission/return processes and bounds state error a posteriori, achieving high agreement (1.5×10⁻⁹) between approximate and full calculations at resonance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To model and control coherence degradation in a qubit chain induced by a moving fluxon, particularly how source profile and field memory affect internal qubit populations and coherence.

Method: Derivation of modulation equations for joint quantum amplitudes; construction of a unitary passage operator incorporating translational packet, vacuum dressing, and discrete oscillatory modes; application of resonant normal form theory to separate slow amplitude evolution from nonresonant corrections; perturbative analysis of ordered emission/return amplitudes and ground-band coefficients; a posteriori error bounding.

Result: A second-order correction from ordered emission/return modifies internal qubit population and coherence; the cubic ground-band approximation matches the expanded full calculation to 1.5×10⁻⁹ in tested observables; at velocity-selected resonance, mode contribution after 81 passages is ~6×10⁻⁸; error bounds are established a posteriori.

Conclusion: The derived equations accurately capture fluxon-induced decoherence mechanisms in exchange-coupled qubit chains, enabling predictive control of coherence through tailored source profiles and memory-aware field design.

Abstract: We derive modulation equations for the joint quantum amplitudes of a circulating fluxon and an exchange-coupled qubit chain whose endpoint interacts with the field. A unitary passage operator combines the translational packet, vacuum dressing, and discrete oscillatory modes. A resonant normal form separates the slow amplitude evolution from nonresonant corrections. Ordered emission and return amplitudes generate a second-order correction that changes the population and coherence of an internal qubit. The next ground-band coefficient gives compensating diagonal and near-resonant off-diagonal contributions to the accumulated error. We bound the state error a posteriori. At a velocity-selected resonance, direct propagation over 81 passages gives a mode contribution of order $6\times10^{-8}$; the cubic ground-band approximation agrees with an expanded full calculation to $1.5\times10^{-9}$ in the tested observables. The equations describe how the source profile and field memory affect coherence inside the chain.

</details>


### [23] [A decomposition of Hamiltonian matrices with symplectic kernels and its application to Williamson's diagonal form](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29967)
*Shaowu Huang,Yingxin Huang*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper generalizes a symplectic decomposition for Hamiltonian matrices with symplectic kernels and provides an alternative proof of Williamson’s diagonalization theorem for positive-semidefinite matrices possessing such kernels.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend Ikramov's earlier result on symplectic eigenvalues of positive definite matrices to a broader class—Hamiltonian matrices with symplectic kernels—and to offer a new derivation of Williamson's classical diagonal form.

Method: Constructs a symplectic decomposition for Hamiltonian matrices whose kernel is symplectic; leverages structural properties of Hamiltonian and symplectic matrices, including canonical forms and invariance under symplectic similarity.

Result: A novel decomposition theorem for Hamiltonian matrices with symplectic kernels is established; as a corollary, an independent, structure-based proof of Williamson’s diagonal form for positive-semidefinite matrices with symplectic kernels is obtained.

Conclusion: The decomposition unifies and generalizes prior results on symplectic spectral theory, reinforcing the centrality of symplectic geometry in the analysis of Hamiltonian and positive-semidefinite matrix structures.

Abstract: In this paper, we establish a decomposition of Hamiltonian matrices with symplectic kernels, which extends the main result in [K. D. Ikramov, On the Symplectic Eigenvalues of Positive Definite Matrices. Moscow Univ. Comput. Math. Cybern., 42(2018)1-4]. As an application, we present another proof of Williamson's diagonal form for positive-semidefinite matrices with symplectic kernels.

</details>


### [24] [Derivation of BCS functionals and gap equations from quantum many-body fermionic system](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30367)
*Xuwen Chen,Jiahao Wu,Zhifei Zhang*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes well-posedness (existence, uniqueness, and positivity) of solutions to discrete and continuous BCS gap equations for finite- and infinite-size 3D spin-1/2 Fermi systems with general dispersion and attractive interaction; proves emergence of SU(2) symmetry under quasi-freeness; and shows convergence of critical temperature and a positive energy/mass gap below T_c.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously justify the BCS theory for finite-size quantum Fermi systems—particularly addressing existence, uniqueness, and physical relevance of gap equation solutions, symmetry emergence, and thermodynamic limit behavior—beyond standard continuum approximations.

Method: Analytical study of the discrete BCS gap equation on finite lattices/bounded domains; fixed-point and monotonicity arguments for existence/uniqueness of maximal nonnegative solutions; symmetry analysis under quasi-free states; thermodynamic limit via functional convergence to the continuous BCS functional and gap equation.

Result: Existence of a positive maximal solution to the discrete BCS gap equation below a size-dependent T_c; uniqueness of any nonzero nonnegative solution; emergence of SU(2) symmetry in the finite system under quasi-freeness; rigorous derivation of the continuous BCS functional and gap equation in the infinite-volume limit; convergence of T_c; existence of a positive energy (mass) gap for T ∈ [0,T_c); confirmation that Cooper divergence arises from finite-to-infinite density-of-states scaling.

Conclusion: The BCS gap equation is mathematically well-posed both discretely and continuously for realistic finite-size Fermi systems; its nonzero solutions are physically meaningful (corresponding to many-body superconducting states), symmetric, and stable under the thermodynamic limit—thereby providing a rigorous foundation for BCS phenomenology including energy gaps and critical temperature behavior.

Abstract: We consider 3D single-species spin-$1/2$ Fermi systems with general dispersion relation and general attractive interaction initially inside a finite box. Up to some critical temperature $T_c$ determined by the interaction and system size, we establish, for the expected discrete BCS gap equation, existence of positive maximal solution. We then prove the uniqueness of nonzero (not identically zero) nonnegative solution. That is, any nonzero nonnegative solution, if exist or obtained via other methods, must equal to the positive maximal solution. Building on such a well-posedness result and assuming quasi-freeness, we prove the emergence of the $SU(2)$ symmetry of the finite system. Finally, letting the system size tend to infinity, we prove the emergence of the continuous BCS functional and gap equation for which we also have the uniqueness of nonzero nonnegative solution, and the convergence of the critical temperature. In particular, our work also implies there is a positive energy (hence mass) gap for aforementioned Fermi systems for $[0,T_c)$, any nonzero nonnegative solution for the gap equations are from the many-body system it suppose to describe, and the Cooper divergence of the density of states from a finite to an infinite system.

</details>


### [25] [Quantum walks and conformal mappings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30403)
*Fedor Kuyanov,Michael Drmota,Mikhail Skopenkov,Alexey Ustinov*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives uniform asymptotic formulae for the wave function of the discrete quantum walk (Feynman checkers), revealing Airy-function–governed oscillatory-to-exponential transitions and a novel gamma-function–governed transition near light speed, via conformal-mapping techniques applicable beyond this model.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To obtain a uniform asymptotic description of the wave function across the entire xt-plane for the elementary discrete quantum walk (Feynman checkers), capturing both known and previously unobserved transition regimes—particularly near relativistic speeds.

Method: Asymptotic analysis of integral representations of the wave function using conformal mappings; derivation of two general theorems yielding uniform asymptotics involving the Airy and gamma functions.

Result: A uniform asymptotic formula covering the whole xt-plane; identification of two distinct transition regimes: one governed by the Airy function (oscillatory ↔ exponential decay) and a new one governed by the gamma function (as particle speed approaches light speed).

Conclusion: The asymptotic structure of the quantum walk is richer than previously understood, with relativistic-speed behavior encoded by the gamma function; the developed conformal-mapping methodology yields broadly applicable asymptotic theorems.

Abstract: We study the most elementary discrete model of an electron moving along a line, known as Feynman checkers, quantum walk, or the six-vertex model with special complex weights, and provide a uniform asymptotic formula for the wave function in the whole xt-plane. In addition to the well-known appearance of the Airy function that describes the transition between the oscillating and exponentially decaying regimes, we observe a second transition governed by the gamma function when the electron speed approaches the speed of light. We prove two general theorems producing uniform asymptotic formulae involving the Airy and gamma functions, using conformal mappings associated with the underlying integral representations. They are applicable in several other situations as well.

</details>


### [26] [Uniform Ionization Bounds in Molecular Thomas--Fermi--Dirac--von Weizsäcker Theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30418)
*Shuang Wu*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proves a universal bound on the maximal number of electrons $ N $ that can be bound by $ K $ nuclei in the molecular Thomas--Fermi--Dirac--von Weizsäcker (TFDW) theory: $ N \leq Z + C_K $, resolving a longstanding ionization conjecture; it further establishes uniform bounds on screened potentials, electronic radii, and ionization energy for $ N \geq Z $.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the molecular large-charge ionization problem posed in Frank–Nam–Van Den Bosch (CPAM 2018), i.e., whether a fixed number $ K $ of nuclei can bind arbitrarily many electrons in the TFDW model.

Method: Rigorous variational analysis of the TFDW energy functional; use of screening estimates, kinetic energy inequalities (including the von Weizsäcker term), and comparison with the simpler Thomas--Fermi (TF) model; careful handling of multi-nuclear geometry and charge dependence.

Result: For any fixed $ K $, there exists $ C_K > 0 $ such that every TFDW minimizer with $ K $ nuclei and total nuclear charge $ Z $ satisfies $ N \leq Z + C_K $, independent of nuclear positions and individual charges; for $ N \geq Z $, the screened potential and electronic radius of TFDW minimizers are comparable to those of neutral TF minimizers, and the ionization energy is uniformly bounded.

Conclusion: The TFDW theory obeys a 'quantitative neutrality principle' at large $ Z $: excess electrons beyond $ Z $ are universally limited, confirming physical expectations of molecular stability and validating the model’s predictive power for ionization behavior.

Abstract: We study the molecular Thomas--Fermi--Dirac--von Weizsäcker theory. For each fixed number $K$ of nuclei, we prove that every minimizer of mass $N$ satisfies $N\leq Z+C_K$, where $Z$ is the total nuclear charge, and $C_K>0$ is independent of the nuclear charges and positions. This answers the molecular large-charge ionization question posed in [R. L. Frank, P. T. Nam and H. Van Den Bosch, CPAM, 2018]. For $N\geq Z$, we also compare the screened potentials and electronic radii of Thomas--Fermi--Dirac--von Weizsäcker minimizers with those of the corresponding neutral molecular Thomas--Fermi minimizers and establish a uniform bound on the ionization energy.

</details>

TL;DR: This paper studies perturbations of embedded eigenvalues in abstract Friedrichs–Lee Hamiltonians, proving the existence of resonance poles via contour deformation and contraction mapping on complex domains.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how embedded eigenvalues behave under perturbation in abstract Friedrichs–Lee Hamiltonians, particularly regarding resonance formation.

Method: Contour deformation in the complex plane combined with construction of a contraction mapping on complex domains.

Result: Existence of resonance poles is rigorously established for the perturbed system.

Conclusion: Resonance poles emerge from embedded eigenvalues under perturbation, confirmed via analytic continuation and fixed-point arguments.

Abstract: In this paper, we consider a perturbation problem of embedded eigenvalues for an abstract Friedrichs--Lee Hamiltonian and analyze its spectral properties. By deforming the contour and constructing a contraction mapping on complex domains, we prove the existence of resonance poles.

</details>


### [28] [Density of states for random Schrödinger operators with linearly correlated heavy-tailed disorder](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30529)
*Margherita Disertori,Luca Fresta,Liza Schonlau*
