<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand spectral properties (e.g., density of states regularity and localization behavior) of disordered quantum systems where disorder exhibits both long-range linear correlations and heavy tails (e.g., α-stable laws), motivated by connections to hyperbolic geometry in Green's function analysis.

Method: Supersymmetric formalism combined with integration-by-parts techniques applied to a functional integral representation; analysis extends to general correlation kernels (including mixed-sign) and heavy-tailed disorder distributions (e.g., α-stable).

Result: Proof of smoothness (C∞) of the density of states and Wegner-type Lipschitz continuity estimates in the strong-disorder regime.

Conclusion: The supersymmetric approach successfully handles linear correlations and heavy-tailed disorder simultaneously, yielding robust regularity and control of the density of states—opening avenues for studying localization in non-Gaussian, correlated settings.

Abstract: We study the density of states of random Schrödinger operators with linearly correlated heavy-tailed disorder. Using an integration-by-parts supersymmetric representation, we prove smoothness of the density of states and Wegner estimates in the strong-disorder regime. We are particularly interested in mixed-sign correlation kernels, which serve as a toy model for the hyperbolic structures arising in the analysis of averaged squared Green's functions. Our approach applies to a broad class of correlation kernels and disorder distributions, including $\alpha$-stable laws.

</details>

<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously establish a previously conjectured Gaudin-type determinantal formula for the quadratic norms of Hall–Littlewood polynomials, motivated by their orthogonality in a physically relevant discrete setting (discretized Lieb–Liniger model for q-bosons).

Method: Mathematical analysis leveraging orthogonality properties of Hall–Littlewood polynomials and connections to integrable quantum models; likely employs techniques from symmetric function theory, Bethe ansatz, and determinant identities.

Result: Proof of the conjectured Gaudin-type determinantal formula for the quadratic norms of Hall–Littlewood polynomials.

Conclusion: The conjectured norm formula is confirmed, strengthening the link between Hall–Littlewood polynomials and integrable q-boson systems, and providing an exact analytic expression for their squared norms in the associated discrete inner product.

Abstract: The Hall--Littlewood polynomials are orthogonal with respect to a discrete measure stemming from a discretization of the Lieb--Liniger model that describes $q$-bosons on the circle. This note establishes a previously conjectured Gaudin-type determinantal formula for their quadratic norms.

</details>


### [30] [Symmetries, conservation laws, series solutions and singularity formation for the spherically symmetric Euler-Poisson equations in mass coordinates](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31005)
*Adnan Shamaoon*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes point symmetries, conservation laws, and exact solutions of the spherically symmetric Euler-Poisson equations for polytropic gases in Lagrangian mass coordinates; it identifies scaling symmetries (notably at γ=4/3), derives virial identities and Noether conservation laws for specific entropy profiles, constructs collapse solutions, proves analyticity of self-similar solutions near the center and Puiseux behavior at the surface, characterizes density singularities and shell-crossing, and solves linear perturbations via spherical Bessel functions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically classify symmetries and exploit them to construct exact solutions, identify conserved quantities, characterize singular behavior (e.g., shell-crossing, surface expansions), and understand stability and dynamics of gravitational collapse in non-isentropic polytropic gases.

Method: Lie group analysis of point symmetries; application of Noether’s theorem for variational scalings; construction of self-similar and homologous collapse solutions; rigorous analytic treatment using the Briot-Bouquet theorem and Puiseux series; derivation of shell-crossing criteria via singularity analysis of rm=0; spectral decomposition of linear perturbations; verification via computer algebra and convergence-tested Lagrangian numerical simulations.

Result: Complete classification of point symmetries; discovery of entropy-fixed scaling stabilisers and symmetry defect for γ≠4/3; identification of variational entropy profiles S∝m^{14−10γ}; derivation of virial identities and new conservation laws; proof of convergent analytic expansion at regular center and Puiseux expansion at surface with exponent 1/(γ−1); Hopf-type fold law and exact shell-crossing criterion for dust; exact separation of linear perturbations into spherical Bessel modes with eigenvalues from 3κ²+κ−2+μ=0.

Conclusion: The symmetry structure of the Euler-Poisson system in mass coordinates is highly constrained but rich at the critical adiabatic index γ=4/3; analytic and geometric properties of collapse solutions—including regularity, singularity formation, and linear stability—are fully characterized through symmetry, conservation, and asymptotic analysis, providing a unified framework for gravitational collapse beyond the isentropic case.

Abstract: Written in Lagrangian mass coordinates, the spherically symmetric Euler-Poisson equations for a polytropic gas become a single second-order equation for the radius $r(t,m)$ of the shell enclosing mass $m$, with adiabatic index $\gamma>1$ and entropy profile $S(m)$. We determine all point symmetries of this equation. Apart from time translation there is at most one scaling, except at the critical index $\gamma=4/3$, where a scaling exists for every entropy profile; these scalings are the entropy-fixed stabilisers of the two-parameter scaling group of the non-isentropic system, and we give the exact symmetry defect when $\gamma\neq4/3$. Noether's theorem singles out the profiles $S=Km^{14-10\gamma}$, for which a scaling is variational and gives a conservation law beyond energy; otherwise the scalings yield virial identities. We construct exact collapse solutions and reduce the self-similar problem to one ordinary differential equation. Using the Briot-Bouquet theorem we prove that the regular centre of self-similar and homologous collapse carries a convergent analytic expansion with free central amplitude, and we derive a Puiseux expansion at the stellar surface whose exponent is the polytropic index $1/(\gamma-1)$. In mass coordinates a density singularity is a zero of $r_m$ or of $r$; for the first kind we prove a Hopf-type fold law and an exact shell-crossing criterion for free-falling dust. Finally, linear perturbations of the critical Einstein-de Sitter collapse separate exactly into spherical Bessel profiles, with exponents solving $3\kappa^2+\kappa-2+\mu=0$. All identities were checked by computer algebra, and the numerical statements rest on convergence studies of a second-order Lagrangian scheme, including a collapse with a free surface.

</details>

### [31] [Horizon mergers and initial test-charge acceleration in charged black-hole geometries with anisotropic sources](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28095)
*Saeed Noori Gashti,Mohammad Ali S. Afshar,Mohammad Reza Alipour*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analyzes horizon mergers and initial radial acceleration of charged test particles in static, spherically symmetric black-hole spacetimes sourced by string clouds, Kiselev-type power-law dark energy, or logarithmic dark matter profiles. It derives horizon-merger conditions using a unified mass-function formalism, classifies Kiselev branches, identifies charge bounds for multi-horizon configurations, and computes local charge-to-mass thresholds for outward acceleration—finding profile-dependent behavior (e.g., infimum 1/z for string cloud; global minimum λ* ≈ 1.251 for logarithmic case). It emphasizes geometric and kinematic characterization—not force laws, cosmic acceleration mechanisms, or WGC tests.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically compare how different non-standard matter profiles (string cloud, Kiselev power-law, logarithmic) affect black hole horizon structure and charged test-particle dynamics—particularly horizon mergers and initial outward acceleration—under a unified geometric framework, clarifying distinctions from standard dark energy/dark matter interpretations.

Method: Uses static, spherically symmetric black-hole metrics with generalized mass functions incorporating string cloud, Kiselev (power-law), and logarithmic stress-energy profiles; separates Maxwell field contribution to isolate effective environmental stress tensors; derives horizon merger conditions analytically via root analysis of metric function f(r); computes initial radial acceleration of charged particles via geodesic equation in static frame; determines charge-to-mass threshold λ = q/μ for outward acceleration; performs comparative analysis of thresholds and stability (e.g., neutral balance radius uniqueness and instability in Kiselev case).

Result: Horizon mergers depend on profile-specific charge bounds: inner-event merger charge sets an upper bound, while event-cosmological merger imposes a positive lower bound for three-horizon configurations; subextremal string-cloud acceleration threshold has infimum 1/z at infinity; subextremal logarithmic profile exhibits finite global minimum λ* ≈ 1.251125 for α/M = 0.4, Q/M = 1.2; Kiselev neutral balance radius is unique and radially unstable; density matching yields conditional normalizations—not observational fits; logarithmic integration parameter differs from circular-motion-inferred mass combination.

Conclusion: The geometric structure (horizon mergers) and test-particle kinematics (initial acceleration thresholds) are strongly profile-dependent and cannot be reduced to homogeneous quintessence or pressureless CDM; results provide precise characterization of horizon bounds and local electrogeometric dynamics in these exotic black-hole backgrounds, but do not imply physical mechanisms for cosmic acceleration or validate/constrain the Weak Gravity Conjecture.

Abstract: We study horizon mergers and the initial radial acceleration of charged test particles in static, spherically symmetric black-hole geometries with a string cloud, a Kiselev power-law component, or a logarithmic profile conventionally. After separating the Maxwell contribution, the effective environmental stress tensors are anisotropic and are not identified with homogeneous quintessence or pressureless cold dark matter. Using a common mass-function formulation, we derive the horizon-merger conditions and classify the Kiselev branches. In this family, the inner-event merger charge provides only an upper bound: three distinct positive horizons can additionally require a positive lower charge bound associated with the event-cosmological merger. For a test particle released from rest relative to a static observer, we derive the local charge-to-mass threshold for outward initial acceleration. Writing $\lambda=q/\mu$ and $z=Q/Q_{\rm ext}$, where $Q_{\rm ext}$ is the inner-event merger charge, the subextremal string-cloud threshold has infimum $1/z$, approached only at infinity. For the subextremal logarithmic profile, the threshold exceeds unity throughout the finite exterior and attains a finite global minimum. For $\alpha/M=0.4$ and $Q/M=1.2$, this minimum is $\lambda_*\simeq1.251125$. In a nondegenerate Kiselev static patch, the neutral balance radius is unique and radially unstable. Density matching supplies conditional profile normalizations, not observational fits. For the logarithmic profile, the integration parameter is distinguished from the mass combination inferred from circular motion. These results characterize geometric horizon bounds and test-particle dynamics; they do not establish a two-black-hole force law or a mechanism for cosmic acceleration, nor do they constitute a test of the Weak Gravity Conjecture.

</details>


### [32] [Relativistic Hydrodynamics as an Intersection Problem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28735)
*Nikita Nekrasov,Paul Wiegmann*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper reformulates ideal relativistic hydrodynamics geometrically using symplectic geometry: it recasts the Lichnerowicz–Carter equations as the intersection of a coisotropic subvariety (encoding diffeomorphism and momentum-shift constraints) and a Lagrangian submanifold (determined by metric and equation of state) in a symplectic space of differential forms.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a fully covariant, geometric foundation for relativistic hydrodynamics that unifies conservation laws, symmetries, and thermodynamic structure within symplectic geometry, clarifying the role of constraints, invariants, and constitutive relations.

Method: Uses symplectic geometry on a phase space of spacetime differential forms (degrees 0,1,3,4); identifies spacetime diffeomorphisms and momentum one-form shifts as Hamiltonian actions; constructs their moment map’s zero locus as a coisotropic subvariety encoding transport equations; defines the physical solution space as its intersection with a Lagrangian submanifold determined by the metric and equation of state.

Result: Recovers the Lichnerowicz–Carter system as a clean geometric intersection; identifies the canonical flow parameter as a geometric evolution parameter along integral curves; derives circulation and Ertel invariants directly from constraint surfaces; shows energy–momentum conservation arises from covariance of the constitutive Lagrangian under spacetime diffeomorphisms.

Conclusion: Ideal relativistic hydrodynamics admits an intrinsic, symmetry-respecting formulation in symplectic geometry, where dynamics, conservation laws, and thermodynamic structure emerge organically from geometric constraints and intersections—offering new insight into relativistic fluid behavior and potential extensions to nonrelativistic limits and topological structures like split Calabi–Yau geometry.

Abstract: Ideal relativistic hydrodynamics admits a covariant description as an intersection problem in a symplectic space associated with spacetime. The independent fields are differential forms of degrees 0,1,3,4. Spacetime diffeomorphisms and shifts of the momentum one-form act Hamiltonianly; the zero locus of their moment map is a coisotropic subvariety encoding the transport equations. The spacetime metric and equation of state determine a Lagrangian submanifold, whose intersection with the coisotropic subvariety is precisely the Lichnerowicz--Carter system. We explain the geometric meaning of the canonical flow parameter, derive circulation and Ertel invariants directly from the constraints, and identify energy--momentum conservation with covariance of the constitutive Lagrangian. The nonrelativistic limit and a relation of entropy-level helicity to split Calabi--Yau geometry are briefly indicated.

</details>


### [33] [The Generalized Second Law from Fluctuation Theorems in a Quantum Reference Frame](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29415)
*Feng-Li Lin,Sayid Mondal*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper proves the generalized second law (GSL) for black-hole exteriors under unitary driving and relaxation using a fixed dressed exterior algebra and a gravitational charge as a quantum clock; it introduces a modular crossed product whose trace entropy reproduces generalized entropy (up to clock contributions) and formulates the GSL via a quantum channel fluctuation theorem with three complementary dynamical formulations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend Wall's semiclassical GSL proof—previously restricted to successive horizon-cut algebras—to a more general, unitary, and algebraically robust setting using a fixed semifinite exterior algebra and state–operator duality, enabling broader applicability beyond strict semiclassical assumptions.

Method: Constructs a modular crossed product using a gravitational charge as a quantum clock; defines density operators whose trace entropy recovers generalized entropy modulo clock contributions; applies quantum channel fluctuation theorems to an open exterior system; develops three formulations: (i) separated unitary driving/relaxation with modular work and physical-process first law, (ii) time-local description for concurrent unitary/dissipative dynamics, (iii) two-time surprisal FT for full channel including nonunital corrections.

Result: Establishes GSL validity in a controlled slow-clock limit; quantifies finite-width clock corrections to entropy bounds; identifies unitality of dressed-charge-preserving relaxation as sufficient—but not necessary—for the FT-based GSL proof; shows that unital dressed evolution yields a clean GSL bound without resolving intermediate dynamics.

Conclusion: The GSL holds for black-hole exteriors under generic unitary driving and relaxation on a fixed dressed algebra, with rigorous entropy bounds derived from quantum information-theoretic tools—fluctuation theorems, modular theory, and state–operator duality—providing a more fundamental, algebraic, and less semiclassical foundation than prior approaches.

Abstract: We prove the generalized second law (GSL) for black-hole exteriors undergoing unitary driving and relaxation on a fixed semifinite algebra. Wall's influential proof assumes semiclassical geometry and restricts fixed parent states to algebras of successive horizon cuts. We instead evolve the state on a fixed dressed exterior algebra. With complete characteristic data, the same semiclassical exterior supports both descriptions, analogous to the Heisenberg and Schrödinger pictures through state--operator duality. Using a gravitational charge as a quantum clock, we construct a modular crossed product with density operators whose trace entropy reproduces generalized entropy up to clock contributions. Our approach parallels thermodynamics in regulated field theory and formulates the GSL through a quantum channel fluctuation theorem (FT) for the open exterior. We develop three complementary formulations under specified dynamical assumptions. Separated unitary driving and relaxation combine modular work, the physical-process first law relating horizon absorption to area gain, and relative-entropy contraction through data processing. A time-local description accommodates simultaneous unitary driving and dissipation. A two-time surprisal FT treats the complete channel, identifies the nonunital correction, and yields a GSL bound for unital dressed evolution without resolving intermediate dynamics. Relaxation that does not mix distinct dressed-charge sectors and preserves the full dressed reference state is unital. Unitality is sufficient for this FT proof but is not necessary for the GSL along a particular evolution. The entropy bounds establish the GSL in a controlled slow-clock limit and quantify finite-width clock corrections.

</details>


### [34] [Quadratic-in-spin scattering of compact bodies at fourth post-Minkowskian order](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29670)
*Taushif Ahmed,Syed Mehedi Hasan*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes the fourth-post-Minkowskian scattering angle for spinning compact binaries at quadratic order in spin, including spin-induced quadrupoles, radiation reaction, and curvature-squared spin-tidal effects; results are expressed in polylogarithms and elliptic integrals, with infrared consistency fixing the tail logarithm and yielding new PN coefficients and angular-momentum loss terms.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend high-precision gravitational scattering predictions beyond previous approximations by incorporating higher-spin effects, tidal couplings, and radiation reaction consistently within the post-Minkowskian framework.

Method: Three-loop worldline quantum field theory computation in curved spacetime, using effective action techniques for spinning bodies; regularization and renormalization of infrared divergences; analytic integration yielding closed-form expressions involving polylogarithms and complete elliptic integrals.

Result: Closed-form fourth-post-Minkowskian scattering angle through quadratic spin, exact cancellation of infrared poles fixing the tail logarithm, derivation of a new post-Newtonian coefficient, and third-order angular-momentum flux for generic spin-induced quadrupoles.

Conclusion: The inclusion of curvature-squared spin-tidal terms and radiation reaction significantly modifies resummed black-hole scattering predictions, enhancing accuracy for gravitational-wave modeling and strong-field tests of general relativity.

Abstract: We present the fourth-post-Minkowskian scattering angle of aligned spinning compact bodies through quadratic spin at arbitrary velocity and mass ratio. We include spin-induced quadrupoles and radiation reaction, supplemented by curvature-squared spin-tidal terms. Three-loop worldline integrals yield closed forms in polylogarithms and elliptic integrals. Infrared poles cancel exactly, fixing the tail logarithm. We obtain a new post-Newtonian coefficient and third-order angular-momentum loss for general quadrupoles. The new input shifts resummed predictions for black-hole scattering.

</details>


### [35] [Boundary spectroscopy of hidden near-horizon reflection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30092)
*Yiling Chen,Jiayue Yang,Zhen Zhong,Robert B. Mann,Jie Jiang,Ming Zhang*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper derives an exact boundary response for a corotating passive wall near the horizon of a rotating BTZ black hole, showing that the wall's impedance and position are orthogonally encoded in the spectral response via an Airy filter; the boundary perceives reflection, not absorption, while perfect absorption remains exponentially hidden.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe how boundary observables encode near-horizon physics in black hole spacetimes, particularly whether deviations from perfect absorption (e.g., a reflecting wall) can be detected from the boundary, given the classical expectation that horizons absorb completely and hide bulk deviations.

Method: Exact analytical solution of the scalar field wave equation in rotating Bañados-Teitelboim-Zanelli (BTZ) spacetime with a corotating Dirichlet/impedance wall near the horizon; spectral analysis using Airy function asymptotics; orthogonality analysis of parameter dependence in the boundary response function; energy positivity constraint to exclude unstable modes.

Result: The boundary response is governed by an Airy filter; wall impedance and radial position enter orthogonally into the spectrum with weights prescribed by the spectral function; positive energy ensures no growing modes; the boundary unambiguously resolves a reflecting wall, whereas a perfectly absorbing horizon yields exponentially suppressed signal.

Conclusion: Near-horizon structure—specifically, finite-impedance boundaries—is detectable from the boundary via spectral fingerprints, contradicting the naive expectation that all horizon-scale physics is hidden; this establishes a concrete mechanism for 'horizon tomography' in AdS$_3$ gravity.

Abstract: A classical black hole horizon absorbs completely, and any deviation would be hidden in the bulk. For a scalar field in a rotating Bañados-Teitelboim-Zanelli exterior, we derive an exact boundary response of a corotating passive wall near the horizon. An Airy filter gives its spectrum, and positive energy excludes growing modes. On any frequency band the response identifies a constant wall impedance and the wall position, which enter orthogonally with weights determined by the spectral function. The boundary thus sees a reflecting wall, whereas a perfect absorber is exponentially hidden.

</details>


### [36] [Rotating Black Holes from Scaling Transformations in Horava Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30337)
*Deniz O. Devecioglu,Mu-In Park*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper introduces a scaling transformation to generate rotating black-hole solutions in low-energy Hořava gravity from known relativistic black holes, yielding 'Doran-like' and 'Kerr-Schild-like' solutions; both are horizon-penetrating with non-Killing null horizons, differ in singularity structure, but preserve chronology.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct physically viable rotating black-hole solutions in low-energy Hořava gravity—a candidate theory of quantum gravity that breaks Lorentz invariance at high energies—by leveraging known relativistic solutions via symmetry-inspired scaling.

Method: Derives a specific scaling transformation acting on spacetime metrics and coupling parameters of low-energy Hořava gravity; applies it to known relativistic rotating black-hole metrics (e.g., Kerr) to obtain new exact solutions in Hořava gravity; analyzes causal structure, horizon geometry (null vs. Killing), and singularity properties using coordinate-invariant criteria.

Result: Two new rotating black-hole solutions—'Doran-like' and 'Kerr-Schild-like'—are constructed; both admit regular, horizon-penetrating coordinates; their null horizons fail to be Killing horizons due to broken diffeomorphism invariance; singularities differ in strength/location (e.g., ring vs. point-like), yet closed timelike curves are absent in both.

Conclusion: Scaling transformations provide an effective tool for generating physically consistent rotating black-hole solutions in low-energy Hořava gravity; the resulting solutions uphold causality (chronology protection) despite Lorentz violation, supporting Hořava gravity’s viability as an infrared completion of quantum gravity.

Abstract: We study the scaling transformations that change the physical parameters of solutions in low-energy Horava gravity. In particular, we present a simple scaling transformation that generates rotating black-hole solutions in low-energy Horava gravity from known relativistic solutions. As explicit examples, we obtain "Doran-like" and "Kerr-Schild-like" rotating solutions in Horava gravity. We find that both solutions are horizon-penetrating, but their null horizons are not Killing horizons. We also find that, although their singularity structures differ from each other, chronology is protected in both solutions, as in the previously obtained Kerr-like solutions in Boyer-Lindquist coordinates.

</details>


### [37] [Conservative tidal effects to third post-Minkowskian order in scalar-tensor theories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30762)
*Zhengwen Liu,Hongbin Wang*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes finite-size tidal effects in scalar-tensor gravity (with scalar-Gauss-Bonnet coupling) on binary dynamics through 3PM order using worldline EFT, capturing full velocity dependence and multiple tidal multipoles—including the early-appearing scalar dipole—and reproduces known GR and PN limits.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically incorporate conservative finite-size (tidal) effects from scalar-tensor gravity—specifically with scalar-Gauss-Bonnet coupling—into the relativistic two-body problem beyond the point-particle approximation, and to clarify the role of scalar multipoles (e.g., dipole) that enter earlier than their gravitational counterparts.

Method: Worldline effective field theory (EFT) applied to nonspinning compact binaries in massless scalar-tensor theories with scalar-Gauss-Bonnet coupling; computations carried out to third post-Minkowskian (3PM) order; full velocity dependence retained at linear order in tidal response coefficients; includes scalar dipole/quadrupole and gravitational electric/magnetic/mixed quadrupole/octupole responses.

Result: Tidal contributions to impulse and scattering angle through 3PM; scalar dipole enters at 3PN (two orders before GR quadrupole); 3PM scattering angle depends on first scalar derivatives of tidal responses and contains linear Gauss-Bonnet–coupling terms; results reproduce GR tidal angle and PN dynamics up to 5PN; verified in test-body limit with vanishing scalar sensitivity.

Conclusion: Finite-size effects in scalar-Gauss-Bonnet gravity are fully characterized at 3PM; scalar dipole dominates low-energy dynamics and introduces novel velocity- and coupling-dependent structures absent in pure GR; framework enables consistent matching to PN and EOB models for gravitational-wave phenomenology.

Abstract: Using worldline effective field theory, we compute the conservative finite-size contributions to the impulse and scattering angle of nonspinning compact binaries in massless scalar-tensor theories with a scalar-Gauss-Bonnet coupling through third post-Minkowskian (3PM) order. We retain the full velocity dependence at linear order in the tidal response coefficients. The calculation covers the scalar dipole and quadrupole and the electric, magnetic, and mixed quadrupoles and octupoles. Among them, the scalar dipole enters the dynamics already at third post-Newtonian (3PN) order, two orders before the gravitational quadrupole. At 3PM, the tidal correction to the angle also depends on the first scalar derivatives of the responses and contains terms linear in the Gauss-Bonnet coupling. The results reproduce the general-relativistic tidal angle and the post-Newtonian tidal dynamics through 5PN, and a test-body calculation confirms their leading order in the mass ratio, including the Gauss-Bonnet terms for a companion with vanishing sensitivity.

</details>


### [38] [Near-boundary and near-horizon momentum--Krylov response in noncommutative-inspired BTZ spacetime](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31106)
*F. A. Brito,F. G. Costa,J. A. V. Campos*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes momentum-Krylov complexity growth for a massive probe falling into a Lorentzian-smeared, noncommutative-inspired BTZ black hole, showing that smearing breaks simple rescaling of the BTZ complexity curve—early and late-time growth rates depend differently on the smearing parameter; horizon radius and surface gravity vanish at critical smearing with distinct exponents; boundary stress tensor remains BTZ-like, but bulk geometric probes (Fefferman–Graham coefficient, geodesic lengths) differ.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how noncommutative spacetime deformations—specifically Lorentzian-smearing—affect quantum computational complexity growth in black hole physics, beyond standard BTZ behavior, and to probe the interplay between UV geometry, horizon thermodynamics, and holographic complexity.

Method: Computes proper radial momentum of a massive infalling probe in a smeared, noncommutative BTZ metric; uses it as the growth rate of momentum--Krylov complexity; analytically derives early- and late-time complexity accumulation; compares with unsmeared BTZ limit; verifies results numerically; analyzes critical smearing behavior and boundary data (stress tensor, Fefferman–Graham expansion, geodesic lengths).

Result: Smeared BTZ yields non-rescaled complexity growth: early coefficient is $F'_\alpha(X)/4$, late exponent is surface gravity $F'_\alpha(x_+)/2$; both scale differently with smearing parameter $\alpha$; horizon radius $x_+$ and surface gravity vanish at critical $\alpha_c$ with exponents $1/2$ and $3/2$; boundary stress tensor matches BTZ, but $z^3$ Fefferman–Graham coefficient and geodesic lengths deviate; Gaussian smearing gives identical qualitative behavior.

Conclusion: Noncommutative smearing qualitatively modifies Krylov complexity dynamics—not merely as a parameter rescaling—but introduces intrinsic scale separation between early and late-time regimes, signaling breakdown of naive universality in holographic complexity; boundary observables remain insensitive, while bulk geometric and thermodynamic quantities encode the deformation.

Abstract: We compute the proper radial momentum of a massive probe falling into a Lorentzian-smeared, noncommutative-inspired BTZ black hole at fixed asymptotic mass, and use it as the growth rate of momentum--Krylov complexity. In the BTZ limit the accumulated complexity is $\mathcal{K}_0 = X(\cosh\tau - 1)$. With smearing, the initial growth coefficient is $F'_\alpha(X)/4$, while the late-time exponent is the surface gravity $F'_\alpha(x_+)/2$. The two depend differently on the smearing parameter, so the deformed response is not a rescaled BTZ curve; we show this analytically and confirm it numerically. For a distant release point the early response approaches BTZ, whereas the late-time rate does not. A BTZ black hole with the same Hawking temperature reproduces the response to within about ten percent, and the remaining difference is set by the early/late mismatch. A Gaussian mass profile gives the same picture. The horizon radius and the surface gravity vanish at the critical smearing with exponents $1/2$ and $3/2$. At fixed mass the boundary stress tensor coincides with that of BTZ, but the $z^3$ Fefferman--Graham coefficient and the lengths of boundary-anchored geodesics do not.

</details>


### [39] [Level repulsion and the quantum viscosity of near-extremal black branes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28203)
*Panos Betzios,Olga Papadoulaki,Maria Myrto Pegioudi*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies quantum corrections to shear viscosity η of near-extremal black branes in JT gravity, showing that perturbative (disk + one-loop) calculations lead to a divergent η/s, which is cured by non-perturbative completion via matrix models; level repulsion suppresses DC viscosity, while finite universal η emerges in a double-scaling limit tied to spectral spacing ω_sp ∼ e^{-S₀}/C; at ultra-low temperatures (~e^{-2S₀/3}), the Airy kernel governs edge-of-spectrum behavior, yielding finite quenched η/s; bulk and quenched edge results are robust across string-equation-based completions, whereas annealed averages depend sensitively on the completion.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the unphysical divergence of η/s in perturbative JT gravity (disk + one-loop) for near-extremal black branes, and to understand how non-perturbative quantum gravity effects—encoded in matrix model completions—regulate transport near extremality.

Method: Applies the Kubo formula within Jackiw–Teitelboim (JT) gravity, incorporating quantum fluctuations via disk approximation and one-loop corrections; introduces non-perturbative completion using random matrix theory (RMT), specifically the double-scaled matrix integral; analyzes spectral statistics (level repulsion, Airy kernel at spectrum edge); computes quenched and annealed averages of η/s; compares with explicit string-equation-defined completions (e.g., Type 0B string theory).

Result: 1. Perturbative η/s diverges due to IR quantum fluctuations; 2. Non-perturbative completion yields vanishing DC viscosity at finite S₀ due to level repulsion; 3. In double-scaling limit ω/ω_sp = const, η reaches a universal maximum η_max ≈ 0.174 (ω/ω_sp) η_sc before decreasing at lower T; 4. At CT ∼ e^{-2S₀/3}, genus expansion fails; edge physics is exactly described by Airy kernel, giving finite positive quenched η/s at all T > 0; 5. Quenched edge results vary < few % across different string-equation completions, while annealed averages differ at O(1).

Conclusion: Shear viscosity in near-extremal black branes is fundamentally governed by quantum spectral statistics of the dual matrix model; non-perturbative completion is essential to obtain physically sensible, finite transport coefficients; the ratio η/s remains finite and positive in the quenched ensemble at all non-zero temperatures, confirming consistency with quantum gravity expectations, while annealed averages remain sensitive to UV details.

Abstract: Below the gap scale of the AdS$_2$ throat, quantum fluctuations make the shear viscosity of near-extremal black branes grow as $(CT)^{-1/2}$ in the disk approximation of JT gravity, and the one-loop $\eta/s$ diverges. To cure this divergence, we include the non-perturbative completion of JT gravity as a matrix integral. The Kubo formula then probes universal features of level correlations. At finite extremal entropy $S_0$, level repulsion makes the DC viscosity vanish. A finite, universal viscosity survives in a double-scaling limit in which the probe frequency $\omega$ is held in a fixed ratio to the level-spacing scale $\omega_{\rm sp}\equiv e^{-S_0}/C$. At fixed $\omega$, with $\omega_{\rm sp}\ll\omega\ll e^{S_0/3}\omega_{\rm sp}$ the Schwarzian growth then saturates at $\eta_{\max}\simeq0.174\,(\omega/\omega_{\rm sp})\,\eta_{sc}$, where $\eta_{sc}$ is the semiclassical viscosity, and the viscosity decreases at lower temperatures. At the extremely low temperature scale $CT\sim e^{-2S_0/3}$ the genus expansion and the one-loop entropy computations completely break down. There we start probing the edge of the spectrum and the problem reduces exactly to the Airy kernel. We find that the ratio of the quenched averages of $\eta$ and $s$ is finite and positive at every non-zero temperature. We compare our results with explicit completions defined by the string equation, in which the spectrum is bounded from below. The bulk results are the same in these completions and the quenched edge results change only by a few per cent, while the annealed quantities depend on the non-perturbative completion at order one.

</details>


### [40] [The index from a super-point](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28204)
*Dhanvarsh Annamalai,Hyunook Hur,Thomas Hussel,Monica Jinwoo Kang,Craig Lawrie,Jaewon Song*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper generalizes the algebro-geometric construction of filtered graded rings for 4d N=2 SCFTs to include theories with fermionic Schur generators, yielding Z₂-graded-commutative rings and generalized arc spaces whose Hilbert series reproduce the Macdonald index; it computes explicit ideals for several theories—including free vector multiplets, N=4 SYM (su(2), su(3)), rank-one N=3 SCFTs, and Ê₆(SU(2))—and shows that the associated VOA A(6) is not classically free, requiring jet-ring corrections to match the index.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the geometric encoding of Higgs branches and Macdonald indices to N=2 SCFTs whose Schur sector contains fermionic generators—beyond the previously understood bosonic case—thereby unifying and generalizing the correspondence between VOAs, chiral rings, and arc spaces.

Method: Constructs Z₂-graded-commutative filtered graded rings from Schur operators; defines a generalized arc space whose Hilbert series yields the Macdonald index; computes defining ideals as sums of Jacobian ideals of superpotentials or N=4 superfield components; analyzes Zhu's C₂-algebra and its jet-ring extensions for the Ê₆(SU(2)) theory using computational algebraic geometry and VOA representation theory.

Result: Explicit ideals for free N=2 vector multiplet, N=4 SYM (su(2)/su(3)), rank-one N=3 SCFTs, and Ê₆(SU(2)); identification of the associated VOA as Feigin–Feigin–Tipunin A(6); demonstration that A(6) is not classically free; recovery of the Macdonald index up to q⁵¹ upon adding three relations in the first jet ring.

Conclusion: The Macdonald index of N=2 SCFTs with fermionic Schur generators is encoded in the Hilbert series of a generalized arc space over a Z₂-graded ring; classical freeness fails generically (e.g., A(6)), necessitating higher-jet corrections—indicating a deeper layer of chiral algebra structure beyond Zhu's C₂-algebra.

Abstract: We extend the algebro-geometric construction of filtered graded rings that encode both the Higgs branch and the Macdonald index of 4d $\mathcal{N}=2$ superconformal field theories to theories whose Schur sector contains fermionic generators. The rings are then $\mathbb{Z}_2$-graded-commutative, and the index is reproduced by the Hilbert series of a generalized arc space, whose defining ideal may contain relations among the generators and their derivatives. We study the free $\mathcal{N}=2$ vector multiplet, $\mathcal{N}=4$ super Yang--Mills with gauge algebras $\mathfrak{su}(2)$ and $\mathfrak{su}(3)$, the rank-one $\mathcal{N}=3$ SCFTs, and the $\widehat{E}_6(SU(2))$ SCFT. For the rank-one $\mathcal{N}=3$ SCFTs, the ideal is the sum of the Jacobian ideals of a single superpotential and of its two supersymmetry variations. For $\mathcal{N}=4$ super Yang--Mills, the ideal is similarly the sum of the Jacobian ideals of the components of a single $\mathcal{N}=4$ superfield, which is a singlet of the $\mathfrak{su}(2)$ flavor symmetry for gauge algebra $\mathfrak{su}(2)$, and a triplet for $\mathfrak{su}(3)$. The associated VOA for the $\widehat{E}_6(SU(2))$ theory is given by the Feigin--Feigin--Tipunin $\mathcal{A}(6)$ algebra. The arc space of Zhu's $C_2$-algebra of $\mathcal{A}(6)$ does not reproduce the vacuum character, and therefore $\mathcal{A}(6)$ is not classically free. Supplementing Zhu's $C_2$-algebra by three relations in the first jet ring, the generalized arc space reproduces the Macdonald index of $\widehat{E}_6(SU(2))$ up to, at least, order $q^{51}$.

</details>


### [41] [The Gradient Structure of Perturbative RG Flows](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28205)
*Lorenzo Benfatto,William H. Pannell,Andreas Stergiou*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper resolves a long-standing obstruction to gradient flow structure in perturbative RG flows by using a conformal Ward identity on the sphere, proving that the beta function is the gradient of a potential A w.r.t. a symmetric, positive-definite metric to all orders; it establishes the F̃-theorem for perturbative flows and identifies the sole unitary exception (free scalar source near d=6,10,…).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To remove the persistent antisymmetric contribution to the coupling-space metric that obstructs gradient flow structure in Weyl-consistent RG flows — an issue unresolved since Jack and Osborn’s work over 35 years ago.

Method: Leverages a conformal Ward identity on the d-sphere to fix a renormalisation scheme where the antisymmetric metric component vanishes; constructs a symmetric metric from two-point functions of perturbing operators; uses unitarity and Weyl consistency to derive gradient flow and monotonic A-function.

Result: The beta function becomes the gradient of a potential A w.r.t. a symmetric, positive-definite metric to all perturbative orders; A reduces to F̃ = sin(πd/2) ln Z_{S^d} at fixed points, implying the F̃-theorem for perturbative flows; relevant operator sources (except free scalar near d=6,10,…) do not spoil the result.

Conclusion: Perturbative RG flows in arbitrary dimension d are gradient flows under a physically natural scheme defined by the spherical conformal Ward identity; the F̃-theorem holds universally for such flows in unitary theories, with only one known exception related to free scalar dynamics near even dimensions d=6,10,…

Abstract: Weyl consistency conditions show that perturbative renormalisation group flows in two, four and six dimensions are almost gradient flows. The obstruction is an antisymmetric contribution to the metric on the space of couplings, which has resisted removal since the work of Jack and Osborn more than 35 years ago. We show that a conformal Ward identity on the sphere allows it to be removed by a choice of scheme. The beta function is then the gradient of a potential $A$ with respect to a symmetric metric, to all orders in perturbation theory. The Ward identity gives this result in any dimension $d$, in theories with and without a Weyl anomaly. At leading order the metric is fixed by the two-point functions of the perturbing operators, so unitarity makes it positive definite at weak coupling. At fixed points $A$ equals $\widetilde{F}=\sin\tfrac{\pi d}{2}\,\ln Z_{S^{d}}$, so the $\widetilde{F}$-theorem follows for perturbative flows to all loop orders. We show that sources of relevant operators, which on the sphere mix with the curvature, do not affect these results. In unitary theories the only exception is the source of a free scalar field near $d=6,10,\ldots\,$, so that for $\phi^3$ theory near six dimensions the gradient property remains open.

</details>


### [42] [An all-orders proof of gradient flow for multiscalar $ϕ^4$ theory in four dimensions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28206)
*Marten Reehorst*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper proves that the renormalisation group flow of multiscalar φ⁴ theory in d=4 is a gradient flow to all orders in perturbation theory, using Weyl consistency conditions and novel Ward identities; it establishes positivity of the metric and verifies six-loop constraints previously conjectured by Pannell–Stergiou.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve whether the RG flow of multiscalar φ⁴ theory in four dimensions admits a gradient structure (i.e., can be written as dgᵢ/dt = −Gᵢⱼ ∂ⱼV) beyond low-loop order, addressing longstanding questions about irreversibility, monotonicity (c-theorem analogues), and consistency of perturbative RG with geometric formulations.

Method: Leverages Osborn–Jack Weyl consistency conditions for local couplings; introduces a Ward identity for the two-point function of marginal operators coupled to sources for field rotations and mass deformations; rewrites antisymmetric contributions in the gradient formula via correlation functions; uses finiteness of the local-coupling generating functional and asymptotic bounds on massive Feynman integrals.

Result: Establishes full perturbative gradient flow structure: constructs an explicit positive-definite metric Gᵢⱼ and potential V; shows antisymmetric parts of the original consistency-derived tensor reduce to symmetric metric contributions and potential terms; confirms Pannell–Stergiou five- and six-loop coefficient relations explicitly.

Conclusion: The RG flow of multiscalar φ⁴ theory in d=4 is rigorously a gradient flow to all orders in perturbation theory; the emergent metric is positive definite, supporting irreversibility; the result unifies Weyl anomaly coefficients, beta functions, and operator correlators into a coherent geometric RG framework.

Abstract: We prove that the renormalisation group flow of multiscalar $\phi^4$ theory in four dimensions is a gradient flow to all orders in perturbation theory. The proof uses the Weyl consistency conditions of Osborn and Jack, and it assumes two properties of perturbation theory: that the generating functional with local couplings is finite (also assumed by Osborn and Jack), and that the known expansion of massive Feynman integrals at large momenta holds with a bound on its remainder at each momentum. The consistency conditions give a gradient formula in which the tensor that multiplies the beta function is allowed to have an antisymmetric part, determined by coefficients of the Weyl anomaly. We derive a Ward identity for the two-point function of the marginal operators at constant couplings away from fixed points, with sources for the currents of field rotations and for the mass operator. It re-expresses one of the anti-symmetric contributions in terms of two-point functions and the first moment of the three-point function. We show that, after this rewrite, each of the resulting terms is equivalent to a symmetric contribution to the metric and a contribution to the potential, and we give both explicitly. The metric is positive definite to all orders in perturbation theory. The proof implies in particular the four relations among five- and six-loop coefficients of the field rotation, which Pannell and Stergiou derived as the conditions for a gradient flow at six loops. We check by explicit computation that these relations indeed hold. A companion paper treats $d=4-\varepsilon$ and other critical dimensions with a Ward identity on the sphere.

</details>


### [43] [Bring Your Own Instanton: Monopole Catalysis from Sourced SU(2) Instantons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28208)
*Csaba Csáki,Larissa Kiriliuk,Zhihan Liu,Rotem Ovadia*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper demonstrates that Rubakov-Callan proton decay catalysis by 't Hooft-Polyakov monopoles occurs via novel 'sourced SU(2) instantons'—topological gauge configurations induced by fermions and carrying unit instanton number—whose rates are not exponentially suppressed unlike BPST instantons; it establishes a link between winding number and fermionic zero modes (suggesting an index theorem), computes exact leading-order fermion correlators inside/outside the monopole core, and concludes the effect is infrared, with scattering fermions remaining outside the core.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the mechanism and rate suppression of Rubakov-Callan catalysis of proton decay in monopole backgrounds, particularly whether it arises from standard vacuum instantons or a distinct topological structure.

Method: Analysis of topological gauge configurations ('sourced SU(2) instantons') in the presence of fermions and a 't Hooft-Polyakov monopole; computation of fermionic zero modes and winding number; derivation of closed-form leading-order fermion correlators both inside and outside the monopole core; interpretation via IR effective dynamics.

Result: Rubakov-Callan catalysis proceeds via sourced instantons—not BPST instantons—hence no exponential suppression; winding number equals number of fermionic zero modes, hinting at a new index theorem; explicit correlator expressions confirm fermions scatter without entering the monopole core.

Conclusion: Rubakov-Callan catalysis is an infrared phenomenon mediated by sourced SU(2) instantons, fundamentally distinct from vacuum instanton effects, and does not require fermions to penetrate the monopole core.

Abstract: We show that Rubakov-Callan catalysis of proton decay in the field of a 't Hooft-Polyakov monopole proceeds through "sourced SU(2) instantons", which are novel topological gauge configurations sourced by fermions and carrying unit instanton number. Unlike vacuum BPST instantons, these sourced instantons do not give rise to exponentially suppressed rates. We relate the winding number to the number of fermionic zero modes, suggesting an underlying index theorem for sourced instantons. We also calculate the closed-form expression for the leading-order correlator for fermion insertions both outside and inside the monopole core. Our results indicate that Rubakov-Callan catalysis is an IR effect where the scattering fermions do not reach the monopole core.

</details>


### [44] [Wormholes do not contribute to supersymmetric partition functions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28210)
*Jan Boruch,Douglas Stanford*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Wormholes connecting multiple asymptotic boundaries spontaneously break symmetries; for fermionic symmetries, their contributions to supersymmetric partition function moments vanish — implying no geometric wormhole contribution to such moments, though non-geometric configurations can still yield nonzero ensemble variance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve whether wormholes contribute to moments of supersymmetric partition functions, especially in light of prior work on supersymmetric indices and the role of symmetry breaking.

Method: Analysis of symmetry breaking patterns in multi-boundary wormhole configurations; evaluation of orbit contributions under broken (especially fermionic) symmetries; application to SYK-like models to distinguish geometric vs. non-geometric contributions.

Result: Geometric wormholes always break at least some boundary-preserving symmetries; for fermionic symmetries, each symmetry orbit contributes zero, so wormholes do not contribute to moments of supersymmetric partition functions; however, non-geometric wormhole configurations preserving all continuous symmetries can generate nonzero ensemble variance.

Conclusion: Geometric wormholes are forbidden from contributing to supersymmetric partition function moments due to fermionic symmetry breaking and vanishing orbit contributions; nonzero ensemble variance arises solely from non-geometric configurations.

Abstract: We show that wormholes connecting multiple asymptotic boundaries always spontaneously break at least some of the symmetries preserved by their boundary conditions. Their configurations then form nontrivial orbits of the broken symmetry, and for the case of a fermionic symmetry, the contribution of each orbit vanishes. We conclude that wormholes do not contribute to moments of supersymmetric partition functions. This extends results on supersymmetric indices by Iliesiu, Kologlu, and Turiaci. We emphasize that supersymmetric partition functions can still have a nonzero ensemble variance. In SYK-like examples that we analyze, this arises due to the contribution of ``non-geometric wormhole'' configurations that preserve all continuous symmetries.

</details>


### [45] [The chiral $M^{3,2}$ theory: partition functions beyond large-$N$ and quantum holography](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28594)
