Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper identifies families of Bethe vacua in the chiral $M^{3,2}$ theory with $N^{3/2}$-scaling free energies, derives all-order perturbative $1/N$ expansions, links baryonic magnetic flux to vacuum degeneracy at a special Chern–Simons coupling, and proposes an all-order Airy formula for the $S^3$ partition function—validated via four-derivative and one-loop holographic tests.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand large-$N$ behavior and holographic duals of 3D $\mathcal{N}=2$ supersymmetric gauge theories, particularly how distinct Bethe vacua contribute to the partition function and how their structure matches predictions from AdS$_4$ gravity.

Method: Analytic and high-precision numerical analysis of the Bethe ansatz equations; systematic $1/N$ expansion of the free energy; asymptotic evaluation of the three-sphere partition function in both 't Hooft and Cardy limits; comparison with supergravity on AdS$_4 \\times M^{3,2}$ including higher-derivative and quantum corrections.

Result: Closed-form all-order $1/N$ expansions for free energies of Bethe vacua scaling as $N^{3/2}$; identification of baryonic magnetic flux as the quantum number distinguishing vacua at mixed Chern–Simons coupling; derivation of an all-order Airy-function expression for $Z_{S^3}$; precise matching of four-derivative and one-loop supergravity corrections.

Conclusion: The $M^{3,2}$ theory exhibits rich vacuum structure consistent with holography; the Airy formula and $N^{3/2}$ scaling confirm its interpretation as the dual of M-theory on AdS$_4 \\times M^{3,2}$, with quantum gravity corrections fully captured by field-theoretic data.

Abstract: We identify families of Bethe vacua in the chiral $M^{3,2}$ theory whose individual free energies grow as $N^{3/2}$. High-precision data support closed forms for their all-order perturbative $1/N$ expansions. At a distinguished mixed Chern--Simons coupling, the baryonic magnetic flux distinguishes members of this family of vacua. We also study the three-sphere partition function numerically in the 't Hooft limit and Cardy limit, and propose an all-order Airy formula. The four-derivative and one-loop corrections are precision tests of holography.

</details>


### [46] [Massive Particles in Carrollian Holography](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28780)
*Bin Chen,Zezhou Hu,Reiko Liu,Wen-Jie Ma*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs a 3D Carrollian holographic description of a massive scalar field in 4D flat spacetime, introducing a bulk-to-boundary integral transform that respects Poincaré symmetry, encodes mass via infinite primary states, imposes local boundary constraints via null-state decoupling, and yields correlators and OPE data—enabling future links between massive amplitudes and Carrollian correlators.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how massive particles are encoded in boundary operators within Carrollian holography—a key open problem due to the tension between mass, infinite-dimensional symmetry, and absence of bulk spin.

Method: Constructs a three-dimensional Carrollian boundary theory for a four-dimensional massive scalar; derives an explicit bulk-to-boundary integral transform; analyzes representation theory (primary states and descendants); identifies null-state decoupling as equivalent to local boundary constraints; computes two- and three-point functions and formal OPE coefficients; handles Lorentzian signature via analytic continuation in complex momentum space.

Result: Nonzero mass necessitates infinitely many linearly independent primary states; descendant relations induced by zero spin yield a local differential constraint on boundary fields, equivalent to null-state decoupling; the integral transform preserves full Poincaré symmetry and maps bulk scalar data to constrained boundary fields; explicit 2- and 3-point correlators and formal OPE coefficients are obtained; analytic continuation to complex momenta is essential for Lorentzian signature.

Conclusion: The construction provides the first concrete, symmetry-respecting Carrollian holographic encoding of a massive scalar, establishing a foundational framework for connecting massive scattering amplitudes to boundary correlators and for bulk reconstruction from Carrollian data.

Abstract: Understanding how massive particles are encoded in boundary operators is a central problem in Carrollian holography. We construct a three-dimensional Carrollian description of a massive scalar in four-dimensional flat spacetime, together with an explicit bulk-to-boundary integral transform. In this construction, nonzero mass requires infinitely many linearly independent primary states, while the absence of bulk spin imposes relations among their descendants. We show that these relations are equivalent to null-state decoupling and give a local constraint on the boundary fields. The integral transform preserves the full Poincaré symmetry and maps bulk scalar data to fields satisfying this constraint. We also obtain two- and three-point functions and the associated formal operator product expansion coefficients. The integral transform evaluates bulk data at complex momenta, making analytic continuation essential in Lorentzian signature. This construction provides a concrete starting point for relating massive scattering amplitudes to Carrollian correlators and for reconstructing bulk fields from boundary data.

</details>


### [47] [Thermodynamics of hard wall model of AdS/QCD at finite chemical potential with Gauss-Bonnet corrections](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28884)
*Dharm Veer Singh,Shobhit Sachan*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates the confinement/deconfinement transition in a hard-wall AdS/QCD model at finite quark density within five-dimensional Einstein–Maxwell–Gauss–Bonnet gravity, incorporating back-reaction; it derives analytic transition lines, analyzes how Gauss–Bonnet coupling affects transition temperature and phase structure, and computes density-dependent ρ-meson properties in the confined phase.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the hard-wall AdS/QCD model to finite quark density with consistent thermodynamic treatment, including gravitational back-reaction and higher-curvature (Gauss–Bonnet) corrections, and to reconcile predicted deconfinement temperatures with lattice QCD while probing mesonic properties in dense confined matter.

Method: Analytic thermodynamic analysis of charged Gauss–Bonnet black hole (deconfined phase) versus thermally charged, IR-cutoff AdS (confined phase); derivation of transition conditions via free energy comparison; determination of infrared gauge field boundary condition as function of Gauss–Bonnet coupling α; WKB approximation for meson spectra in the confining background.

Result: Closed-form transition lines at fixed quark chemical potential and fixed quark number density; coexistence region at fixed density; negative α raises Tc and extends confined phase; α within causality bound yields Tc(μ=0) ≈ lattice value (~155 MeV); ρ-meson and radial excitation masses increase with density (~1% rise at nuclear density), explained by reduced effective confining size via WKB.

Conclusion: Gauss–Bonnet curvature corrections provide a controllable deformation that improves phenomenological agreement with lattice QCD and enables quantitative study of cold, dense confined matter; the model supports a robust confined phase up to nuclear densities with calculable, density-dependent hadronic observables.

Abstract: We study the confinement/deconfinement transition of the hard wall model of AdS/QCD at finite quark density in five-dimensional Einstein--Maxwell--Gauss--Bonnet gravity, with the back-reaction of the quark matter on the geometry included. The deconfined phase is a charged Gauss--Bonnet black hole, and the confined phase is thermally charged AdS cut off in the infrared. The infrared value of the gauge field, which Lee, Park and Sin fixed by requiring a consistent quark number, becomes a function of the Gauss--Bonnet coupling. We obtain the transition line in closed form at fixed quark chemical potential and at fixed quark number density. At fixed density the transition passes through a region where the two phases coexist. A negative Gauss--Bonnet coupling raises the transition temperature and extends the confined phase to higher density. A value inside the causality bound gives a transition temperature at zero density close to the lattice value. In the dense confined phase we compute the masses and decay constants of the $\rho$ meson and its radial excitations. All masses increase with density. A WKB analysis relates the increase to a shorter effective size of the confining region, and at nuclear density the $\rho$ mass rises by about one percent.

</details>


### [48] [Finite-Temperature Stabilization of a KKLT Modulus in Three-Dimensional Lattice Simulations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29000)
*Saya Mahmoudi,Furkan Utku Biber,Hyukjung Kim,Heeseung Zoe*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper uses 3D Langevin lattice simulations to show that cosmological stabilization of a KKLT modulus can fail locally despite global (spatially averaged) capture—due to tachyonic amplification of long-wavelength fluctuations near the unstable barrier.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess whether homogeneous KKLT modulus stabilization remains valid in the presence of spatial inhomogeneities, thermal effects, dissipation, and stochastic noise during early-universe evolution.

Method: Three-dimensional Langevin lattice simulations incorporating an evolving thermal bath, friction (dissipation), spatial gradients, and Gaussian white noise; supplemented by linear Langevin theory and discretization convergence tests.

Result: Near the critical capture threshold, trajectories linger near the unstable barrier (~1 e-fold), enabling tachyonic growth of long-wavelength fluctuations; localized regions cross the barrier even as the spatially averaged field recollapses—revealing a narrow stochastic layer where barrier-crossing fraction is discretization-independent.

Conclusion: Spatially averaged modulus capture does not ensure local stability; stochastic inhomogeneities can induce irreversible barrier crossing in subregions, challenging the robustness of KKLT stabilization in realistic cosmological settings.

Abstract: We investigate the cosmological stabilization of a KKLT modulus through three-dimensional Langevin lattice simulations incorporating an evolving thermal bath, dissipation, spatial gradients, and stochastic fluctuations. While the spatially averaged modulus closely follows homogeneous evolution away from the capture threshold, near-critical trajectories linger near the unstable potential barrier for approximately one e-fold, allowing long-wavelength fluctuations to undergo tachyonic amplification and drive localized regions across the barrier even as the mean field turns back toward the stabilized vacuum. Linear Langevin theory reproduces the fluctuation growth, while convergence tests identify a narrow stochastic layer in which the barrier-crossing fraction shows no systematic dependence on the discretization at a fixed effective distance from the threshold. These results demonstrate that capture of the spatially averaged modulus does not guarantee that every spatial region remains within the stabilizing barrier.

</details>


### [49] [Exact stress-tensor fluctuations from conformal symmetry and black hole radiation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29060)
*José Navarro-Salas,Roberto Balbinot,Alessandro Fabbri*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes local stress-tensor fluctuations in 2D semiclassical gravity using conformal symmetry, showing relative fluctuations scale as 1/c; results support semiclassical validity for large central charge c.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess the reliability of semiclassical gravity by quantifying stress-tensor fluctuations—essential because semiclassical gravity assumes small fluctuations around the expectation value of the stress tensor.

Method: Leverages 2D conformal symmetry to exactly determine local fluctuations of null stress-tensor components T_±±; defines T² via operator product expansion with point-splitting parameter α; uses conformal mapping to locally inertial coordinates to obtain variance in closed form.

Result: Relative fluctuations scale as 1/c; explicit variances computed at infinity (22/5c), horizon outgoing (−44α/3c), and horizon ingoing ((238+720α)/5c); all reduce to 22/5c for Möbius-covariant prescription (α = −3/10).

Conclusion: For large central charge c, stress-tensor fluctuations are parametrically suppressed, validating the semiclassical approximation in 2D black hole backgrounds.

Abstract: Semiclassical gravity is sourced by the expectation value of the stress tensor, and it is reliable only if the fluctuations around this value are small. In two dimensions, conformal symmetry captures the essential physics of Hawking radiation in its s-wave sector and determines the mean value exactly, for any background and any quantum state. We show that it also determines the local fluctuations of the null components $T_{\pm\pm}$. The square of the stress tensor is defined by the operator product expansion, up to a multiple $\alpha$ of $\partial_\pm^2T_{\pm\pm}$ that labels point-splitting prescriptions. Its transformation law under the conformal map to locally inertial coordinates gives the variance in closed form. The relative fluctuation scales as $1/c$, with $c$ the central charge. For the Schwarzschild black hole it is $22/5c$ for the Hawking flux at infinity, that of flat-space thermal radiation. At the horizon it is $-44\alpha/3c$ for the outgoing flux and $(238+720\alpha)/5c$ for the ingoing flux that drives the backreaction. Both reduce to $22/5c$ for $\alpha=-3/10$, which corresponds to the Möbius-covariant prescription. For large $c$ these results support the semiclassical approximation.

</details>


### [50] [Symmetry resolved spread complexity for BMN matrix model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29425)
*Dibakar Roychowdhury*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies symmetry-resolved spread complexity in the BMN matrix model under a U(1) subgroup of SO(6), analyzing cTFD dynamics across confined (low-T, gapped) and deconfined (finite-T, continuum) phases; it finds distinct Lanczos coefficient scalings, charge-dependent complexity deviations, and holographic interpretations linking spread complexity to string vs. black-brane physics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To quantify how global symmetry decomposition affects quantum complexity growth in a controlled gauge/gravity duality setting—specifically, to probe whether symmetry resolution reveals phase-sensitive scrambling signatures and holographic distinctions between horizonless and black-brane geometries.

Method: Decomposes the charged thermofield double (cTFD) state into fixed-U(1)-charge sectors; computes return amplitude, spectral moments, and Lanczos coefficients (a_n^{(q)}, b_n^{(q)}) for spread complexity; analytically models spectral density in both low-temperature confined (gapped, harmonic-oscillator-like) and finite-temperature deconfined (thermal Gaussian) regimes; incorporates charge-dependent Boltzmann weights, vacuum shifts, and degeneracies; interprets results via Lin-Maldacena droplets and D0-brane black hole horizons.

Result: In the low-T confined phase: diagonal Lanczos coefficients drift linearly (a_n^{(q)} ∝ n), off-diagonal ones scale as √n with thermal suppression; spread complexity launch is sub-quadratic; ΔC(t) ≥ 0 due to inter-sector coherence. In the finite-T deconfined phase: a_n^{(q)} = E̅_q (constant), b_n^{(q)} = σ_E√n (universal), and C^{(q)}(t) ∼ σ_E²(q,T) t² at early times. Charge dispersion and sector degeneracies break complexity equipartition.

Conclusion: Symmetry-resolved spread complexity serves as a sensitive diagnostic of quantum chaotic phase structure in matrix models, distinguishing coherent (stringy) from dissipative (black-hole-like) dynamics; its sector-resolved scaling laws and positivity bounds encode both microscopic spectral features and emergent holographic geometry.

Abstract: We investigate the symmetry-resolved spread complexity of the Berenstein-Maldacena-Nastase (BMN) matrix model under a global $U(1)$ symmetry acting within the transverse $SO(6)$ scalar sector. Decomposing the time-evolved charged thermofield double (cTFD) state into orthogonal fixed-charge sectors, we examine the return amplitude, spectral moments, tridiagonal Lanczos coefficients, and early-time spread complexity across the low-temperature confined ($\beta\mu \gg 1$) and finite-temperature deconfined ($T \gg \mu$) phases. In the low-temperature gapped regime, effective sector dynamics map onto decoupled, displaced harmonic oscillators with a Casimir-like zero-point offset, yielding factorized moments, linear drift in diagonal Lanczos coefficients $a_n^{(q)}$, and sub-linear $\sqrt{n}$ off-diagonal growth with thermally suppressed initial hopping. Charge-dependent Boltzmann weights, vacuum shifts, and degeneracies break complexity equipartition, while inter-sector coherence and charge dispersion enforce an early-time positivity bound $\Delta C(t) = C_{\text{total}}(t) - \bar{C}(t) \ge 0$. In the \emph{finite}-temperature continuum phase, modeling the planar large-$N$ spectral density as a thermal Gaussian gives constant diagonal coefficients $a_n^{(q)} = \bar{E}_q$, universal square-root scaling $b_n^{(q)} = \sigma_E \sqrt{n}$, and a quadratic spread launch $\mathcal{C}^{(q)}(t) = \sigma_E^2(q,T)\,t^2 + \mathcal{O}(t^4)$ governed by the backreacted sector energy variance. Holographically, these regimes distinguish coherent string fluctuations in horizonless Lin-Maldacena (LM) droplet geometries from dissipative scrambling near charged ten-dimensional black D0-brane horizons.

</details>


### [51] [Scattering amplitudes in AdS$_5\times$ S$^5$ from the pure spinor superstring](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29622)
*Luis F. Alday,Thiago Fleury,Tobias Hansen,Lucas N. S. Martins,Dennis Zavaleta*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper develops a BRST-invariant zero-mode integration prescription for the pure spinor superstring on AdS₅×S⁵, enabling first-principles worldsheet computation of two- and three-point half-BPS supergravity amplitudes, with normalized three-point results matching planar N=4 SYM.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the long-standing obstacle of zero-mode integration in the pure spinor formalism on AdS₅×S⁵ — a key bottleneck preventing direct worldsheet amplitude computations in this maximally supersymmetric holographic background.

Method: Constructs a BRST-invariant measure for zero-mode integration that incorporates AdS₅×S⁵-specific contributions absent in flat space; implements it via picture-changing operators or a non-minimal regulator; applies it to genus-zero correlators with arbitrary external half-BPS supergravity states.

Result: First explicit worldsheet computations of two- and three-point amplitudes for half-BPS supergravity states in AdS₅×S⁅; normalized three-point amplitudes exactly match predictions from planar N=4 super Yang-Mills theory.

Conclusion: The proposed prescription fully resolves the zero-mode integration problem for genus-zero pure spinor amplitudes on AdS₅×S⁵ and establishes quantitative worldsheet-level confirmation of the AdS/CFT correspondence for protected correlators.

Abstract: We formulate a BRST-invariant prescription for the integration of zero modes in the pure spinor superstring on $AdS_5\times S^5$. The measure includes contributions absent in flat space and admits a description in terms of picture changing operators or a non-minimal regulator. The prescription is valid for genus-zero amplitudes with arbitrary multiplicity and external states and removes a central obstacle to amplitude computations in this background. We use it to perform the first direct worldsheet computation of two and three-point amplitudes of half-BPS supergravity states. The properly normalised three-point amplitudes exactly reproduce the results of planar ${\mathcal N}=4$ super Yang-Mills theory.

</details>


### [52] [A note on Holographic Renormalization for Polarized IKKT](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29677)
*Matteo Guglielmetti*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs a boundary counterterm for the on-shell action of type IIB supergravity dual to polarized IKKT matrix model vacua, using a variational principle with asymptotically constrained off-shell fluctuations; renormalization yields agreement with the matrix model upon fixing D1-brane charge via RR 2-form dualization.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To consistently define and renormalize the on-shell gravitational action for type IIB supergravity solutions dual to vacua of the polarized IKKT matrix model, enabling precise holographic comparison with matrix model predictions.

Method: Constructs a boundary counterterm guided by the variational principle; imposes fall-off conditions on off-shell fluctuations consistent with asymptotic equations of motion; implements fixed D1-brane charge ensemble off-shell via dualization of the RR two-form potential.

Result: The renormalized on-shell action of supersymmetric saddles matches the IKKT matrix model prediction when evaluated in the fixed D1-brane charge ensemble.

Conclusion: A well-defined, renormalized holographic dictionary is established for polarized IKKT vacua, confirming consistency between type IIB supergravity and the matrix model at the level of the on-shell action.

Abstract: We construct a boundary counterterm for the on-shell action of type IIB supergravity solutions dual to vacua of the polarized IKKT matrix model. The construction is based on the variational principle, with off-shell fluctuations whose fall-off conditions are based on the asymptotic equations of motion. The renormalized on-shell action of the supersymmetric saddles reproduces the matrix- model result once we pass to the ensemble of fixed D1-brane charges, which we implement off-shell by dualizing the RR two-form potential.

</details>


### [53] [Infrared finite scattering theory: Non-relativistic Coulomb scattering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29691)
*Kartik Prabhu,Shivam Upadhyay*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper develops a perturbative formulation of the Dollard scattering operator for non-relativistic Coulomb scattering, proving all-order cancellation of infrared divergences; it reformulates the problem in the Heisenberg picture via a time-dependent canonical transformation and extends the LSZ reduction formalism to accommodate long-range asymptotics, enabling future generalization to QFT with infrared issues.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the lack of explicit perturbative implementations of Dollard's asymptotic scattering framework for Coulomb potentials, and to lay groundwork for extending infrared-safe scattering theory to relativistic QFT with massless fields and long-range interactions.

Method: Perturbative expansion of the Dollard scattering operator; explicit verification of divergence cancellation up to second Born order; Heisenberg-picture reformulation using a time-dependent canonical transformation that eliminates logarithmic Coulomb corrections from asymptotic phase-space variables; construction of a modified LSZ reduction formalism tied to Dollard’s asymptotic dynamics.

Result: Demonstration of complete cancellation of Coulomb (infrared) divergences to all orders in the Dollard perturbative series; recovery of the exact distributional Coulomb scattering amplitude including forward-scattering; derivation of a well-defined Heisenberg S-matrix and an asymptotically adapted LSZ formalism linked rigorously to the Dollard operator.

Conclusion: The Dollard framework admits a consistent, fully perturbative implementation for Coulomb scattering, and its structural features—especially the canonical asymptotic dressing—provide a blueprint for generalizing the LSZ formalism to infrared-divergent relativistic QFTs, such as QED or gravity.

Abstract: We revisit non-relativistic quantum-mechanical scattering in a Coulomb potential. The long-range nature of the interaction leads to divergent integrals in conventional S-matrix calculations using the Born series. Dollard showed that a well-defined scattering operator can be constructed by replacing the free Hamiltonian $ H_0 $ with an appropriate asymptotic Hamiltonian $ H_{\rm D} $. However, explicit perturbative calculations within Dollard's framework have remained scarce. We develop a perturbative formulation of the Dollard scattering operator in which the Coulomb divergences cancel to all orders. Perturbatively, we demonstrate this cancellation explicitly through second Born order and show that the resulting amplitude reproduces the known exact distributional Coulomb amplitude, including its forward-scattering contribution. With the aim of generalizing our analysis to quantum field theory, we reformulate the problem in the Heisenberg picture. We construct a new set of canonical variables through a time-dependent canonical transformation that removes the logarithmic Coulomb correction from the asymptotic phase-space coordinates. Using these variables, we construct the corresponding Heisenberg S-matrix through several complementary approaches. This leads to a formulation of the Lehmann--Symanzik--Zimmermann (LSZ) reduction formalism adapted to the modified asymptotic dynamics, which we relate to the Dollard scattering operator. This construction provides a framework for extending the LSZ formalism to infrared divergences in relativistic quantum field theories with long-range interactions and massless fields.

</details>


### [54] [Bounds on entanglement fluctuations at holographic interfaces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29699)
*Pallab Basu,Vishnu Jejjala*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates the minimal possible entanglement fluctuations at fixed entropy in holographic conformal interfaces with a scalar field, deriving rigorous bounds relating entropy, capacity, and energy transmission, and showing that unbounded capacity can be achieved at finite entropy in a long Janus throat geometry.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine the fundamental lower bound on entanglement fluctuations for a given entropy in holographic systems, particularly for conformal interfaces with scalar field support.

Method: Analytical derivation using holographic duality, gravitational response analysis, and constraints from the background Einstein equations; focuses on scalar-field-supported conformal interfaces and Janus throat geometries.

Result: Derives inequality $c_\text{eff}\leq c_\text{cap}\leq c_\text{eff}^2/c_{LR}$ linking effective central charge, capacity, and left-right energy transmission; shows coupled gravitational response comprises transport minus a positive scalar term bounded by Einstein equations; demonstrates unbounded capacity at finite entropy coefficient in long Janus throats with gapped scalar response.

Conclusion: Entanglement fluctuations can be made arbitrarily small relative to entropy in certain holographic interface geometries, constrained only by universal central-charge–based bounds; the Janus throat provides a concrete realization where capacity diverges while entropy remains finite.

Abstract: How small can entanglement fluctuations be at fixed entropy? For a class of holographic conformal interfaces supported by a scalar with a potential, we prove $c_\text{eff}\leq c_\text{cap}\leq c_\text{eff}^2/c_{LR}$, relating entropy, capacity, and energy transmission. The coupled gravitational response is a transport term minus a positive scalar contribution; the background Einstein equation bounds that subtraction. A long Janus throat allows unbounded capacity at a finite entropy coefficient while the scalar response remains gapped.

</details>


### [55] [Lie Algebra Cohomology and Seiberg-Witten Theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29713)
*Ahsan Z. Khan*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes the algebra of perturbative holomorphic-topological local operators in pure N=2 SU(2) gauge theory, confirming a conjecture that it is a specific Poisson vertex algebra generated by a Virasoro element X (c=0) and an odd primary Y (weight 3), with relation X²=0; equivalently, it computes certain relative Lie algebra cohomology and self-Ext groups in the coherent Satake category, using factorization, depth arguments, and algebra deformations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To prove a conjecture about the structure of the algebra of perturbative holomorphic-topological local operators in pure N=2 SU(2) gauge theory, and to relate it to representation-theoretic objects—namely, relative Lie algebra cohomology and self-Ext groups in the coherent Satake category.

Method: Uses factorization algebras on configuration spaces of points on the affine line, a depth filtration argument, and two controlled deformations of the coefficient algebra C[z,w]/(w²).

Result: The algebra of perturbative holomorphic-topological local operators is shown to be the Poisson vertex algebra generated by a Virasoro element X of central charge zero and an odd primary Y of weight three, subject only to X² = 0; equivalently, H*(sl₂[z,w]/(w²), sl₂) and self-Ext groups of the unit in the coherent Satake categories for SL₂ and PGL₂ are computed.

Conclusion: The conjectured Poisson vertex algebra structure is rigorously established, providing a precise algebraic characterization of the holomorphic-topological sector and linking it concretely to geometric representation theory via Lie algebra cohomology and the coherent Satake category.

Abstract: We compute the algebra of perturbative holomorphic-topological local operators of pure $\mathcal N=2$ gauge theory with gauge group $SU(2)$, proving a conjecture of the author: it is the Poisson vertex algebra generated by a Virasoro element $X$ of central charge zero and an odd primary $Y$ of weight three, subject only to $X^2=0$. Equivalently, we compute the relative Lie algebra cohomology $H^*(\mathfrak{sl}_2[z,w]/(w^2),\mathfrak{sl}_2)$ and the self-Ext groups of the unit in the coherent Satake category for $SL_2$ and $PGL_2$. The proof uses factorization on configuration spaces of points on the affine line, a depth argument, and two deformations of the coefficient algebra $\mathbb C[z,w]/(w^2)$.

</details>


### [56] [Charged spin-$\frac32$ in a chiral Feshbach--Villars model: hyperbolic propagation, Landau-level instability, and exceptional points](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30225)
*Haryanto M. Siahaan*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analyzes a Pauli-coupled second-order wave equation for spin-3/2 fields, revealing spectral reality conditions tied to the gyromagnetic ratio g and magnetic field strength; it identifies exceptional points, loss of positive-definite conserved forms, and first-order spectral instabilities under electrostatic perturbations—without asserting fermionic quantization.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine conditions under which the spectrum of a spin-3/2 relativistic wave equation remains real in electromagnetic backgrounds, distinguishing spectral instability from loss of hyperbolicity, and probing the role of the free gyromagnetic ratio g in defining physical consistency.

Method: Analyzes a sixteen-component pseudo-Hermitian Hamiltonian (Feshbach–Villars form) derived from a Pauli-coupled second-order wave equation on the (3/2,0)⊕(0,3/2) representation; employs covariant characteristic analysis, Landau quantization, exceptional point diagnostics, and first-order perturbation theory on degenerate spin manifolds with Gaussian-damped electrostatic potentials.

Result: Exact spin-j Landau spectrum is real iff |g|j ≤ 1; at g=2, j=3/2 exhibits a second-order exceptional point and loss of time-independent positive-definite conserved quadratic form at |qB|=m²/2; nonreal eigenvalues emerge for m²/6 < |qB| < m²/2 under small electrostatic perturbations on the lowest spin-3/2 manifold, except at a special well range; g=2 spin-1/2 limit recovers Dirac spectral reality, while g=1/j eliminates first-order imaginary corrections.

Conclusion: Spectral reality in this spin-3/2 system is tightly constrained by g and field strength, with exceptional points marking critical thresholds for both spectral stability and conservation law existence; results clarify that spectral instability does not imply loss of hyperbolicity and do not support fermionic quantization.

Abstract: We study a Pauli-coupled second-order wave equation on $(\frac32,0)\oplus(0,\frac32)$ with a free gyromagnetic ratio $g$. Its Feshbach--Villars form is a sixteen-component pseudo-Hermitian Hamiltonian system with lightlike covariant characteristics in smooth electromagnetic backgrounds. The solution space is chirality doubled; a covariant physical single-species reduction is not assumed. The exact spin-$j$ Landau spectrum remains real at every field strength precisely when $|g|j\le1$. At $g=2$, the critical spin-$\frac32$ channel reaches zero at $|qB|=m^2/2$, where each chiral block develops a second-order exceptional point. This threshold also marks the loss of a time-independent positive-definite conserved quadratic form for the channel. We also include a bounded, Gaussian-damped harmonic electrostatic potential and its associated electric field. The complete first-order problem on a degenerate manifold reduces to $V\pm iC$, with $V$ and $C$ the projected potential and spin--electric coupling. On the lowest two-state spin-$\frac32$ manifold, nonreal eigenvalue branches arise for $m^2/6<|qB|<m^2/2$ at sufficiently small nonzero perturbation amplitude, except at a special well range where the first-order splitting vanishes. The spin-$\frac12$, $g=2$ limit satisfies the Dirac spectral-reality check, whereas $g=1/j$ has no imaginary first-order correction for this perturbation. These results separate spectral instability from loss of hyperbolicity; they do not establish a physical fermionic quantization.

</details>


### [57] [Matter-decorated trumpets in double-scaled SYK](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30296)
*Matteo Beccaria*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs matter-decorated wormholes in sine-dilaton gravity beyond the Jackiw–Teitelboim (JT) limit by leveraging the double-scaled SYK (DSSYK) boundary dual; it defines trumpets with matter insertions via end-of-the-world branes, derives exact gluing weights sector-by-sector in chord number using Rogers polynomials, and recovers known JT and classical bulk results as limits.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the geometric gluing construction of wormholes to include matter fields beyond the JT limit—where only the matter propagator on a fixed background is understood—by exploiting the exact solvability of DSSYK as a holographic dual to sine-dilaton gravity.

Method: Uses DSSYK as the boundary theory; defines matter-decorated trumpets via end-of-the-world brane traces with Gaussian matter insertions; implements exact sector-wise gluing over chord numbers crossing the neck; computes trumpet amplitudes as Rogers polynomial transforms; derives positive, combinatorially weighted gluing kernels accounting for chord twists, windings, and closed loops.

Result: Closed-form expressions for matter-decorated trumpets and gluing weights; exact sector-by-sector reconstruction of the matter-decorated double trumpet; recovery of known JT-limit gluing and classical bulk geodesic dressing as limiting cases; proof of uniqueness under the adopted ETH two-matrix model definition.

Conclusion: The matter-decorated wormhole in sine-dilaton gravity admits a complete, exact, and positive-weighted gluing construction in DSSYK, resolving a longstanding limitation of the JT approximation and reinforcing DSSYK as a precise non-perturbative dual.

Abstract: In sine-dilaton gravity, the double trumpet, the wormhole amplitude without matter, is obtained by gluing two trumpets along a closed geodesic of discrete length and summing over this length. When the wormhole carries the propagator of a matter field of dimension $\Delta$, joining the two asymptotic boundaries and crossing the neck, such a gluing is known only in the Jackiw--Teitelboim (JT) limit. We construct the gluing beyond JT in double-scaled SYK (DSSYK), the proposed boundary dual of sine-dilaton gravity, whose disk amplitudes it reproduces. In this context, we adopt the definition of the matter-decorated wormhole in terms of the ETH two-matrix model with Gaussian matter. We build matter-decorated trumpets from an end-of-the-world brane trace with a matter insertion, and prove that the wormhole is glued from them exactly, sector by sector in the number of chords through the neck, with a positive weight that accounts for the twists and windings of these chords and for the closed loops around the neck. Trumpets and weight are obtained in closed form, the former as transforms of Rogers polynomials, and we discuss the uniqueness of the construction. In the JT limit, both reproduce the known gluing separately, and not only in combination. In the classical limit, each trumpet takes the form expected in the bulk: a pure-gravity sine-dilaton trumpet dressed by the factor that a probe matter geodesic contributes at its boundary.

</details>


### [58] [Darboux transformation for the ASDYM equations: Solitons and instantons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30433)
*Shangshuai Li*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper uses a one-fold Darboux transformation for anti-self-dual Yang–Mills equations to express the Lagrangian density in terms of τ-functions; it parametrizes soliton τ-functions via Grassmannians Gr(N,M;ℂ), constructs associated fields, and reinterprets the ADHM instanton construction through the same Darboux framework—recovering Osborn’s Lagrangian formula and yielding the gauge connection.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify and clarify the geometric and algebraic structures underlying solitons and instantons in anti-self-dual Yang–Mills theory by leveraging Darboux transformations and τ-function formalism.

Method: Application of a one-fold Darboux transformation to the anti-self-dual Yang–Mills equations; τ-function representation of the Yang–Mills Lagrangian; parametrization of τ-functions using complex Grassmannians Gr(N,M;ℂ) for exponential solitons; reinterpretation of the ADHM construction as a Darboux transformation.

Result: Explicit τ-function expression for the Yang–Mills Lagrangian density; Grassmannian parametrization of soliton τ-functions and corresponding field construction; Darboux-theoretic reformulation of ADHM, recovery of Osborn’s Lagrangian formula, and derivation of the gauge connection for instantons.

Conclusion: The one-fold Darboux transformation provides a unifying framework for both soliton and instanton solutions of the anti-self-dual Yang–Mills equations, linking τ-function methods, Grassmannian geometry, and the ADHM construction in a coherent and computationally effective way.

Abstract: Using a one-fold Darboux transformation for the anti-self-dual Yang--Mills equations, we express the Yang--Mills Lagrangian density in terms of $\tau$-functions. For solitons constructed from exponential functions, we show that the associated $\tau$-functions admit a parametrization by arbitrary points in the Grassmannian Gr(N,M;$\mathbb{C}$), and construct the corresponding Yang--Mills fields. For instantons arising from the Atiyah--Drinfeld--Hitchin--Manin (ADHM) construction, we reinterpret the ADHM construction in terms of a one-fold Darboux transformation, recover Osborn's formula for the Yang--Mills Lagrangian density, and obtain the corresponding gauge connection.

</details>


### [59] [Non-invertible Duality Defects in Four-Dimensional $\mathbb{Z}_N$ Lattice Gauge Theories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30489)
*Satoshi Yamaguchi*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs topological interfaces realizing dualities in 4D Z_N lattice gauge theories with generalized plaquette actions; at self-dual points, these become non-invertible duality defects, and their interplay with center symmetry defects yields new crossing relations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically construct and characterize topological interfaces implementing known dualities in 4D Z_N lattice gauge theories, and to uncover the structure of non-invertible symmetries arising at self-duality, including their fusion and junctions with center symmetry defects.

Method: Explicit construction of topological interfaces via local building-block weights on the lattice; proof of topological invariance using discrete gauge invariance and duality transformations; analysis of defect junctions and derivation of crossing relations using consistency conditions and symmetry algebra constraints.

Result: Topological duality interfaces are rigorously constructed and shown to become non-invertible duality defects at self-dual couplings; center symmetry defects and their junctions with duality interfaces are classified; multiple crossing relations governing their mutual braiding and fusion are derived.

Conclusion: The duality interfaces provide a concrete lattice realization of non-invertible symmetries in 4D gauge theories; the interplay between duality and center symmetry defects reveals a richer symmetry structure than previously appreciated, encoded in precise topological crossing relations.

Abstract: We consider four-dimensional $\mathbb{Z}_N$ lattice gauge theories with generalized plaquette actions. These theories are known to exhibit dualities. We construct topological interfaces implementing these dualities by assigning a weight to each building block and proving topological invariance. At self-dual couplings, these interfaces become duality defects representing non-invertible symmetries. We also study center symmetry defects and their junctions with the duality interfaces and derive several crossing relations.

</details>


### [60] [Dirichlet walls beyond JT gravity and their quantum group interpretation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30567)
*Luca Griguolo,Jacopo Papalini,Lorenzo Russo,Domenico Seminara,Alex Tarana*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper canonically quantizes sinh dilaton gravity with finite Dirichlet boundary conditions, yielding a finite-difference Schrödinger equation solved by q-hypergeometric wavefunctions — a quantum deformation of JT gravity’s hypergeometric solutions — and identifies the cutoff Hamiltonian as the reduced Casimir of the quantum group $U_q(\mathfrak{sl}(2,\mathbb{R}))$, with scattering states given by modular double hyperbolic matrix elements.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend Jackiw–Teitelboim (JT) gravity to a more general dilaton gravity model (sinh dilaton gravity) while preserving solvability and incorporating finite cutoff effects, thereby probing quantum gravity beyond the near-AdS$_2$ limit and elucidating the role of quantum groups in holography.

Method: Canonical quantization of sinh dilaton gravity with finite Dirichlet boundary conditions; reduction to a two-dimensional effective AdS$_2$ geometry parameterized by a spacelike geodesic length and its conjugate momentum; derivation of a finite-difference Schrödinger equation; identification of solutions as $q$-hypergeometric functions; interpretation of the Hamiltonian as the reduced Casimir of $U_q(\mathfrak{sl}(2,\mathbb{R}))$; use of modular double representation theory for scattering states.

Result: The wavefunction of the universe is a regular $q$-hypergeometric function at vanishing geodesic length; the finite-cutoff Hamiltonian coincides with the reduced Casimir of $U_q(\mathfrak{sl}(2,\mathbb{R}))$; the two linearly independent scattering solutions are identified as hyperbolic matrix elements of the modular double $U_q(\mathfrak{sl}(2,\mathbb{R})) \otimes U_{\tilde q}(\mathfrak{sl}(2,\mathbb{R}))$.

Conclusion: Sinhal dilaton gravity at finite cutoff admits an exact quantum mechanical description governed by quantum group symmetry, establishing a concrete link between 2D dilaton gravity, $q$-deformed special functions, and modular double representations — providing a controlled setting for studying quantum gravitational effects beyond JT gravity.

Abstract: We canonically quantize sinh dilaton gravity with finite Dirichlet boundary conditions. The reduced phase space is formulated in terms of the length of a two-sided spacelike geodesic in an effective AdS$_2$ geometry and its conjugate momentum. Canonical quantization leads to a finite-difference Schrödinger equation whose wavefunctions of the universe are given by $q$-hypergeometric functions. These furnish a quantum deformation of the hypergeometric wavefunctions of JT gravity. We further show that the finite cutoff Hamiltonian derived from the bulk analysis admits a natural interpretation as the reduced Casimir of $U_q(\mathfrak{sl}(2,\mathbb{R}))$ acting on hyperbolic matrix elements. The two scattering wave solutions are hyperbolic matrix elements of the modular double $U_q(\mathfrak{sl}(2,\mathbb R))\otimes U_{\tilde q}(\mathfrak{sl}(2,\mathbb R))$, and the wavefunction of the universe is their unique combination regular at vanishing length.

</details>


### [61] [Gradient formula from the sphere for perturbative RG flows near critical dimensions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31050)
*Marten Reehorst*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper establishes a gradient formula for renormalisation group flows in $d = d_c - \varepsilon$ dimensions, showing that the beta function (on the sphere) is a gradient flow of a potential proportional to the sphere free energy $\tilde F$, with a symmetric metric built from the two-point function; results hold to all orders for several scalar and fermionic theories under a finiteness hypothesis, while gauge theories remain conjectural.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously establish a gradient property for perturbative RG flows near critical dimension, extending Zamolodchikov's $c$-theorem to higher dimensions and curved backgrounds (specifically the sphere $S^d$), and to connect the gradient potential to the sphere free energy $\tilde F$—a central quantity in the $F$-theorem and conformal field theory.

Method: Uses renormalised functional methods on $S^d$; derives Ward identities (including anomaly-free combination), exploits local Weyl anomaly and conformal Killing vector symmetry; constructs a renormalisation scheme where the gradient structure persists even with relevant operator sources; verifies assumptions via finiteness of renormalised correlators in specific QFTs (e.g., $\phi^4$, Yukawa, sextic/cubic scalars).

Result: A fully renormalised, scheme-independent gradient formula for the beta function: $\beta^i = g^{ij} \frac{\delta \tilde{F}}{\delta g_j}$, where $g^{ij}$ is a symmetric, nondegenerate metric given by the renormalised two-point function on $S^d$, and $\tilde{F}$ is the sphere free energy; holds to all orders in perturbation theory for specified scalar/fermion theories; extends to relevant deformations via Weyl variation of sources.

Conclusion: Perturbative RG flows near critical dimension admit a geometric gradient structure on the space of local couplings over $S^d$, with $\tilde{F}$ as the natural potential; this provides a rigorous, background-dependent generalisation of monotonic $c$- and $F$-theorems, contingent on finiteness and locality of the renormalised functional—established for several QFTs but still assumed for interacting gauge theories.

Abstract: We study the gradient property of perturbative renormalisation group flows in $d=d_c-\varepsilon$, with a potential and a metric regular at $\varepsilon=0$ up to logarithms of gauge couplings. We state properties of the renormalised functional with local couplings on the sphere $S^d$: finiteness, a local Weyl anomaly, the Ward identity of a conformal Killing vector, rotation properties and statements on the free theory. From them we derive a Ward identity without anomaly for a combination of the functional and its anomaly. Its first variation is a gradient formula whose symmetric metric contains the two-point function on the sphere and whose potential at fixed points is, in an extended form of the properties, proportional to the sphere free energy $\tilde F$ up to a constant. Sources of relevant operators enter through their Weyl variation, and in a scheme which we construct the gradient formula persists, with a nondegenerate metric if a matrix of the free theory is invertible. For multiscalar $\phi^4$ theory and for scalars and Dirac fermions with Yukawa and quartic couplings in $d=4-\varepsilon$, for sextic scalars in $d=3-\varepsilon$ and for cubic scalars in $d=6-\varepsilon$, we derive all these properties from a finiteness hypothesis. The gradient formula then holds to all orders for the beta function defined on the sphere. For interacting gauge theories the properties remain assumptions.

</details>


### [62] [Electromagnetic Memory as a Collider Observable](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31107)
*Felipe Diaz,Hernán A. González,Oriana Labrin,Jakob Salzer*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper establishes a Ward identity linking electromagnetic memory and charge correlators as inclusive observables, computes massless multipoint and massive two-point memory correlators for lepton pairs, and shows their finiteness at first radiative order with recoil-induced Sudakov logarithms.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify soft radiation (memory) and hard charged-track (charge correlator) measurements via large gauge symmetry, resolving how inclusive observables connect infrared physics across detector types.

Method: Application of the Ward identity of asymptotic large gauge symmetry; computation of electromagnetic memory correlators for lepton pair production using quantum electrodynamics in the soft limit; celestial CFT techniques for free-boson representation; analysis of infrared behavior including recoil effects at leading radiative order.

Result: All massless n-point memory correlators and the massive two-point memory correlator are explicitly computed; the coincident limit of the latter yields the soft photon number; correlators admit a finite celestial free-boson representation at O(α), featuring back-to-back Sudakov logarithms from recoil.

Conclusion: Electromagnetic memory and charge correlators are intrinsically related through large gauge symmetry as inclusive observables; the finiteness and celestial structure of memory correlators support an infrared-finite formulation of QED scattering amplitudes, with implications for celestial holography and collider phenomenology.

Abstract: Electromagnetic memory and charge correlators are measured by different detectors, sensitive to soft radiation and to hard charged tracks. We show that the Ward identity of large gauge symmetry relates these quantities as inclusive observables. For lepton pairs produced by an electromagnetic current, we compute all massless multipoint memory correlators and the massive two-point function, whose coincident limit gives the soft photon number. The correlators admit a celestial free-boson representation and remain finite at first radiative order, where recoil generates back-to-back Sudakov logarithms.

</details>


### [63] [Tensionless M5-brane in light-cone gauge. The spinor moving frame method is wanted](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31189)
*Igor Bandos*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper fully fixes the light-cone gauge for the tensionless limit of the M5-brane action, resolving a nontrivial κ-symmetry gauge-fixing problem—caused by vanishing gauge field strength—using a spinor moving frame built from a nondegenerate vector quadratic in the field strength and the PST scalar derivative; it also extends the formalism to standard tensionless p-branes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To consistently perform light-cone gauge fixing for the recently derived tensionless M5-brane action, where conventional κ-symmetry projector methods fail due to the vanishing/nontrivial behavior of the gauge field strength in the tensionless limit.

Method: Adaptation of the spinor moving frame method: construction of a Lorentz-covariant moving frame based on a nonvanishing, nondegenerate vector formed quadratically from the worldvolume gauge field strength and the derivative of the PST scalar; applied to both the tensionless M5-brane and comparison tensionless p-branes without worldvolume gauge fields.

Result: Complete light-cone gauge fixing achieved; explicit gauge-fixed action and equations of motion obtained; symmetries (e.g., residual worldvolume reparametrizations, fermionic constraints) identified; spinor moving frame formulation successfully generalized to null-p-branes.

Conclusion: The spinor moving frame method provides a robust framework for κ-symmetry gauge fixing in tensionless branes with worldvolume gauge fields, overcoming pathologies associated with vanishing field strength; it unifies treatment of tensionless M5-branes and simpler null-p-branes under a common covariant formalism.

Abstract: We perform the complete light-cone gauge fixing for the tensionless limit of the M-theory 5-brane the action for which was recently obtained in [1]. The problem of gauge fixing of local fermionic $\kappa$-symmetry becomes quite nontrivial because the projector of that is constructed from the gauge field strength which in its turn is required to be nonvanaishing and nontrivial in this limit. To resolve this problem the spinor moving frame method has been adapted. The corresponding moving frame is constructed on a vector quadratic in the gauge field strength, which is required to be nonvanishing and nondegenerate in the tensionless limit, and the derivative of the PST (Pasti-Sorokin-Tonin) scalar. The symmetries of the gauge fixed action and equations of motion are briefly discussed. To make a comparison with the case of usual tensionless p-branes (null-p-branes), those which do not carry worldvolume gauge fields, we also elaborate their spinor moving frame formulation.

</details>


### [64] [Massive de Sitter correlators at one loop](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31214)
*Amara McCune*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes exact squeezed-limit coefficients for primordial correlator signals from fermion and massive field loops during inflation—going beyond late-time approximations—and finds significant enhancements (up to 100×) over standard estimates, especially with chemical potentials; it also characterizes phase drift away from the squeezed limit.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Late-time estimates for loop-induced primordial correlator signals (e.g., from fermions or massive fields coupled to the inflaton) are widely used but lack accuracy in the squeezed limit and beyond; exact de Sitter computations are needed to quantify systematic errors and improve observational forecasts.

Method: Uses finite Mellin-Barnes representations of exact massive de Sitter correlators to compute squeezed-limit bispectrum coefficients and their off-squeezed corrections; separates contributions into hard (loop) and soft (vertex) parts, computing both exactly—not just the hard part as in late-time approximations.

Result: Exact squeezed coefficients differ from late-time estimates by a universal mass- and coupling-dependent factor; enhancements reach 10–30× for heavy SM loops without chemical potential, and up to ~30× for helical vectors and ~100× for fermion triangles with chemical potential; phase drift away from squeezed limit is derived for scalar and fermion loops.

Conclusion: Late-time approximations substantially underestimate loop-induced non-Gaussianity in primordial correlators, particularly when chemical potentials or heavier masses are involved; exact de Sitter calculations are essential for robust interpretation of CMB/LSS data and model discrimination.

Abstract: Fermions and massive fields charged under an unbroken symmetry couple to the inflaton only in pairs, so their signals in primordial correlators begin at one loop. Templates for these signals are mostly late-time estimates. Starting from a representation of massive de Sitter correlators as finite Mellin-Barnes integrals, we compute the exact squeezed coefficients of these signals and their corrections away from the squeezed limit. The leading coefficient factorizes into a hard part and a soft vertex. Late-time estimates treat the hard part exactly but approximate the soft vertex by its late-time limit, whereas we compute both exactly. Without a chemical potential, the exact coefficient differs from the late-time one by a universal factor that depends only on the masses in the loop and the coupling at the soft vertex. For the heavy Standard Model loops, the exact signals are ten to thirty times larger than their late-time estimates. With a chemical potential, the ratio depends on the model, and at our benchmarks it reaches a factor of thirty for helical vectors and about a hundred for the fermion triangle, where we agree with recent exact results. Away from the squeezed limit the leading term soon fails, mainly through a drift in the phase of the oscillation, which we derive for scalar and fermion loops.

</details>


### [65] [Nonabelianization map for the $W_{3,c=2}$ conformal blocks. Part I](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31232)
*Qianyu Hao*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper generalizes the nonabelianization construction from Virasoro (c=1) to W₃ (c=2) conformal blocks, expressing them via Heisenberg blocks on a branched triple cover using gl₃ spectral networks and soliton degeneracies; it derives new fermionic formulas and verifies the construction for (123)-type branch points.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the nonabelianization framework—previously established for Virasoro conformal blocks—to the richer algebraic structure of the W₃-algebra, enabling geometric and spectral-network-based computation of higher-rank CFT correlators.

Method: Constructs a generalized nonabelianization map from c=2 W₃-conformal blocks on a Riemann surface C to Heisenberg blocks on a branched triple cover \tilde{C}, incorporating \mathfrak{gl}_3 spectral networks and their soliton degeneracies; expresses resulting W₃ blocks as fermion correlation functions; validates the map on a minimal example with two (123)-type branch points.

Result: Derives novel explicit formulas for W₃ conformal blocks in terms of fermionic correlators governed by Heisenberg blocks on \tilde{C}; confirms correctness for the simplest nontrivial triple cover featuring (123) monodromy, reproducing the expected two-point correlator and associated \tau-function.

Conclusion: The nonabelianization map extends naturally to W₃-algebra blocks, but crucially depends on soliton degeneracies absent in the Virasoro case—demonstrating that spectral network data beyond the web topology is essential for higher-rank algebras; this provides a geometric pathway toward quantization and exact computation in W-algebra CFTs.

Abstract: This work is inspired by the nonabelianization construction of Virasoro conformal blocks. We start the process of defining the nonabelianization map which expresses $c=2$ $W_3$-algebra conformal blocks on a Riemann surface $C$ from Heisenberg blocks on a branched triple cover $\tilde{C}$ of $C$. This generalized nonabelianization map uses the datum of a $\mathfrak{gl}_3$ spectral network on $C$ and we find that this map also depends on the soliton degeneracies corresponding to the spectral network even though the soliton data didn't show up explicitly in the Virasoro case. This nonabelianization construction defines new formulas for $W_3$-algebra conformal blocks on $C$ in terms of fermion correlation functions determined by the Heisenberg blocks on $\tilde{C}$. We provide a check of the proposed construction associated to the more challenging $(123)$ type branch points which start to show up in three-fold covers. To be more precise, the simplest example that we focus on has only two of the $(123)$ type branch points and we show that in this example, our nonabelianization construction gives the desired two-point correlator and $\tau$-function.

</details>

### [66] [Regge-eikonal description of high-energy proton-proton elastic scattering: a single-channel Gaussian benchmark confronted with TOTEM data at $\sqrt{s}=13$ TeV](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28140)
*Ji Li*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper eikonalizes a single-Pomeron Regge Born amplitude with Gaussian t-dependence in impact-parameter space, yielding closed-form expressions for cross sections and slope using exponential integrals; it shows that a single-Gaussian opacity fails to simultaneously describe TOTEM 13 TeV total/elastic cross sections and the diffractive dip position; adding signature phase restores consistency with ρ measurement but not the dip shape; multi-component opacities improve but do not fully reproduce the dip-bump structure and large-t tail.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess whether a minimal eikonal model—based on a single Pomeron exchange with Gaussian impact-parameter profile—can simultaneously describe high-energy pp total/elastic cross sections and the characteristic diffractive dip in dσ/dt observed by TOTEM at 13 TeV.

Method: Eikonalization of a Gaussian-profiled single-Pomeron Regge Born amplitude in impact-parameter space; analytical evaluation yielding closed-form expressions for σ_tot, σ_el, σ_inel, and B (forward slope) in terms of the entire exponential integral Ein(a) and series I₁(a); comparison with numerical integration and Born limit; fitting to TOTEM 13 TeV data using Donnachie-Landshoff trajectory and two-parameter calibration; extensions including signature phase, two-component opacity, and Good-Walker two-channel eikonal.

Result: A single Gaussian opacity cannot simultaneously fit σ_tot, σ_el/σ_tot, and the dip position (0.244–0.315 GeV² vs measured 0.47 GeV²); inclusion of signature phase yields ρ = 0.098 (consistent with TOTEM/ATLAS) but fills—not shifts—the zero; two-component opacity reduces dip excess from factor 15 to 2, yet fails to reproduce dip-bump structure and large-|t| tail concurrently; Good-Walker eikonal moves minimum further from data.

Conclusion: The failure of minimal single-Gaussian eikonal models to reproduce both integrated observables and diffractive structure signals the need for more sophisticated opacity profiles—e.g., non-Gaussian, energy-dependent, or multi-Pomeron—indicating richer partonic dynamics beyond simple absorptive screening.

Abstract: A single-Pomeron Regge Born amplitude with a Gaussian dependence on the momentum transfer is eikonalized in impact-parameter space, and the resulting single-channel, purely absorptive amplitude is evaluated in closed form. The total, elastic and inelastic cross sections and the forward slope are expressed through the entire exponential integral Ein(a) and a related series $I_1(a)$, and the closed forms are verified against direct numerical integration and against the weak-coupling Born limit. Two parameter choices are confronted with the TOTEM measurements at $\sqrt{s}=13$ TeV. With the Donnachie-Landshoff trajectory and a normalization fixed at 7 TeV, the model gives $\sigma_{tot}=139.2$ mb against the measured $110.6\pm3.4$ mb and places the first diffractive zero at $|t|=0.244$ GeV$^2$ against the measured dip at 0.47 GeV$^2$. Calibration of the two parameters to the measured $\sigma_{tot}$ and $\sigma_{el}/\sigma_{tot}$ describes the differential cross section to within five per cent for $|t|\lesssim0.1$ GeV$^2$, but places the zero at 0.315 GeV$^2$ and overestimates the cross section at the measured dip by more than an order of magnitude. A single Gaussian opacity therefore cannot simultaneously accommodate the integrated cross sections and the position of the diffractive dip. Restoring the signature phase gives $\rho=0.098$, in agreement with the TOTEM and ATLAS measurements, and fills the zero without moving it; a two-component opacity reduces the excess at the dip from a factor of 15 to a factor of 2 without reproducing the dip-bump structure and the large-$|t|$ tail simultaneously; a two-channel Good-Walker eikonal on a Gaussian profile moves the minimum further away from the measured dip.

</details>
