*ADMX Collaboration,Taj A. Dyson,Mohamed H. Awida,Chelsea L. Bartram,Christian Boutan,Thomas Braine,James H. Buckley,Thomas J. Caligiure,Madeleine Carhart,Gianpaolo Carosi,Aaron S. Chou,Nicholas Christman,John Clarke,Edward J. Daw,Nick Du,Leanne D. Duffy,Sean Durham,Kieran Enzian,Graeme Flower,Chandrashekhar Gaikwad,Joseph R. Gleason,Cyrus Goodman,Maxim Goryachev,Charles Hanretty,Michael T. Hatzon,Alexander T. Hipp,Julia Hoffman,Matthew Hollister,Mark Jones,Rakshya Khatiwada,On Kim,Stefan Knirck,Daria Kowsari,Chao-Lin Kuo,Erik W. Lentz,Morgan J. Lynn,Ben T. McAllister,Donald V. Mitchell,Claude Mostyn,Kater W. Murch,Trinh N. L. Nguyen,Noah S. Oblath,Emma C. I. Paterson,Mitchell G. Perry,Aaron Quiskamp,Sephora Ruppert,B. Jordan Russell,Gray Rybka,Pierre Sikivie,Raj A. Singh,James Sinnis,Mitchell E. Solano,Andrew Sonnenschein,Neil S. Sullivan,David B. Tanner,Matthew S. Taubman,Jonathan R. Tedeschi,Rick J. Tesarek,Michael E. Tobar,Matthew O. Withers,Andrew K. Yi,Barkotel F. Zemenu,Dan Zhang,Zijun C. Zhao,Kaiwen Zheng*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: ADMX-EFR is a next-generation haloscope designed to extend axion dark matter searches from 8–16 μeV (2–4 GHz) with projected sensitivity below the DFSZ benchmark in 36 months, using an 18-cavity array, low-noise cryogenic electronics, and digital coherent signal combination.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the axion dark matter search range beyond 8 μeV (2 GHz), where previous ADMX runs left a gap, and probe theoretically motivated QCD axion parameter space—particularly the DFSZ model—while maintaining high sensitivity via enhanced scan rate and reduced system downtime.

Method: Design and simulation-based performance projection of ADMX-EFR: an 18-cavity (1 m each) haloscope operating in a high-field magnet and dilution refrigerator; individual low-noise amplifier chains per cavity; digital coherent combination of signals; frequency-dependent scan rate estimated from cavity/electronics simulations and prior ADMX diagnostic data; downtime modeling integrated into sensitivity projection.

Result: Projected instantaneous scan rate and final axion-photon coupling sensitivity; sensitivity expected to reach below the DFSZ benchmark across 8–16 μeV (2–4 GHz) within 36 months of runtime.

Conclusion: ADMX-EFR represents a scalable, high-throughput extension of the haloscope technique, enabling definitive exploration of a key axion DM mass range unprobed by prior experiments, with robust sensitivity projections grounded in empirical and simulated performance metrics.

Abstract: The QCD axion is a dark matter (DM) candidate particle which can also solve the long-standing strong charge-parity problem. The Axion Dark Matter eXperiment (ADMX) searches for resonantly-enhanced conversion of axion DM into photons in a strong magnetic field. Previous versions of the experiment probed most QCD axion DM from 2.7 $\mu$eV to 4.2 $\mu$eV, with ongoing and planned runs poised to cover up to 8 $\mu$eV (2 GHz). We present the design of a haloscope with 18 cavities 1 m in length read out using individual low-noise amplifier chains and digital coherent signal combination called ADMX extended frequency range (EFR). We discuss details on magnet and cryogenic infrastructure, cavity design, cold electronics and efficiency of coherent signal combination. We use detailed cavity and electronics simulations, as well as diagnostic data from previous ADMX runs, to estimate frequency-dependent instantaneous scan rate. When paired with a comprehensive study of experiment downtime, a projection of final axion coupling sensitivity is produced. Under the stated assumptions, the projected sensitivity is projected to reach below the DFSZ axion benchmark model from 8 $\mu$eV to 16 $\mu$eV (2 GHz to 4 GHz) in 36 months.

</details>


### [68] [Lorentz-invariant fiducial decay selection for the ATLAS $H\to ZZ^{*}\to 4\ell$ STXS measurement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30272)
*ATLAS Collaboration*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper recasts ATLAS Higgs boson STXS measurements in the H→4ℓ channel into a Lorentz-invariant fiducial (LIF) region to decouple decay-side acceptance from EFT-dependent kinematics, enabling robust EFT interpretations without EFT-dependent efficiency corrections.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Standard ATLAS STXS measurements in H→ZZ*→4ℓ use a fiducial acceptance based on SM decay kinematics; however, in EFT interpretations, that acceptance becomes Wilson-coefficient dependent—introducing model dependence into what should be a production-only measurement. The motivation is to define a truly model-independent fiducial region for EFT reinterpretation.

Method: The authors define a single Lorentz-invariant fiducial (LIF) region directly on the H→4ℓ decay final state, absorbing all EFT-dependent acceptance effects into the signal parametrisation. Reconstruction-efficiency corrections are validated as EFT-independent using 13.6 TeV MC simulation (165 fb⁻¹). They quantify LIF acceptance stability across STXS bins and Wilson coefficient variations, and assess leakage and efficiency shifts.

Result: The LIF acceptance in the SM is A^LIF_SM = 0.8546, production-independent at 0.07% and stable across STXS bins at 0.19%. <1% of reconstructed signal originates outside LIF; reconstruction efficiency varies by ≤6% over probed EFT parameter space—comparable to the original fiducial analysis. Thus, published STXS results can be reliably rescaled to the LIF phase space.

Conclusion: The LIF framework successfully isolates EFT dependence into the theoretical signal model while keeping experimental efficiencies effectively EFT-independent, enabling consistent, reusable, and interpretation-agnostic STXS measurements for EFT analyses.

Abstract: ATLAS measurements of Higgs boson production-mode cross-sections and Simplified Template Cross Sections (STXS), $\sigma\cdot B$, in the $H\to ZZ^{*}\to 4\ell$ decay channel rely on selection factors that fold in a decay-side fiducial acceptance evaluated with Standard Model decay kinematics. In an effective-field-theory interpretation that acceptance depends on the Wilson coefficients themselves. We recast these production-side $\sigma\cdot B$ results into a single Lorentz-invariant fiducial (LIF) decay region defined by the $H\to 4\ell$ decay, absorbing the EFT-dependent fiducial acceptance into the signal parametrisation, while the remaining reconstruction-efficiency corrections are shown to be largely EFT-independent. Using the Higgs boson signal Monte Carlo simulation at $\sqrt{s}=13.6$ TeV, corresponding to an integrated luminosity of 165 fb$^{-1}$, the LIF acceptance in the Standard Model is found to be $A^{LIF}_{SM}=0.8546$, production-independent to 0.07% and stable across STXS bins to 0.19%. Less than 1% of the reconstructed signal events originate from outside the LIF region, and the reconstruction efficiency for events in the region changes by at most 6% over the probed ranges of the EFT Wilson coefficients, as in the fiducial phase space of the published analysis. Consequently, the existing inclusive STXS cross-section measurements can be rescaled to the corresponding LIF fiducial phase space, allowing for EFT interpretations without EFT-dependent corrections for the reconstruction efficiency.

</details>


### [69] [Geometry-conditioned differentiable surrogate with adjoint Monte Carlo sampling for scintillator detector co-design](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30580)
*Lorenzo Pepa,Mohammad Ali Marashli,Nicolas Babio Elguero,Tommaso Dorigo,Irene Fagnani,Carlo Gulizia,Pablo Martinez Ruiz del Arbol,Fredrik Sandin,Pietro Vischia*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper introduces a differentiable neural surrogate model for photon transport in scintillator-based imaging detectors, trained via an adjoint (reciprocal) Monte Carlo method to enable efficient, gradient-based optimization of light-guide geometry.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Direct Monte Carlo simulation of photon transport is computationally prohibitive for iterative light-guide geometry optimization; there is a need for fast, differentiable surrogates to enable detector readout co-design and improved information extraction from particle interactions.

Method: The authors construct a modular, event-driven forward Monte Carlo model for scintillation photon generation and transport, and derive its reciprocal (adjoint) formulation that launches weighted photons from the light-guide readout face to compute spatially and temporally resolved optical response. They use this adjoint model to generate training data for a geometry-conditioned neural network surrogate predicting collection probabilities and conditional photon transport-time distributions across a parametrized family of curved, mirrored, single-clad light guides.

Result: The trained surrogate achieves high accuracy on held-out geometries and enables natively differentiable, gradient-based optimization of light-guide design; forward-reciprocal benchmarks confirm fidelity while revealing limitations in extrapolation beyond training geometry bounds.

Conclusion: The differentiable surrogate provides a scalable foundation for rapid optical-response analysis and closed-loop detector readout co-design, significantly accelerating the optimization of scintillator-based imaging systems.

Abstract: Optimizing light-guide geometries for scintillator-based imaging detectors requires repeated photon-transport evaluations, making direct Monte Carlo simulation computationally costly. Differentiable surrogates can accelerate these evaluations and support detector readout co-design aimed at improving information extraction from particle interactions. In this work we describe a surrogate model for photon transport. We develop it starting from a modular, event-driven Monte Carlo model for the generation and transport of scintillation photons, and a reciprocal Monte Carlo construction (an adjoint model) of a spatially and temporally resolved optical response field. The latter evaluates the collectible light output and its temporal structure from a scintillator volume by launching weighted photon trajectories from the light-guide readout face. We apply this approach to a scintillator cell with two mirrored, curved, single-clad light guides. We employ the adjoint model to train a geometry-conditioned neural surrogate that predicts collection probabilities and conditional transport-time distributions across a constrained guide-design family. The surrogate is natively differentiable, lending itself to be an element of a differentiable pipeline for gradient-based optimization. A forward-reciprocal benchmark and validation on held-out geometries establish the accuracy of the trained model and identify its limitations. The developed tools provide a foundation for rapid optical-response studies and detector readout co-design.

</details>


### [70] [Unbinned Likelihoods for Probability Densities with Poles](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30877)
*Méril Reboud,Danny van Dyk,Matteo Verzeletti*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: Proposes a pole-subtraction method to accelerate unbinned likelihood fits involving narrow interfering resonances, enabling efficient numerical convolution of the smooth remainder while preserving accuracy.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Unbinned likelihood fits become computationally prohibitive when narrow resonances (widths ≪ detector resolution) interfere with nonresonant amplitudes, because standard convolution must resolve sub-resolution structures.

Method: Analytically subtracts one simple-pole term per resonance from the truth-level probability density; the remaining smooth component is convolved numerically on a grid spaced by detector resolution—not resonance width—enabling efficient multidimensional and real-axis-only evaluation.

Result: Demonstrated high accuracy, significant speedup, and negligible statistical bias on two benchmark problems; the subtraction preserves likelihood integrity while decoupling resolution-driven and physics-driven scales.

Conclusion: Pole subtraction is a general, scalable, and broadly applicable technique for accelerating unbinned likelihood fits in particle physics analyses involving pole-dominated densities.

Abstract: Unbinned likelihood fits to detector-level data become prohibitively expensive when narrow resonances interfere with a nonresonant amplitude. The convolution of the truth-level probability density with the detector resolution must then resolve the resonances, whose widths are much smaller than the resolution. We propose a simple approach that subtracts one simple-pole term per resonance from the probability density. This term captures the resonant contribution in closed form, and the smooth remainder is convolved numerically on a grid whose spacing is set by the resolution alone. The approach applies to multidimensional densities and to densities known only on the real axis. Using two benchmark problems, we quantify the accuracy, computational cost, and statistical impact of the approach. We emphasise that the subtraction is a general approach to the computational problems of unbinned likelihoods in the presence of poles in their probability density and that it is applicable to a wide variety of problems in experimental particle physics.

</details>


### [71] [Distillation of a Normalising Flow into a Compact Symbolic Expression for Real-Time Anomaly Detection at the LHC Level-1 Trigger](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30896)
*Justin Bollhalder,Florencia Canelli,Valentina Guglielmi,Roope Oskari Niemi,Maurizio Pierini,Chang Sun,Francesco Vaselli*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper proposes distilling a normalizing-flow-based anomaly detection model into an explicit symbolic mathematical formula using SymbolNet with adaptive pruning, enabling ultra-low-latency (42.8 ns), hardware-efficient (0.81% LUTs, 0.44% DSP) inference on FPGA for Level-1 trigger applications at the LHC.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable unbiased, real-time anomaly detection before the Level-1 Trigger at the LHC—where strict latency constraints (< few µs) preclude expressive models like normalizing flows—requiring extreme model compression without sacrificing discrimination performance.

Method: Knowledge distillation from a normalizing-flow teacher to a symbolic student via SymbolNet, incorporating adaptive pruning for simultaneous feature selection and functional-form learning; final expression is implemented and validated on a Xilinx Virtex UltraScale+ VU13P FPGA.

Result: A 577-operation symbolic expression matching the teacher's test-set discrimination and generalizing across four new physics benchmarks; achieves 9-cycle (42.8 ns) latency, using only 0.81% of LUTs and 0.44% of DSP slices on FPGA.

Conclusion: Symbolic distillation enables physics-grade anomaly detection at Level-1 trigger speeds and resource budgets, bridging the gap between high-capacity ML models and real-time hardware constraints in high-energy physics.

Abstract: At the CERN Large Hadron Collider, anomaly detection techniques are becoming popular alternatives to traditional signature-aware new physics searches. For this approach to avoid any bias induced by the real-time event selection applied in the trigger system of ATLAS and CMS, it should be implemented before the first stage of selection, the Level-1 Trigger. This requires deciding to accept or reject each collision within a few microseconds, a latency budget that excludes the most expressive anomaly-detection models. Knowledge distillation has successfully addressed this issue by replacing a complex anomaly-detection model with a lighter student network. This work takes the compression one step further, distilling an anomaly detection model based on a normalising flow into an explicit mathematical formula. Using the SymbolNet symbolic-regression framework with adaptive pruning, the student is a learned functional form based on a chosen subset of the inputs, providing at once feature selection and compression through knowledge distillation. The final expression contains 577 operations and matches the teacher's discrimination on a test dataset, generalising to four benchmark new physics scenarios. Implemented on a Xilinx Virtex UltraScale+ VU13P FPGA, the symbolic student has a nine-cycle latency, corresponding to 42.8\,ns at the post-route maximum clock frequency, and uses 0.81\% of the device's LUTs and 0.44\% of its DSP slices.

</details>


### [72] [Underlying-event properties in proton-proton collisions at $\sqrt{s}=13.6$ TeV measured with the ALICE detector at the LHC](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31077)
*Jesús Eduardo Muñoz Méndez*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper presents ALICE measurements of the underlying event in pp collisions at 13.6 TeV, analyzing charged-particle and transverse-momentum densities in toward/transverse/away regions relative to the leading particle; observes a steep rise then plateau in transverse-region densities above 5 GeV/c, and compares results to lower-energy data and PYTHIA 8/EPOS4 models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To characterize the underlying event (UE) — soft QCD activity uncorrelated with hard scattering — at the new LHC Run 3 energy of 13.6 TeV, probing its evolution with center-of-mass energy and testing hadronization and multiparton interaction models.

Method: Analysis of LHC Run 3 pp collision data at √s = 13.6 TeV recorded by ALICE; measurement of charged-particle multiplicity density (dN_ch/dη) and summed transverse-momentum density (Σp_T / Δη) in toward, transverse, and away azimuthal regions defined relative to the leading charged particle’s direction; differential analysis versus p_T^leading.

Result: Transverse-region particle and Σp_T densities rise steeply for p_T^leading < 5 GeV/c and saturate (plateau) above that threshold; energy scaling shows increased UE activity at 13.6 TeV vs. lower energies; PYTHIA 8 (tune Monash) underestimates transverse densities, while EPOS4 qualitatively reproduces the plateau but overestimates magnitude.

Conclusion: The observed plateau in transverse UE densities above p_T^leading ≈ 5 GeV/c confirms onset of hard-scattering dominance and validates the 'leading particle' UE definition at record energy; discrepancies with PYTHIA 8 highlight need for improved multiparton interaction and color reconnection modeling, whereas EPOS4’s better agreement suggests strengths in its core-corona treatment and collective effects.

Abstract: This contribution reports measurements characterizing the underlying event at midrapidity in pp collisions at $\sqrt{s}=13.6$ TeV, based on LHC Run 3 data collected by ALICE. The charged-particle number and summed transverse-momentum densities are measured as a function of the leading-particle transverse momentum ($p_{\rm T}^{\rm leading}$) in the toward, transverse, and away regions, defined relative to the azimuthal direction of the leading charged particle. The particle densities in the transverse region are observed to increase steeply at low $p_{\rm T}^{\rm leading}$, followed by a plateau for $p_{\rm T}^{\rm leading}>5$ GeV/$c$. The results are compared with measurements at lower center-of-mass energies to illustrate the energy dependence of the underlying-event activity, and with predictions from PYTHIA 8 and EPOS4.

</details>

# hep-lat [[Back]](#toc)

### [73] [Thermodynamics of QCD+QED from the lattice](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29423)
*G. Endrődi,E. Garnacho-Velasco,G. Markó,L. Pethő*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper reviews lattice QCD+QED simulations at finite temperature, highlighting non-perturbative electromagnetic effects, center symmetry implications for the phase diagram, algorithmic challenges, and presents novel thermodynamic results.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: At finite temperature, QED coupling in QCD+QED may induce non-perturbative effects—unlike the well-understood perturbative zero-temperature regime—potentially altering the phase structure due to exact center symmetry of the combined theory.

Method: Lattice field theory simulations of QCD+QED at finite temperature; analysis of center symmetry, thermodynamic observables, and development of new algorithms to handle long-range electromagnetic interactions and gauge constraints.

Result: Novel lattice results on QCD+QED thermodynamics; identification of QED-induced modifications to phase structure; characterization of new features (e.g., symmetry restoration patterns, screening effects) and computational bottlenecks.

Conclusion: Finite-temperature QCD+QED exhibits qualitatively distinct, non-perturbative behavior requiring dedicated lattice methodologies; full first-principles mapping of its phase diagram remains an open challenge demanding algorithmic innovation.

Abstract: QED corrections to observables in QCD are by now well-understood at zero temperature, where QED effects are perturbative and required for precision physics observables, such as the neutron-proton mass difference or the muon anomalous magnetic moment, among many others. However, there are indications in the literature that at finite temperature the introduction of QED can lead to non-perturbative effects, affecting the structure of the phase diagram, since the combined theory is exactly center-symmetric. In this proceedings article, we analyze the structure of the combined theory and the different strategies to simulate it on the lattice, as well as presenting novel results of lattice QCD+QED simulations at finite temperature, focusing on understanding the role of the electromagnetic coupling in the thermodynamics of this system. We also discuss new features that arise in the combined theory and the challenges that these simulations entail, which require the development of new algorithms in order to understand the phase diagram of QCD+QED from first principles.

</details>


### [74] [Hadron spectroscopy of QC2D at finite density](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29900)
*Kei Iida,Etsuko Itou,Kotaro Murakami,and Daiki Suenaga*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper studies how hadron masses depend on chemical potential in two-color QCD at low temperature using lattice Monte Carlo simulations; it finds a shift in meson mass hierarchy across the superfluid transition, near-zero mass for the $U(1)_B$ Nambu–Goldstone mode in the superfluid phase, and evidence of partial chiral-symmetry restoration.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand dense matter phases and symmetry breaking patterns in QCD-like theories—specifically, how hadron spectra evolve with baryon chemical potential in two-color QCD, which serves as a computationally tractable surrogate for studying color-superconductivity and chiral dynamics in real QCD.

Method: Lattice Monte Carlo simulations on a $32^4$ lattice at $T \approx 40\,\mathrm{MeV}$; computation of all meson and diquark correlation functions including disconnected diagrams; extrapolation to zero diquark source to extract the Nambu–Goldstone boson mass; comparison of chiral partner correlators to assess chiral-symmetry restoration.

Result: In the hadronic phase, meson mass hierarchy resembles that of three-color QCD; across the superfluid transition, $\sigma$ and $a_1$ become lighter while pion mass increases with density; the $U(1)_B$ Nambu–Goldstone mode acquires nearly vanishing mass in the superfluid phase after zero-source extrapolation; chiral partners show degeneracy trends suggesting partial chiral-symmetry restoration.

Conclusion: Two-color QCD exhibits rich in-medium hadron spectroscopy: superfluidity induces qualitative changes in mass ordering and reveals emergent approximate symmetries; the results support the interpretation of the lightest diquark mode as the Goldstone boson of $U(1)_B$ breaking and provide quantitative evidence for partial chiral-symmetry restoration at high density.

Abstract: We investigate the chemical-potential dependence of hadron masses in two-color QCD (QC$_2$D) at low temperature using lattice Monte Carlo simulations. We calculate all possible meson and diquark correlation functions, including disconnected diagrams, on a $32^4$ lattice at $T\approx40\,\mathrm{MeV}$. In the hadronic phase, we obtain a meson mass hierarchy similar to that of three-color QCD. After the superfluid transition, the ordering changes: the $\sigma$ and $a_1$ channels become lighter, while the pion mass increases with density. We also examine the diquark source dependence of the Nambu--Goldstone mode of $U(1)_B$ symmetry breaking and find an almost vanishing mass in the superfluid phase after extrapolation to zero source. Finally, we compare correlation functions for chiral partners and discuss indications of partial chiral-symmetry restoration. This contribution is based on our recent study of hadron spectra in dense QC$_2$D~\cite{Iida:2026dmw}.

</details>


### [75] [Fermionic 2+1D SU(2) quantum link models: an ED/DMRG reliability study](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28590)
*Daming Li*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper benchmarks sparse exact diagonalization (ED) and matrix-product-state DMRG for a 2+1D SU(2) quantum link model with dynamical fermions on small lattices, validating correctness and quantifying numerical accuracy across geometries up to 3×3; it establishes a reliability envelope for tensor-network simulations of non-Abelian gauge theories.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Controlled reliability studies of non-Abelian lattice gauge theories with dynamical fermions—critical targets for tensor networks and quantum simulation—are scarce, especially on small systems where exact validation is feasible.

Method: Two complementary Hilbert-space methods are employed: (i) sparse exact diagonalization with explicit cross-site Jordan–Wigner strings, and (ii) matrix-product-state DMRG enforcing total-fermion-number conservation and using a local-term-derived matrix product operator (MPO); both use a five-state {5} truncation for SU(2) links and two-color four-state sites for staggered fermions; validation includes Gauss-law commutation, Hermiticity, pure-gauge limit, and MPO–ED component-wise comparison.

Result: ED and DMRG agree to 10⁻¹³ on 2×2 lattices; agreement degrades to ~10⁻⁵ on 2×3–2×5 ladders; on 3×3, color neutrality dictates G² = 3/4 as exact lower bound at half-filling (odd fermion number), while one hole/doublon restores neutrality and reduces residual to ~2×10⁻⁵ at bond dimension χ = 2048; independent exact-Gauss fPEPS confirms results in the even-filling 3×2 sector.

Conclusion: The study defines a reproducible reliability envelope for non-Abelian gauge-fermion tensor-network benchmarks, revealing geometry- and filling-dependent accuracy limits and guiding requirements for larger-scale simulations—e.g., increased bond dimensions or alternative tensor-network geometries.

Abstract: Non-Abelian lattice gauge theory with dynamical fermions is a central target for tensor-network and quantum-simulation methods, but controlled reliability studies on small systems are scarce. We benchmark two complementary Hilbert-space approaches for a 2+1D SU(2) quantum link model with one staggered fermion flavour on small two-dimensional lattices: sparse exact diagonalization (ED) and matrix-product-state density-matrix renormalization group (DMRG). The link variables use the five-state $\{5\}$ truncation, and fermions are represented by two-colour four-state sites. We implement ED with explicit cross-site Jordan--Wigner strings and DMRG with total-fermion-number conservation and a matrix product operator (MPO) built from local terms. We validate the link algebra, Gauss-law commutation, Hermiticity, the pure-gauge limit, and the MPO against ED component by component. The $2\times2$ methods agree to $10^{-13}$ and the $2\times3$--$2\times5$ ladders are controlled at or below the $10^{-5}$ level. The $3\times3$ geometry illustrates the role of colour neutrality: at half filling the odd fermion number makes $G^2=3/4$ the exact lower bound, while one hole or one doublon restores neutrality and lowers the residual to $\sim2\times10^{-5}$ at $\chi=2048$. An independent exact-Gauss fPEPS provides a reference for the even-filling $3\times2$ sector. The results define a reproducible reliability envelope for non-Abelian gauge-fermion benchmarks and show where larger-scale simulations require larger bond dimensions or different tensor-network geometries.

</details>


### [76] [Progress on finite temperature Sp(4) lattice gauge theory with matter fields](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30493)
*Alexis Verney-Provatas,Chris Allton,Ed Bennett,Luigi Del Debbio,Ryan C. Hill,Deog Ki Hong,Jong-Wan Lee,C.-J. David Lin,Biagio Lucini,Maurizio Piai,Davide Vadacchino*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper investigates the finite-temperature phase structure of Sp(4) lattice gauge theory with two fundamental Wilson fermions—motivated by its relevance to SIMP composite dark matter—and finds evidence for a crossover at light fermion masses and a first-order transition at heavy masses, using Polyakov loop susceptibility scaling on small temporal lattices.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine the nature (order and strength) of the thermal phase transition in Sp(4) lattice gauge theory with two fundamental fermions, which serves as a UV-complete realization of composite dark matter in the SIMP paradigm and may produce a detectable stochastic gravitational wave signal from the early universe.

Method: Lattice Monte Carlo simulations across a 2D parameter space (bare fermion mass and inverse gauge coupling); analysis of finite-volume scaling of Polyakov loop observables—particularly the peak of its susceptibility—using multiple histogram reweighting to distinguish first-order transitions from crossovers; focus on small-Nt lattices for exploratory study.

Result: Strong evidence for a crossover in the light fermion mass regime; preliminary evidence for a first-order phase transition in the heavy fermion mass regime; no conclusive evidence yet for a second-order transition.

Conclusion: The phase structure of Sp(4) gauge theory is mass-dependent, with implications for the gravitational wave signal strength; results justify dedicated continuum-extrapolated studies of Sp(2N) theories to assess their viability as SIMP dark matter models.

Abstract: We explore the finite temperature behaviour of the Sp(4) lattice gauge theory coupled to two dynamical Wilson fermions transforming in the fundamental representation. The continuum theory provides a realisation of composite dark matter within the Strongly Interacting Massive Particle (SIMP) paradigm. Its thermal phase transition in the early universe might have generated an observable relic stochastic gravitational wave signal. Evaluating the sensitivity of future gravitational wave experiments to this signal requires knowledge of the strength of the phase transition. We explore the two dimensional parameter space of the theory, defined by the bare fermion mass and inverse gauge coupling. For this exploratory study we focus on lattices with a small number of sites in the temporal dimension. We analyse the finite volume scaling behaviour of Polyakov loop observables. Specifically the scaling of the peak of the susceptibility, informed by multiple histogram reweighting, is used to discriminate between the presence of a phase transition or crossover. In the light fermion mass regime we find strong indications of a crossover, while for heavy fermions we find preliminary evidence of a first-order phase transition. These findings motivate further studies of the continuum limit for this and other Sp(2N) gauge theories.

</details>


### [77] [Identifying the conformal window by chiral phase boundaries](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30669)
*Jan Philipp Klinger,Reinhold Kaiser,Owe Philipsen,Jonas Schaible*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper investigates the conformal window in many-flavour QCD by studying thermal chiral transitions using unimproved staggered fermions; it finds evidence that N_f=7 exhibits a continuum chiral transition (suggesting broken chiral symmetry), while N_f=8 remains chirally symmetric in the massless continuum limit, consistent with conformal hyperscaling — implying 7 < N_f^* ≤ 8.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To distinguish between QCD-like (chirally broken) and conformal (chirally symmetric) behaviour in many-flavour gauge theories, particularly whether thermal chiral crossovers persist in the continuum chiral limit — a key signature separating theories inside vs. outside the conformal window.

Method: Numerical lattice QCD simulations with unimproved staggered fermions; mapping of chiral phase boundaries in the bare parameter space (N_τ, β, am, N_f); comparison of pseudo-critical surfaces for N_f = 7 and N_f = 8; consistency checks with conformal hyperscaling relations.

Result: For N_f = 7, the thermal chiral transition persists in the continuum chiral limit, indicating spontaneous chiral symmetry breaking; for N_f = 8, no such transition is found in the massless continuum limit, and the observed pseudo-critical surface obeys conformal hyperscaling — strongly supporting conformality.

Conclusion: Eight-flavour QCD lies within the conformal window, while seven-flavour QCD does not; thus the critical number of flavours satisfies 7 < N_f^* ≤ 8.

Abstract: Once the number of massless fermion flavours reaches a critical value $N_f^*$, many-flavour QCD enters the conformal window, where chiral symmetry remains unbroken in the vacuum. At non-zero quark mass, chiral symmetry is explicitly broken, and thermal chiral crossovers can also occur for $N_f\geq N_f^*$. Their presence alone therefore does not distinguish a QCD-like theory from a mass-deformed conformal theory. They differ, however, in whether a thermal transition persists in the continuum chiral limit. To address this, we map out the chiral phase boundaries of unimproved staggered fermions in the bare lattice parameter space $(N_\tau,\beta,am,N_f)$, comparing $N_f=7$ and $N_f=8$. Our results indicate that the thermal chiral transition persists in the continuum chiral limit for $N_f=7$, whereas the massless continuum theory with $N_f=8$ remains chirally symmetric in the vacuum. Furthermore, we demonstrate that the pseudo-critical surface for eight flavours observed for finite quark masses is consistent with conformal hyperscaling, providing further evidence that eight-flavour QCD lies within the conformal window and suggesting $7<N_f^*\leq8$.

</details>


### [78] [Leading-order dynamical QED corrections to the thermodynamics of isospin-asymmetric QCD](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31087)
*Gergely Endrődi,Ádám Fischer,Wei-Ping Huang,Gergely Markó*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper computes the leading-order electromagnetic correction to QCD thermodynamics at nonzero isospin chemical potential μ_I using a lattice QCD+QED perturbative expansion in e, avoiding the sign problem by working at e=0 and measuring the e² coefficient via current-current correlators with a novel planar source.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine how dynamical photons affect the nature of the pion condensation transition in QCD at μ_I ≠ 0 — specifically, whether they convert the second-order transition into a first-order one — despite the complex-action sign problem preventing direct QCD+QED simulation at μ_I ≠ 0.

Method: Perturbative expansion of free energy density f in electromagnetic coupling e around e = 0; measurement of ∂²f/∂e²|ₑ₌₀ as convolution of photon propagator and electromagnetic current-current correlator; use of a newly proposed planar source on existing (2+1)-flavor pure-QCD ensembles at nonzero μ_I and pionic source λ; extrapolation to λ → 0.

Result: ∂²f/∂e²|ₑ₌₀ is consistent with being μ_I-independent below the pion condensation onset and exhibits a sharp rise across the onset, suggesting enhanced electromagnetic sensitivity precisely at the transition.

Conclusion: The observed behavior of the leading-order QED correction supports the expectation that dynamical photons drive the pion condensation transition first-order, and demonstrates feasibility of probing QCD+QED thermodynamics via e-expansion on pure-QCD lattices.

Abstract: We present a first lattice calculation of the leading-order effect of dynamical photons on the thermodynamics of QCD at nonzero isospin chemical potential $\mu_I$, across the onset of charged-pion condensation. In QCD this onset is a second-order transition, which dynamical photons are expected, on general grounds, to drive first order. However, a direct simulation is not possible since unequal quark charges render the QCD+QED action complex at nonzero $\mu_I$. We therefore expand the free energy density $f$ in the electromagnetic coupling $e$ around $e=0$, where the sign problem is absent. The leading-order expansion coefficient, $\partial^2 f/\partial e^2|_{e=0}$, is the convolution of the photon propagator with the electromagnetic current-current correlator, which we measure with a newly proposed planar source on existing (2+1)-flavor pure-QCD ensembles at nonzero $\mu_I$ and pionic source $\lambda$. At physical quark masses and a single lattice spacing, after extrapolating to vanishing $\lambda$, we find that $\partial^2 f/\partial e^2|_{e=0}$ is, within errors, independent of $\mu_I$ below the onset and rises sharply across it.

</details>


<div id='hep-ph'></div>

### [79] [Superheavy gravitational dark matter from runaway bubble collisions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30113)
*Cheng-Wei Chiang,Van Que Tran*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes that stable, gravitationally coupled particles (e.g., Dirac fermions) can be produced as dark matter via gravitational pair production during first-order cosmological phase transitions—specifically through collisions of runaway bubble walls mediated by graviton exchange. The mechanism yields sufficient abundance even for superheavy candidates (~10^17 GeV) at low reheating temperatures where conventional freeze-in fails.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the origin of dark matter for extremely heavy, feebly interacting particles that evade standard thermal and freeze-in production mechanisms—particularly when reheating temperature is far below the particle mass.

Method: Computes gravitational particle production during vacuum bubble wall collisions in a first-order phase transition, using effective field theory and graviton exchange; derives analytic yield formula for a Dirac fermion based on Hubble rate H, transition rate β, reheating temperature T_reh, and reduced Planck mass M_P.

Result: Obtains yield Y_X ≈ 6.8×10⁻³ (H/β)(T_reh/M_P)³; shows production remains efficient up to masses ~ (H/β)M_P/ν² (ν ∼ O(1)), enabling viable dark matter up to ~10¹⁷ GeV even for T_reh ≪ m_X.

Conclusion: Gravitational particle production from bubble wall collisions provides a robust, minimal mechanism for generating superheavy dark matter without non-gravitational couplings—complementing or superseding freeze-in in low-reheating scenarios.

Abstract: A stable particle that couples only through gravity is pair-produced in collisions of runaway bubble walls by graviton exchange. For a transition of duration $1/\beta$, a Dirac fermion reaches the yield $Y_X\simeq6.8\times10^{-3}(H/\beta)(T_{\rm reh}/M_{\rm P})^3$, where $H$ is the Hubble rate of the vacuum energy, $T_{\rm reh}$ the reheating temperature and $M_{\rm P}$ the reduced Planck mass. The yield is flat up to masses near $(H/\beta)M_{\rm P}/\nu^2$, where $\nu$ of order one sets the wall-field excursion. Gravity alone then gives dark matter up to about $10^{17}$~GeV at reheating temperatures far below its mass, where freeze-in fails.

</details>


### [80] [Exact analytic solutions for the $n=1$ chameleon field in planar systems with an arbitrary number of uniform layers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30712)
*Chad Briddon,Mario Pitschmann*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives exact analytic solutions for the n=1 chameleon scalar field in a planar multi-layered (N-region) uniform-density system, improves upon prior approximate treatments of the CANNEX experiment by modeling its full 15-layer structure, and finds a ~7% increase in predicted chameleon pressure—though insufficient to extend current parameter-space constraints.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome the scarcity of exact analytic solutions for non-linear screened scalar field models (e.g., chameleon), and to improve phenomenological forecasts for fifth-force experiments like CANNEX by moving beyond oversimplified single-object approximations.

Method: Constructs exact analytic solutions for the n=1 chameleon field equation in a piecewise-uniform 1D (planar) geometry with N discrete density layers; applies these solutions to the full 15-layer physical configuration of the CANNEX experiment (including individual dielectric and metallic layers); computes chameleon-induced pressure and compares with prior approximate (e.g., slab-averaged or two-body) treatments.

Result: Exact solutions are obtained for arbitrary N; applied to CANNEX, they yield up to ~7% higher chameleon pressure versus previous two-object approximations; however, this enhanced signal remains below projected experimental sensitivity thresholds and thus does not open new parameter space.

Conclusion: Exact analytic solutions for multi-layer chameleon configurations are feasible and quantifiably improve theoretical modeling of precision fifth-force experiments, but current experimental sensitivities—even with full structural fidelity—still cannot constrain previously unexplored regions of the chameleon parameter space.

Abstract: Scalar fields have long been investigated as a potential dark energy candidate. Of these models, screened scalar fields like the chameleon have received interest due to their ability to avoid observational constraints. However, the non-linear nature of these models has led to there being only a small number of exact analytic solutions within the literature. In this work, we present exact analytic solutions for the $n=1$ chameleon model in a planar system consisting of $N$ discrete regions of uniform density. We compare these to the relevant approximate solutions within the literature. We also use our solutions to forecast constraints from CANNEX, a recent experiment devised to measure Casimir forces and detect hypothetical fifth forces. Unlike in previous works, we consider the full fifteen-layered setup of CANNEX, as opposed to treating the plate and wall as single uniform objects. Doing so revealed an increase in the induced chameleon pressure of up to $\sim 7 \%$. However, the resulting gain is insufficient to probe new regions of parameter space at the projected sensitivities.

</details>


### [81] [Fate of Vainshtein Screening for UV/IR Mixing with a $P(X)$ Higgs Classicalizer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30764)
*Eléana Berteaux,Florian Nortier*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates whether Higgs classicalization—a non-Wilsonian UV-completion mechanism—can be realized in gauged k-essence/k-chameleon $P(X)$ models; it finds that Vainshtein screening fails due to strong-coupling breakdown inside the screening region, ruling out this specific class for Higgs classicalization, but does not exclude the possibility in other frameworks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the little hierarchy problem by embedding classicalization in the electroweak Higgs sector, requiring a light force carrier and semiclassical object formation above the strong-coupling scale.

Method: Analysis of Vainshtein screening conditions in gauged $P(X)$ theories (k-essence and k-chameleon), including attempts to combine with chameleon screening via gauge kinetic function; examination of strong-coupling behavior within the screening region.

Result: Vainshtein screening fails in all considered $P(X)$ models—even with engineered chameleon synergy—due to strong-coupling breakdown inside the screening region.

Conclusion: The $P(X)$ class of gauged k-essence and k-chameleon models is unsuitable for Higgs classicalization; however, this result is model-specific and does not constitute a general no-go theorem.

Abstract: Classicalization is a non-Wilsonian framework in which an effective field theory self-completes above its strong-coupling scale by generating semiclassical objects that decay into many soft quanta, thereby realizing ultraviolet-infrared mixing. A key requirement of this mechanism is that the force carrier be lighter than the strong-coupling scale. A natural application would be to embed classicalization in the electroweak Higgs sector to address the little hierarchy problem. In this work, we investigate whether Higgs classicalization can occur within the $P(X)$ class of gauged k-essence and k-chameleon models. For classicalization to occur, the theory must support Vainshtein screening around a sharply localized source. Our results show that it fails to do so, even when one attempts to engineer a symbiosis with chameleon screening through a gauge kinetic function, owing to a strong-coupling breakdown inside the screening region. These $P(X)$ theories therefore fail to provide a suitable framework for Higgs classicalization. Nevertheless, we emphasize that our results, obtained for a specific class of models, should not be interpreted as a no-go theorem for Higgs classicalization.

</details>


### [82] [Revealing Dark Matter in the Dark-Electron Rate of Xenon Detectors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28211)
*Lillian Santos-Olmsted,Rebecca K. Leane,Carlos Blanco,John F. Beacom*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes using the raw dark-electron rate in xenon-based dark matter experiments to perform an annual-modulation search for sub-GeV dark matter scattering off electrons—improving sensitivity from S ≲ B to S ≲ √B without new hardware.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing xenon experiments suffer severely reduced exposure in the single-electron regime due to aggressive background suppression, limiting sensitivity to sub-GeV dark matter–electron scattering; annual modulation offers a statistically more powerful discriminant in low-signal, high-background regimes.

Method: Leverages continuously recorded or trigger-window-resolved dark-electron rates from existing DAQ systems (XENONnT, LZ, PandaX-4T, etc.) and models annual modulation signal and background to estimate projected sensitivity via S ≲ √B scaling.

Result: Projected sensitivity improvements are dramatic across current and next-generation xenon experiments (e.g., XENONnT, LZ, PandaX-4T, PandaX-xT, XLZD), enabling viable exploration of previously inaccessible sub-GeV DM–electron parameter space.

Conclusion: Annual-modulation analysis of the dark-electron rate is a hardware-free, immediately applicable strategy that significantly enhances sub-GeV dark matter discovery potential in xenon time projection chambers.

Abstract: There is high interest in improving experimental sensitivity to sub-GeV dark matter (DM) that scatters with electrons, as compelling theoretical models seem within reach. At the lowest DM masses, most of the expected signal lies in the single-electron regime, but so do large backgrounds. Xenon experiments therefore either do not yet search there at all or, as in XENONnT, apply cuts so severe that only $\sim$$10^{-4}$ of the exposure is retained, with sensitivity set by the criterion $S \lesssim B$, where those are the signal and background counts. We advocate that xenon detectors instead utilize most of their dark-electron rate (i.e., the total raw electron hit rate) and perform an annual-modulation search, for which the sensitivity scales as $S \lesssim \sqrt{B}$. Modern DAQs already record this rate, continuously in triggerless systems such as the XENONnT and PandaX-4T DAQs, and within trigger windows in LZ, so no new hardware is required. Using measured dark-electron rates, we estimate the sensitivity of XENONnT, LZ, PandaX-4T, PandaX-xT, and XLZD. We find that dramatic gains in sensitivity are possible, motivating new analyses of present and future xenon data.

</details>


### [83] [Vector boson scattering in composite Higgs models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28214)
*Rosy Caliri,Manuel Kunkel,Werner Porod*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies vector boson scattering in Composite Higgs models with a fermionic origin and a single pseudo-Nambu-Goldstone Higgs, showing that although LHC data constrain new resonances to be above ~2 TeV, $WZjj$ production—especially with longitudinally polarized bosons—exhibits significant SM-deviating tails in kinematic distributions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe the electroweak symmetry breaking mechanism via vector boson scattering, particularly in fermionic Composite Higgs models where only one pseudo-Nambu-Goldstone boson plays the role of the Higgs.

Method: Analyzes $WZjj$ production as a benchmark process; examines kinematic distribution tails (e.g., invariant masses, rapidities) for deviations from Standard Model predictions, with emphasis on longitudinal vector boson polarization.

Result: Significant deviations from SM predictions are found in the high-energy tails of distributions—even when resonances are excluded below ~2 TeV by current LHC data—especially for longitudinally polarized $W$ and $Z$ bosons.

Conclusion: Vector boson scattering in $WZjj$ provides a sensitive probe of Composite Higgs dynamics beyond direct resonance searches, with longitudinal polarization enhancing sensitivity to anomalous EWSB effects.

Abstract: Vector boson scattering is a powerful tool to test the mechanism of electroweak symmetry breaking. We focus on Composite Higgs models with an underlying fermionic description and assume that only one pseudo Nambu-Goldstone boson contributes to electroweak symmetry breaking, which we identify as the Higgs state of the Standard Model. In these scenarios, LHC data imply lower bounds on the predicted resonances below 2 TeV depending on the scenario. Using $WZjj$ production as a benchmark, we demonstrate that the tails of various distributions nevertheless show significant deviations from Standard Model predictions. This holds in particular for the case of longitudinally polarised vector bosons.

</details>


### [84] [Timelike electric dipole form factors of the baryon octet: short-distance kernels and baryonic rescattering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28492)
*Chao-Qiang Geng,Xiang-Nan Jin,Chia-Wei Liu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes the leading-twist contribution of quark electric dipole moments (EDMs), especially d_s, to timelike baryon octet form factors at the J/ψ mass using lattice light-cone distribution amplitudes; it finds the Σ/Λ short-distance ratio deviates from the nonrelativistic quark model, attributes enhanced Σ contribution to kaon exchange, derives |d_s| < 1.3×10⁻¹⁵ e·cm from BESIII data, and shows joint analysis with rescattering improves sensitivity by >25%.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To constrain the strange-quark electric dipole moment (d_s) — a probe of CP violation beyond the Standard Model — via precision measurements of timelike baryon form factors at charmonium energies, where lattice QCD inputs enable controlled hadronic predictions.

Method: Computes leading-twist contributions of quark EDMs to timelike electromagnetic form factors of the baryon octet (Λ, Σ, Ξ) at the J/ψ mass using lattice QCD-calculated light-cone distribution amplitudes; incorporates meson-exchange modeling (especially K-exchange) for Σ–Λ mixing; performs error propagation over lattice input ranges, includes rescattering effects with 1 GeV cutoff, and assesses renormalization-scale dependence.

Result: Short-distance Σ/Λ ratio = −0.080⁺⁰.⁰³⁷₋₀.₀₉₈, exceeding the nonrelativistic value −1/3; Λ form factor ≈ 5×10⁻⁴ d_s; BESIII data yields |d_s| < 1.3×10⁻¹⁵ e·cm (95% CL); joint Λ/Σ⁺/Ξ fits with rescattering improve Λ-only limits by >25%; renormalization-scale variation alters projected limits by ~factor of two.

Conclusion: Lattice-informed analysis of timelike baryon form factors provides a competitive, theoretically robust constraint on d_s; inclusion of dynamical hadronic effects (meson exchange, rescattering) is essential for accurate interpretation, and future high-statistics data at BESIII and STCF will significantly tighten EDM limits.

Abstract: We calculate the leading-twist contribution of quark electric dipole moments (EDMs) to the timelike form factors of the baryon octet at the $J/\psi$ mass using lattice light-cone distribution amplitudes. For a strange-quark EDM $d_s$, the short-distance $\Sigma/\Lambda$ ratio is $-0.080^{+0.037}_{-0.098}$ over the lattice input ranges. This range lies above the nonrelativistic quark-model value $-1/3$. In the meson-exchange model, kaon exchange feeds the large $\Xi$ source into the $\Sigma$ channel and makes the $\Sigma$ and $\Lambda$ form factors comparable in magnitude. The short-distance $\Lambda$ form factor is about $5\times10^{-4}d_s$. The BESIII measurement then gives $|d_s|<1.3\times10^{-15}\,e\,\mathrm{cm}$ at 95% confidence level. With rescattering at a $1\,\mathrm{GeV}$ cutoff, joint fits to projected $\Lambda$, $\Sigma^+$ and $\Xi$ sensitivities lower the $\Lambda$-only upper limits by more than 25% at BESIII and the Super Tau-Charm Facility. Renormalization-scale variation changes the projected limits by about a factor of two.

</details>


### [85] [Quark and gluon gravitational form factors of the nucleon at next-to-leading order](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30124)
*Ulaş Özdem*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes nucleon gravitational form factors (A, J, D, and trace form factor c̄) using three-point QCD sum rules at next-to-leading order in αₛ for both quark and gluon energy-momentum tensors in the MS-bar scheme; finds significant O(αₛ) reductions in all form factors, good agreement with lattice QCD for A_tot, J_tot, D_tot in 1–2.5 GeV², and identifies systematic limitations—especially non-vanishing gluon condensate contributions and Borel-window constraints—that affect reliability of A and J but not D.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide first-principles, continuum-based predictions for nucleon gravitational form factors—key to understanding mass, spin, and mechanical structure—and assess reliability of QCD sum rules for energy-momentum tensor matrix elements, especially under operator mixing, renormalization, and condensate contamination.

Method: Three-point QCD sum rules at NLO in αₛ for quark and gluon EMT operators in the MS-bar scheme; uses antisymmetric Borel moments for protected estimators of D and c̄; separates quark/gluon contributions; incorporates two-point perturbative and condensate terms; validates via nucleon EMT conservation identities and comparison to lattice QCD.

Result: O(αₛ) corrections reduce A_tot by 32–40%, J_tot by 22–27%, |D_tot| by 21–27%; at Q² = 1.5 GeV²: A_tot = 0.337 ± 0.113, J_tot = 0.206 ± 0.100, D_tot = −0.206 ± 0.121; D_tot agrees well with lattice and is most reliable due to dominant perturbative correlator; A_tot and J_tot suffer from large non-scaling gluon-condensate terms and underestimate gluon contributions relative to lattice; c̄ reported only at LO with condensates due to missing finite g_μν term.

Conclusion: QCD sum rules at NLO yield quantitatively useful, albeit systematically limited, predictions for nucleon gravitational form factors; D is robust and lattice-consistent, while A and J require improved treatment of gluonic vacuum structure and operator mixing; antisymmetric Borel analysis is essential for protected extraction of D and c̄; results highlight tension between condensate dominance and lattice expectations in the gluon sector.

Abstract: We compute the quark and gluon gravitational form factors $A$, $J$ and $D$ of the nucleon in three-point QCD sum rules at next-to-leading order in $\alpha_s$ for the quark and gluon energy-momentum tensors (EMT), treating both at the same order in the $\overline{\rm MS}$ scheme. The nucleon EMT also tests the method, showing where the sum rules are reliable and why. Protected estimators of $D$ and of the trace form factor $\bar c$ require antisymmetric Borel moments, since none with equal Borel masses alone is protected. The $\mathcal{O}(\alpha_s)$ corrections reduce $A_{\rm tot}$ by $32$--$40\%$, $J_{\rm tot}$ by $22$--$27\%$ and $|D_{\rm tot}|$ by $21$--$27\%$, since the perturbative share of the two-point function is much larger. The total form factors agree with lattice QCD for $1\le Q^2\le2.5$~GeV$^2$. At $Q^2=1.5$~GeV$^2$, $A_{\rm tot}=0.337\pm0.113$, $J_{\rm tot}=0.206\pm0.100$ and $D_{\rm tot}=-0.206\pm0.121$. $D$ is the most reliable result: its correlator is mostly perturbative, and its quark and gluon parts agree with the lattice. In $A$ and $J$, a local gluon-condensate term that does not fall with $Q^2$ is the largest, and their gluon shares are well below the lattice, possibly because the gluon EMT couples at this order only through gluon exchange and the condensate. A working region exists only near $\beta=1$, and the constant part of transitions cannot be separated within the Borel windows. The individual conservation identities deviate at one point by two standard deviations, consistent with a missing finite term in the $g_{\mu\nu}$ structure, and $\bar c$ is therefore given at leading order with condensates.

</details>


### [86] [Towards a Global CE$ν$NS Picture](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30638)
*Heerak Banerjee,Philipp Eller*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a Bayesian, model-agnostic framework using a generalized kinematic basis to extract and interpret the CEνNS differential cross-section from COHERENT CsI and LAr data, yielding joint spectral + normalization constraints and enabling combined analysis via an open-source Julia package.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome model-dependence in CEνNS cross-section extraction and enable robust, shape-sensitive inference from low-statistics, background-limited nuclear recoil data.

Method: Constructs a flexible, physics-motivated parametric basis from universal kinematic terms (e.g., nuclear form factor, energy transfer dependence); applies Bayesian inference to COHERENT CsI and liquid argon datasets; performs standalone and combined analyses; implements full forward modeling with automatic differentiation and parallelization in Julia.

Result: Achieves normalization constraints consistent with COHERENT Collaboration results, plus novel quantitative constraints on spectral shape parameters; combined analysis improves sensitivity to cross-section features degenerate in individual detectors (e.g., low-energy behavior, form factor deviations).

Conclusion: The framework provides a rigorous, reusable, and extensible approach for model-independent CEνNS phenomenology, facilitating robust comparison across experiments and BSM model testing without committing to specific interaction Lagrangians.

Abstract: We present a general framework for extracting and interpreting the Coherent Elastic Neutrino-Nucleus Scattering (CE$\nu$NS) differential cross-section from experimental data. A generalized parametric basis, constructed from common kinematic terms recurring in generalized neutrino interaction models, is employed to numerically constrain the shape and normalization of the observed CE$\nu$NS response. The framework was applied to the COHERENT CsI and liquid argon datasets using Bayesian inference, yielding normalization constraints comparable to those reported by the Collaboration while providing additional constraints on the correlated spectral shape. A combined analysis was also performed, exploiting the complementary sensitivities of the two datasets to constrain features of the differential cross-section that are less accessible to either dataset individually. The framework has been implemented in the open-source Julia package this http URL, which supports modular forward modeling, parallel computation, and automatic differentiation.

</details>


### [87] [A general formalism for electromagnetic production of two mesons on the nucleon](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30755)
*Reddy Malik Priandika,Samson Clymton,Terry Mart*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a general covariant formalism for electromagnetic two-meson production on the nucleon (γᵥN → Φ₁Φ₂B), extending the Berends et al. decomposition to double-meson channels; it defines 16 gauge- and Lorentz-invariant current matrices (4 vanish for real photons), maps Dirac amplitudes to CGLN-like Pauli amplitudes, yields compact expressions for five-fold differential cross sections and response functions, and demonstrates gauge invariance restoration via tree-level Born calculations for γp → π⁰π⁰p and γp → π⁺π⁰n near threshold.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a systematic, gauge-invariant, and model-independent framework for analyzing double-meson photo- and electroproduction—addressing the lack of a unified formalism comparable to single-meson CGLN or Berends decompositions, especially for coupled-channel studies across broad energy ranges.

Method: Covariant decomposition of the hadronic current into 16 gauge- and Lorentz-invariant Dirac matrices (generalizing Berends et al.); mapping to 16 CGLN-like Pauli amplitudes; derivation of the five-fold differential cross section and transverse/longitudinal/interference response functions; explicit tree-level calculation using nucleon Born diagrams for γp → π⁰π⁰p and γp → π⁺π⁰n near threshold, including verification of gauge invariance restoration upon summing diagrams and incorporating hadronic form factors.

Result: A complete, minimal, and physically transparent basis for double-meson production amplitudes; explicit analytic expressions linking Dirac amplitudes to observables; demonstration that individual Feynman diagrams violate gauge invariance but the full Born amplitude restores it—even with form factors; compact, symmetric forms for cross sections and response functions.

Conclusion: The formalism provides a versatile, rigorous, and practical foundation for theoretical modeling and experimental analysis of double-meson photo- and electroproduction, enabling consistent single- and coupled-channel treatments, direct connection to Feynman diagrams, and robust implementation of gauge invariance.

Abstract: We develop a general formalism for the electromagnetic production of two mesons on the nucleon, $\gamma_{\rm v}N\to\Phi_1\Phi_2B$, that can be used in single-channel as well as coupled-channel analyses over a wide range of energies. In the spirit of the decomposition introduced by Berends et al. for single-meson electroproduction, the hadronic current is expanded in a set of 16 gauge- and Lorentz-invariant matrices, four of which vanish for real photons. The corresponding Lorentz-invariant (Dirac) amplitudes can be obtained from any reaction model and are mapped onto 16 CGLN-like Pauli amplitudes, from which the five-fold differential cross section and its transverse, longitudinal, and interference response functions follow directly. Although these matrices are not all linearly independent, they follow directly from the covariant decomposition of the current, so the amplitudes can be read off from the Feynman diagrams and the observables take a simpler, more symmetric form. As a first application, we calculate the $\gamma p\to\pi^0\pi^0p$ and $\gamma p\to\pi^+\pi^0n$ reactions near threshold from tree-level nucleon Born terms. This example shows explicitly how the Dirac amplitudes are read off from Feynman diagrams and how gauge invariance, which is violated by the individual diagrams, is restored in the total amplitude and preserved when hadronic form factors are included. The formalism thus provides a versatile basis for future theoretical and phenomenological studies of double-meson photo- and electroproduction. A more complete abstract is provided in the paper.

</details>


### [88] [Light on GeV-scale seesaw](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30780)
*Enrico Bertuzzo,Michele Frigerio,Salvador Urrea*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores how transition dipole couplings between sterile neutrinos and photons can enhance production and open new decay channels, enabling next-generation experiments like T2K and SHiP to probe the minimal seesaw parameter space—previously inaccessible—via charged-lepton pair signatures from off-shell photons.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The minimal seesaw mechanism predicts tiny active-sterile mixing, rendering GeV-scale sterile neutrinos undetectable in current beam-dump and accelerator-neutrino experiments; however, oscillation data require at least two sterile neutrinos (N₁, N₂), motivating exploration of additional interactions—specifically photon-coupled transition dipoles—to enhance observability.

Method: The authors analyze sterile-neutrino production and decay via electromagnetic transition dipoles, compute event rates for charged-lepton pair signatures from off-shell photon decays, and assess sensitivity of T2K near detector and SHiP to the seesaw parameter space as a function of sterile-neutrino mass m_N and dipole strength d.

Result: T2K near detector can probe m_N ≲ 500 MeV and SHiP can probe m_N ≲ 5 GeV; for d ≳ (10 TeV)⁻¹, SHiP can resolve decay channels dependent on active-sterile mixing, yielding a distinctive signature hierarchy.

Conclusion: Transition dipole couplings enable direct experimental tests of the minimal seesaw mechanism by making its parameter space accessible to near-future experiments through lepton-pair signatures, turning previously unreachable predictions into falsifiable observables.

Abstract: The next generation of beam-dump and accelerator-neutrino experiments will probe GeV-scale sterile neutrinos with unprecedented sensitivity. However, the tiny active-sterile mixing predicted by the minimal seesaw mechanism is expected to remain out of reach. Oscillation data require at least two sterile neutrinos, $N_1$ and $N_2$, which may couple to the photon through a transition dipole. Such a coupling can radically change the picture, both by strongly enhancing sterile-neutrino production and by opening new decay channels. We identify charged-lepton pairs produced by an off-shell photon as the most promising experimental signature. We show that the seesaw band can be probed by the T2K near detector for sterile-neutrino masses $m_N\lesssim 500$ MeV, and by SHiP for $m_N\lesssim 5$ GeV. For a sufficiently large dipole, $d\gtrsim (10~\mathrm{TeV})^{-1}$, SHiP can selectively measure decay channels that require nonzero active-sterile mixing. This would give rise to a distinctive hierarchy of signatures, providing a direct test of the seesaw mechanism.

</details>


### [89] [Diffractive Photoproduction of High $p_T$ Photons in Ultraperipheral Collisions at the LHC](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30966)
*João V. Bulhões,Victor P. Goncalves*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies diffractive photoproduction of high-pT photons in ultraperipheral LHC collisions (pp, pPb, PbPb), incorporating NLO BFKL Pomeron corrections to estimate cross sections and differential distributions; results indicate measurable rates that can constrain BFKL dynamics and advance high-energy QCD understanding.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To constrain BFKL dynamics and improve understanding of high-energy QCD through a clean diffractive probe—photoproduction of large-transverse-momentum photons—in ultraperipheral collisions.

Method: Theoretical calculation of cross sections and differential distributions for diffractive photoproduction of high-pT photons in ultraperipheral pp, pPb, and PbPb collisions, using multiple BFKL Pomeron models and including next-to-leading-order (NLO) corrections to the BFKL characteristic function.

Result: Predicted cross sections are sizable across all three collision systems; differential distributions show sensitivity to BFKL modeling and NLO corrections, supporting feasibility of experimental measurement at the LHC.

Conclusion: Diffractive photoproduction of large-pT photons in ultraperipheral collisions is a promising channel for testing and constraining BFKL evolution in high-energy QCD; future measurements can discriminate between BFKL implementations and refine theoretical understanding.

Abstract: We investigate diffractive photoproduction of large-transverse-momentum photons in ultraperipheral collisions at the LHC, considering different collision systems and alternative descriptions of the BFKL Pomeron. We estimate cross sections and differential distributions including NLO corrections to the BFKL characteristic function and assess the prospects for measuring this process in ultraperipheral pp, pPb, and PbPb collisions. We find that the cross sections are sizable, and that future measurements in this channel can help constrain BFKL dynamics and improve our understanding of high-energy QCD.

</details>


### [90] [A proposal for an LHC search for baryon number violation in complementarity with trilepton nucleon decay searches](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31142)
*Amit Bhoonah,Francis M. Burk,Da Liu,Tong Ou,Deepak Sathyan*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper bridges low-energy nucleon decay limits (Super-K) and high-energy collider searches (LHC) for Baryon Number Violation (BNV) at the TeV scale, deriving model-independent EFT constraints on $B - L/3$-conserving operators, showing Super-K trilepton decay bounds are stronger than charm-baryon ones, and proposing a dedicated LHC search for a diquark → leptoquark + dilepton signature yielding $\bar{t} \ell^+ \ell^+ \cancel{E}_T$, with optimized cuts extending sensitivity beyond current same-sign dilepton analyses.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile stringent low-energy trilepton nucleon decay limits from Super-Kamiokande with TeV-scale Baryon Number Violation, and to motivate and enable a complementary, dedicated LHC search using existing data — exploiting the complementarity between rare-process and collider probes of BNV.

Method: Effective field theory analysis to derive model-independent bounds on higher-dimensional $B - L/3$-conserving BNV operators; comparison of loop-induced trilepton nucleon decay vs. tree-level charm-baryon decay sensitivities; UV completion via an s-channel diquark resonance decaying to leptoquark + dilepton; collider phenomenology study including event generation, detector simulation (Delphes), and development of optimized selection cuts targeting $\bar{t} \ell^+ \ell^+ \cancel{E}_T$ final states using LHC Run 2 data.

Result: Super-K trilepton decay limits constrain the BNV scale to $\mathcal{O}(1{-}30)$ TeV — stronger than charm-baryon bounds and leaving room for LHC discovery; the proposed diquark model yields a distinctive $\bar{t} \ell^+ \ell^+ \cancel{E}_T$ signature; optimized cuts significantly extend reach beyond ATLAS/CMS same-sign dilepton searches, achieving sensitivity complementary to Super-K and projected Hyper-K limits using only Run 2 data.

Conclusion: Low-energy trilepton nucleon decay limits and TeV-scale BNV collider searches are highly complementary; dedicated LHC analyses targeting $B - L/3$-type BNV signatures — especially those involving top quarks and same-sign leptons with missing energy — are well-motivated, feasible with existing data, and capable of probing unexplored parameter space inaccessible to current nucleon decay experiments.

Abstract: We investigate the compatibility of existing limits on trilepton nucleon decay by the Super-Kamiokande (Super-K) experiment with Baryon Number Violation (BNV) at the TeV scale and motivate a dedicated search using existing data at the LHC. Working within an effective field theory framework, we derive model-independent constraints on higher-dimensional operators associated with $B - L/3$ conserving texture of BNV. We demonstrate that existing Super-K limits on loop-level trilepton nucleon decay provide significantly stronger constraints compared to tree-level charm-baryon decays induced by these operators. These constraints translate into bounds of $O(1-30)$ TeV on the BNV scale, leaving open the possibility of an LHC search for $B - L/3$ type BNV. Motivated by this complementarity, we investigate the collider phenomenology of a representative UV completion featuring an $s$-channel diquark resonance that decays into a leptoquark--dilepton pair, producing a distinctive final state containing an anti-top quark, two charged anti-leptons, and missing transverse energy. Our results indicate that, although same-sign dilepton searches by ATLAS and CMS already place indirect constraints on this model, a dedicated analysis based on the selection cuts we develop significantly extends the reach for our chosen benchmarks. In particular, using only Run 2 data, we demonstrate complementary reach to limits on trilepton nucleon decay searches set by the Super-K experiment and to the projected reach of Hyper-Kamiokande. The selection cuts are applicable beyond our representative model.

</details>


### [91] [Scotogenic dark matter from gauged $L_μ+L_τ$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28223)
*J. Echeverria-Puentes,Sergey Kovalenko,Pablo San Martin Sartori,Carlos A. Vaquera-Araujo,Yoxara Villamizar,Jilberto Zamora Saa*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a novel scotogenic model with gauged $L_\mu + L_\tau$ symmetry, where spontaneous breaking by two units yields a remnant matter parity $M_P$, stabilizing a singlet-like CP-even scalar as viable Majorana neutrino mass-generating dark matter; it satisfies relic density, electroweak precision (up to $\epsilon \sim 10^{-2}$), and current leptophilic $Z'$ constraints.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a theoretically consistent and phenomenologically viable scotogenic framework that simultaneously explains light neutrino masses (via loop-induced Majorana masses) and provides a stable dark matter candidate, while embedding the symmetry in a gauged $L_\mu + L_\tau$ setup that avoids anomalies and yields an automatic matter parity.

Method: The authors build a renormalizable quantum field theory with gauged $U(1)_{L_\mu + L_\tau}$, introduce two scalar singlets (one charged under the gauge group, one neutral) to break the symmetry by two units, thereby generating $M_P$ as a remnant $\mathbb{Z}_2$ symmetry; they compute one-loop neutrino masses, analyze scalar dark matter annihilation channels ($s$-channel $Z'$, Higgs portal), derive thermal relic density via Boltzmann equation, and confront constraints from electroweak precision observables (e.g., $S$, $T$), beam-dump experiments (e.g., NA64, E141), and LHC dilepton and mono-$X$ searches.

Result: A viable parameter space exists where a predominantly singlet CP-even scalar constitutes thermal-relic-compatible dark matter ($m_h \sim 50\text{--}500\,\text{GeV}$, $g_{Z'} \sim 0.01\text{--}0.3$, $\epsilon \lesssim 10^{-2}$); $Z'$ mass $M_{Z'} \gtrsim 100\,\text{MeV}$ evades all current leptophilic bounds; invisible decays $Z' \to \nu\nu$ dominate for $M_{Z'} > 2m_{\text{DM}}$, enabling mono-photon/mono-jet signatures at colliders.

Conclusion: The model successfully unifies neutrino mass generation and dark matter stabilization within a minimal, anomaly-free gauged $L_\mu + L_\tau$ framework; it predicts testable collider and direct detection signatures distinct from conventional WIMP paradigms, particularly through leptonic $Z'$ couplings and matter-parity-protected scalar DM.

Abstract: In this work, we introduce the first scotogenic model based on a gauged $L_{\mu} + L_{\tau}$ symmetry whose spontaneous breaking by two units leaves a remnant matter parity $M_P$. The resulting light active neutrinos are of Majorana nature and the lightest electrically neutral state mediating the scotogenic loop is stabilized by $M_P$ and can be identified as a weakly interacting massive particle dark matter candidate. We analyze the phenomenology of a predominantly singlet-like CP-even scalar dark matter candidate and the representative regions of parameter space compatible with the observed thermal relic abundance. The $Z'$ boson is compatible with a broad range of parameters relevant for dark matter. In particular, electroweak precision constraints do not lead to significant tensions for kinetic mixing values up to $\epsilon\sim 10^{-2}$. We discuss the implications of existing searches for leptophilic gauge bosons and the conditions under which visible and invisible collider signatures become relevant.

</details>


### [92] [Chiral and deconfinement thermodynamics of the Polyakov-quark linear sigma model with and without charm quark: an SU(3) versus SU(4) comparison](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28429)
*Nourhan M. Rfeek,Hossam Wahid,Fatima M. Rfeek,Alexandra Friesen,Yuri Kalinovsky,Saleh O. Allehabi,Azzah A. Alshehri,Ashraf F. El-Sherif,Abdel Nasser Tawfik*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper uses the Polyakov-loop extended linear-sigma model (PLSM) to compare 3-flavor (u,d,s) and 4-flavor (u,d,s,c) formulations of strongly interacting matter, isolating charm-quark thermal effects; it reproduces lattice-QCD thermodynamics, finds charm is thermally suppressed near chiral crossover (Tc ≈ 210–218 MeV), and confirms smooth crossover behavior at all accessible μB.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To isolate the thermal contribution of the charm quark to strongly interacting matter by comparing three-flavor (u,d,s) and four-flavor (u,d,s,c) PLSM formulations on equal footing, while simultaneously describing chiral dynamics and confinement consistently with lattice-QCD data.

Method: Uses the Polyakov-loop extended linear-sigma model (PLSM) in mean-field approximation; incorporates chiral symmetry breaking (explicit and spontaneous) and confinement via a logarithmic Polyakov-loop potential calibrated to continuum lattice-QCD equation of state; computes condensates, Polyakov loop, pressure, trace anomaly, entropy density, and pseudocritical boundaries across T and μB.

Result: Charm quark remains thermally suppressed across the chiral crossover (mc ≫ Tc); SU(3) and SU(4) equations of state nearly coincide below ~2Tc after SB normalization; entropy density agrees with HotQCD 2+1(+1)-flavor data; chiral transition temperatures are Tc = 210 MeV (SU(3)) and 218 MeV (SU(4)), with small curvature parameters; hadron-parton transition remains a smooth crossover for all accessible μB.

Conclusion: The charm quark contributes negligibly to bulk thermodynamics near the chiral crossover due to its large mass; its main effect is to raise the ideal-gas (Stefan-Boltzmann) limit and introduce a high-temperature tail in the EoS; the smooth crossover nature of the transition is robust against inclusion of charm and persists across the μB range studied.

Abstract: Using the Polyakov-loop extended linear-sigma model (PLSM), we investigate strongly interacting matter at finite temperature and baryon density by comparing the three-flavor (u,d,s) and four-flavor (u,d,s,c) formulations on equal footing, with the aim of isolating the thermal contribution of the charm quark.
