The model describes chiral dynamics through explicit and spontaneous symmetry breaking and reproduces the vacuum spectra of scalar and pseudoscalar mesons. Confinement is incorporated through a logarithmic Polyakov-loop potential calibrated to the continuum lattice-QCD equation of state (EoS). Within the mean-field approximation, we calculate light-, strange-, and charm-quark condensates, the Polyakov loop, and bulk thermodynamic observables, including pressure, trace anomaly, and entropy density, over broad ranges of temperature and baryon chemical potential. We also determine the chiral and deconfinement pseudocritical boundaries and represent them as phase boundaries and three-dimensional order-parameter surfaces. After tuning the Polyakov sector to lattice-QCD pressure and trace-anomaly data, the resulting entropy density agrees with HotQCD 2+1(+1)-flavor results throughout the crossover region. At zero baryon density, the non-strange condensate melts at Tc = 210 MeV in SU(3) and 218 MeV in SU(4), with curvature parameters kappa=0.018 and 0.017. Because mc>>Tc, charm remains thermally suppressed across the crossover. After normalization to the corresponding Stefan-Boltzmann limits, the SU(3) and SU(4) EoS nearly coincide below T~2Tc. Charm mainly contributes through a higher ideal-gas limit, a gradual high-temperature tail, and the flavor ordering u,d->s->c. For all baryon chemical potentials accessible within mean-field theory, the hadron-parton transition remains a smooth crossover.

</details>


### [93] [Compactification and non-zero density in (2+1)-dimensional four-fermion model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29823)
*K. G. Klimenko,A. N. Kozhakin,R. N. Zhokhov*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates how compactification and fermion density affect chiral symmetry breaking in a (2+1)-dimensional Nambu–Jona-Lasinio (NJL) model with one compact spatial dimension and periodic boundary conditions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the interplay between dimensional reduction (via compactification), finite density, and chiral symmetry breaking in low-dimensional strongly-coupled fermionic systems.

Method: Analysis of the phase diagram of a (2+1)-dimensional NJL-type model with one spatial dimension compactified on a circle, under periodic boundary conditions for fermions; thermodynamic and symmetry-breaking properties are studied as functions of compactification radius and fermion chemical potential/density.

Result: Compactification modifies the critical behavior and phase structure—e.g., shifts critical temperature/density for chiral restoration—and introduces nontrivial dependence on the compactification radius; periodic boundary conditions support persistent chiral condensation at finite density depending on radius scaling.

Conclusion: Chiral symmetry breaking is sensitive to both geometric compactification and fermion density; the phase diagram exhibits rich structure due to dimensional crossover and boundary-condition-dependent fermionic mode spectra.

Abstract: The phase diagram of (2+1)-dimensional NJL type model has been studied with one compact dimension. The effect of compactification and fermion density on the chiral symmetry breaking is considered for periodic boundary conditions.

</details>


### [94] [Chiral crossover responses in a magnetic field: Finite-$μ_5$ matching in the NJL model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30535)
*Jingyi Chao*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper determines the coefficient $c_r$ of a chiral chemical potential-dependent interaction term in the NJL model by matching scalar-susceptibility peak-temperature ratios to unitarized chiral perturbation theory at zero magnetic field, and demonstrates that fixing $c_r$ significantly reduces regularization-induced ambiguity in pseudocritical temperature responses under finite $\mu_5$ and $eB > 0$.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the undetermined coefficient $c_r$ in the chiral chemical potential-dependent correction to the thermodynamic potential, and to assess how vacuum regularization choices (SC vs. PV) combined with magnetic-field treatment schemes (D vs. MFDS) affect pseudocritical temperature responses in the presence of both $\mu_5$ and $eB$.

Method: The authors employ the Nambu–Jona-Lasinio (NJL) model with two vacuum regularization schemes—soft-cutoff (SC) and Pauli-Villars (PV)—each paired with either direct magnetic-field treatment (D) or magnetic finite-difference scheme (MFDS). They fix $c_r$ by matching the $\mu_5 = 0$ scalar-susceptibility peak-temperature ratio to unitarized chiral perturbation theory predictions, then evaluate the resulting reduction in spread of pseudocritical temperature shifts across the four treatments at nonzero $eB$ and $\mu_5$.

Result: Matching to chiral perturbation theory yields $c_{\\mathrm{SC}} = -0.07112$ and $c_{\\mathrm{PV}} = 0.22479$. Fixing these coefficients reduces the four-treatment spread of normalized $\mu_5$-induced mass-inflection temperature changes by factors of 7.5–32.6 depending on $eB$ and $\mu_5$, and reduces the spread in scalar-susceptibility peak-temperature ratio by up to a factor of 74 at $eB = 0.1\\,\\text{GeV}^2$, $\mu_5 = 0.15\\,\\text{GeV}$.

Conclusion: The coefficient $c_r$ is physically constrained by chiral dynamics at $eB = 0$, and its calibration robustly suppresses regularization dependence in finite-$\\mu_5$ critical behavior even at nonzero magnetic fields—without requiring additional $eB$-dependent tuning.

Abstract: We investigate how independent information at finite chiral chemical potential $\mu_5$ constrains pseudocritical-temperature responses in the Nambu-Jona-Lasinio model under a magnetic field. Vacuum calibration alone leaves the coefficient $c_r$ of the additional interaction $\delta\Omega_r=c_r\mu_5^2(M-m_0)^2$ undetermined. We compare soft-cutoff (SC) and Pauli-Villars (PV) vacuum regularizations, each combined with the direct treatment (D) or the magnetic finite-difference scheme (MFDS). The reference SC and PV treatments exhibit opposite finite-$\mu_5$ responses already at $eB=0$. Matching the $eB=0$ scalar-susceptibility peak-temperature ratio predicted by unitarized chiral perturbation theory gives $c_{\mathrm{SC}}=-0.07112$ and $c_{\mathrm{PV}}=0.22479$. Holding these coefficients fixed at $eB=0.1,0.2,0.4$ GeV$^2$ reduces the four-treatment spread of the normalized finite-$\mu_5$-induced mass-inflection temperature change by factors of approximately $7.5$-$15.3$ at $\mu_5=0.15$ GeV and $18.1$-$32.6$ at $\mu_5=0.30$ GeV. For the distinct scalar-susceptibility peak-temperature ratio at $\mu_5=0.15$ GeV, the spread decreases by factors of approximately $74$, $27$, and $14$ at the same magnetic fields, respectively. The reduction in regularization-dependent finite-$\mu_5$ responses thus persists at the sampled nonzero magnetic fields without further matching.

</details>


### [95] [New analytic constraints for the soft anomalous dimension bootstrap at three loops](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31168)
*Einan Gardi,Luke Lippstreu,Andrew J. McLeod,Laura Walsh,Zehao Zhu,Enrico Zunino*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives new constraints on the soft anomalous dimension at three loops using sequential discontinuities of Feynman integrals, enabling strengthened bootstrap determination of the massless and first-ever bootstrap construction of the one-mass quadrupole function.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve the bootstrap determination of the soft anomalous dimension—especially for amplitudes involving massive partons—by exploiting analytic properties of sequential discontinuities in non-overlapping two-particle channels.

Method: Re-examines the relation between unitarity cuts and sequential discontinuities of Feynman integrals; applies these to constrain the quadrupole function (four-parton soft correlator); verifies constraints analytically for massless and one-mass cases; integrates them into a bootstrap framework alongside symmetry, collinear, Regge, and branch-point constraints.

Result: New vanishing constraints on sequential discontinuities in non-overlapping two-particle channels for all quadrupole webs at three loops; substantially improved massless bootstrap; first complete bootstrap determination of the one-mass quadrupole function; each function fixed up to one coefficient, matched to diagrammatic results.

Conclusion: Sequential discontinuity constraints are powerful, complementary analytic tools that significantly advance the bootstrap program for the soft anomalous dimension, paving the way for bootstrapping multi-mass three-loop configurations.

Abstract: The soft anomalous dimension governs the universal long-distance singularities of gauge theory amplitudes. It exhibits a highly constrained analytic structure and has been shown to be amenable to bootstrap methods. In this paper we revisit the relation between unitarity cuts and sequential discontinuities of Feynman integrals and use it to derive new constraints on the soft anomalous dimension. At three loops, we show that certain sequential discontinuities in non-overlapping two-particle channels vanish for every web contributing to the quadrupole function that correlates four coloured partons. These constraints hold for arbitrary parton masses, and we explicitly verify them for the known massless and one-mass cases. We then demonstrate their constraining power by substantially strengthening the massless bootstrap and carrying out the first bootstrap construction of the one-mass quadrupole function. The new constraints are largely complementary to those arising from the absence of unphysical branch points, symmetries, and collinear and Regge limits. With their help, we determine each quadrupole function within the assumed function space up to a single coefficient, which we fix by matching to existing diagrammatic results. These results open the way to bootstrapping the three-loop soft anomalous dimension for amplitudes with several massive partons.

</details>


### [96] [Two-loop correction to the complex-bion amplitude in a quasi-exactly solvable sextic oscillator](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29701)
*Adrian M. Escobar-Ruiz*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes a quasi-exactly solvable sextic potential using diagrammatic methods; it confirms exact ground-state energy at positive coupling via vanishing perturbative corrections, and identifies complex neutral bions—not instantons—as the dominant nonperturbative contribution to energy at negative coupling, with two-loop diagrammatic calculation matching exact-WKB.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the nonperturbative structure of a quasi-exactly solvable sextic potential, particularly how factorization constraints, instanton calculus, and complex saddles (e.g., neutral bions) govern ground-state energy across coupling sign changes.

Method: Two diagrammatic approaches: (i) Feynman diagram expansion to third order to verify perturbative cancellation at positive coupling; (ii) constrained-family two-loop calculation of local median amplitude for the complex neutral bion contribution after analytic continuation to negative coupling; comparison with exact-WKB.

Result: At positive coupling: ground-state energy $E_0$ is exactly fixed by factorization; all perturbative coefficients vanish—confirmed diagrammatically to third order. At negative coupling: instanton one-loop contribution vanishes; leading nonperturbative correction arises from a complex neutral bion; two-loop diagrammatic sum yields $c_{\rm 2L}^{\rm diag} = -0.750000(5)$, matching exact-WKB prediction $-3/4$.

Conclusion: Factorization enforces exact solvability and perturbative triviality at positive coupling; nonperturbative physics at negative coupling is governed not by real instantons but by complex neutral bions, whose contribution is quantitatively captured by constrained diagrammatics and consistent with exact-WKB.

Abstract: In this study, a quasi-exactly solvable sextic potential is analyzed by two diagrammatic calculations. At positive coupling, factorization fixes the ground-state energy $E_0$ exactly and forces every perturbative coefficient to vanish. Using only Feynman diagrams, we verify this cancellation explicitly through third order. After continuation to negative coupling, the formal zero mode becomes nonnormalizable. A real instanton remains, but its one-instanton contribution to \(E_0\) vanishes. In particular, the leading shift in energy arises from a complex neutral bion. Taking an exact constrained family, we calculate its local median amplitude through two loops. The direct diagrammatic sum gives $c_{\rm 2L}^{\rm diag}=-0.750000(5)$, in excellent agreement with the exact-WKB value $-3/4$.

</details>


### [97] [Heavy Quark-antiquark Systems in a Galilean Covariant MIT Bag Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28160)
*G. X. A. Petronilo,M. V. T. Machado,R.R. Luz,A.E. Santana*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper extends the MIT bag model to be Galilean covariant using a 5D formalism, derives a Dirac-like equation and boundary conditions for quark dynamics in a confining bag, obtains analytical spinor solutions and eigenfrequencies, and validates the model against charmonium/bottomonium spectra—showing reasonable agreement for heavy mesons but failure for light-quark systems due to non-relativistic limitations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile the MIT bag model with Galilean covariance for heavy quark-antiquark systems while retaining analytical tractability in a non-relativistic framework.

Method: Constructs a Galilean-covariant Dirac-like equation in 5-dimensional spacetime for quarks confined within a bag; derives consistent boundary conditions; solves analytically for spinor wavefunctions and eigenfrequencies.

Result: Analytical expressions for spinor wavefunctions and eigenfrequencies; numerical mass and hyperfine splitting predictions matching experimental data for charmonium and bottomonium, but not for light mesons.

Conclusion: The Galilean-covariant MIT bag model is viable for heavy-quark mesons under non-relativistic approximation, but breaks down for light-quark systems—highlighting intrinsic limits of non-relativistic treatments in QCD-inspired models.

Abstract: This work presents a Galilean covariant extension of the MIT bag model to describe heavy quark-antiquark systems within a non-relativistic framework. Using a 5-dimensional formulation of Galilean covariance, a Dirac-like equation governing quark dynamics inside a confining bag is constructed, and the corresponding boundary conditions are derived. Analytical solutions for the spinor wavefunctions and eigenfrequencies are obtained. Numerical results show some agreement with experimental masses and hyperfine splittings for heavy mesons, particularly charmonium and bottomonium states. However, the approach is less reliable for light-quark systems, indicating the limitations of the non-relativistic approximation.

</details>


### [98] [Unveiling the low- and high-energy frontier: a simultaneous SMEFT-PDF fit with parity violation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28216)
*Ella Cole,Tom Tong,Maria Ubiali*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper performs a global SMEFT analysis combining high- and low-energy data—including parity-violation, beta decays, kaon/pion decays, Drell-Yan (neutral & charged current), Higgs, EWPOs, and diboson data—while simultaneously fitting SMEFT Wilson coefficients and proton PDFs using SIMUnet; it quantifies SMEFT–PDF interplay and shows robust bounds with only moderate PDF uncertainty growth.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve the rigor and consistency of SMEFT global fits by incorporating low-energy parity-violating observables via LEFT matching, extending high-energy datasets (notably adding charged-current Drell-Yan), and jointly fitting Wilson coefficients and PDFs to account for hadronic uncertainties.

Method: Systematic SMEFT-to-LEFT matching; global likelihood fit of 15 independent Wilson coefficients using diverse experimental inputs; simultaneous inference of proton PDFs and SMEFT parameters within the public SIMUnet framework; quantification of SMEFT–PDF correlations and their impact on parameter uncertainties.

Result: Tightened and robust constraints on 15 SMEFT Wilson coefficients; demonstration that PDFs remain stable under SMEFT inclusion, with only moderate uncertainty inflation; first consistent incorporation of charged-current Drell-Yan into a global SMEFT+PDF fit; quantified reduction of theoretical bias from factorized PDF treatment.

Conclusion: Joint SMEFT–PDF fitting is essential for reliable EFT interpretations of collider and precision data; the extended dataset and consistent LEFT matching significantly enhance sensitivity to BSM physics, particularly in flavor- and electroweak-charged sectors, while maintaining control over hadronic systematics.

Abstract: We present a comprehensive global analysis of Standard Model Effective Field Theory (SMEFT) operators utilising a diverse set of high- and low-energy experimental probes. By systematically matching the SMEFT to the Low-Energy Effective Field Theory (LEFT), we incorporate parity-violating observables into a global fit of 15 independent Wilson coefficients. This low-energy dataset, comprising parity-violation observables, neutron and nuclear $\beta$-decays, as well as leptonic and semileptonic kaon and pion decays, is combined with high-energy measurements including high-invariant-mass Drell-Yan data in both neutral- and charged-current channels, with the latter included for the first time, Higgs boson measurements, Electroweak Precision Observables (EWPOs) from LEP, and diboson production at LEP and the LHC. Crucially, this framework features a simultaneous fit of both the proton Parton Distribution Functions (PDFs) and the SMEFT Wilson coefficients using the public {\tt SIMUnet} framework. We quantify the direct interplay between SMEFT operators and hadron structure, demonstrating the robustness of the SMEFT bounds and the relative stability of the resulting PDFs with respect to the inclusion of SMEFT effects, with only a moderate increase in their uncertainties.

</details>


### [99] [WIMPs as FIMPs: the Inert Doublet Model and low-temperature reheating](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28217)
*Geneviève Bélanger,Nicolás Bernal,Andreas Goudelis,Alexander Pukhov,Thomas Reggio*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper shows that WIMPs in the Inert Doublet Model can undergo freeze-in instead of freeze-out if the Universe's reheating temperature is sufficiently low—challenging the conventional thermal freeze-out paradigm and opening previously excluded parameter space.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The conventional assumption that WIMPs acquire relic abundance via thermal freeze-out depends on the early Universe’s thermal history; this work questions that assumption by exploring non-standard cosmologies with low reheating temperatures.

Method: Analytical and numerical analysis of dark matter production in the Inert Doublet Model under varying reheating scenarios—both instantaneous and finite-duration—accounting for chemical decoupling, freeze-out timing (during radiation domination or reheating), and freeze-in with possible annihilation backreactions.

Result: Identifies three distinct production regimes: (i) standard freeze-out during radiation domination, (ii) freeze-out during reheating, and (iii) freeze-in—some with non-negligible backreaction—and shows that low reheating temperatures allow viable WIMP dark matter in otherwise overclosed regions of IDM parameter space.

Conclusion: WIMPs are not inherently tied to freeze-out; their production mechanism (freeze-out vs. freeze-in) depends on cosmological history—thus, WIMPs can effectively behave as FIMPs, blurring the traditional classification.

Abstract: Weakly Interacting Massive Particles (WIMPs) are conventionally assumed to acquire their dark matter relic abundance through thermal freeze-out. However, this association relies on assumptions concerning the thermal history of the early Universe. We revisit the Inert Doublet Model (IDM), a representative WIMP framework in which dark matter interacts with the Standard Model through electroweak gauge interactions, even when its Higgs-portal coupling vanishes. We demonstrate that sufficiently low reheating temperatures can prevent chemical equilibrium, allowing a genuine WIMP to acquire its relic abundance through freeze-in rather than freeze-out. Considering both instantaneous and finite-duration reheating, we identify different production histories, including freeze-out during radiation domination, freeze-out during reheating, and freeze-in with potentially significant annihilation backreactions. These scenarios open regions of the IDM parameter space that would otherwise be excluded by dark matter overabundance. Our results demonstrate that WIMPs can behave as FIMPs, depending on the thermal history of the Universe.

</details>


### [100] [TeV-Scale Leptogenesis in the Linear Seesaw Framework](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28224)
*A. E. Cárcamo Hernández,J. Echeverria-Puentes,Stephen F. King,José W. F. Valle*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores resonant leptogenesis within the linear seesaw mechanism using TeV-scale quasi-Dirac heavy neutrinos, identifying viable parameter regions and benchmark scenarios for baryon asymmetry generation in flavoured and strong-washout regimes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the smallness of neutrino masses and simultaneously generate the observed baryon asymmetry via low-energy resonant leptogenesis, leveraging the natural resonance enhancement in the linear seesaw framework.

Method: Analytical and numerical analysis of baryon asymmetry generation in the purely flavoured and strong-washout regimes; considers two scalar sectors — SM Higgs doublet vs. leptophilic scalar doublet — for heavy-neutrino decays; identifies phenomenologically allowed parameter space and benchmark points.

Result: Viable leptogenesis solutions are found both near and away from the resonant maximum; distinct benchmark scenarios emerge depending on the dominant scalar mediator (SM Higgs or leptophilic doublet); complementary experimental probes (e.g., collider signatures, lepton-flavour violation) are identified.

Conclusion: The TeV-scale linear seesaw provides a testable, minimal framework for resonant leptogenesis, with concrete predictions accessible to current and near-future experiments.

Abstract: The linear seesaw mechanism relates the smallness of neutrino masses to the mass splitting within a quasi-Dirac pair of heavy neutrinos, thereby providing a natural framework for realising resonant leptogenesis at low energies. We investigate this possibility within its simplest effective realisation, in which neutrino masses are mediated by a quasi-Dirac pair at the TeV scale. We estimate the generation of the baryon asymmetry in the purely flavoured and strong-washout regimes, identifying phenomenologically viable parameter regions and benchmark scenarios in which the heavy-neutrino decays are dominated either by the SM Higgs doublet or by a second, leptophilic scalar doublet responsible for neutrino mass generation. We find viable leptogenesis solutions both near and away from the resonant maximum and discuss complementary phenomenological probes of the framework.

</details>


### [101] [Higgs-portal dark matter beyond kinetic equilibrium](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28230)
*Giorgio Arcadi,Michele Benaco,Mattia Di Mauro,Sami Nurmi,Kimmo Tuominen*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper re-examines Higgs-portal dark matter (scalar, vector, fermion) in the resonant regime without assuming kinetic equilibrium, solving the full momentum-dependent Boltzmann equation; it finds the standard momentum-averaged approach underestimates relic abundance—especially for fermionic pseudoscalar couplings (up to factor-10 error in coupling)—and shows momentum-dependent treatment systematically tightens direct detection constraints.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess the validity and accuracy of the standard momentum-averaged Boltzmann treatment for Higgs-portal DM freeze-out in the resonant regime, particularly when kinetic equilibrium cannot be assumed.

Method: Solves the full momentum-dependent Boltzmann equation numerically for scalar, vector, and fermionic Higgs-portal DM models in the resonant region; compares relic abundances against the conventional momentum-averaged approximation; incorporates up-to-date experimental constraints from direct detection (e.g., XENONnT), indirect detection (e.g., Fermi-LAT), and collider searches (e.g., LHC Higgs measurements).

Result: The standard momentum-averaged treatment underestimates relic density below resonance: negligibly for fermions with pure scalar coupling, up to factor ~2 for scalar/vector DM, and up to factor ~10 for fermionic DM with pseudoscalar coupling. Momentum-dependent results systematically strengthen direct detection limits due to more accurate velocity/momentum spectra.

Conclusion: Momentum-dependent treatment is essential for robust relic density prediction in resonant Higgs-portal DM, especially for fermionic pseudoscalar scenarios; using standard approximations risks misinterpreting viable parameter space and weakening exclusion limits.

Abstract: We revisit the scalar, vector, and fermion Higgs-portal dark matter models in the resonant regime without assuming kinetic equilibrium during the freeze-out. We compute the relic abundance by solving the full momentum-dependent Boltzmann equation and compare the results with the standard momentum-averaged treatment. We find that the standard treatment significantly underestimates the relic abundance below the resonance, apart from the fermionic case with a purely scalar portal coupling where the difference is negligible. The deviation from the standard treatment is largest for fermionic models with pseudoscalar interactions, corresponding to factor of 10 in terms of the coupling that reproduces the correct relic abundance. For the scalar and vector cases the corresponding difference is up to a factor of 2. We confront the resulting parameter space with the latest constraints from direct and indirect detection, as well as collider experiments. We find the full momentum-dependent results systematically strengthen the direct detection constraints.

</details>


### [102] [The Nambu--Jona-Lasinio model at isospin density in closed form: what a regularization can change](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28323)
*T. G. Khunjua*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives an exact closed-form expression for the zero-temperature thermodynamic potential of the two-flavour NJL model at isospin chemical potential, using Hermite reduction to express the divergent momentum integral in terms of boundary terms and incomplete elliptic integrals; it clarifies regularization dependence—showing that momentum-only cutoffs (sharp/smooth) differ only by vacuum-determined constants, whereas level-wise cutoffs violate Silver Blaze and yield physically distinct models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve ambiguities in regularization of the divergent mean-field thermodynamic potential in the isospin-asymmetric NJL model, particularly concerning ultraviolet sensitivity, Silver Blaze compliance, and physical equivalence of different cutoff schemes.

Method: Exact analytical evaluation of the momentum-integral thermodynamic potential via Hermite reduction; classification of divergences (power vs. logarithmic) using elliptic-curve geometry; comparison of momentum-global vs. level-wise cutoff prescriptions; analysis of vacuum subtraction and medium separation scheme.

Result: The integral reduces exactly to an algebraic boundary term plus three incomplete elliptic integrals (first, second, third kind); power divergences are fully contained in the boundary term; the logarithmic divergence arises from the puncture at infinity on the elliptic curve; sharp and smooth global momentum cutoffs yield potentials differing only by vacuum-fixed constants; level-wise cutoff violates Silver Blaze and generates unphysical isospin density below condensation onset.

Conclusion: The closed-form solution makes regularization choices explicit and reveals that finite-cutoff models are physically inequivalent—not mere approximations of a unique continuum limit—especially when cutoffs act asymmetrically on quasiparticle levels; only global momentum cutoffs preserve fundamental properties like Silver Blaze and continuity with the vacuum.

Abstract: At zero temperature, the mean-field thermodynamic potential of the two-flavour Nambu-Jona-Lasinio model at isospin chemical potential reduces to a divergent momentum integral of two quasiparticle levels. In practice this integral is cut off at momenta not far above the chemical potentials being probed. We evaluate it exactly, at an arbitrary cutoff, using the Hermite reduction. The result is an algebraic boundary term and three incomplete elliptic integrals, one of each kind. Their modulus, parameter and amplitude at the end of the path are fixed by the triangle formed by the two levels at zero momentum. The power divergences reside in the boundary term, while the remaining divergence is logarithmic: the momentum axis is an open path on an elliptic curve, and the ultraviolet is the puncture at which it ends. The closed form makes the role of regularization explicit. As the cutoff is removed, sharp and smooth cutoffs acting on the momentum alone differ only by an additive constant and by two terms that the vacuum inputs fix. The medium separation scheme reproduces the resulting potential up to an additive constant. By contrast, a cutoff applied separately to the two levels generates an isospin density below the onset of condensation and violates the Silver Blaze property. The closed form gives the state that continues the vacuum along the isospin axis without numerical momentum integration. With the same vacuum fit, a cutoff kept finite gives a visibly different state. The two differ by explicit terms that vanish only as the cutoff is removed: they are different models, not two regularizations of one.

</details>


### [103] [Probing tensor-polarized gluon TMDs through \texorpdfstring{$J/ψ$}{J/psi} electroproduction off the deuteron](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28626)
*Xiupeng Xie,Zhun Lu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies tensor-polarized gluon TMDs in semi-inclusive $J/\psi$ electroproduction off the deuteron, deriving cross-section contributions for $LL$, $LT$, and $TT$ tensor polarizations, defining ten normalized observables, and analyzing their kinematic dependences using a spin-1 spectator model; it identifies distinctive features such as common zeros in linearly polarized gluon moments at $Q^2 = M_\psi^2$ and probes T-odd gluon TMDs via beam-helicity-dependent sine moments.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To access poorly constrained tensor-polarized gluon transverse-momentum-dependent distributions (TMDs) — particularly $f_{1LL}^g$, $f_{1LT}^g$, $h_{1LT}^g$, $f_{1TT}^g$, $h_{1TT}^{\perp g}$, $g_{1LT}^g$, and $g_{1TT}^g$ — through measurable observables in semi-inclusive $J/\psi$ electroproduction on a spin-1 target (deuteron), leveraging its tensor polarization sensitivity beyond spin-1/2 nucleons.

Method: At leading order in the color-octet NRQCD framework, the authors compute the differential cross section for semi-inclusive $J/\psi$ production, decomposing it into $LL$, $LT$, and $TT$ tensor-polarization channels; they define ten normalized observables for unpolarized and longitudinally polarized electron beams; gluon TMD inputs are taken from a spin-1 spectator model; kinematic dependences ($q_T$, $y$, $Q^2$, collision energy) are studied; moments of TMDs (including azimuthal sine modulations) are analyzed to isolate specific combinations and test zeros and T-odd effects.

Result: Ten experimentally accessible observables are identified; ratios of gluon TMDs directly govern key observables involving $f_{1LL}^g$, $f_{1LT}^g+h_{1LT}^g$, and $f_{1TT}^g-h_{1TT}^{\perp g}$; linearly polarized gluon moments vanish simultaneously at $Q^2 = M_\psi^2$ due to adopted long-distance matrix elements; beam-helicity-dependent sine moments provide clean probes of the T-odd gluon TMDs $g_{1LT}^g$ and $g_{1TT}^g$; fixed-scale model estimates neglecting transverse recoil and quarkonium formation effects are provided.

Conclusion: Semi-inclusive $J/\psi$ electroproduction on the deuteron offers a unique channel to disentangle multiple tensor-polarized gluon TMDs, especially the T-even combinations and the chiral-odd, T-odd $g_{1LT}^g$ and $g_{1TT}^g$. The predicted kinematic signatures — notably the universal zero and helicity-dependent azimuthal modulations — provide concrete benchmarks for future EIC measurements.

Abstract: We investigate tensor-polarized gluon transverse-momentum-dependent distributions (TMDs) in semi-inclusive $J/\psi$ electroproduction off the deuteron. At leading order in the color-octet channels of nonrelativistic QCD, we derive the cross-section contributions associated with longitudinal-longitudinal ($LL$), longitudinal-transverse ($LT$), and transverse-transverse ($TT$) tensor polarization and define ten normalized observables for unpolarized and longitudinally polarized electron beams. Using gluon TMDs obtained in a spin-1 spectator model, we study their $q_T$, $y$, $Q^2$, and collision-energy dependences. The observables involving $f_{1LL}^g$, $f_{1LT}^g+h_{1LT}^g$, and $f_{1TT}^g-h_{1TT}^{\perp g}$ depend directly on ratios of gluon TMDs. The moments involving linearly polarized gluons exhibit a common zero at $Q^2=M_\psi^2$ for the adopted long-distance matrix elements, whereas the beam-helicity-dependent sine moments probe the T-odd distributions $g_{1LT}^g$ and $g_{1TT}^g$. We present fixed-scale model estimates that neglect transverse recoil from soft radiation and quarkonium formation.

</details>


### [104] [Strong First-Order Phase Transition in a Pseudo-Nambu-Goldstone Boson Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28630)
*Yuichiro Nakai,Masanori Tanaka,Yaoduo Wang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Proposes a calculable two-scalar-field model with collective symmetry breaking to realize strong first-order cosmological phase transitions along a pNGB direction, enabling detectable gravitational-wave signals without fine-tuning.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a theoretically robust, calculable model for strong first-order phase transitions in the early Universe that avoids fine-tuning and maintains perturbative control, while producing observable gravitational-wave signatures.

Method: Constructs a two-scalar-field model with collective symmetry breaking to protect the pseudo-Nambu-Goldstone boson potential from quadratically divergent radiative corrections; computes finite-temperature effective potential, bubble nucleation rate, transition strength (α, β/H*), and resulting gravitational-wave spectrum.

Result: Identifies viable parameter regions where strong first-order phase transitions complete successfully; predicts GW spectra within sensitivity ranges of upcoming experiments (e.g., LISA, BBO, DECIGO).

Conclusion: The pNGB-based model with collective symmetry breaking provides a natural, perturbatively controlled framework for strong first-order phase transitions yielding potentially observable stochastic gravitational-wave backgrounds.

Abstract: We propose a novel, calculable model for realizing strong first-order phase transitions in the early Universe, in which the phase transition occurs along a pseudo-Nambu-Goldstone boson (pNGB) direction. The model involves two scalar fields whose interactions exhibit collective symmetry breaking, protecting the pNGB potential from quadratically divergent radiative corrections induced by these interactions. This structure allows strong first-order phase transitions to be realized without severe parameter tuning while maintaining perturbative control. We investigate the finite-temperature effective potential and bubble nucleation dynamics, identifying parameter regions where strong first-order phase transitions can successfully complete. The strength and characteristic time scale of the transitions are evaluated, and the resulting gravitational-wave (GW) spectra are computed. The predicted GW signals can fall within the sensitivity ranges of planned GW experiments.

</details>


### [105] [Emerging Topics for Hard Probes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28688)
*Carlota Andres*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper is a plenary talk summary presenting selected emerging topics in jet and heavy-flavor physics within relativistic heavy-ion collisions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To highlight recent developments and open questions in jet quenching and heavy-flavor energy loss as probes of the quark-gluon plasma.

Method: Review and synthesis of current experimental and theoretical progress, based on a plenary presentation at Hard Probes 2026.

Result: Identification and discussion of key emerging topics, including novel observables, precision tomography techniques, and discrepancies between data and state-of-the-art models.

Conclusion: Jet and heavy-flavor observables remain indispensable and increasingly precise tools for characterizing QGP properties; future progress hinges on integrated theory–experiment efforts and next-generation measurements.

Abstract: These proceedings present a personal selection of emerging topics in jet and heavy-flavor physics in relativistic heavy-ion collisions, based on a plenary talk delivered at Hard Probes 2026 in Nashville (USA).

</details>


### [106] [Study of the $N^*(1535)$ and $N^*(1650)$ resonances from $πN$ $S_{11}$ scattering data and lattice QCD spectra](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28691)
*Zhi-Xuan Li,Derek B. Leinweber,Anthony W. Thomas,Jia-Jun Wu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper uses a symmetry-constrained nonperturbative Hamiltonian framework (NPHF) with coupled-channel dynamics to simultaneously describe the N*(1535) and N*(1650) S₁₁ resonances, combining infinite-volume πN scattering data and finite-volume lattice QCD spectra; it finds the spin-1/2 bare state is heavier than the spin-3/2 one—contrary to naive quark-model expectations—and confirms both bare states and meson-baryon couplings are essential.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the structure and composition of the two lowest-lying odd-parity nucleon resonances N*(1535) and N*(1650), particularly testing conventional quark-model assignments against coupled-channel and lattice QCD constraints.

Method: Nonperturbative Hamiltonian formalism (NPHF) with SU(3)-symmetry–constrained bare-state–channel vertices and Weinberg-Tomozawa channel-channel interactions; joint fit to infinite-volume πN S₁₁ scattering data and finite-volume lattice QCD energy spectra; Hamiltonian eigenvector analysis for model-basis composition; contamination function to match operator-mixing patterns with lattice correlation-matrix eigenvectors.

Result: Both bare states (spin-1/2 and spin-3/2 three-quark) and coupled-channel dynamics are essential; the spin-1/2 bare state is found heavier than the spin-3/2 one—opposite to standard quark-model predictions; contamination analysis supports operator assignments and aligns with relativistic wavefunction analyses.

Conclusion: The symmetry-constrained NPHF provides a unified, quantitatively consistent description of the S₁₁ resonances, reconciling scattering and lattice data, and challenges simplistic quark-model interpretations by highlighting the critical role of dynamical chiral couplings and bare-state mass inversion.

Abstract: We investigate two of the lowest-lying odd-parity nucleon resonances, $N^*(1535)$ and $N^*(1650)$, within a nonperturbative Hamiltonian framework (NPHF). The coupled-channel model contains two three-quark bare states with total quark spins $1/2$ and $3/2$, together with four meson-baryon channels: $\pi N$, $\eta N$, $K\Lambda$, and $K\Sigma$. Constraints from SU(3) flavor symmetry and the bare-state spin wave functions are imposed on the bare-state-channel vertices and the channel-channel Weinberg-Tomozawa interactions. This substantially reduces the number of free parameters. We combine fits to infinite-volume $\pi N$ $S_{11}$ scattering data with finite-volume lattice QCD spectra. We use Hamiltonian eigenvectors to analyze the model-basis compositions. A contamination function then compares the resulting operator-mixing pattern with lattice correlation-matrix eigenvectors. Within the scenarios considered, both bare states and coupled-channel interactions play important roles in describing the two resonances. From our combined infinite and finite volume analysis, in contrast to conventional quark-model expectations, the spin-$1/2$ bare state is heavier than the spin-$3/2$ bare state. The contamination analysis is consistent with the associated operator assignment and with a recent analysis of the relativistic wavefunctions associated with these states. The symmetry-constrained NPHF provides a unified description of the two $S_{11}$ resonances. The combined analysis also constrains their model interpretation.

</details>


### [107] [Evolution and Prospects of Higgs Boson Searches Beyond the Standard Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28742)
*Andre Sopczak*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This review traces the historical evolution of searches for Higgs bosons beyond the Standard Model (BSM) across LEP, Tevatron, and LHC experiments, emphasizing how collider-specific experimental conditions shape search strategies and how subtle anomalies (e.g., small excesses, non-standard decays, suppressed couplings) inform theoretical models of extended Higgs sectors. It serves as both a pedagogical resource and a roadmap for future BSM Higgs physics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The 2012 Higgs discovery confirmed the Standard Model’s electroweak symmetry breaking mechanism but left open fundamental questions—such as naturalness, dark matter, and hierarchy—that motivate extended Higgs sectors in BSM theories. Thus, searching for additional Higgs bosons remains a central probe of new physics.

Method: A comparative historical and conceptual review: synthesizes experimental approaches (detector capabilities, luminosity, center-of-mass energy, background handling) and theoretical frameworks (e.g., Two-Higgs-Doublet Models, supersymmetry, composite Higgs) across LEP, Tevatron, and LHC eras; highlights reinterpretation of anomalies and strategy adaptation to collider-specific constraints.

Result: No confirmed observation of BSM Higgs bosons to date; however, exclusion limits have been progressively tightened, and several intriguing (but statistically insignificant) excesses have motivated refined analyses and new search channels—especially in challenging final states (e.g., fully hadronic, low-mass, or high-multiplicity decays).

Conclusion: BSM Higgs searches are not merely incremental exclusions but dynamic, theory–experiment feedback loops: experimental subtleties drive theoretical reinterpretation, and theoretical developments guide detector optimization and analysis innovation. The minimal Higgs sector remains viable, but the search continues with increasing sophistication—and growing opportunity—for discovery.

Abstract: The search for Higgs bosons Beyond the Standard Model (BSM) began intensively at the Large Electron-Positron (LEP) collider (1989-2000), continued at the Tevatron (2001-2011), and entered a new era with the start of the Large Hadron Collider (LHC) in 2010. While the discovery of a Higgs boson in 2012 established the Standard Model mechanism of electroweak symmetry breaking, it also opened a wide landscape of open questions that motivate ongoing BSM Higgs searches. This review traces the evolution of these searches across experiments, highlighting both experimental techniques and underlying theoretical ideas in an accessible manner. Emphasis is placed on how different collider environments shaped search strategies and how seemingly subtle experimental effects, such as small excesses, non-standard decay topologies, or suppressed couplings, can provide insight into the possible structure of extended Higgs sectors. By connecting historical developments with current analyses and future prospects, this article aims not only to summarise results but also to provide students and newcomers with intuitive insight into how Higgs searches are designed, interpreted, and continuously refined. It illustrates how experimental challenges translate into physics opportunities, thereby inviting the next generation of researchers to contribute to one of the central questions in particle physics: whether the Higgs sector is minimal or part of a more complex structure.

</details>


### [108] [Neutral and Charged Pion Modes in Magnetized Quark Matter with Chiral Imbalance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28838)
*Shijie Zhai,Lang Yu,Jie Mei,Mei Huang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies neutral and charged pion correlations in a two-flavor NJL model at finite temperature, under external magnetic field and chiral chemical potential μ₅, using mean-field and RPA methods; it finds distinct Landau-level contributions in neutral vs. charged channels, and μ₅- and B-dependent mass shifts with implications for lattice QCD.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how external magnetic fields and chiral imbalance (via μ₅) affect pion properties—particularly masses—in hot QCD-like matter, motivated by anomalous transport phenomena and discrepancies/needs in lattice QCD simulations.

Method: Two-flavor Nambu–Jona-Lasinio (NJL) model analyzed within mean-field approximation and single-channel random phase approximation (RPA); pion masses extracted from positive-energy zeros of real parts of inverse propagators at zero spatial momentum; explicit treatment of Landau quantization and Dirac structure in neutral vs. charged channels.

Result: Neutral-pion mass shows weak μ₅ dependence at low T but its rapid decrease shifts to higher T with increasing μ₅; charged-pion mass monotonically decreases with μ₅; at T = 0 and fixed μ₅, increasing B lowers m_π⁰ but raises m_π±; neutral channel has no Landau cut due to Dirac trace cancellation, while charged channel exhibits both pair-production and thermal-scattering contributions.

Conclusion: The distinct magnetic and chiral-chemical-potential dependences of neutral and charged pion masses arise from fundamental differences in their quantum-number-coupled dynamics—especially Landau-level transitions—and provide a semi-quantitative explanation for observed trends in lattice QCD.

Abstract: We investigate neutral and charged pion correlations in a two-flavor Nambu--Jona-Lasinio (NJL) model at finite temperature in the presence of a uniform magnetic field and a prescribed chiral chemical potential $\mu_5$, using the mean-field approximation and the single-channel random phase approximation (RPA). The pion masses are defined by the selected positive-energy zeros of the real parts of the corresponding inverse propagators. We demonstrate that, at zero external spatial momentum, the complete Dirac trace in the neutral channel eliminates mixed-branch contributions, thereby precluding a finite-energy Landau cut. In contrast, the charged channel allows non-diagonal Landau-level transitions and exhibits both pair-production and thermal-scattering contributions. With increasing $\mu_5$, the neutral-pion mass exhibits only a weak variation at low temperature, while the rapid decrease of the mass shifts toward higher temperatures. The charged-pion mass decreases with increasing $\mu_5$ over the selected solution segments. This behavior may help account for the lattice QCD results. At zero temperature and fixed $\mu_5$, increasing the magnetic field lowers the neutral-pion mass but raises the charged-pion mass.

</details>


### [109] [A billion colour states: exact soft-gluon evolution beyond leading colour for many-jet QCD](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29072)
*Bakar Chargeishvili*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a scalable, full-colour method for computing soft-gluon evolution at one loop using an orthogonal, representation-based colour basis; it enables exact evolution for up to 15 partons (827M-dimensional colour space), reveals significant subleading-colour effects in multi-jet processes, and invalidates the leading-colour approximation beyond ~8 partons.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome the exponential growth of the colour space and computational intractability of full-colour QCD calculations for multi-parton processes, especially for soft-gluon evolution where colour interference is essential but traditionally neglected beyond leading colour.

Method: Constructs an orthogonal, representation-sequence-labelled colour basis for arbitrary quark/gluon configurations; derives closed-form norms and local action rules for colour operators; implements memory-efficient, on-the-fly basis vector generation and matrix-free soft anomalous dimension application; optimises for CPU/GPU parallelism.

Result: Exact one-loop soft-gluon evolution computed for up to 14 gluons on a single GPU and 15 partons (827,888,726 dimensions) on one compute node; quantitative validation against known few-parton results; leading-colour error quantified: median overestimation of 1.3× (8 partons), 1.8× (10), 6.6× (14); non-negligible singlet probabilities (~% level) among hard jet pairs, absent in leading colour.

Conclusion: Subleading-colour contributions are numerically large and physically relevant in many-jet final states; the $1/N_c^2$ suppression is insufficient to justify leading-colour approximations beyond modest parton multiplicities; the proposed basis and algorithm enable practical, exact full-colour evolution for phenomenologically relevant multi-jet topologies.

Abstract: We present a new method, and its implementation, for constructing orthogonal colour bases and computing colour factors for any process with quarks, antiquarks and gluons, and use it to compute one-loop soft-gluon evolution exactly in full colour, keeping all colour interference. Each basis vector is labelled, independently of $N_c$, by the sequence of colour representations that appear when the partons are coupled one at a time. We derive a closed formula for the norms of the basis vectors, and colour operators act only on the part of a label between the partons involved, through small tables. Basis vectors can therefore be generated and used one at a time, without listing the complete basis: norms and matrix elements of single basis vectors are obtained for $10^4$ gluons in seconds. The same locality lets the soft anomalous dimension act on colour states without being stored. This allows optimised implementations on CPUs and GPUs, with which we evolve colour states exactly for up to fourteen gluons on a single GPU and for fifteen gluons, a colour space of $827\,888\,726$ dimensions, on a single compute node. We validate the basis and the evolution against independent codes and published results for processes with few partons, the only ones for which such results are available. We then measure the accuracy of leading colour as a function of the number of partons. For exclusive jet rates with a veto on soft radiation outside the jets, at a ratio of about 14 between the hard and the veto scale, leading colour overestimates the full-colour result by a median factor of 1.3 for eight partons, 1.8 for ten and 6.6 for fourteen. Pairs of hard jets are in a colour singlet with probabilities of several per cent, which vanish at leading colour. The formal $1/N_c^2$ suppression of subleading colour therefore does not make it small for many-jet final states.

</details>


### [110] [Quantifying the Impact of Galactic Dark Matter Halo Models on LZ Results](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29197)
*Jialiang Shao,Andrea Addazi,Xinran Wang,Guanwen Fang,Yi-Fu Cai*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper examines how anisotropic dark matter velocity distributions—departing from the standard isotropic, Maxwellian halo model—affect the interpretation of LZ direct-detection results, finding modest (~±30%) impact above ~40 GeV but order-of-magnitude variation below ~15 GeV and significant implications for single-event inelastic interpretations near kinematic thresholds.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Standard dark matter direct-detection limits assume an isotropic, Maxwellian Standard Halo Model (SHM), but observational and simulation evidence indicates the Galactic dark matter halo is radially or scale-dependently anisotropic; this work assesses how such departures bias or alter limit interpretations, especially near kinematic thresholds where sensitivity depends critically on the high-velocity tail.

Method: The authors adopt four self-consistent anisotropic halo models (isotropic, Osipkov-Merritt, axisymmetric, triaxial) from Belli et al., apply a common Galactic escape-speed truncation, and recast LZ elastic-scattering limits via event-rate ratios relative to the isotropic SHM; for the 248 keV nuclear-recoil candidate, they use a background-free single-event likelihood to map allowed inelastic mass splittings under each model.

Result: For elastic scattering: above ~40 GeV, anisotropy induces ≤±30% shifts in exclusion limits; below ~15 GeV, model spread widens to ~1 order of magnitude due to high-velocity tail sensitivity. For the 248 keV event: anisotropic models extend kinematically allowed inelastic mass splittings into regions where solar-neutrino constraints on Higgsino interpretations weaken—but viability requires consistency with fixed Higgsino cross section, not just kinematics.

Conclusion: Halo-model dependence is modest at high WIMP masses but becomes critical near detection thresholds; robust interpretation of low-energy or single-event signals demands explicit treatment of velocity distribution anisotropy, particularly its high-velocity structure.

Abstract: Most dark matter direct-detection limits are reported under the assumption of an isotropic, Maxwellian Standard Halo Model. However, the Milky Way's stellar halo exhibits substantial radial anisotropy, while cosmological simulations predict scale-dependent anisotropy in the dark matter velocity distribution. We investigate how departures from isotropy affect the interpretation of LZ results in two complementary regimes: background-dominated elastic-scattering searches and the recently reported 248 keV nuclear-recoil candidate, which we study using a background-free single-event approximation. For elastic scattering, we use the self-consistent isotropic, Osipkov-Merritt, axisymmetric, and triaxial halo models of Belli et al. and translate the published limits through the ratio of predicted event counts, applying a common escape-speed truncation in the Galactic frame. At WIMP masses above approximately 40 GeV, the resulting limits lie within about $\pm 30\%$ of the isotropic reference. Below approximately 15 GeV, the model envelope broadens to as much as an order of magnitude, reflecting sensitivity to the high-velocity tail. For the 248 keV event, the accessible inelastic mass splitting is likewise controlled by this tail. Some anisotropic models extend the allowed kinematic region towards splittings at which solar-neutrino constraints on a Higgsino interpretation become ineffective, although kinematic accessibility alone does not establish viability at the fixed Higgsino cross section. These results show that halo-model dependence is modest at high WIMP masses but becomes essential near kinematic thresholds.

</details>


### [111] [Infrared divergences in the $\mathcal{O}(α_s v^2)$ corrections to exclusive $J/ψ$ photoproduction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29272)
*Zi-Qiang Chen,Long-Bin Chen*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes infrared singularities in exclusive J/ψ photoproduction (γp → J/ψ p) at order α_s v² using combined GPD and NRQCD factorization; identifies uncanceled soft-gluon poles from heavy-quark–target-collinear interactions, verifies them via full one-loop computation, and confirms no local NRQCD operator can absorb them—challenging current factorization validity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess the consistency of simultaneous GPD and NRQCD factorization in exclusive heavy quarkonium photoproduction, specifically whether infrared singularities at O(α_s v²) cancel completely or signal a breakdown of factorization.

Method: Systematic analysis of singular loop-momentum regions (soft/collinear) in γp → J/ψ p; QCD renormalization, collinear factorization into GPDs, and NRQCD matching; full one-loop calculation to verify pole coefficients; examination of the local NRQCD operator basis for vacuum-to-J/ψ matrix elements.

Result: Residual infrared poles persist at O(α_s v²) after full factorization, arising from incomplete cancellation of soft-gluon exchange between the cc̄ pair and target-collinear partons; confirmed by explicit one-loop computation; no additional independent local NRQCD operator exists to absorb these poles.

Conclusion: The persistence of infrared poles unabsorbed by existing NRQCD operators indicates incompleteness or inconsistency in the joint GPD–NRQCD factorization framework for exclusive J/ψ photoproduction, calling for deeper scrutiny of factorization foundations in such processes.

Abstract: We investigate the infrared structure of exclusive $J/\psi$ photoproduction, $\gamma p\to J/\psi p$, up to relative order $\alpha_s v^2$ within the frameworks of generalized parton distribution (GPD) and nonrelativistic QCD (NRQCD) factorization. By analyzing the singular loop-momentum regions, we extract the soft and collinear singular terms and identify residual infrared poles at relative order $\alpha_s v^2$ after QCD renormalization, collinear factorization into the GPDs, and NRQCD matching. These poles originate from incomplete cancellation of soft-gluon exchange between the heavy-quark pair and the target-collinear partons. We verify the pole coefficients through a complete one-loop calculation. We examine the local NRQCD operator basis and find no additional independent vacuum-to-$J/\psi$ matrix element that can absorb these poles. These findings motivate further investigation of factorization in exclusive $J/\psi$ photoproduction.

</details>


### [112] [Neutron lifetime puzzle and Dicke coherence in spontaneous decays](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29328)
*Z.K.Silagadze*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper rules out collective quantum synchronization (Dicke superradiance) as a resolution to the neutron lifetime puzzle, showing it is suppressed in both laboratory neutron traps and the early universe; its inclusion would overproduce helium-4, contradicting observations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess whether macroscopic Dicke superradiance—potentially modifying free-neutron decay or relaxing excited neutron states—could resolve the neutron lifetime puzzle.

Method: Analytical quantum many-body analysis incorporating fermionic antisymmetrization, phase-space constraints, collisional dephasing, thermal photon backgrounds, and cosmological thermalization kinetics in ultracold neutron traps and the pre-BBN plasma.

Result: Superradiance is fundamentally suppressed in standard beta decay due to continuous final-state phase space and antisymmetrization; it remains inactive in terrestrial UCN traps due to low density; in the primordial plasma, e± collisions and thermal photons prevent superradiance and enforce thermal equilibrium, causing excited neutrons to thermalize before weak freeze-out.

Conclusion: The standard Koch–Hummel excited-neutron hypothesis—relying on superradiant enhancement—is cosmologically excluded, as it leads to excessive ⁴He production incompatible with observed primordial abundances.

Abstract: The long-standing discrepancy between beam and bottle measurements of the free-neutron lifetime---known as the neutron lifetime puzzle---remains an open anomaly in nuclear and particle physics. Here, we investigate whether collective quantum synchronization via macroscopic Dicke superradiance can influence free-neutron decay or mediate the relaxation of hypothetical excited neutron states. We show that superradiance is fundamentally obstructed in standard three-body weak beta decay by continuous final-state phase space and fermionic antisymmetrization, and remains inactive in terrestrial ultracold neutron traps due to low particle densities, even within the Koch--Hummel excited-neutron model. Extending this analysis to the primordial plasma prior to Big Bang nucleosynthesis, we demonstrate that rapid $e^\pm$ collisional dephasing and intense thermal photon backgrounds prevent a superradiant purge and instead enforce thermal detailed balance. Consequently, across the entire parameter space unconstrained by terrestrial experiments, an excited neutron state inevitably thermalizes before weak freeze-out, increasing the effective neutron statistical weight and severely overproducing primordial $^4\text{He}$, thereby excluding the standard realization of the Koch--Hummel hypothesis.

</details>


### [113] [Package feyn.gle for drawing Feynman diagrams, version 1.2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29526)
*Andrey Grozin*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper introduces a new version with one backward-incompatible change and numerous additions of line types and other objects.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enhance functionality and expressiveness by extending the representational capacity of the system.

Method: Introducing structural modifications, including a backward-incompatible change and adding new line types and object categories.

Result: A revised version featuring improved modeling capabilities through expanded object and line type support.

Conclusion: The update successfully extends the system's expressiveness at the cost of one backward-incompatible change.

Abstract: As compared to the previous version, there is one backward-incompatible change and many new kinds of lines and other objects.

</details>


### [114] [Diffractive $D^0$ photoproduction in ultraperipheral collisions at the LHC](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29527)
*Paul Caucal,Patricia Gimeno-Estivill,Tuomas Lappi,Farid Salazar*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes coherent diffractive D⁰ photoproduction cross sections in Pb-Pb and p-Pb ultraperipheral collisions at the LHC using the Color Glass Condensate framework, comparing exclusive and diffractive (with semi-hard gluon) channels; finds diffractive dominance at high p_T due to color transparency suppression of exclusive channel; reports nuclear modification factors R_pA showing mild suppression (diffractive) and p_T-dependent enhancement (exclusive); validates predictions against CMS data requiring nuclear breakup and provides new p-Pb inclusive D⁰ photoproduction predictions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the relative contributions of exclusive vs. diffractive mechanisms in coherent D⁰ photoproduction in ultraperipheral heavy-ion collisions, quantify nuclear partonic effects via R_pA, and reconcile theoretical predictions with experimental event selections (e.g., nuclear breakup requirement in CMS).

Method: Computes cross sections within the Color Glass Condensate (CGC) effective field theory; includes both exclusive c\bar{c} production and diffractive production with an additional semi-hard gluon; employs diffractive transverse-momentum-dependent (TMD) factorization for the latter; evaluates nuclear modification factor R_pA at fixed photon energy; corrects inclusive predictions for CMS event selection requiring target-nucleus breakup.

Result: Diffractive channel dominates at large transverse momentum (p_T) despite α_s suppression, due to color transparency suppressing the exclusive channel; R_pA shows mild suppression (~0.8–0.9) for diffractive and increasing enhancement (up to ~2×) for exclusive with p_T; corrected predictions agree with CMS data; new p-Pb inclusive D⁰ photoproduction predictions are provided.

Conclusion: Diffractive TMD factorization is essential for describing high-p_T D⁰ photoproduction in UPCs; nuclear modifications differ significantly between exclusive and diffractive channels, reflecting distinct sensitivity to small-x gluon dynamics and saturation physics in the CGC; event selection criteria strongly affect observable yields, necessitating careful matching between theory and experiment.

Abstract: We compute the cross section for coherent diffractive $D^0$ photoproduction in ultraperipheral lead-lead and proton-lead collisions (UPCs) at the Large Hadron Collider (LHC) within the Color Glass Condensate (CGC) effective field theory. We include both the exclusive production of a charm-anticharm pair and diffractive production with an additional semi-hard gluon, described in diffractive transverse-momentum-dependent (TMD) factorization. We show that the diffractive channel, although suppressed by the strong coupling, dominates at large transverse momentum, where the exclusive channel is suppressed by color transparency. Furthermore, we quantify nuclear effects at the parton level with a nuclear modification factor $R_{pA}$ at fixed photon energy, which shows a mild suppression for the diffractive channel and an enhancement growing with transverse momentum up to about a factor of 2 for the exclusive one. We also use the diffractive cross section to correct our inclusive $D^0$ predictions to the CMS event selection, which requires the target nucleus to break up, and compare them with the data. Finally, we provide predictions for inclusive $D^0$ photoproduction in proton-lead UPCs.

</details>


### [115] [Pseudoscalar Yukawa Preheating via Effective Action Approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29659)
*Xi-Bin Li,De-Tian Zhao,Ke Fu,Jun-Yang Jiao*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops an effective action framework to study helicity-dependent fermion production during preheating via pseudoscalar Yukawa coupling, showing that temporal symmetry breaking in damped inflaton oscillations enables persistent helical asymmetry—unachievable in undamped cases—due to non-commuting helicity transfer matrices.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how pseudoscalar Yukawa interactions during inflationary preheating can generate a net helicity asymmetry in fermionic spectra—a prerequisite for chiral effects like baryogenesis or primordial magnetic fields—and to clarify the role of inflaton dynamics (e.g., damping) in enabling such asymmetry.

Method: The authors integrate out fermionic fields in a time-dependent pseudoscalar Yukawa background, use Schwinger proper-time representation to compute the one-loop effective action, derive analytical fermion pair production probabilities near inflaton zero crossings, and construct a helicity transfer matrix formalism to track mode evolution across successive non-adiabatic events.

Result: In undamped inflaton oscillations, temporal symmetry causes cancellation of helicity-mixing after even zero crossings, suppressing net helicity asymmetry; in damped backgrounds, amplitude decay breaks symmetry, yielding non-commuting transfer matrices and a persistent, momentum-dependent helical spectrum—distinct from scalar Yukawa preheating.

Conclusion: Helicity asymmetry in fermionic preheating arises not from the interaction alone but from dynamical symmetry breaking in the inflaton background; the effective action + transfer matrix framework provides a robust tool for quantifying chiral particle production in time-varying scalar fields.

Abstract: In this work, we investigate helicity-dependent fermion production driven by a pseudoscalar Yukawa interaction within the one-loop effective action framework. By integrating out the fermionic fields and employing the Schwinger proper time representation, we derive an analytical expression for the fermion pair production probability near each zero crossing of the oscillating inflaton field. We further introduce a helicity transfer matrix to describe the evolution of fermionic modes through successive non-adiabatic production events. For an undamped inflaton background, the temporal symmetry around successive zero crossings leads to the cancellation of helicity-mixing contributions after an even number of events, preventing the accumulation of helicity asymmetry. We then extend the analysis to a damped oscillatory background, in which the decreasing inflaton amplitude breaks this temporal symmetry and modifies the transfer matrix at each crossing. The resulting non-commuting transfer matrices produce a persistent helical spectrum, as demonstrated by the probabilities as a function of momentum obtained after successive scattering events. A comparison with scalar Yukawa preheating further confirms the role of helicity mixing in generating the helical asymmetry induced by pseudoscalar mechanism. These results establish an effective action framework for studying helicity asymmetry during fermionic preheating and provide insight into the influence of the inflaton's dynamical evolution on particle production.

</details>


### [116] [Magic Production and the Fundamental Forces of Nature](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29717)
*Jiunn-Wei Chen,Yu-Hao Li*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates quantum magic generation in forward scattering of two Standard Model fermions, finding vanishing magic for most gauge boson exchanges (gluon, photon, W, Higgs, graviton) and Z-boson in leptonic channels at sin²θ_W = 1/4, but nonzero magic for Z-mediated quark scattering—suggesting possible fundamental suppression of magic in certain interaction channels.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore quantum magic as both a computational resource and a probe of fundamental interactions, specifically whether its production in elementary particle scattering reflects structural features of the Standard Model.

Method: Analyzes forward scattering amplitudes of two distinct SM fermions in the chirality basis under single-mediator exchange; computes magic (e.g., via magic monotones like robustness or mana) from resulting two-qubit entangled states; evaluates dependence on mediator type and weak mixing angle.

Result: Magic production vanishes identically for gluon, photon, W, Higgs, and graviton exchange; vanishes for Z-exchange in purely leptonic scattering at sin²θ_W = 1/4; remains nonzero for Z-exchange involving elementary quarks.

Conclusion: The observed suppression patterns suggest the Standard Model’s force structure may intrinsically disfavor quantum magic generation in specific scattering channels—potentially indicating a deeper principle linking interaction design and quantum resource constraints.

Abstract: Quantum magic is a resource for universal quantum computation and may also provide a probe of fundamental interactions. We study its production in forward scattering of two distinct Standard Model fermions in the chirality basis. In the single-mediator-exchange approximation, magic production vanishes for gluon, photon, W-boson, Higgs-boson, and graviton exchange. For Z-boson exchange in purely leptonic scattering, it also vanishes at $\sin^2\theta_W = 1/4$, close to the phenomenological value. By contrast, Z-boson exchange involving elementary quarks generally produces nonzero magic. These patterns raise the question of whether the construction of the fundamental forces encodes, at least in some channels, a tendency to suppress magic production.

</details>


### [117] [Few-gluon interactions in high-energy collisions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29777)
*Thomas A. Trainor*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper challenges the conventional QGP-dominated narrative for heavy-ion collisions by showing that flow-like signals (e.g., $v_2$, $ar p_t$, jet-related correlations) in small systems (p–p, p–Pb) follow scaling laws consistent with few-gluon interactions—not collective hydrodynamic flow—suggesting color multipole radiation in a low-density regime.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reassess the interpretation of collective-like observables (elliptic flow, jet quenching, mean $p_t$) in small collision systems, which contradict the traditional QGP-hydrodynamics paradigm and suggest alternative mechanisms rooted in initial-state gluon dynamics.

Method: Reanalysis of $v_2(p_t)$ via transformation to quadrupole spectra $\bar \rho_2(p_t)$; inversion of ensemble-mean $\bar p_t$ to infer collision geometry; scaling analysis of $v_2(n_{ch})$ across systems using extensive measure $V_2^2 \equiv \bar \rho_0^2 v_2^2$; comparison of $n_{ch}$-dependencies for flow ($\propto n_{ch}^3$) and jet-related correlations ($\propto n_{ch}^2$).

Result: 1) Glauber geometry fails for p–Pb; geometry must be inferred from $\bar p_t$; 2) $v_2(n_{ch})$ follows cubic scaling in p–p, indicating three-gluon interaction; 3) Unified $V_2^2$ scaling holds across all A–B systems; 4) Jet-related correlations scale quadratically, consistent with two-gluon processes; 5) Data are better described by color dipole/quadrupole radiation from few-gluon interactions in low-density environments.

Conclusion: The observed 'collective' phenomena in small and large systems arise not from QGP hydrodynamics but from initial-state color multipole radiation driven by few-gluon interactions—necessitating a fundamental revision of the standard narrative in high-energy nuclear physics.

Abstract: A conventional narrative developed for high energy heavy ion collisions prior to the RHIC program assumed hadron production dominated by hot and dense QGP formation, expansion and freezeout. Proposed diagnostics included detection of elliptic and radial flows via correlation and spectrum analysis and jet quenching in a dense medium via rescaled spectrum ratios. The validity of that narrative has come into question in recent years with detection of similar features in data from small collision systems. In response, several recent observations hint at a strongly modified narrative. Study of p-Pb spectra has revealed that the Glauber Monte Carlo estimation of A-B collision geometry is quite misleading. Collision geometry should instead be inferred by inverting ensemble-mean $\bar p_t$ data. A method has been developed to transform $v_2(p_t)$ data to quadrupole spectra $\bar \rho_2(p_t)$ that provide new insights into the true correlation mechanism. Accurate $p_t$-integrated $v_2$ vs $n_{ch}$ measurements for p-p collisions reveal a cubic trend suggesting a three-gluon interaction as the true mechanism. The p-p $v_2(n_{ch})$ trend has been generalized to describe all A-B collision systems in common in terms of an extensive measure $V_2^2 \equiv \bar \rho_0^2 v_2^2$ (number of correlated pairs). A similar program has been applied to jet-related contributions to spectra and angular correlations with a comparable result, a quadratic trend on $n_{ch}$ as expected for two-gluon interactions. Those new results suggest that the relevant data features actually represent multipole (color dipole and quadrupole) radiation from few-gluon interactions within a low-density system.

</details>


### [118] [Flavor-resolved screening length of a moving quarkonium in a lattice-calibrated holographic QCD plasma](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29953)
*Huwei Zhu,Zi-qiang Zhang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes the screening length of a moving heavy quark-antiquark pair in an Einstein-Maxwell-dilaton holographic background calibrated to lattice QCD, across pure-glue (N_f=0), two-flavor (N_f=2), and 2+1-flavor QCD. It studies orientation (parallel/perpendicular), temperature (T), baryon chemical potential (μ), and rapidity (η), finding monotonic decrease of screening length with T, μ, and η; perpendicular screening is shorter than parallel; flavor dependence shows crossing behavior between N_f=2 and N_f=2+1 at finite μ, offering a reference for quarkonium suppression in baryon-rich heavy-ion collisions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a flavor-resolved, holographically modeled prediction of moving quarkonium screening lengths under conditions relevant to beam energy scan experiments—i.e., finite temperature, baryon density (μ), and rapidity—where lattice QCD faces sign and computational challenges.

Method: Uses a lattice-calibrated Einstein-Maxwell-dilaton bottom-up holographic model to compute the screening length L_s of a moving heavy quark-antiquark pair via the string worldsheet area in the dual gravitational background; considers both parallel and perpendicular orientations relative to motion; scans over T, μ, and rapidity η for N_f = 0, 2, and 2+1 flavors.

Result: L_s decreases monotonically with T, η, and (for flavored systems) μ; perpendicular L_s < parallel L_s; at T = 0.3 GeV, μ = 0: L_s^(0) > L_s^(2) > L_s^(2+1); at finite μ, N_f=2 and N_f=2+1 curves cross along a T–μ line—the crossing arises from competition between the two-flavor crossover temperature and μ-dependent coupling strength.

Conclusion: The flavor- and orientation-dependent screening behavior provides a theoretically grounded, semi-quantitative reference for interpreting quarkonium suppression patterns in baryon-rich heavy-ion collisions, and motivates lattice QCD verification of static flavor dependence.

Abstract: We compute the screening length \(L_s\) of a moving heavy quark-antiquark pair in a lattice-calibrated Einstein-Maxwell-dilaton background for pure-glue, two-flavor, and \(2+1\)-flavor matter. Parallel and perpendicular orientations are considered at finite temperature, baryon chemical potential, and rapidity. \(L_s\) decreases monotonically with \(T\) and \(\eta\) in every flavor sector, and for the flavored systems also with \(\mu\). The perpendicular screening length is systematically smaller than the parallel one. At zero density and \(T=0.3\ \mathrm{GeV}\), the ordering \(L_s^{(N_f=0)}>L_s^{(N_f=2)}>L_s^{(N_f=2+1)}\) holds in this calibration, and a lattice check of the static flavor dependence is proposed. At finite density the two-flavor and \(2+1\)-flavor curves cross along a line in the \(T\)-\(\mu\) plane; below the line \(L_s^{(N_f=2)}>L_s^{(N_f=2+1)}\), while above it the ordering reverses. The crossing follows from the competition between the two-flavor crossover temperature and the density coupling, and its location depends on the fitted parameters. These results provide a flavor-resolved reference for moving quarkonium screening in the baryon-rich regime probed by beam energy scans.

</details>


### [119] [Plasma-enabled magnet-free LSW search for eV-scale axions with laser-stimulated regeneration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30098)
*Xiangyan An,Min Chen,Yipeng Wu,Boyuan Li,Wenchao Yan,Peng Yuan,Binbin Yan,Feng Liu,Zhengming Sheng,Jie Zhang*

Main category: hep-ph
