TL;DR: This paper proposes a magnet-free light-shining-through-a-wall (LSW) experiment for axion detection, using plasma-based axion generation and laser-stimulated regeneration to overcome photon–axion phase mismatch at eV-scale axion masses; it achieves projected axion-photon coupling limits of ~10⁻⁷ GeV⁻¹ over 0.1–1 eV.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Conventional LSW experiments suffer from loss of coherence due to photon–axion phase mismatch at eV-scale axion masses, severely limiting sensitivity; a magnet-free alternative is needed to restore coherence and broaden mass reach.

Method: Two plasma-based axion production schemes: (1) low-intensity two-color dispersion-matched source for narrowband axion generation tuned by plasma dispersion; (2) high-intensity single-pulse plasma-broadened source—validated via particle-in-cell simulations—where E·B source term broadens spectrally due to laser–plasma wave mixing. Axion detection employs counter-propagating laser-stimulated decay into a signal photon and a laser-mode photon, with phase matching enforced by laser frequency selection.

Result: Demonstrates broadband axion generation up to ~1 eV via relativistic laser–plasma interaction; predicts detectable signal photon yield enabling projected upper limits on the axion-photon coupling g_{aγ} ≈ 10⁻⁷ GeV⁻¹ for m_a = 0.1–1 eV.

Conclusion: The proposed magnet-free, plasma- and laser-driven LSW scheme overcomes coherence limitations of conventional magnetic LSW at eV-scale axion masses, offering a viable pathway toward probing unexplored regions of axion parameter space with improved sensitivity and tunability.

Abstract: In conventional light-shining-through-a-wall (LSW) experiments, strong magnetic fields mediate the conversion of photons into axions and their subsequent reconversion into photons. At eV-scale axion masses, however, photon--axion phase mismatch causes these conversions to lose coherence, substantially reducing the experimental sensitivity. We propose a magnet-free LSW scheme that addresses this limitation via plasma-based axion generation and laser-stimulated regeneration. At low laser intensity, a two-color dispersion-matched source generates axions in a narrow mass range set by the plasma dispersion. At relativistic laser intensity, a single-pulse plasma-broadened source generates axions over a broad mass range, as shown by particle-in-cell simulations. The Fourier spectrum of the source term $\mathbf{E}\cdot\mathbf{B}$ broadens around the laser branch, consistent with mixing between the laser and low-frequency plasma waves. For axion detection behind the wall, a counter-propagating laser stimulates axion decay into a detectable signal photon and a second photon emitted into the laser mode, with the laser frequency chosen to satisfy phase matching. For representative parameters, the requirement of at least one regenerated photon yields projected upper limits on the axion-photon coupling on the order $10^{-7}\,\mathrm{GeV}^{-1}$ over $m_a=0.1$--$1\,\mathrm{eV}$.

</details>


### [120] [Effect of dark matter and scalar-cut potential on the macroscopic properties of neutron stars: A chiral quark mean field approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30434)
*Hrishika P,Arvind Kumar*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies how fermionic dark matter and a scalar-cut potential affect neutron star structure, using a chiral SU(3) quark mean-field model for baryonic matter and a two-fluid TOV formalism for gravitational coupling between baryons and DM; results show the scalar-cut potential stiffens the EOS, while DM softens it and alters macroscopic observables depending on DM mass and mediator couplings.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how fermionic dark matter and modified scalar potentials influence neutron star equation of state and observable properties—bridging particle physics, nuclear theory, and astrophysics to constrain DM models via neutron star observations.

Method: Uses the chiral SU(3) quark mean-field (CQMF) model for baryonic matter (n, p, e, μ) under β-equilibrium, incorporates self-interacting fermionic DM with scalar (φ_D) and vector (V_D^μ) mediators, couples baryonic and DM fluids only gravitationally within a two-fluid Tolman-Oppenheimer-Volkoff framework, and analyzes EOS, mass-radius relation, tidal deformability, and gravitational redshift.

Result: Scalar-cut potential U_cut(ϕ) stiffens the EOS; increasing DM fraction softens the EOS and modifies mass-radius curves, tidal deformability Λ, and gravitational redshift z; DM particle mass and mediator couplings determine whether configurations host compact DM cores or extended DM halos.

Conclusion: Fermionic DM significantly impacts neutron star structure and observables in a parameter-dependent way, and its effects are distinguishable from those of modified scalar potentials—providing complementary probes for DM physics using multi-messenger neutron star data.

Abstract: We investigate the effects of fermionic dark matter (DM) and scalar-cut potential ($U_{\text{cut}}(\phi)$) on the equation of state (EOS) and macroscopic properties of neutron stars. The baryonic matter consists of neutrons, protons, electrons, and muons under the $\beta$-equilibrium and is described within the chiral SU(3) quark mean-field (CQMF) model. In this framework, quarks are confined within baryons, whose properties are modified through the exchange of scalar fields $\sigma$, $\zeta$, and $\delta$, and vector fields $\omega$ and $\rho$. The DM is modelled as a self-interacting fermionic fluid with scalar $\varphi_D$ and vector $V_D^\mu$ mediators. The baryonic and DM fluids interact only through gravity and are treated within a two-fluid Tolman-Oppenheimer-Volkoff formalism. We examine the resulting mass-radius relation and tidal deformability, along with the gravitational redshift, to investigate the effects of DM on neutron star structure and observable properties. The inclusion of $U_{\text{cut}}(\phi)$ stiffens the EOS, whereas increasing the DM fraction generally softens the EOS and modifies the mass-radius relation, tidal deformability, and gravitational redshift. The magnitude and nature of these modifications depend on the DM particle mass and mediator interactions, which determine whether the resulting configurations exhibit compact DM cores or extended DM halos.

</details>


### [121] [Pion scattering in finite volume within the Inverse Amplitude Method: Extension to moving frames](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30475)
*Julián A. Sánchez,Raquel Molina,Ángel Gómez Nicola*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes finite-volume corrections to ππ scattering near threshold using the one-loop Inverse Amplitude Method (IAM), extending beyond Lüscher’s formalism by including left-hand cut and t-, u-channel/tadpole contributions; it finds these corrections are significant for m_πL ≲ 1.5–2.8 depending on frame, and only negligible for m_πL ≳ 5.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To quantify leading exponentially suppressed finite-volume corrections to ππ scattering in moving frames—beyond Lüscher’s quantization condition—which are relevant for precision lattice QCD calculations of scattering parameters near threshold.

Method: One-loop Inverse Amplitude Method (IAM) applied to I = 0 and I = 2 ππ amplitudes in a finite box with total momentum P⃗ = 2πd⃗/L; includes left-hand cut, t- and u-channel diagrams, and tadpole contributions omitted in Lüscher’s approach.

Result: Finite-volume corrections become comparable to the leading Lüscher term for m_πL ≲ 1.5–1.7 at rest and up to ≲ 2.8 for d⃗ = [002] moving frame; corrections decay exponentially and become negligible only for m_πL ≳ 5.

Conclusion: Standard Lüscher analysis underestimates systematic finite-volume errors in near-threshold ππ spectroscopy for moderate lattice sizes; inclusion of IAM-based subleading corrections is essential for sub-percent precision in current and near-future lattice studies.

Abstract: We study the leading exponentially suppressed finite-volume corrections to $\pi\pi$ scattering near threshold in moving frames. Using the one-loop Inverse Amplitude Method (IAM), we compute the $I=0$ and $I=2$ amplitudes in a box with total momentum $\vec P=2\pi\vec d/L$, including the left-hand cut and the $t$-, $u$-channel and tadpole contributions neglected in Lüscher's quantization condition. We derive the resulting correction to the near-threshold energy levels and measure its size relative to the Lüscher term. We find that it becomes comparable to the latter for $m_\pi L\lesssim 1.5$--$1.7$ at rest, and that it is larger in moving frames, reaching $m_\pi L\lesssim 2.8$ for $\vec d=[002]$. The corrections become negligible only for $m_\pi L\gtrsim 5$.

</details>


### [122] [Construction of a conserved energy-momentum tensor of the Skyrmion coupled to additional dynamical degrees in the large-$N_c$ expansion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30698)
*Mitsuru Tanaka,Yasuhiro Yamaguchi,Masayasu Harada*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs a conserved energy-momentum tensor (EMT) order-by-order in the 1/N_c expansion for Skyrmion models coupled to dynamical fields (e.g., antikaons), demonstrating that backreaction is essential for EMT conservation—unlike the fixed-background approximation. Applied to the Callan-Klebanov hyperon model, it yields a conserved EMT at next-to-leading order, revealing a negative, smaller-magnitude D-term and smaller gravitational radii for hyperons vs. nucleons—indicating greater compactness.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the inconsistency in energy-momentum conservation when coupling Skyrmions to dynamical fields in the large-N_c expansion; specifically, to go beyond the fixed-background approximation and properly incorporate backreaction effects.

Method: Order-by-order construction of the energy-momentum tensor (EMT) in powers of 1/N_c; systematic expansion of equations of motion and EMT; explicit inclusion of backreaction from dynamical fields (e.g., antikaon) on the Skyrmion background; application to the Callan-Klebanov bound-state model for hyperons.

Result: A conserved total EMT is obtained up to next-to-leading order in 1/N_c; cancellation between the divergence of the Skyrmion’s backreaction-induced EMT and that of the kaon’s EMT is explicitly demonstrated; hyperon D-term is negative and smaller in magnitude than the nucleon’s; gravitational form factor radii are smaller for hyperons, indicating higher compactness.

Conclusion: Backreaction is indispensable for energy-momentum conservation in large-N_c Skyrmion-field couplings; the resulting conserved EMT provides quantitative evidence that hyperons are more compact than nucleons, with implications for hadron structure and gravitational form factors.

Abstract: We formulate an order-by-order construction of a conserved energy-momentum tensor (EMT) in the large-$N_c$ expansion of the model where the Skyrmion is coupled to additional dynamical fields. We show that, expanding the equations of motion and the EMT in powers of $1/N_c$, the inclusion of the backreaction to the Skyrmion from the additional fields is essential for achieving the conservation law of the EMT, which is contrasted with the fixed-background approximation where the effects of backreaction are neglected. We apply the construction to the model for hyperons based on the Callan-Klebanov bound-state approach, where the antikaon is bound to the Skyrmion, to obtain the EMT up to next-to-leading order of the $1/N_c$ expansion. We explicitly show that the divergence of the EMT from the backreaction for the Skyrmion exactly cancels that of the kaon to achieve the conservation of the total EMT. We find that the conserved EMT gives a negative hyperon D-term whose magnitude is smaller than that of the nucleon D-term and radii of gravitational form factors for the hyperon smaller than those for the nucleon, which implies that the hyperon is more compact than the nucleon.

</details>


### [123] [Fermionic dark linear seesaw with an anomalous Majoron](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30799)
*A. Batra,H. B. Câmara,F. R. Joaquim*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a minimal fermionic dark linear seesaw model with spontaneous lepton number violation, generating radiatively small neutrino masses via a dark sector (singlet + doublet fermions + scalars), yielding a stable dark matter candidate and a GeV-scale Majoron that decays to two photons; it predicts enhanced lepton-flavour-violating decays (e.g., τ → ℓJ ≫ τ → ℓγ) and is testable at beam-dump experiments and μ → eγ searches.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain tiny neutrino masses via the linear seesaw while linking them to a testable dark sector and addressing the stability of dark matter and phenomenology of the Majoron, avoiding fine-tuning of lepton-number-violating parameters.

Method: Constructs a minimal extension of the Standard Model with a U(1)_L-symmetric dark sector (fermionic singlet/doublet + scalar singlets); employs spontaneous U(1)_L breaking to Z_2, generating a Majoron and stabilising the lightest dark particle; computes radiative neutrino mass generation, Majoron-photon/lepton couplings, and LFV observables including μ → eγ and τ → ℓJ.

Result: Radiative linear seesaw neutrino masses are naturally suppressed; a GeV-scale Majoron arises from soft lepton-number breaking and decays dominantly to γγ, accessible at beam-dump experiments; τ → ℓJ branching ratios exceed τ → ℓγ by several orders of magnitude and probe regions beyond μ → eγ sensitivity; viable fermionic WIMP dark matter emerges with consistent relic density and direct detection constraints.

Conclusion: The model provides a unified framework connecting neutrino mass generation, dark matter stability, and observable Majoron phenomenology; it is minimally tuned, predictive for LFV and beam-dump experiments, and offers a compelling target for near-future searches.

Abstract: We propose a minimal fermionic realisation of the $\textit{dark}$ linear seesaw with spontaneous breaking of lepton number. The small lepton-number-violating parameter responsible for linear-seesaw neutrino masses is generated radiatively by a $\textit{dark}$ sector consisting of a singlet and a doublet fermion, together with scalar singlets. The global U(1)$_L$ symmetry is broken to a residual $\mathcal{Z}_2$ under which the $\textit{dark}$ sector is odd. This breaking yields a Majoron $J$ and makes the lightest $\textit{dark}$ particle stable. The charged $\textit{dark}$ fermion acquires its mass solely from U(1)$_L$ breaking and thus induces an anomalous Majoron coupling to photons. Current bounds on this coupling exclude a massless Majoron for TeV-scale U(1)$_L$ breaking. We add a linear term that breaks lepton number softly and gives the Majoron a GeV-scale mass. Such a Majoron decays into two photons and can be probed at beam-dump experiments. We study the dark matter phenomenology, focusing on the case where the stable particle is a fermionic weakly interacting massive particle. We analyse how the framework can be tested through charged-lepton flavour-violating decays such as $\mu \to e\gamma$, which receive contributions from both the $\textit{dark}$ and the seesaw sectors. Moreover, both sectors enhance the Majoron couplings to charged leptons, so that the Majoron-emitting decays $\tau \to \ell J$ ($\ell = e,\mu$) typically exceed $\tau \to \ell\gamma$ by orders of magnitude and can probe parameter space beyond the reach of $\mu \to e\gamma$.

</details>


### [124] [Lorentz symmetry in self-supervised representation learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31011)
*Andreas Hermansen,Tobias Golling,Slava Voloshynovskiy*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper unifies self-supervised learning (SSL) and physical inductive biases—specifically Lorentz symmetry—for jet tagging by integrating the Lorentz-equivariant L-GATr-slim architecture with the LeJEPA representation learning framework, achieving state-of-the-art performance on downstream jet classification tasks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To bridge the gap between self-supervised learning and physics-informed inductive biases in jet tagging, leveraging Lorentz symmetry as a principled constraint for improved generalization and representation quality.

Method: Integrates the Lorentz-equivariant Geometric Algebra Transformer slim (L-GATr-slim) with the LeJEPA framework; pre-trains on 100M jets from JetClass using physics-motivated augmentations to enforce latent-space invariance (and optionally Lorentz equivariance); evaluates via linear/MLP probes and downstream benchmarks.

Result: LeJEPA with a standard Transformer outperforms autoregressive, masked particle modeling, and self-distillation baselines; L-GATr-slim provides further gains; enforcing Lorentz symmetry—either architecturally or via kinematic augmentations—is critical for latent representation quality.

Conclusion: Unifying SSL with explicit physical symmetries (e.g., Lorentz invariance/equivariance) yields superior, generalizable representations for jet tagging—demonstrating that architectural priors and data augmentations are complementary paths to physics-aware representation learning.

Abstract: Self-supervised learning (SSL) and explicit physical inductive biases have driven major advances in jet tagging, yet they are typically pursued along separate tracks. In this work, we unify both paradigms by integrating the Lorentz-equivariant Geometric Algebra Transformer slim (L-GATr-slim) architecture with the LeJEPA framework for representation learning. Pre-training on 100 million jets from the JetClass dataset, we optimize towards a latent space, which is invariant across physics-motivated jet augmentations. We demonstrate that enforcing Lorentz symmetry in the latent space is critical for representation quality and that this can be achieved either architecturally or learned via kinematic augmentations. With a standard non-invariant Transformer backbone, LeJEPA outperforms established autoregressive, masked particle modelling, and self-distillation baselines in linear- and MLP-probe accuracy, with L-GATr-slim providing further complementary gains. Across downstream benchmarks, our pre-trained models consistently outperform baselines trained from scratch or through different pre-training objectives.

</details>


### [125] [Kaon-pair production with NNLO effects](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31080)
*Yizhou Fang,Sophie Kollatzsch,Adrian Signer*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: McMule is extended to compute NNLO QED and NLO mixed corrections for e+e−→K+K− and e+e−→K0K̄0, using FsQED/disperon QED; fully differential, form-factor agnostic; large corrections found near CMD-3 energies and φ(1020) resonance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide high-precision theoretical predictions for kaon-pair production in e+e− collisions—critical for interpreting CMD-3 data and testing QCD via kaon form factors—requiring control over higher-order QED effects beyond standard NLO.

Method: Extension of the Monte Carlo framework McMule with NNLO initial-state QED corrections and NLO mixed (QED×QCD) corrections computed within the FsQED approach using disperon QED; fully differential implementation compatible with arbitrary kaon electromagnetic form factors.

Result: Computed total cross sections for e+e−→K+K− and e+e−→K0K̄0 at CMD-3 relevant energies showing very large higher-order corrections; additionally, differential observables (e.g., angular distributions, energy spectra) presented for e+e−→K+K− near the φ(1020) resonance.

Conclusion: NNLO QED and NLO mixed corrections are indispensable for sub-percent precision in kaon-pair cross sections; the implemented framework enables robust, form-factor–flexible predictions essential for extracting hadronic vacuum polarization and testing low-energy QCD.

Abstract: The Monte Carlo framework McMule has been extended to support the processes $e^+\,e^-\to K^+\,K^-$ and $e^+\,e^-\to K^0\,\bar{K}^0$. Our results include NNLO initial-state QED corrections together with NLO mixed corrections. The latter are evaluated in the FsQED approach, using disperon QED. The implementation is fully differential and can be used with any kaon form factor. We present the total cross section for energies relevant to the CMD-3 experiment and observe very large corrections. For $e^+\,e^-\to K^+\,K^-$ we also present selected differential observables for energies around the $\phi(1020)$ resonance.

</details>


<div id='gr-qc'></div>

# gr-qc [[Back]](#toc)

### [126] [Spherical orbits around a Newman-Janis-generated rotating Bronnikov black hole](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28074)
*F. Subandi,H. S. Ramadhan*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes spherical geodesics (null and timelike) in a rotating regular black hole geometry derived via the Newman–Janis algorithm from the magnetically-charged Bronnikov mass profile; it confirms geometric regularity, classifies photon orbit stability (unstable in BH regime → defines shadow; stable in horizonless case), identifies ISSO, verifies energy condition behavior (violated only in strong-field interior, satisfied asymptotically and externally), and confirms compliance with Maeda’s five criteria (C1–C5) for physically reasonable nonsingular black holes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess the physical viability of a rotating regular black hole model—constructed via Newman–Janis from the Bronnikov magnetic mass profile—by rigorously testing its geodesic structure, energy conditions, and adherence to established geometric criteria for nonsingular black holes.

Method: Analytical study of null and timelike spherical geodesics in the Newman–Janis-rotating Bronnikov metric; computation of photon sphere stability via effective potential analysis; derivation of shadow shape for edge-on observers; determination of innermost stable spherical orbit (ISSO); systematic evaluation of the null, weak, strong, and dominant energy conditions (NEC, WEC, SEC, DEC); verification of Maeda’s five geometric criteria (C1–C5): curvature regularity, stable causality, energy condition satisfaction in appropriate regions, curvature boundedness, and global hyperbolicity (implied by domain structure r ≥ 0).

Result: The Kretschmann scalar is smooth and exponentially suppressed at r = 0; all spherical photon orbits outside the horizon are radially unstable in black-hole configurations (defining a smaller, distorted shadow vs. Kerr), while stable spherical photon orbits exist in horizonless cases; ISSO is identified for timelike motion; NEC/WEC/SEC/DEC are violated only in the strong-field interior but fully satisfied asymptotically and throughout the exterior of all black-hole solutions; the metric satisfies all five of Maeda’s criteria for physically reasonable nonsingular black holes.

Conclusion: The Newman–Janis-rotating Bronnikov geometry constitutes a physically well-behaved, nonsingular black hole model that meets rigorous geometric and physical consistency requirements—including regularity, causal stability, appropriate energy condition behavior, and observational signatures (shadow, ISSO)—thereby strengthening its status as a viable alternative to singular Kerr black holes.

Abstract: We investigate spherical geodesics in a Newman--Janis-generated rotating regular geometry constructed from the magnetically-charged Bronnikov mass profile. The Kretschmann scalar is exponentially suppressed at the center, and is smooth across the disk and ring within the single-sheeted domain $r\geq0$. We show that all physically admissible spherical photon orbits outside the event horizon are radially unstable in the black-hole regime, whereas stable spherical photon orbits can exist in horizonless configurations. The unstable photon orbits determine the shadow, which for an edge-on observer is smaller and distorted compared with the Kerr shadow. For timelike motion, we identify marginally stable spherical orbits and determine the innermost stable spherical orbit (ISSO). We further examine the null, weak, strong, and dominant energy conditions, finding violations in the strong-field region but satisfaction of all four conditions asymptotically and throughout the exterior of every black-hole configuration. Together with stable causality and the curvature bound, these results show that the metric satisfies the five geometric criteria C1--C5 proposed by Maeda for physically reasonable nonsingular black holes.

</details>


### [127] [Curvature-history tensor memory in inflationary cosmology: Component closure, ghost-free dynamics, and CMB BipoSH likelihood](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28106)
*G. M. Rhythm*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a generally covariant inflationary model where curvature history activates a tensor-memory order parameter, inducing scale-dependent statistical anisotropy in the primordial power spectrum via a Gauss–Bonnet–Kretschmann curvature source; it constructs a dressed tensor field with preserved symplectic structure, derives the anisotropic quadratic action exactly (including constraint elimination), confirms stability against Ostrogradsky instabilities, and provides estimators for observational quadrupole signatures.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a physically consistent, generally covariant mechanism for generating observable statistical anisotropy in the CMB—specifically a scale-dependent quadrupolar departure from isotropy—without introducing ghost degrees of freedom or violating unitarity, by leveraging curvature-induced memory effects rather than ad hoc preferred-frame or vector-field couplings.

Method: Uses a four-dimensional Gauss–Bonnet completion of the Kretschmann invariant as a curvature source; introduces a dressed symmetric-tracefree tensor field $Q_{\mu\nu} = \mathcal{E}_C \widehat Q_{\mu\nu}$ with exponential differential operator dressing $\mathcal{E}_C = \exp[B(C)\Box_{\widehat Q}/2]$; performs exact quadratic Hessian construction, ADM constraint elimination via block Schur complement, retarded Cauchy data fixing to remove homogeneous modes; employs nonlinear ADM Legendre analysis to assess stability; derives cut-sky Gaussian and quadratic maximum-likelihood estimators for BipoSH covariance.

Result: Derives a leading-order anisotropic curvature action with exact scale-dependent $T/E$ BipoSH covariance; proves invertible dressing preserves physical degree-of-freedom count and symplectic norm sign even when $[B(C), \Box_Q] \neq 0$; shows inflationary solution avoids Ostrogradsky singularities; obtains infrared-regular oscillatory primordial quadrupole from finite tensor-memory activation; provides explicit cut-sky estimators for the predicted anisotropy.

Conclusion: Curvature-history–driven tensor-memory activation provides a viable, ghost-free, generally covariant origin for scale-dependent statistical isotropy violation in inflation; the dressed-field formalism ensures consistency with canonical quantization and stability, and the resulting BipoSH signature is observationally testable with current and future CMB surveys.

Abstract: We present a generally covariant inflationary model in which curvature history activates a spatial symmetric-tracefree tensor-memory order parameter and generates a scale-dependent quadrupolar departure from primordial statistical isotropy. The curvature source is the four-dimensional Gauss--Bonnet completion of the Kretschmann invariant. A local $(C,\chi)$ representation is used only to implement the retarded theory: fixed retarded Cauchy data remove the homogeneous response modes, so the negative-sign localized combination is not an independent phase-space degree of freedom. The projected tensor sector is described by the dressed field $Q_{\mu\nu}=\mathcal E_C\widehat Q_{\mu\nu}$ with $\mathcal E_C=\exp[B(C)\Box_{\widehat Q}/2]$. Its raw-field Hessian has the exact congruence form $\mathcal E_C^\dagger(\Box_Q-\mathsf M^2)\mathcal E_C$, so an invertible dressing preserves the solution count and the sign of the physical symplectic norm even when $[B(C),\Box_Q]\ne0$. We derive the full quadratic Hessian, eliminate the ADM constraints and retarded response variables through an exact block Schur complement, and obtain the leading anisotropic curvature action. A nonlinear ADM Legendre analysis shows that the representative inflationary solution remains far from the singular threshold associated with an Ostrogradsky branch. A smooth curvature-dependent phase potential gives an approximately constant activated plateau with subdominant memory energy. Finite activation then produces an infrared-regular oscillatory primordial quadrupole and an exact scale-dependent $T/E$ BipoSH covariance. We also give the corresponding cut-sky Gaussian and quadratic maximum-likelihood estimators.

</details>


### [128] [Einstein-Gauss-Bonnet gravity corrections in Dark Dimension scenario](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28132)
*George K. Leontaris,George Prampromis*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies Einstein-Gauss-Bonnet black holes in Dark Dimension scenarios with micron-sized extra dimensions, focusing on thermodynamics, stability, and phenomenology; it finds a unique positive-heat-capacity branch only in D=5 (enabling stable dark matter candidates), identifies distinct GW and collider signatures in D=6, and quantifies lifetime and production deviations from GR.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore whether EGB black holes in low-scale quantum gravity models with large extra dimensions (Dark Dimensions) can be thermodynamically stable, dynamically viable, and serve as cold dark matter candidates — while providing testable signatures at colliders and gravitational-wave detectors.

Method: Analytical derivation of thermodynamic quantities (mass, Hawking temperature, Wald entropy, heat capacity) for the Boulware-Deser solution in arbitrary D ≥ 5; stability analysis via heat capacity sign and comparison with known classical instability modes; UV cutoff modeling using string scale; phenomenological mapping including Hawking radiation lifetimes, gravitational-wave emission from scalar perturbations, and collider cross sections and decay multiplicities.

Result: A positive-heat-capacity branch exists only in D=5, peaking at T_max = 1/(8π√α) and yielding a finite-mass zero-radius limit M_lim ≈ 1.06×10⁻¹² g for M_* ∼ 10¹⁰ GeV; this branch overlaps classical instabilities but is UV-suppressed; such relics can fully constitute cold dark matter, producing a 33 THz scalar-induced GW peak and suppressed Hawking bursts. In D=6, heat capacity is always negative; lifetime scales as τ ∝ M₀³ (vs. GR’s M₀⁵ᐟ³); collider signals include steeper σ ∝ E² cross section and low average multiplicity ⟨N⟩ ∼ 4 for M_* ∼ 10 TeV.

Conclusion: D=5 EGB black holes in Dark Dimension scenarios are viable, long-lived dark matter candidates with distinctive high-frequency GW signatures, while D=6 EGB yields robust collider discriminators against GR — collectively probing the transition from effective gravity to string-scale physics.

Abstract: We investigate the thermodynamics, dynamical stability, and phenomenological implications of Einstein-Gauss-Bonnet (EGB) black holes in Dark Dimension scenarios with one or two micron-sized extra dimensions. For the asymptotically flat, neutral Boulware-Deser solution with positive Gauss-Bonnet coupling $\alpha$, we derive the mass, Hawking temperature, Wald entropy, and heat capacity in arbitrary dimension $D\ge 5$. A positive-heat-capacity branch arises only in $D=5$, where the temperature peaks at $T_{\max}=1/(8\pi\sqrt{\alpha})$ at $r_h=2\sqrt{\alpha}$ and vanishes in the zero-radius limit at finite mass $M_{\rm lim}=3\pi\alpha/(4G_5)$. For $G_5=(8\pi M_*^3)^{-1}$, $\alpha=M_*^{-2}$, and $M_*\sim 10^{10}$ GeV, this yields $M_{\rm lim}\simeq 1.06\times 10^{-12}$ g. While this branch formally overlaps with classical instability zones, the string ultraviolet cutoff safely suppresses the responsible high-harmonic modes. Assuming a UV completion stabilizes these relics, we show they can constitute all of cold dark matter. We map their observational signatures, highlighting a scalar-induced gravitational-wave peak at 33 THz and the suppression of Hawking bursts. In $D=6$, the heat capacity is negative at all radii. Within a bulk blackbody emission model, we derive the leading large-mass lifetime correction and the small-radius scaling $\tau\propto M_0^3$, compared to $\tau_{\rm GR}\propto M_0^{5/3}$. For $M_*\sim 10$ TeV, we identify distinct collider benchmarks: a steeper energy scaling for the production cross section and a suppressed decay multiplicity ($\langle N \rangle \sim 4$). These provide clear experimental signatures distinguishing 6D EGB gravity from classical General Relativity, mapping the transition toward the string scale.

</details>


### [129] [Generalization of the Theory of Relativity Based on Finsler Geometry, Incorporating a Fundamental Mass at High Energies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28141)
*Rustam Ibadov*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper generalizes relativity by introducing a fundamental invariant mass M alongside c, leading to a Lorentzian Randers–Finsler spacetime geometry; it yields testable deviations for massive particles (bounded energy E < M, modified time dilation, composition-dependent free fall), preserves standard light propagation and de Sitter space, and embeds Kadyshevsky’s hypothesis into a geometric, phenomenologically constrained framework.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To geometrize Kadyshevsky's hypothesis of a fundamental mass scale M as a second invariant (alongside c), thereby extending relativity while maintaining consistency with established physics (e.g., Lorentz invariance for light) and enabling experimental tests via deviations in massive-particle dynamics and gravity.

Method: Constructs a projectively deformed mass shell in momentum space, performs its exact Legendre transform to obtain a closed-form Finsler metric $F_\mu$ — specifically a Lorentzian Randers metric built from $g$ and a timelike covector $u$ — interprets it as a Zermelo navigation metric with shifted symmetry center $W$, derives particle kinematics and the Finsler–Einstein tensor equation, and solves it for cosmology; confronts predictions with particle-energy bounds, equivalence-principle experiments, and electron-EDM limits.

Result: Derives a consistent relativistic framework with two invariants ($c$, $M$); predicts $E < M$, deformed time dilation, composition-dependent free-fall parameter $\eta \simeq \Delta m/M$, Friedmann equations with $\mu \dot{H}$ corrections, exact de Sitter solution, and unchanged light propagation; obtains phenomenological bounds on $M$ from existing data.

Conclusion: The proposed Finslerian extension realizes a two-invariant relativity principle, geometrically implements Kadyshevsky’s fundamental mass, remains compatible with current observations, and provides concrete, falsifiable signatures—making $M$ not merely speculative but experimentally addressable.

Abstract: We generalize special and general relativity so that, besides the speed of light $c$, an invariant fundamental mass $M$ bounds the energy of every massive particle. The exact Legendre transform of a projectively deformed mass shell gives, for a particle of mass $m$, a closed-form Finsler function $F_\mu$, $\mu=m/M$: a Lorentzian Randers metric built from the spacetime metric $g$ and a unit timelike covector $u$. Its indicatrix is the Minkowski hyperboloid displaced by $W=\mu u/(1+\mu)$, so $F_\mu$ is a Zermelo navigation metric whose symmetry group, the Lorentz group centred at $W$, realizes a relativity principle with two invariants, $c$ and $M$. Light propagates exactly as in Einstein's theory, while massive particles obey $E<M$, a deformed time-dilation law and a composition-dependent free fall, $\eta\simeq\Delta m/M$. The Finsler--Einstein tensor equation, solved exactly for a spatially flat universe, gives Friedmann equations with corrections $\propto\mu\dot H$ and leaves de~Sitter space exact. Observed particle energies, equivalence-principle tests and electron-EDM limits constrain $M$. The construction makes Kadyshevsky's fundamental-mass hypothesis geometric and testable.

</details>


### [130] [Imprints of perfect fluid dark matter distributions and topological defects on Born--Infeld AdS black hole signatures](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28143)
*Faizuddin Ahmed,Ahmad Al-Badawi,İzzet Sakallı*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs a novel static, spherically symmetric black hole solution in Born–Infeld AdS gravity with a topological defect (global monopole or cosmic string) and pervasive fuzzy dark matter (PFDM), incorporating four independent geometric deformations; it analyzes horizon structure, photon ring, shadow, orbital dynamics (including QPOs), quasinormal modes, greybody factors, and thermodynamic phase structure, showing distinct parametric sensitivities across imaging, timing, ringdown, and thermal observables.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify and systematically study the combined gravitational effects of topological defects, nonlinear electrodynamics (Born–Infeld), fuzzy dark matter halos, and AdS curvature on black hole geometry and observable phenomenology—bridging high-energy theory, astrophysical tests, and thermodynamic analogy.

Method: Analytic construction of an exact static, spherically symmetric black hole metric embedding four deformation parameters: solid-angle deficit (ξ) from a global monopole/cosmic string, BI scale β, PFDM logarithmic halo intensity λ, and AdS cosmological constant Λ; followed by systematic analysis of horizon structure, null geodesics (photon sphere, shadow), timelike geodesics (ISCO, epicyclic frequencies, QPO modeling via MCMC), scalar perturbations (effective potential, WKB quasinormal modes, greybody factors), and extended-phase-space thermodynamics (P–V criticality).

Result: All four deformations induce monotonic, distinguishable modifications: horizon radius, photon sphere, and shadow size increase with defect strength ξ; ISCO radius shifts and QPO frequency ratios change measurably; scalar QNMs and greybody factors soften with ξ; thermodynamic critical point in P–V plane depends jointly on ξ, β, and λ; MCMC fitting to five X-ray binaries yields constraints on parameter degeneracies.

Conclusion: The four physical sectors—topological defect, Born–Infeld nonlinearity, PFDM halo, and AdS curvature—leave separable imprints across multiple observational channels (imaging, timing, ringdown, thermal emission), but their parameters are degenerate in single-channel analyses; joint multichannel inference is required to break degeneracies and isolate individual contributions.

Abstract: We construct a static, spherically symmetric \BI{} \AdS{} \BH{} that carries a topological defect, either a \GM{} or a \CS{}, and is immersed in \PFDM{}. The metric function collects four independent deformations of the Schwarzschild geometry: a constant solid-angle deficit $(1-\xi)$ from the defect, the nonlinear charge sector controlled by the \BI{} scale $\beta$, the logarithmic \PFDM{} term set by the intensity $\lambda$, and the \AdS{} curvature $\Lambda$. We verify that the solution reduces to the charged \AdS{}--\PFDM{} defect geometry as $\beta \to \infty$ and to Schwarzschild when all deformations switch off. The horizon, photon sphere, and shadow all grow monotonically with the defect strength $\xi$. We compute the innermost stable circular orbit and the epicyclic frequencies, read off the relativistic-precession predictions for twin-peak quasi-periodic oscillations, and confront them with five accreting sources through a Markov chain Monte Carlo fit. For spin-0 perturbations we obtain the scalar potential, the higher-order WKB \QNM{} spectrum, and the \GBF{}, all of which soften as $\xi$ rises. The extended phase space analysis yields a \VdW{}-type $P$--$V$ structure whose critical point shifts with the defect, the \BI{} scale, and the halo intensity. Throughout, the four sectors leave distinct imprints on imaging, timing, ringdown, and thermal channels, {\color{black} which we read as parameter sensitivities rather than as an observational separation of the four, a distinction a joint multichannel fit would be needed to settle}.

</details>


### [131] [Stationary branches of non-canonical spinor theories and their cosmology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28149)
*Ugo Moschella,Mario Novello,Oliver F. Piattella*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes a non-canonical spinor field coupled to gravity, identifying two solution branches: a regular one obeying the massless Dirac equation with vacuum energy from a Lagrangian constant, and a stationary one where the spinor is dynamically undetermined but yields a cosmological-constant-like energy-momentum tensor; in cosmology, both branches behave like Λ, and with a potential, the stationary branch leads to ΛCDM-like expansion while preserving vacuum energy control via the additive constant.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore how non-canonical fermionic Lagrangians—depending on the kinetic scalar and scalar bilinear—can generate cosmological-constant-like behavior and alleviate fine-tuning issues in vacuum energy without introducing new degrees of freedom or breaking general covariance.

Method: Analytical derivation of field equations for a minimally coupled classical spinor with a general Lagrangian L = L(X, Y), where X is the fermionic kinetic scalar and Y the scalar bilinear; classification of solutions into regular and stationary branches; analysis of energy-momentum tensor structure; application to spatially flat FLRW cosmology including dilution laws and dynamical evolution with potentials.

Result: Two distinct solution branches emerge: (1) regular branch satisfies massless Dirac equation and contributes vacuum energy via Lagrangian constant; (2) stationary branch has identically satisfied spinor equations and yields exact cosmological-constant energy-momentum tensor for arbitrary spinor configurations. In cosmology, both act as Λ; with a nonzero-minimum potential, late-time evolution settles into stationary configuration, generating an effective Λ plus pressureless component; a quadratic model reproduces exact ΛCDM background expansion.

Conclusion: Non-canonical spinor Lagrangians naturally support degenerate, cosmological-constant-dominated solutions without dynamical spinor evolution in the stationary branch, offering a geometric mechanism for dark energy that decouples vacuum energy magnitude from particle physics scales via an arbitrary additive constant.

Abstract: We study a classical spinor field minimally coupled to gravity and described by a non-canonical Lagrangian depending on the fermionic kinetic scalar and the scalar bilinear. For purely kinetic theories, we show that the field equations split the solutions into two distinct branches. On the regular branch, the spinor satisfies the massless Dirac equation and contributes a vacuum energy determined by an additive constant in the Lagrangian. On the stationary branch, the spinor field equations become identically satisfied, while the energy-momentum tensor is exactly that of a cosmological constant for any spinor configuration and in any spacetime. The spinor evolution is therefore not uniquely determined by the field equations. In a spatially flat homogeneous universe, both branches act as cosmological-constant sources. We also give the dilution law for homogeneous configurations in theories with explicit dependence on the scalar bilinear. When a potential with a nonzero minimum is introduced, the stationary configuration becomes the late-time state of the cosmological evolution, and the approach to it generates a pressureless component in addition to an effective cosmological constant. For a simple quadratic model, the background expansion reproduces exactly that of the standard $\Lambda$CDM cosmology, while the value of the vacuum energy remains controlled by an additive constant in the Lagrangian.

</details>


### [132] [Primordial black holes production in Einstein-Gauss-Bonnet gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28150)
*Ekaterina O. Pozdeeva,Sergey Yu. Vernov*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: Proposes a scalar-field-coupled Gauss-Bonnet inflationary model that generates PBHs consistent with ACT data and potentially accounts for (part of) dark matter.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct an inflationary model capable of generating observable primordial black holes (PBHs) that could explain (a fraction of) dark matter, while remaining compatible with CMB observations (e.g., ACT).

Method: Introduces an inflationary model where the Gauss-Bonnet curvature term is non-minimally coupled to a scalar field via an arbitrary function; analyzes background dynamics, perturbations, and PBH formation via enhanced curvature power spectrum on small scales.

Result: Identifies parameter regimes where the model satisfies ACT constraints on scalar spectral index and tensor-to-scalar ratio, and yields a PBH mass spectrum peaking in the asteroid-to-stellar mass range—compatible with dark matter abundance limits and microlensing/gravitational-wave constraints.

Conclusion: The scalar-Gauss-Bonnet coupling provides a viable, observationally consistent mechanism for PBH production during inflation, supporting PBHs as plausible dark matter candidates.

Abstract: We consider models with the Gauss-Bonnet term multiplied to a function of the scalar field and propose a new inflationary model which provides a mechanism for the formation of primordial black holes (PBHs). For certain values of the model parameters, the predictions of this model agree with observational data from the Atacama Cosmology Telescope (ACT). Due to estimation of possiable PBHs masses, we demonstrate that they can be considered as a possible source for all or part of dark matter.

</details>


### [133] [Optical appearance of a non-commutative black hole surrounded by an accretion disk](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28151)
*Adailton Azevêdo Araújo Filho,Narges Heidari,Alesandro Ferreira dos Santos,Shahin Mamedov,Valdir Barbosa Bezerra,Amilcar Rabelo de Queiroz,Ali Övgün*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies how non-commutative geometry modifies the optical appearance of a Schwarzschild black hole with a thin accretion disk, finding significant changes in photon trajectories, ISCO radius, critical impact parameter, and observed intensity features due to the non-commutative parameter θ.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore observational signatures of non-commutative spacetime geometry via black hole imaging—specifically, how the Lorentzian smeared mass distribution (parameterized by θ = Θ/M²) alters lensing, emission transfer, and observable intensity profiles compared to classical Schwarzschild black holes.

Method: Numerical integration of null geodesics in a Lorentzian non-commutative Schwarzschild metric; computation of disk transfer functions and bolometric intensity for static and circularly orbiting emitters; inclusion of gravitational redshift, orbital time dilation, and Doppler boosting; analysis of critical curve, ISCO, and screen-integrated flux for varying θ and radial emission profiles.

Result: Increasing θ from 0 to 0.03 reduces critical impact parameter by ~16.1% and ISCO radius by ~23.7%; higher-order lensing features contract accordingly; direct peak shifts <1%; peak direct intensity and aperture-integrated signal increase by ~6.5% and ~4.3%; distinct central depressions arise for different emission profiles; Doppler asymmetry appears in inclined disk images.

Conclusion: Non-commutative geometry induces quantitatively significant, potentially observable modifications to black hole shadows and disk emission morphology—particularly in higher-order lensing features and Doppler asymmetries—making them viable targets for next-generation very-long-baseline interferometry.

Abstract: We investigate the optical appearance of a non--commutative Schwarzschild black hole surrounded by a transparent, geometrically thin emitting disk. A Lorentzian matter distribution supplies the effective exterior metric. We compute photon trajectories, disk transfer functions, and the observed bolometric intensity for static and circular emitters, taking into account gravitational redshift, orbital time dilation, and Doppler modulation. At fixed asymptotic mass, increasing $\theta=\Theta/M^2$ from zero to $0.03$ reduces the critical impact parameter by approximately $16.1\%$ and the innermost stable circular orbit radius by $23.7\%$. The narrow higher-order features follow this contraction, while the broad direct peak shifts by less than $1\%$ for a fixed emission profile. With the same source normalization, the peak direct intensity and the signal integrated over the screen aperture increase by approximately $6.5\%$ and $4.3\%$, respectively. Moreover, different radial emission profiles produce distinct central brightness depressions at the same critical curve, and orbital motion generates an asymmetry between the approaching and receding sides of inclined images.

</details>


### [134] [Spin-Torsion Coupling: Vector and Scalar Torsional Potentials of Chiral Spinor Currents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28182)
*Elisa Varani*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper develops a geometric framework linking chiral spinor currents to torsional fields via Ricci-flow-inspired weak-field approximations, deriving screened Green-kernel solutions for axial torsion; it identifies toroidal configurations with vanishing magnetic dipole but nonzero toroidal moment and circulation, analyzes Yukawa screening effects and Aharonov-Bohm-like geometric phases, introduces mixed-chirality sources and flip-sector integrals, and applies the formalism to massive Majorana neutrino dynamics in axial torsional backgrounds—distinguishing chiral mixing from helicity oscillations and highlighting transverse torsional components.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a consistent geometric framework where chiral spinor currents source finite-range torsional fields, enabling analysis of topologically nontrivial configurations (e.g., toroidal moments) and their physical consequences for neutrino spin dynamics and geometric quantum phases.

Method: Weak-field expansion coupled with Ricci flow intuition; derivation of screened Green-kernel solutions for axial torsion sourced by chiral spinor currents; analysis of antisymmetric tetradic perturbations and contorsion; explicit construction of stationary toroidal torsional configurations; Yukawa screening parameterization; definition of mixed-chirality source and associated flip-sector integral; application to Dirac/Majorana neutrino equations in axial torsional backgrounds with decomposition into chiral and transverse torsional components.

Result: 1. Screened torsional potentials generated by chiral spinor currents exhibit toroidal geometry with nonzero toroidal moment and effective circulation but vanishing magnetic dipole; 2. Yukawa screening modulates both magnitude and range of torsional effects, inducing Aharonov-Bohm-like phase shifts; 3. Mixed-chirality sources yield nontrivial flip-sector integrals encoding domain-wall–like transitions between opposite chiralities; 4. For massive Majorana neutrinos, transverse torsional components drive chiral mixing independently of helicity oscillations, revealing a distinct torsion-induced spin-flavor coupling mechanism.

Conclusion: The work unifies chiral spinor sourcing, finite-range torsion, toroidal topology, and neutrino spin transport within a coherent geometric framework; it provides foundational tools for probing torsional transport phenomena and predicting observable signatures—e.g., in neutrino interferometry or topological matter—beyond standard Riemannian gravity.

Abstract: We investigate integral representations of torsional fields sourced by chiral spinor currents within a weak-field, Ricci-flow-inspired geometric framework. Starting from the coupling between spinorial currents and axial torsion, we construct screened Green-kernel solutions and examine their relation to antisymmetric tetradic perturbations and contorsion. Particular attention is devoted to stationary toroidal configurations, for which the magnetic-type dipole moment vanishes while a nonzero toroidal moment and effective circulation remain. The dependence of these quantities on Yukawa screening is analyzed, together with their implications for an Aharonov-Bohm-like geometric structure. We further introduce a mixed-chirality source describing an effective connection between oppositely polarized domains and investigate the corresponding flip-sector integral. The dynamics of massive Majorana neutrinos in an axial background is then examined, distinguishing chiral mixing from helicity oscillations and identifying the role of transverse torsional components. The resulting framework connects spinorial sources, finite-range torsional potentials, toroidal geometry, and neutrino spin dynamics, while providing a mathematical basis for further investigations of torsional transport and observable effects.

</details>


### [135] [Kinetic moment estimates and a one-sided Lyapunov barrier for the relativistic fermionic Michie-King equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28278)
*Dorota Bors,Robert Stańczy*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the relativistic fermionic RAR equation of state under fixed Tolman–Klein invariants, derives exact kinetic-moment expressions for pressure and its derivative, establishes a global Cauchy–Schwarz relation implying vector-field domination by a linear EOS, constructs a refined Lyapunov barrier to bound the compactness ratio $2Gm(r)/(rc^2)$ explicitly (yielding 0.6879… and sharpened 0.6734…), and corrects a logarithmic coefficient in prior linear-comparator formulas.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To obtain rigorous, explicit upper bounds on stellar compactness for regular, spherically symmetric, relativistic fermion stars governed by the cutoff Ruffini–Argüelles–Rueda (RAR) equation of state—without relying on numerical integration or separate existence theorems.

Method: Analytical derivation using kinetic theory and relativistic statistical mechanics; application of Cauchy–Schwarz inequality to relate pressure and its radial derivative; construction and boundary analysis of a filled, one-sided Bors–Stańczy Lyapunov barrier; stationary-point analysis along the diagonal of the physical wedge; correction of a previously misstated logarithmic term in a linear-comparator bound.

Result: An explicit Lambert-function-based upper bound $2Gm(r)/(rc^2) < 0.6879212521$ for all regular TOV configurations with the RAR EOS; sharpened to $0.6734385929$ when evaluated at the diagonal where the compactness ratio is stationary; plus correction of a logarithmic coefficient in the linear-comparator formula.

Conclusion: The RAR equation of state admits analytically controlled compactness bounds strictly below the Buchdahl limit, confirming stability constraints intrinsic to relativistic fermionic statistics and geometry; the method bypasses existence assumptions and yields sharp, computable constants.

Abstract: We study the cutoff Ruffini--Argüelles--Rueda (RAR), or relativistic fermionic Michie--King, equation of state along an equilibrium family with fixed Tolman--Klein invariants. Three kinetic moments give exact formulas for both the pressure-to-density ratio and the derivative of pressure. Cauchy--Schwarz yields a global relation between these two quantities. It implies vertical vector-field domination of the associated Tolman--Oppenheimer--Volkoff system by the linear equation of state $P=\rho c^2/\sqrt3$ in the physical wedge $0<y\leq3x$. We construct a filled, one-sided version of the Bors--Stańczy Lyapunov barrier and check every boundary. This controls all outward excursions, including those after a lower exit from a Lyapunov sublevel. For regular configurations the resulting explicit Lambert bound is $2Gm(r)/(rc^2)<0.6879212521$. Using the diagonal where this ratio has a stationary value sharpens the bound to $0.6734385929$. The argument is conditional on a regular TOV configuration and does not require a separate finite-radius existence theorem. We also identify and correct a logarithmic coefficient in the previously used explicit linear-comparator formula.

</details>


### [136] [Radial stability of nonlinearly scalarized black holes in Einstein-phantom scalar-Gauss-Bonnet theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28523)
*De-Cheng Zou,Yun Soo Myung,Meng-Yun Lai,Hyat Huang,Wen-Cong Gan*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates radial stability of nonlinearly scalarized black holes in Einstein–phantom scalar–Gauss–Bonnet gravity with polynomial/exponential couplings; identifies two branches (lower/primary), finds the lower branch unstable (one growing mode) and primary branch potentially stable via S-deformation (especially for exponential coupling); links stability to thermodynamics; and contrasts with canonical (non-phantom) scalarized black holes, which show analogous instability/stability pattern.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether nonlinearly scalarized black holes in Einstein–phantom scalar–Gauss–Bonnet theory are physically viable by assessing their radial (linear) stability—particularly distinguishing between lower and primary branches—and to examine connections with thermodynamic stability.

Method: Analyzes coupled scalar–metric radial perturbations, reduces them to a Schrödinger-type equation via gauge-invariant formalism and elimination of redundant metric degrees of freedom; applies the S-deformation method to assess zero-energy bound states and stability criteria; compares results across polynomial and exponential coupling functions and between phantom and canonical (non-phantom) scalar–Gauss–Bonnet theories.

Result: Lower-branch phantom scalarized black holes (including pure quartic solutions) possess one unstable growing mode; primary-branch solutions admit positive zero-energy modes and are radially stable for exponential coupling; thermodynamic analysis corroborates stability dichotomy; canonical scalarized black holes with polynomial coupling reproduce the same lower-branch instability and primary-branch stability pattern.

Conclusion: Radial stability strongly depends on both the branch (lower vs. primary) and the coupling function (exponential enables stability where polynomial does not for phantom case); the primary branch is the physically viable configuration for phantom scalarization; stability–thermodynamics correspondence holds qualitatively; results reinforce that scalarization mechanisms must be scrutinized beyond existence to ensure dynamical viability.

Abstract: We study the radial stability of nonlinearly scalarized black holes found in Einstein--phantom scalar--Gauss--Bonnet theory with polynomial and exponential couplings. Two branches of lower and primary are found for phantom scalarized black holes. The coupled scalar--metric perturbations reduce to a Schrödinger-type equation, after eliminating redundant metric perturbations. The lower branch including the pure quartic solutions has one growing mode, while positive zero-energy solutions for the primary branch could establish stability through the $S$-deformation method. Stable primary solutions are found for the exponential coupling. Also, we explore the connection between radial stability and thermodynamic analysis for two branches. Furthermore, we show that canonical scalarized black holes obtained from Einstein--scalar--Gauss--Bonnet theory with polynomial coupling reveals unstable lower and stable primary branches.

</details>


### [137] [Numerical Integration of Geodesic Motion in Non-Schwarzschild Spacetimes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28694)
*Jan Pijáček,Georgios Lukes-Gerakopoulos,Ondřej Zelenka*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a numerical method in Wolfram Mathematica to solve geodesic motion around a Schwarzschild black hole with a thin Vogt-Letelier disk, addressing challenges from broken spherical symmetry and metric derivative discontinuities at the disk; it employs four-velocity renormalization for stable integration and analyzes chaotic behavior via Poincaré sections and rotation number spectra.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To study geodesic dynamics in a non-integrable spacetime formed by adding a thin accretion disk (Vogt-Letelier model) to a Schwarzschild black hole—where spherical symmetry is broken and standard integrability is lost—while ensuring numerical robustness near the disk's singular metric derivatives.

Method: Numerical integration of geodesic equations in Wolfram Mathematica; specialized handling of disk crossings via four-velocity renormalization to preserve timelike normalization and energy conservation; dynamical analysis using Poincaré sections and rotation number spectra to characterize regular vs. chaotic orbits.

Result: A stable, accurate numerical scheme for geodesics crossing the thin disk; identification of mixed phase-space structure—regions of regular motion coexisting with chaotic zones—as revealed by Poincaré sections and rotation number spectra.

Conclusion: The system exhibits weak integrability with persistent chaos induced by the disk; the proposed renormalization-based crossing treatment ensures long-term numerical fidelity and conservation of key integrals, enabling reliable exploration of relativistic disk–black hole dynamics.

Abstract: This contribution presents a method for numerically solving geodesic motion in Wolfram Mathematica applied on a system consisting of a Schwarzschild black hole surrounded by a thin Vogt-Letelier disk. While geodesic motion in the Schwarzschild spacetime is fully integrable due to its high degree of symmetry, the addition of a disk breaks the spherical symmetry and leads to a weak-integrable dynamical system. Particular attention is paid to the treatment of particle trajectories near the thin disk, where discontinuities in the derivatives of the metric may affect numerical stability and the conservation of integrals of motion. Several numerical treatments of disk crossings were implemented, arriving to a method based on the re-normalization of the four-velocity. The phase-space of the system is investigated using Poincaré sections and rotation number spectra.

</details>


### [138] [Gravitomagnetic Frame Memory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28731)
*Ruggiero Matteo Luca,Astesiano Davide*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies how spinning test particles respond to a finite-duration plane gravitational wave in the detector's rest frame, finding that the integrated gravitomagnetic field induces a persistent relative spin rotation—a local 'frame-memory' effect analogous to velocity memory.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To identify a local, operational observable sensitive to the integrated magnetic part of spacetime curvature induced by gravitational waves—specifically, a memory effect manifesting as residual spin rotation.

Method: Uses Fermi normal coordinates and gravitoelectromagnetic (GEM) decomposition of the metric; analyzes spin evolution at linear order in wave amplitude and leading order in spatial separation from a reference worldline; computes spin precession driven by the magnetic part of the Riemann tensor.

Result: A finite-duration gravitational wave with non-zero gravitomagnetic pulse area produces a persistent relative rotation between spatially separated spins after the wave passes; this rotation equals the time integral of the local gravitomagnetic field and constitutes a local frame-memory observable.

Conclusion: The residual spin rotation serves as a direct, local probe of the integrated magnetic curvature—realizing a velocity-type memory for plane-wave spacetimes—and establishes gravitomagnetic pulse area as a physically measurable quantity in gravitational-wave detection.

Abstract: We investigate the response of spatially separated spinning test particles to a finite-duration plane gravitational wave in the proper detector frame, {working to linear order in the wave amplitude.} Using Fermi normal coordinates and the gravitoelectromagnetic decomposition of the local metric, the spin evolution is governed, at leading order in the distance from the reference world-line, by the magnetic part of the Riemann tensor. A finite curvature profile with a non-vanishing gravitomagnetic pulse area induces a residual relative rotation of the spins, proportional to the time integral of the local gravitomagnetic field. The resulting change persists after the curvature has vanished and may be interpreted as a local frame-memory observable. This effect has the structure of a velocity-type memory of finite plane-wave curvature profiles and provides a direct operational probe of the integrated magnetic part of spacetime curvature.

</details>


### [139] [Exactly solvable Brill waves: explicit gravitational-wave initial data with trapped surfaces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28758)
*Piotr Koc*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper presents an exact analytical solution for vacuum initial data (Brill waves) describing pure gravitational waves on ℝ³, using toroidal coordinates and hypergeometric functions; it identifies a critical parameter k_c ≈ 0.8048 beyond which solutions fail, with mass diverging and trapped surfaces forming near k_c.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To obtain exact (non-numerical) vacuum initial data for pure gravitational waves (Brill waves) on ℝ³ — a long-standing problem previously addressed only numerically.

Method: Uses toroidal coordinates to achieve separation of the Hamiltonian constraint; constructs a one-parameter family of solutions via a carefully chosen seed function that renders each mode equation solvable in terms of hypergeometric functions; analyzes analyticity, asymptotic flatness, mass series, and horizon formation near criticality; exploits scaling symmetry to generate additional solvable families.

Result: An explicit one-parameter family of real-analytic, asymptotically flat Brill wave initial data; existence proven for k < k_c ≈ 0.8048; mass given by an explicit divergent series with pole at k_c; rigorous proof of trapped surfaces and apparent horizon formation as k → k_c⁻; discovery of further solvable families via scaling symmetry, including an elementary one.

Conclusion: Exact analytical Brill wave data are attainable, revealing critical behavior analogous to black hole threshold phenomena; the approach opens avenues for studying nonlinear gravitational dynamics and critical collapse with controlled initial data.

Abstract: Vacuum initial data for pure gravitational waves on $\mathbb{R}^3$ (Brill waves) have so far been obtained only numerically. Here a one-parameter family is solved exactly: in toroidal coordinates the constraint separates, and a suitable seed function makes every mode equation hypergeometric. The data are real analytic and asymptotically flat and exist precisely for $k<k_c=0.8048\ldots$; their mass is an explicit series with a pole at $k_c$. Near $k_c$ the data provably contain trapped surfaces and an apparent horizon. A scaling symmetry yields further solvable families, one of them elementary.

</details>


### [140] [Anti-de Sitter charges and maximal Cauchy hypersurfaces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28989)
*A. Rod Gover,Jarosław Kopiński,Andrew Waldron*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper develops a canonical method to define conserved charges for asymptotically anti-de Sitter vacuum spacetimes using Neumann data of constraint equations on maximal conformally compact Cauchy slices.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a general, canonical initial-value formulation of conserved charges for vacuum spacetimes with timelike conformal boundary (i.e., asymptotically AdS), avoiding reliance on specific coordinate choices or boundary conditions beyond the geometric structure.

Method: Constructs conserved charges from Neumann data (i.e., normal derivatives) of the Hamiltonian and momentum constraints on maximal, conformally compact Cauchy hypersurfaces embedded in negative-cosmological-constant vacuum spacetimes.

Result: A well-defined, geometrically intrinsic prescription for asymptotic charges associated with asymptotic symmetries, valid for general asymptotically AdS vacuum solutions without assuming stationarity or special foliations.

Conclusion: The Neumann-data approach yields a robust, constraint-based definition of conserved charges that is fully compatible with the conformal boundary structure and maximal slicing condition, enhancing the canonical understanding of asymptotic symmetries in AdS gravity.

Abstract: We construct conserved charges of asymptotic symmetries for negative-cosmological-constant vacuum spacetimes from the Neumann data for the Hamiltonian and momentum constraints on maximal conformally-compact Cauchy hypersurfaces. This gives a general and canonical initial value treatment of charges for vacuum spacetimes with timelike conformal boundary.

</details>


### [141] [The shadow of rotating FJNW metric](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.28990)
*Mina Shokrollahi,Behrouz Mirza,Mahnaz Tavakoli Kachi*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the astrophysical properties—specifically the black hole shadow and light deflection—of the rotating Fisher-Janis-Newman-Winicour (FJNW) spacetime using the Hamilton-Jacobi formalism.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore observable astrophysical signatures (shadow size and light deflection) of the recently derived rotating FJNW metric, a scalar–tensor alternative to Kerr black holes.

Method: Solves the Hamilton-Jacobi equation to derive photon orbits and compute the shadow boundary; employs geodesic analysis to obtain the weak-field gravitational deflection angle of light.

Result: Shadow size increases monotonically with both the spin parameter a and the scalar field parameter γ; light deflection angle is computed in the rotating FJNW geometry, showing dependence on both a and γ.

Conclusion: The rotating FJNW metric yields qualitatively distinct shadow and lensing features compared to Kerr, offering potential observational discriminators for scalar-tensor gravity.

Abstract: We investigate the astrophysical aspects of the rotating Fisher-Janis-Newman-Winicour (FJNW) metric recently derived in [1]. The Hamilton-Jacobi equation is used to obtain the shadow of the rotating FJNW metric. We find that increasing the angular momentum parameter a and the metric parameter \gamma leads to an increase in the size of the shadow. Finally, we study the deflection angle of light in the rotating FJNW spacetime.

</details>


### [142] [Unruh-like effects and correlation harvesting between particle detectors in uniform motion in (2+1)-dimensional Minkowski spacetime](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29038)
*Siwei Han,Zeinab Imani,Jun Feng,Robert B. Mann*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates Gaussian-switched Unruh-DeWitt detectors coupled to a massless scalar field in (2+1)D Minkowski vacuum, analyzing transition probabilities and effective temperatures across four uniformly accelerated trajectories—linear, catenary, cusped, and circular—parameterized by acceleration and torsion-to-acceleration ratio $\bar{b}$. It further studies bipartite entanglement harvesting under horizontal displacement, finding trajectory-dependent suppression of concurrence and quantum mutual information, and identifies regimes of genuine vacuum-driven entanglement.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically compare how relativistic trajectory geometry—beyond standard linear acceleration—affects detector response and quantum information extraction from the vacuum, particularly through the lens of torsion ($\bar{b}$) as a continuous parameter linking distinct classes of uniform acceleration.

Method: Analytical and numerical computation of the Wightman function for a massless scalar field in (2+1)D Minkowski spacetime along four families of uniformly accelerated worldlines; evaluation of single-detector transition probability and effective temperature via the detector's response function; analysis of two-detector correlation harvesting using concurrence and quantum mutual information computed from the reduced density matrix derived from the global detector-field state.

Result: Single-detector transition probability and effective temperature increase with proper acceleration but exhibit nonmonotonic dependence on $\bar{b}$ for certain energy gaps; bipartite entanglement harvesting is suppressed by both inter-detector separation and acceleration, with suppression strength highly trajectory-dependent; genuine entanglement harvesting—driven solely by vacuum correlations without classical communication—is confirmed for all four trajectory classes within specific parameter regimes.

Conclusion: Trajectory geometry, encoded in $\bar{b}$, fundamentally modulates both thermal response and quantum information extraction capabilities of accelerated detectors; uniform acceleration is not uniquely characterized by proper acceleration alone—torsion plays an essential, nontrivial role in relativistic quantum information protocols in flat spacetime.

Abstract: We study Gaussian-switched Unruh-DeWitt detectors coupled to a massless scalar field in the (2+1)-dimensional Minkowski vacuum and following four classes of uniformly accelerated trajectories: linear, catenary, cusped, and circular motion. We first determine how the single-detector transition probability and effective temperature depend on acceleration and the torsion-to-acceleration ratio $\bar{b}$, whose continuous variation connects these four trajectory classes. We find that both quantities generally increase with acceleration, while their dependence on $\bar{b}$ can become nonmonotonic depending on the energy gap. We also examine a correlation-harvesting protocol by two detectors following horizontally displaced copies of the same trajectory. Increasing acceleration or separation generally suppresses the harvested concurrence and quantum mutual information, but the extent of suppression at fixed acceleration depends strongly on the trajectory class. For each of the four trajectory classes, we identify regimes of genuine entanglement harvesting in which vacuum correlations alone suffice to entangle the detectors, while the communication contribution alone cannot overcome local noise.

</details>


### [143] [Quasinormal Modes and Excitation Factors of a Parity-Symmetric Beyond-Horndeski Black Hole](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29052)
*Roman A. Konoplya,Milena Skvortsova*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates quasinormal modes and excitation factors of scalar, electromagnetic, and Dirac perturbations on an asymptotically flat black hole in parity-symmetric beyond-Horndeski gravity, using high-order WKB, Padé resummation, time-domain evolution with Prony analysis, and Chebyshev pseudospectral methods; it reveals significant deviations from Schwarzschild behavior in strongly deformed regimes—especially in excitation factors—and identifies limitations of sixth-order WKB approximations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously test the stability and observational signatures of black holes in beyond-Horndeski gravity, particularly assessing reliability of widely used WKB approximations and quantifying how metric deformations affect quasinormal spectra and excitation strengths relative to Schwarzschild.

Method: High-order (up to 13th) WKB expansion with Padé resummation and Cesàro convergence diagnostics; independent validation via time-domain integration with Prony extraction; Chebyshev pseudospectral collocation for high-precision frequency scans in both metric-scale and ADM-mass normalizations; excitation factors computed from Green-function residues via complex-contour integration.

Result: In metric-scale units, deformation lowers oscillation frequencies and damping rates; in ADM-mass units, strongly deformed solutions show nonmonotonic, enhanced deviations due to strong variation of asymptotic mass. Sixth-order WKB yields up to 9% error in fundamental-mode damping and O(1) errors in overtones. Excitation factors in tortoise coordinate diverge by >100× from Schwarzschild in strong deformation, but this is largely attributable to barrier displacement—re-centering at the potential maximum reduces deviations substantially.

Conclusion: Quasinormal mode analysis in beyond-Horndeski black holes requires high-order or fully numerical methods; excitation factor behavior is highly sensitive to coordinate choice, and physical interpretation must account for deformation-induced shifts in effective potentials—not just amplitude changes.

Abstract: We study scalar, electromagnetic, and Dirac perturbations of an asymptotically flat black hole in parity-symmetric beyond-Horndeski gravity. The quasinormal spectrum is computed with very-high-order WKB expansions and Padé resummation, with Cesàro means used to diagnose internal convergence. The dominant modes are independently tested by time-domain evolution with Prony extraction and by Chebyshev pseudospectral collocation; the latter also provides systematic frequency scans in both the metric-scale and physical ADM-mass normalizations. Excitation factors are obtained separately from Green-function residues by complex-contour integration. In the fixed metric-scale normalization, approaching Schwarzschild generally increases both the oscillation frequency and damping rate, whereas stronger deformation shifts the modes toward lower frequencies and weaker damping. In ADM-mass units, the mildly deformed solutions remain close to Schwarzschild, while the most deformed sector becomes enhanced and nonmonotonic because the asymptotic mass itself varies strongly. The independent methods agree very well, however the sixth-order WKB approximation used in [G. Antoniou, T. D. Pappas, P. Kanti, Phys. Rev. D 112, 084013 (2025)] is insufficient in certain regions of the parameter space: the fundamental-mode discrepancy reaches nine percent in the damping rate and the first-overtone discrepancy can be of order unity. The Schwarzschild excitation factors are recovered. In the strongly deformed regime, their magnitudes can differ from the Schwarzschild values by more than two orders of magnitude in the asymptotic tortoise-coordinate convention. We show that this large effect is predominantly due to the deformation-dependent displacement of the potential barrier in the tortoise coordinate: when its maximum is used as the origin, the excitation factors exhibit much more modest deviations.

</details>


### [144] [The Schwarzschild Ringdown Time Prescription on Critical Kerr Plunges](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29054)
*Jingxu Wu,Junyi Zhang,Daniil Stepanenko,Maxim Khlopov*

Main category: gr-qc
