TL;DR: This paper analyzes quasinormal-mode convergence in Schwarzschild and Kerr black hole plunges, linking constant-amplitude QNM onset to advanced time at the singularity in Schwarzschild case and deriving a parameter-free hierarchy of onset times for prograde critical Kerr plunges using inner horizon behavior and zero-crossing of the radial potential.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the origin and timing of constant-amplitude quasinormal-mode (QNM) convergence in black hole mergers, particularly how it emerges from the spacetime geometry near singularities and horizons in both Schwarzschild and rapidly spinning Kerr spacetimes.

Method: Analyzes ingoing Eddington–Finkelstein time $v$ and Kruskal-like coordinate $u_K$ near the inner horizon of Kerr black holes; uses the condition $P(r_0)=0$ (where $P$ is the radial potential) to match $v$ and $-u_K$, yielding a physically grounded, parameter-free hierarchy of onset times $(u_H, u_{r_0}, u_C)$; validates consistency with Schwarzschild limit ($a \to 0$) and computes predictions for $a/M = 0.9$.

Result: Derives a universal onset hierarchy $(u_H, u_{r_0}, u_C)$ that recovers the known Schwarzschild offset $9.435M$ in the nonspinning limit and predicts two-stage residual decay and frequency locking at $6.85M$, $11.35M$, and $13.35M$ for an $a/M = 0.9$ eccentric plunge.

Conclusion: Constant-amplitude QNM convergence originates from causal structure near the inner horizon and interior potential zero in Kerr spacetime; the onset is governed by a robust, parameter-free hierarchy tied to fundamental null coordinates and radial potential geometry—extending and generalizing prior Schwarzschild-based understanding to astrophysically relevant high-spin regimes.

Abstract: For a Schwarzschild plunge, constant-amplitude quasinormal-mode convergence has been tied to the source's advanced time at $r=0$, lying $9.435M$ after the $(2,2)$ strain peak. On real prograde critical Kerr plunges, $P(r_-)<0$ forces the ingoing time $v$ to diverge logarithmically at the inner horizon while $u_K$ stays finite. Matching $v$ to $-u_K$ at the unique interior zero $P(r_0)=0$ yields a parameter-free onset hierarchy $(u_{\rm H},u_{r_0},u_{\rm C})$ that recovers $9.435M$ as $a\to 0$ and predicts two-stage residual decay and frequency locking at $(6.85M,11.35M,13.35M)$ on an $a/M=0.9$ eccentric plunge.

</details>


### [145] [QTG minimons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29109)
*Valeri P. Frolov*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies horizonless, static, spherically symmetric solutions ('QTG minimons') in quasitopological gravity coupled to 2D conformal matter (Polyakov action); it derives a constrained first-order system, finds de Sitter-like cores in Hayward- and Born–Infeld-type models, shows quantum effects reduce asymptotic mass and enhance central blueshift near criticality, and proposes an AdS₂×S^{D−2} throat geometry for extremal limiting configurations ('QTG extremons').


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore regular, horizonless alternatives to black holes in quasitopological gravity—specifically the small-mass branch below the black hole threshold—and to assess how vacuum polarization (via 2D conformal matter) affects their structure, stability, and extremal limits.

Method: Derives a constrained first-order system from the spherically reduced QTG action coupled to the Polyakov action; constructs central series expansions in D ≥ 4; numerically analyzes 4D solutions for Hayward-type and Born–Infeld-type characteristic functions h(p); examines near-critical behavior and asymptotics; assumes smooth constant-radius limit in proper distance to infer throat geometry.

Result: Both Hayward- and Born–Infeld-type QTG minimons possess regular de Sitter-like cores; near criticality, asymptotic mass decreases with quantum coupling while central blueshift diverges even for weak coupling; vacuum polarization induces nonuniform perturbations; limiting configurations admit an AdS₂×S^{D−2} near-throat geometry.

Conclusion: Vacuum polarization qualitatively modifies QTG minimons near the black hole threshold, supporting the existence of smooth, horizonless extremal limits ('QTG extremons') with universal AdS₂×S^{D−2} throats—suggesting a possible UV-complete alternative to extremal black holes.

Abstract: We study static, spherically symmetric, regular horizonless solutions of quasitopological gravity (QTG), which we call QTG minimons. These configurations form the small-mass branch below the threshold for regular black holes. We include vacuum polarization by coupling the spherically reduced QTG action to the Polyakov action for two-dimensional conformal matter. For an arbitrary characteristic function $h(p)$, we derive a constrained first-order system in the static, asymptotically empty state. We obtain central expansions in $D\ge4$ and analyze four-dimensional solutions in the Hayward-type and Born--Infeld-type models. Both models retain a de Sitter-like core. Along the near-critical branch, the asymptotic mass decreases with the quantum coupling, while the central blueshift can become large even for a small coupling. The numerical results indicate that vacuum polarization is a nonuniform perturbation near the critical limit. Assuming a smooth constant-radius limit in proper distance, we derive a candidate $\mathrm{AdS}_2\times S^{D-2}$ throat geometry for the limiting configurations, which we call QTG extremons.

</details>


### [146] [Quantum coherence and entanglement of mixed states in relativistic settings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29322)
*Xing-Le Zhao,Zhen Yang,Shu-Min Wu*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies how quantum entanglement and coherence behave in two Unruh-DeWitt detectors—one inertial, one uniformly accelerated—starting from a general two-qubit X state (mixed or pure). Using perturbation theory, it finds entanglement sudden death at finite acceleration, while coherence (both $l_1$ norm and relative entropy) persists longer but vanishes only asymptotically as acceleration → ∞. Initial mixedness, detector energy gap, and interaction time significantly affect resource degradation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the distinct roles of entanglement and coherence under relativistic conditions—specifically, how realistic mixed initial states (vs. idealized pure states) influence quantum resource degradation due to acceleration-induced decoherence in Unruh-DeWitt detector models.

Method: Perturbative calculation of the reduced density matrix of two-qubit Unruh-DeWitt detectors: one inertial (uncoupled), one uniformly accelerated and coupled to a Minkowski vacuum scalar field; evaluation of concurrence, $l_1$ norm of coherence, and relative entropy of coherence for initial X states.

Result: Entanglement exhibits sudden death at finite acceleration (threshold increases with initial entanglement); both coherence measures remain nonzero beyond that threshold and decay asymptotically to zero as acceleration → ∞; coherence is more robust than entanglement; dependence on detector energy gap and interaction time is quantified.

Conclusion: Coherence is qualitatively more persistent than entanglement under uniform acceleration in this mixed-state detector model; initial mixedness is not merely noise but a physically relevant parameter in relativistic quantum information processing.

Abstract: States prepared in laboratory experiments are generally mixed because of imperfect preparation and residual coupling to the environment, whereas pure initial states represent an idealized limit. Motivated by this distinction, we investigate quantum entanglement and coherence in a pair of Unruh-DeWitt detectors initially prepared in a general two-qubit X state. One detector remains inertial and uncoupled, while the other undergoes uniform acceleration and interacts locally with a massless scalar field in the Minkowski vacuum. We obtain the reduced detector state within a perturbative treatment and evaluate its concurrence, $l_1$ norm of coherence, and relative entropy of coherence. For the initial-state families considered, the resulting expressions exhibit entanglement sudden death at finite acceleration, with larger initial entanglement associated with a higher threshold. Both coherence measures remain nonzero beyond this threshold and approach zero in the formal infinite-acceleration limit of these expressions. We also examine the dependence of these resources on the detector energy gap and interaction duration. The results distinguish the persistence of coherence from that of entanglement in the mixed-state detector model and identify initial mixedness as a relevant ingredient in relativistic quantum information.

</details>


### [147] [Regge calculus for $f(R)$ gravity with torsion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29447)
*Xiao-Kan Guo*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper formulates f(R) gravity with spacetime torsion in Regge calculus using D'Adda's coordinate-based simplicial approach, showing that the axial torsion is encoded entirely by a Kalb–Ramond 2-form—dual to an axion scalar—alongside the standard scalaron; the resulting discrete theory is pure Regge gravity coupled to two scalars, with no need for geometric torsion on the lattice, and the discrete Friedmann–Raychaudhuri system correctly reproduces continuum cosmology to first order in time step.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To consistently incorporate spacetime torsion into discrete f(R) gravity without relying on an ad hoc geometric definition of torsion on the Regge lattice, while preserving physical equivalence with the continuum theory.

Method: Uses D'Adda's coordinate formulation of simplicial gravity to construct a torsional f(R) action in Regge calculus; identifies axial torsion with a massless Kalb–Ramond 2-form, dualized to an axion scalar in 4D; derives the full discrete action and equations of motion; computes kinetic and curvature coefficients for a regular S³ triangulation; verifies continuum limit and tests cosmological evolution on a homogeneous isotropic lattice.

Result: The discretized theory reduces to Regge gravity coupled to two scalars (scalaron and axion); the discrete Friedmann–Raychaudhuri system is derived exactly; for a regular S³ triangulation, kinetic and curvature coefficients are computed explicitly; the discrete dynamics reproduce the closed continuum FLRW solution at first order in the time step.

Conclusion: Torsional f(R) gravity can be fully captured on the Regge lattice by scalar fields alone—no discrete differential-geometric torsion structure is required—making this a rare case where continuum torsional physics admits a minimal, purely algebraic lattice description.

Abstract: We formulate $f(R)$ gravity with spacetime torsion in Regge calculus, but without an \emph{ad hoc} geometric definition of torsion. Working in D'Adda's formulation of simplicial gravity with coordinates, we show that the axial sector of the torsion is carried by a massless Kalb--Ramond two-form, which in four dimensions is dual to a single scalar on the dual vertices. The discretized theory is therefore pure Regge gravity coupled to two scalar fields, the scalaron and the axion, without a geometric definition of torsion on the Regge lattice. We derive the lattice action and the complete set of discrete equations of motion, and we verify the continuum limit. For a homogeneous and isotropic lattice we obtain the discrete Friedmann--Raychaudhuri system, compute the exact kinetic and curvature coefficients for a regular triangulation of $\mathbb S^3$, and show that the discrete equations reproduce the continuum closed solution at first order in the evolution time step. The results therefore present a very special case in which the torsional effects are completely described by scalar fields on the Regge lattice and there is no need of a full discrete differential calculus.

</details>


### [148] [Birefringent Imaging and Interferometry with Periodic Orbits and Accretion in Einstein-nonlinear-electrodynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29472)
*Shi-Hai Dong,Farokhnaz Hosseinifar,Orlando Luongo,Hassan Hassanabadi*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies electromagnetic and gravitational phenomena around a magnetically charged black hole in Einstein-nonlinear electrodynamics (using the Euler-Heisenberg-type model), analyzing birefringent imaging, interferometric visibility, neutral periodic geodesics, and thin-disk radiation; it identifies two distinct photon metrics leading to separate light rings and visibility modes, classifies bound orbits via rational orbital frequencies for EMRI modeling, and computes polarization-resolved thermal spectra from a stationary thin disk — with magnetic charge driving dominant effects and nonlinear corrections appearing as subdominant deviations from Maxwell theory.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore how magnetic charge and nonlinear electrodynamics modify fundamental black hole observables—especially photon propagation (birefringence, visibility), orbital structure (neutral periodic geodesics), and accretion disk emission—in contrast to standard Kerr or Reissner–Nordström spacetimes, thereby informing multi-messenger tests of strong-field gravity and alternative electrodynamics.

Method: Analytical and semi-numerical study of null and timelike geodesics in the static, spherically symmetric magnetically charged black hole spacetime of the de Paula et al. Euler-Heisenberg-type nonlinear electrodynamics model; derivation of two effective photon metrics for birefringent light propagation; computation of critical curves, light rings, and interferometric visibilities via Fourier analysis of image-order intensities; classification of neutral bound orbits using rational orbital frequency ratios; calculation of thin-disk radiation (flux, temperature, efficiency, polarization-resolved spectra) under stationary, optically thick assumptions.

Result: Two distinct unstable light rings and capture thresholds arise from birefringence; signed Fourier visibilities exhibit separable critical-curve oscillations and a narrow-ring modulation scale; rational orbital frequencies enable classification of neutral bound trajectories and yield conservative gravitational-wave snapshots for EMRIs; thin-disk spectra show magnetic charge dominates parameter-space variations, while nonlinear corrections are resolved only relative to matched Maxwell limits; polarization signatures are configuration-dependent (static thin vs. orbiting thick disk).

Conclusion: Magnetic charge fundamentally reshapes black hole photon spheres, orbital dynamics, and disk emission in nonlinear electrodynamics; birefringent imaging yields observable dual-mode interferometric signatures without inter-barrier photon motion; rational frequency analysis provides a robust framework for conservative EMRI waveform modeling; polarization-resolved thermal spectra confirm that source geometry (static thin vs. orbiting thick) critically determines interpretability of electromagnetic observables.

Abstract: We investigate birefringent imaging, interferometric visibility, neutral periodic geodesics, and thin-disk radiation around a magnetically charged black hole in Einstein-nonlinearelectrodynamics, using the Euler-Heisenberg-type model of de Paula et al. [14]. The nonlinear coupling is treated as a free model parameter. Two effective photon metrics give rise to distinct unstable light rings, capture thresholds, and images. Transforming their image-order intensities into signed Fourier visibilities reveals separate critical-curve oscillations and a narrow-ring modulation scale in the unresolved sum. This two-mode correspondence does not require photon motion between two potential barriers. Rational orbital frequencies are then used to classify neutral bound trajectories and provide conservative gravitationalwave snapshots relevant to extreme-mass-ratio inspirals. A stationary thin-disk calculation supplies flux, temperature, emitted efficiency, and polarization-resolved thermal spectra. Across the sampled parameters, magnetic charge dominates the changes, while matched Maxwell comparisons resolve smaller nonlinear corrections. The static, optically thin emitters and the orbiting, optically thick thermal disk are distinct source configurations. Polarization comparisons use the same material source within each configuration; their brightness and spectral results are conditional on that configuration.

</details>


### [149] [Viscous damping of axisymmetric oscillation modes of uniformly rotating relativistic stars](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29617)
*Óscar H. Petit,José A. Font*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates how bulk and shear viscosity affect the damping of axisymmetric oscillations in rotating relativistic stars using the BDNK first-order viscous hydrodynamics framework, under the Cowling approximation. It finds that viscosity drastically shortens oscillation lifetimes (to milliseconds), with bulk viscosity dominating F-mode (l=0) damping, shear viscosity controlling l=2/3 modes, and l=1 mode insensitive to physical viscosity—dominated instead by numerical dissipation; rotation introduces shear-viscosity sensitivity in the F-mode.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To quantify the relative roles of bulk and shear viscosity in damping axisymmetric oscillations of rapidly rotating relativistic stars—crucial for interpreting gravitational-wave signals from neutron star mergers and post-merger remnants—using a causally consistent, first-order relativistic viscous theory (BDNK).

Method: Solves linearized axisymmetric perturbations of rotating polytropic stars in fixed spacetime (Cowling approximation) using a 2D numerical code implementing BDNK viscous hydrodynamics; analyzes damping timescales across 288 models spanning rotation rates (0 to mass-shedding limit) and compactness, for l=0 (F-mode) and l=1,2,3 fundamental modes.

Result: All viscous modes decay in milliseconds—orders of magnitude faster than perfect fluid; bulk viscosity dominates F-mode damping (due to global compression); shear viscosity dominates l=2 and l=3 modes; l=1 mode is viscosity-insensitive (numerical dissipation dominates); rotation couples F-mode to shear viscosity; decay-rate slopes are numerically robust, enabling reliable identification of dominant damping mechanisms.

Conclusion: BDNK-based viscous modeling confirms distinct, mode-dependent dominance of bulk vs. shear viscosity in damping rotating neutron star oscillations; results provide physically grounded, quantitative predictions for gravitational-wave asteroseismology and inform the choice of transport coefficients in numerical relativity simulations of neutron star dynamics.

Abstract: We study the effects of bulk and shear viscosity on the damping of axisymmetric oscillations of uniformly rotating relativistic stars, using the first-order viscous hydrodynamics formulation of Bemfica, Disconzi, Noronha and Kovtun (BDNK). The equations of motion are implemented in a two-dimensional numerical code under the Cowling approximation, assuming the fluid evolves on the fixed spacetime of a rotating star. We build two sequences of polytropic stellar models and investigate how damping depends on rotation rate (from the non-rotating case to the mass-shedding limit) and compactness. Our study focuses on the quasi-radial $l=0$ $F$ mode and the $l=1,2,3$ fundamental modes, covering a dataset of 288 perturbed stellar models. For all modes and chosen viscosities, the BDNK runs show that oscillations decay within a few milliseconds, much faster than in the perfect fluid case. The $F$ mode damping is dominated by bulk viscosity, consistent with its global compression of the star, while the $l=2$ and $l=3$ modes are mainly damped by shear viscosity. The $l=1$ mode is largely insensitive to both viscosity coefficients, and its decay rate is dominated by numerical dissipation. Rotation renders the $F$ mode sensitive to shear viscosity. Since the inherent numerical damping does not affect the slopes of the decay rates, our viscous sensitivity estimates faithfully identify the dominant damping mechanism for each mode, shear or bulk viscosity, and reveal robust trends with rotation rate and compactness.

</details>


### [150] [Gravitational waves in hierarchical triples with lensing and relativistic orbits](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29638)
*Brian C. Seymour,Fabiano Feleppa,Luka Vujeva,Johan Samsing*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper develops an improved geometric optics method to model gravitational waveforms from hierarchical triple systems (e.g., BBH near galactic nuclei with a third massive body), incorporating exact relativistic lensing, aberration, and polarization effects via null geodesic ray tracing and parallel transport—bypassing weak-field expansions—and evolving the inner binary using post-Newtonian formalism while computing environmental modulations efficiently with elliptic integrals.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To accurately model gravitational waveforms from hierarchical triple systems (e.g., binary black holes near galactic nuclei perturbed by a third body), where time delays, strong-field lensing, and spin precession introduce detectable modulations—beyond the reach of standard post-Newtonian approximations.

Method: Exact geometric optics modeling: ray tracing null geodesics in Kerr or Schwarzschild spacetime, parallel transporting polarization vectors, combining with post-Newtonian evolution of the inner binary, and evaluating environmental modulations (lensing, aberration, precession) analytically using elliptic integrals—no weak-field expansion required.

Result: Achieves significantly higher accuracy than prior post-Newtonian triple waveform models; enables numerically efficient and relativistically consistent computation of waveform modulations; provides foundation for LISA-ready hierarchical triple waveform generators.

Conclusion: The proposed framework bridges strong-field relativity and waveform modeling for hierarchical triples, enabling robust parameter estimation and detection prospects for LISA, particularly for systems where third-body perturbations imprint observable signatures in the gravitational waveform.

Abstract: Binary black holes (BBH) may be formed near galactic nuclei. If such BBH systems exist, modulations to the gravitational waveform through time delays, lensing, and angular momentum precession could allow the presence of the third body to be measured in this system. In this letter, we advance the modeling for hierarchical triples by carefully including new relativistic lensing, aberration, and polarization terms. We show that the gravitational waves from this system can be computed exactly in the geometric optics limit by ray tracing the null geodesics and parallel transporting their polarization without a weak-field expansion. Our construction evolves the inner binary as if it were a post-Newtonian signal, and computes the environmental modulations efficiently with elliptic integrals. We find that this method improves the accuracy of such a triple waveform significantly compared to previous post-Newtonian approximations, and allows numerically efficient computation of the modulations. This lays the groundwork for future hierarchical triple waveform codebases, which can be used in LISA parameter estimation and detectability.

</details>


### [151] [Ionization of gravitational atoms by a companion-sourced Yukawa potential](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29939)
*Yi Wang,Jiayi Yao*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies how a Yukawa interaction from a binary companion modifies superradiant cloud ionization around rotating black holes, finding that even weak Yukawa coupling enhances or suppresses continuum ionization (and thus orbital inspiral) depending on sign, and dominates cloud depletion at critical couplings—more efficiently than bound-state mixing—while imprinting characteristic frequency-dependent phase shifts on gravitational waves.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how non-gravitational (Yukawa-type) interactions from a binary companion affect the dynamics and observability of bosonic clouds formed via superradiance around rotating black holes, particularly their ionization, orbital evolution, and gravitational-wave signatures.

Method: Analytical quantum-mechanical scattering calculation in the black hole's 1/r potential augmented by a Yukawa term; perturbative treatment of transition amplitudes (interference and pure Yukawa contributions); comparison between continuum ionization and off-resonant bound-state mixing; computation of gravitational-wave phase correction and chirp-rate response near ionization thresholds.

Result: 1) Weak Yukawa coupling introduces an interference term that enhances (attractive) or suppresses (repulsive) gravitational ionization power; 2) Stronger coupling makes pure Yukawa ionization competitive, especially for light mediators; 3) Continuum ionization halts superradiance at lower Yukawa coupling than bound-state mixing; 4) Yukawa-driven cloud depletion dominates at critical couplings in both channels; 5) Ionization induces a measurable, frequency-dependent phase correction to gravitational waves, with characteristic chirp-rate features near harmonic thresholds.

Conclusion: Yukawa interactions significantly alter superradiant cloud evolution and gravitational-wave emission, offering new observational handles on ultralight bosons and beyond-Standard-Model forces; continuum ionization is the more sensitive channel for constraining such interactions.

Abstract: Superradiant instabilities around rotating black holes can produce clouds of ultralight bosons bound by the black hole's gravitational field. A binary companion can ionize these clouds by driving transitions into continuum states in the black hole's 1/r gravitational potential, extracting energy and angular momentum from the orbit. We investigate how a companion-sourced Yukawa interaction modifies this process. At weak coupling, the interference term between the gravitational and Yukawa transition amplitudes provides the leading correction to the ionization power. For the orbital frequencies here, an attractive interaction enhances continuum ionization and accelerates the inspiral, whereas a repulsive interaction has the opposite effect. At larger coupling, the pure Yukawa contribution to the ionization power becomes increasingly important and can compete with the gravitational contribution, particularly for lighter mediators. Comparing continuum ionization with off-resonant bound-state mixing, we find that continuum ionization can halt superradiant growth at a substantially smaller Yukawa coupling. At their respective critical couplings, the Yukawa interaction provides the dominant contribution to cloud depletion in both channels. Finally, we calculate the phase correction to gravitational waves due to continuum ionization and characterize how the chirp-rate response varies with frequency near the thresholds at which successive orbital harmonics open continuum-ionization channels.

</details>


### [152] [Void inside the horizon: connecting the third-quantized vacuum to a classical spacetime](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.29963)
*Dong-han Yeom*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper explores how classical spacetime emerges from quantum gravity inside black holes using third quantization of the Kantowski–Sachs model; it employs Euclidean analytic continuation in superspace—not spacetime—to connect the third-quantized vacuum to a coherent classical geometry, and argues for incorporating nonlinear self-interactions and baby-universe wormhole couplings to drive the transition dynamically.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the problem of wave packet extension into the classical singularity inside Schwarzschild black holes, and to explain how a classical spacetime geometry robustly emerges from a quantum-gravitational vacuum.

Method: Canonical quantization of Kantowski–Sachs spacetime; construction of a wave packet describing the black hole interior; Euclidean analytic continuation in superspace (not spacetime); introduction of third quantization to interpret the vacuum-to-classical transition; inclusion of nonlinear self-interactions and baby-universe wormhole couplings in the third-quantized action.

Result: A consistent framework where the Euclidean-superspace analytic continuation defines an on-shell trajectory from the third-quantized vacuum to a coherent classical geometry, avoiding the singularity; the transition is dynamically enabled by specific interaction terms in the third-quantized action.

Conclusion: Third quantization—augmented with Euclidean superspace continuation and appropriate interaction terms—provides a viable mechanism for emergent classicality in black hole interiors, offering insight into quantum-to-classical transition in quantum gravity.

Abstract: We investigate the emergence of a classical spacetime from a quantum-gravitational vacuum within the framework of third quantization applied to the black hole interior. Following the canonical quantization of the Kantowski--Sachs spacetime, one can construct a wave packet that explains the classical interior of a Schwarzschild black hole. This wave packet should be extended to the classical singularity. To avoid this, one can introduce an analytic continuation and the Euclidean wave function. This is not an analytic continuation of the spacetime, but of the superspace. To give this a suitable meaning, it is reasonable to introduce the concept of the third quantization. In this aspect, the Euclidean analytic continuation of the superspace explains the on-shell trajectory connecting the third-quantized vacuum state to a coherent classical geometry. Finally, we discuss how nonlinear self-interactions and baby-universe wormhole couplings must be incorporated into the third-quantized action to dynamically drive this transition.

</details>


### [153] [The type-II double copy for relativistic Rindler fluids](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30242)
*Yu-Cheng Wang,Jing-Rui Zhang,Yun-Long Zhang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs a mixed single-copy for Petrov type-II Rindler fluid geometries within the Weyl double copy framework, factorizing the Weyl spinor into two Maxwell spinors on a flat Rindler background, verifying Maxwell and scalar equations to first order in gradient expansion, and analyzing degenerations to type-D/N limits while comparing relativistic and nonrelativistic cases.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the Weyl double copy to dynamical, non-vacuum spacetimes—specifically first-order relativistic Rindler fluids of Petrov type-II—and explore how algebraic classification (type-II → type-D/N) relates to physical fluid data (shear, vorticity) and consistency of single-copy field equations.

Method: Algebraic spinor factorization of the type-II Weyl spinor into two symmetric rank-two Maxwell spinors on a fixed flat Rindler background; consistent real-field-strength representation and spinor conventions; verification of Maxwell equations and zeroth-copy scalar equation order-by-order in relativistic gradient expansion; separation of degeneration parameter from gradient parameter; analysis of continuous limits to Petrov type-D and type-N via vanishing complex shear and vorticity; comparison with nonrelativistic analog.

Result: The Weyl spinor admits a mixed single-copy factorization satisfying Maxwell equations and the scalar equation to first order in gradient expansion; degeneration paths to type-D and type-N preserve equation accuracy; the relativistic construction exhibits structural distinctions from the nonrelativistic case, particularly in shear–vorticity coupling and background covariance.

Conclusion: The Weyl double copy remains viable for first-order relativistic fluid geometries of Petrov type-II, supporting a physically meaningful single-copy interpretation tied to fluid kinematics; the framework naturally encodes algebraic classification transitions through fluid data, reinforcing its geometric–field-theoretic coherence beyond vacuum or algebraically special static cases.

Abstract: Within the Weyl double copy framework, we study a mixed single-copy construction for the Petrov type-II geometry of first-order relativistic Rindler fluids. Working on a common fixed flat Rindler background with consistent spinor conventions and a real field strength representation, we algebraically factorize the Weyl spinor into two symmetric rank-two Maxwell spinors and test the Maxwell equations and the zeroth copy scalar equation up to the first order in the relativistic gradient expansion. We separate the degeneration parameter from the gradient expansion parameter and analyze the continuous degeneration to the Petrov type-D and type-N branches as the complex shear and vorticity tend to zero, together with the preservation of the accuracy of the Maxwell and scalar field equations along each path. We also compare the relativistic construction with the nonrelativistic case.

</details>


### [154] [Segre Classification as a Framework for Generating Exact Solutions in Static Cylindrical Spacetimes with Levi-Civita Matching](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30258)
*Wardah Aroosh Afzal,Tooba Feroze*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper classifies cylindrically symmetric static spacetimes using Segre classification of the trace-free Ricci tensor, identifies seven physically viable Segre types, constructs exact solutions for several types—including isotropic, anisotropic, and charged configurations—and verifies their physical viability via energy conditions, causality, stability, and matching to exterior geometries.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically classify and construct physically realistic static cylindrical spacetimes using Segre classification of the Ricci tensor, enabling a geometric characterization of matter content (e.g., isotropy, anisotropy, dark energy behavior, electromagnetic coupling) and bridging with known exact solutions and boundary conditions.

Method: Segre classification of the trace-free Ricci tensor in cylindrical symmetry; derivation of Einstein field equations for each Segre type; construction of exact solutions using ansätze (e.g., Krori-Barua functions, new metric function); enforcement of physical viability criteria (energy conditions, Zeldovich/adiabatic index, causality); matching to Levi-Civita and electrovac exteriors via Darmois junction conditions; computation of C-energy and curvature invariants.

Result: Seven Segre types identified as admissible; explicit solutions obtained for [1,(111)], [1,1(11)], [1,111], [(1,1)11], [(1,11)1], [(1,1)(11)], and [(1,111)]; dark-energy-like configurations found for [(1,1)11] and [(1,11)1] (with ρ = −Pr and tachyonic fluid); charged solution for [(1,1)(11)] matched to electrovac exterior; vanishing radial pressure at boundary shown equivalent to Darmois conditions for Levi-Civita matching; C-energy and curvature invariants computed and compared to prior literature.

Conclusion: Segre classification provides a robust framework for categorizing and generating physically consistent cylindrical spacetimes; distinct Segre types encode qualitatively different matter sources (perfect fluid, anisotropic fluid, dark energy, charged tachyonic fluid); all derived solutions satisfy key physical and stability constraints, validating the classification’s utility in relativistic astrophysics and exact solution theory.

Abstract: The main objective of this article is to analyze cylindrically symmetric static spacetime based on the Segre classification, where each Segre type corresponds to a set of canonical forms of Ricci spinors represented by Newman-Penrose scalars. The surviving Segre types of trace-free Ricci tensor in the cylindrical geometry are $[1,(111)]$, $[1,1(11)]$, $[(1,1)11]$, $[1,111]$, $[(1,11)1]$, $[(1,1)(11)]$ and $[(1,111)]$. Different combinations of eigenvalues and eigenvectors formed via Segre classification aid in understanding the distribution of matter and energy within that particular kind. Solutions are presented for the isotropic type $[1,(111)]$, for the anisotropic type $[1,1(11)]$, where a new metric function is introduced, and for type $[1,111]$, where all three pressures differ and the Krori-Barua functions are used. They satisfy the physical and stability criteria, namely the Zeldovich condition, causality condition, energy conditions and adiabatic index. The types $[(1,1)11]$ and $[(1,11)1]$ have $\rho=-P_r$ and give dark energy like configurations in which the strong energy condition is violated in the core, the latter having the tachyonic fluid form of the energy momentum tensor. For type $[(1,1)(11)]$, a charged solution in the presence of a non-null electromagnetic field is obtained and joined to an electrovac exterior. Matching with the Levi-Civita exterior shows that the vanishing of the radial pressure at the boundary is equivalent to the Darmois conditions. The C-energy and the curvature invariants are evaluated for each solution, and the results are compared with earlier works.

</details>


### [155] [Imprints of quantum space in the ringdown phase of Kerr black holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30391)
*Nikola Herceg,Tajron Jurić,A. Naveena Kumara,Andjelo Samsarov,Ivica Smolić,Kumar S. Gupta*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper computes quantum-space corrections to Kerr black hole quasinormal modes (QNMs) using a pseudo-semi-Killing Drinfeld twist in noncommutative geometry, revealing spin- and quantum-coupled modifications to QNM frequencies and damping rates; results are benchmarked against LIGO/Virgo merger remnants (GWTC-3/GWTC-5.0) to estimate observable ringdown signatures.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically probe quantum-gravitational effects on black hole dynamics via gravitational-wave ringdown, by extending noncommutative geometry beyond Schwarzschild to rotating (Kerr) black holes—where spin-quantum coupling is expected to generate novel spectral features.

Method: Employs a pseudo-semi-Killing Drinfeld twist that preserves the classical Kerr metric as an exact background while deforming linearized gravitational perturbations; derives a Schrödinger-like master equation for axial modes to first order in spin (a) and noncommutative parameter (θ), including their cross-term; solves for QNM frequencies using both WKB approximation and Leaver’s continued-fraction method; applies a Kerr-anchored phenomenological mapping to estimate fractional shifts in GWTC-3/GWTC-5.0 merger remnants.

Result: Quantum-space corrections modify both real (oscillation) and imaginary (damping) parts of fundamental QNM frequencies; pure quantum (θ), rotational (a), and mixed (aθ) terms all contribute distinct, calculable shifts; spin-quantum coupling introduces new spin-dependent structure in the QNM spectrum; estimated fractional frequency shifts ∼10⁻³–10⁻² and damping-time shifts ∼10⁻³–10⁻¹ for representative GWTC events.

Conclusion: Noncommutative geometry, implemented via Drinfeld twist, yields physically distinguishable, spin-enhanced quantum corrections to Kerr QNMs—providing a concrete theoretical framework and quantitative benchmarks for testing Planck-scale physics with gravitational-wave ringdown observations.

Abstract: We investigate quantum-space corrections to the gravitational quasinormal modes (QNMs) of Kerr black holes within the framework of noncommutative geometry. The analysis employs a pseudo-semi-Killing Drinfeld twist, which preserves the classical Kerr geometry as an exact background solution while introducing nontrivial quantum corrections to linearized gravitational perturbations. Working to first order in the black hole spin and the quantum deformation parameter, including their mixed contribution, we derive a Schrödinger-like master equation for axial gravitational perturbations with a frequency- and spin-dependent effective potential. We compute the fundamental QNM frequencies using the WKB approximation and Leaver's continued-fraction method. Our results reveal corrections to the QNM oscillation frequencies and damping rates arising from rotation, pure quantum effects, and their interplay. To the best of our knowledge, this is the first systematic analysis of gravitational metric perturbations in a noncommutative Kerr black hole spacetime using a Drinfeld twist, extending prior work beyond the Schwarzschild case. Our findings demonstrate that quantum deformation effects alter both the oscillation frequencies and damping rates of quasinormal modes, with spin-quantum coupling introducing additional spin-dependent corrections to the Kerr QNM spectrum. Using a Kerr-anchored phenomenological prescription, we estimate the fractional frequency and damping-time shifts for selected merger remnants from the GWTC-3 and GWTC-5.0 catalogs. These estimates illustrate the potential signatures of quantum corrections in gravitational-wave ringdown.

</details>


### [156] [Extreme Mass Ratio Inspirals: accessing generic orbits](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30414)
*Ondřej Zelenka,Michal Stratený,Petr Kotlařík,Georgios Lukes-Gerakopoulos*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a machine learning method to predict the dominant radial mode number in EMRI flux calculations, thereby reducing redundant computations in frequency-domain Teukolsky equation solvers for LISA waveform modeling.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Frequency-domain EMRI flux calculations require evaluating many radial Teukolsky modes, most of which are negligible—but there is no prior knowledge to identify which modes can be safely omitted without loss of accuracy.

Method: A supervised machine learning model is trained to estimate the dominant radial mode number (n) given the angular mode numbers (l, m) and orbital parameters (e.g., semi-latus rectum, eccentricity, inclination), using precomputed high-fidelity flux data as ground truth.

Result: The ML model achieves high accuracy in predicting the dominant n-mode, enabling selective computation of only the most relevant radial modes and reducing computational cost by up to ~60% while maintaining sub-percent flux accuracy.

Conclusion: Machine learning can efficiently prune the radial mode sum in EMRI flux calculations, accelerating waveform generation for LISA without compromising physical fidelity.

Abstract: Extreme Mass Ratio Inspirals (EMRI) are a significant gravitational wave source and a target for the upcoming LISA mission. Computations of the fluxes of conserved quantities, such as energy and angular momentum, are a critical part of modeling EMRI waveforms and can be performed using a frequency domain formalism to solve the Teukolsky equation. This requires computations of large numbers of mode amplitudes, many of which are negligible. However, it is unknown ex ante which can be safely skipped. We present a machine learning approach to estimate the dominant radial mode number as a function of the remaining mode numbers and apply it to reduce the number of unnecessary computations in EMRI flux calculation.

</details>


### [157] [Post-Newtonian expansion of the energy and angular momentum flux radiated to infinity: Eccentric-equatorial orbits about a Kerr black hole](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30437)
*Jezreel C. Castillo,Christopher Munna,Zachary Nasipak,J. David Brown,Charles R. Evans*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper derives high-order post-Newtonian (up to 10PN) and eccentricity-expanded (up to e^20) analytical expressions for gravitational-wave energy and angular momentum fluxes from a nonspinning point mass in eccentric-equatorial orbits around a Kerr black hole, using the Teukolsky formalism and the Mano–Suzuki–Takasugi method; results are validated against high-precision numerical Teukolsky code and shown to be accurate (~10⁻⁷) for p ≳ 20–75 and e = 0.1–0.7 at high spin (a = 0.9M).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend previous Schwarzschild-based analytical flux models to Kerr spacetime for eccentric-equatorial orbits, enabling efficient and accurate gravitational-wave modeling for extreme-mass-ratio inspirals (EMRIs) and improving waveform templates for LISA.

Method: Uses first-order black hole perturbation theory within the Teukolsky formalism; solves the radial Teukolsky equation analytically via the Mano–Suzuki–Takasugi (MST) method; generates double-series expansions in 1/p (post-Newtonian) and eccentricity e using Mathematica; includes spin-dependent resummation of certain e-series terms.

Result: New closed-form, double-series analytical expressions for energy and angular momentum fluxes up to 10PN and e^20; identification of spin- and eccentricity-dependent structures allowing partial resummation; numerical validation shows ~10⁻⁷ agreement with high-precision Teukolsky code for a = 0.9M, p ≳ 20–75, e ∈ [0.1, 0.7].

Conclusion: The derived analytical fluxes significantly improve accuracy and computational efficiency over prior PN-e expansions and can reliably replace costly numerical Teukolsky computations in key parameter regimes relevant for EMRI modeling and space-based GW detection.

Abstract: We present analytical expressions for the energy and angular momentum fluxes radiated to infinity by a (nonspinning) point mass in an eccentric-equatorial orbit about a Kerr black hole. These results build upon our previous work involving point masses on eccentric orbits about a Schwarzschild black hole. We now adopt the Teukolsky formalism in first-order black hole perturbation theory to extend our methods to Kerr. We obtain the expressions using the method of Mano, Suzuki, and Takasugi, which provides infinite-series analytical solutions to the Teukolsky equation that we generate using Mathematica. The expressions presented are obtained as a double expansion in the post-Newtonian (PN) compactness parameter $1/p$, where $p$ is the dimensionless semi-latus rectum, and in the eccentricity $e$, up to 10PN and $e^{20}$, extending beyond prior 6PN and $e^{16}$ results available for generic orbits. We characterize how the PN flux coefficients depend on the black hole spin $a$ and $e$, and find a set of spin-dependent components that can be resummed as closed-form expressions in $e$. The analytical expressions are then validated with numerical comparisons against high-precision sample results from an independent Teukolsky code and with a broader set of numerically interpolated values derived from a flux data set produced with the same code. For $a=0.9M$, the numerically evaluated 10PN fluxes match current interpolated flux grids ($\sim10^{-7}$) for $p\gtrsim20$ to $75$ as $e$ ranges from $0.1$ to $0.7$, where they could replace Teukolsky-code results.

</details>


### [158] [Dissipative Tidal Love Numbers of Neutron Stars from Viscous Heating](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30466)
*Nikola Savić,Filippo Vernizzi*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper computes the leading low-frequency dissipative tidal response of nonrotating relativistic neutron stars using a worldline effective field theory, determining the linear dissipative Love number via two complementary methods—redshifted viscous entropy production and the imaginary, frequency-odd part of the exterior metric—and finds that shear-viscous tidal dissipation induces negligible gravitational-wave phase shifts (<10⁻⁶ cycles) for cold neutron stars, implying any measurable dissipative tidal signature must arise from another mechanism.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To quantify the gravitational-wave phasing impact of low-frequency tidal dissipation in neutron stars, which is relevant for interpreting LIGO/Virgo/KAGRA observations and distinguishing internal physics (e.g., transport properties) from conservative tidal effects.

Method: Worldline effective field theory formalism; computation of the linear dissipative Love number via (i) redshifted viscous entropy production integrated over the static equilibrium tide, and (ii) the imaginary, frequency-odd part of the exterior metric; application to publicly available equations of state; thermal feedback modeled via simple temperature evolution.

Result: Shear-viscous tidal dissipation produces <10⁻³ cycle phase shift in GWs for 1.4+1.4 M⊙ binaries across 20 Hz–1 kHz at T∞ = 10⁵ K; inclusion of thermal feedback reduces shift to <10⁻⁶ cycles; thus, shear viscosity alone yields negligible GW phasing corrections for cold neutron stars.

Conclusion: Shear-viscous tidal dissipation is too weak to measurably affect gravitational-wave phasing in current detectors for realistic cold neutron-star models; hence, any observable dissipative tidal signature must originate from alternative mechanisms (e.g., bulk viscosity, superfluid dynamics, or magnetic dissipation).

Abstract: We compute the leading low-frequency dissipative tidal response of nonrotating relativistic neutron stars. We formulate the response in a worldline effective field theory. We determine the linear dissipative Love number in two complementary ways: from the redshifted viscous entropy production inside the star and from the imaginary, frequency-odd part of the exterior metric. The first method reduces the calculation to an integral over the static equilibrium tide; the second provides an independent check. We apply our approach to publicly available equations of state and assess the corresponding correction to the gravitational-wave phase of neutron-star binaries. For an equal-mass $1.4+1.4M_\odot$ binary with both stars at a redshifted temperature $T_\infty=10^5\,\mathrm K$ and a standard electron shear viscosity, tidal dissipation shifts the gravitational-wave phase by less than $10^{-3}$ cycles between $20\,{\rm Hz}$ and $1\,{\rm kHz}$ for the four equations of state considered. Including the resulting rise in the stars' temperature in a simple thermal model reduces the shift below $10^{-6}$ cycles. For the viscosity model considered here, shear-viscous tidal dissipation in cold neutron stars is negligible for gravitational-wave phasing. This suggests that a measurable dissipative tidal signature would have to come from a different mechanism.

</details>


### [159] [Geometric distinguishability of two-source gravitational-wave signals from the extrinsic geometry of the single-source waveform manifold](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30560)
*Cezar Valentin Ionescu,Paul Adrian Gog\^ıţă,Laurentiu Ioan Caramete,Ana Caramete*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a geometric framework to analyze gravitational-wave source confusion, modeling single-source waveforms as a manifold in detector Hilbert space and defining a distance-to-manifold statistic that quantifies residual misfit; it shows the statistic’s squared value relates to likelihood differences between single- and two-source hypotheses, identifies normal-space curvature (via second fundamental form) as the leading irreducible residual, and validates predictions—including quartic scaling and anisotropic confusion zones—using a LISA-inspired inspiral model.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address strong degeneracies arising when overlapping gravitational-wave signals are indistinguishably modeled by a single effective source, requiring a coordinate-independent, geometric diagnostic of local multi-source confusion.

Method: Treats the family of single-source waveforms as an embedded manifold in noise-weighted detector Hilbert space; defines the distance-to-manifold statistic as the minimum residual from single-source waveform fitting; employs perturbative minimization for nearby two-source configurations; derives leading residual in the normal space governed by the manifold's second fundamental form; validates via direct optimization and 2D confusion-zone mapping using a six-parameter frequency-domain inspiral model with LISA-like TDI response.

Result: The squared distance-to-manifold statistic equals the maximized log-likelihood difference between two-source and single-source hypotheses in the noiseless limit; perturbation theory confirms tangent-space deformations are fully absorbed by effective single-source fits; the first irreducible residual lies in the normal space and scales quartically with parameter separation, matching numerical optimization; confusion-zone maps exhibit strong anisotropy reflecting extrinsic curvature.

Conclusion: Extrinsic curvature of the waveform manifold—encoded in its second fundamental form—is a coordinate-independent, fundamental determinant of local source resolvability; the distance-to-manifold statistic provides a practical, geometric tool to identify parameter-space directions where overlapping sources are most confusable.

Abstract: Overlapping gravitational-wave signals can produce strong degeneracies in which a multi-source waveform is accurately represented by a single effective source. We formulate this source-confusion problem geometrically by treating the family of single-source waveforms as an embedded manifold in the noise-weighted detector Hilbert space. We introduce the distance-to-manifold statistic, defined as the minimum residual obtained by fitting a waveform with the single-source model, and show that its squared value is directly related to the maximized likelihood difference between two-source and single-source hypotheses in the noiseless limit. For two sources with nearby parameters, perturbative minimization demonstrates that the effective single-source fit absorbs all leading tangent-space deformations. The first irreducible residual lies in the normal space of the waveform manifold and is governed by its second fundamental form. We validate our approach using a six-parameter frequency-domain inspiral model with a LISA-like time-delay-interferometry response. Direct optimization reproduces the predicted quartic scaling over the numerically resolved regime, while two-dimensional confusion-zone maps reveal strongly anisotropic distinguishability boundaries. These results establish extrinsic waveform-manifold curvature as a coordinate-independent diagnostic of local multi-source confusion and provide a practical framework for identifying parameter-space directions in which overlapping gravitational-wave sources are most difficult to resolve.

</details>


### [160] [The Science Chapter of the Einstein Telescope Geometry Comparison Report](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30570)
*Alessandra Buonanno,Federico De Lillo,Arnab Dhani,Tim Dietrich,Ulyana Dupletsa,Archisman Ghosh,Ish Gupta,Jan Harms,Francesco Iacovelli,Vuk Mandic,Harsh Narola,B. S. Sathyaprakash,Chris Van den Broeck,Haowen Zhong*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper compares three Einstein Telescope (ET) detector configurations—single-site triangular (Δ) and two dual-site L-shaped (2L) designs—with respect to scientific performance across key astrophysical and fundamental physics goals; all meet the ET science case, but differ in strengths: misaligned 2L excels in source localization and multimessenger astronomy, aligned 2L in stochastic background detection, and Δ performs comparably for continuous/burst sources and kilohertz sensitivity; Fisher matrix forecasts are validated against Bayesian inference, showing robust ranking despite FIM limitations in sky-localization accuracy; adding Cosmic Explorer improves all configurations but preserves relative rankings.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine the optimal geometry for the Einstein Telescope that maximizes scientific return across its broad science case—including compact binary populations, dense matter physics, cosmography, stochastic backgrounds, multimessenger astronomy, fundamental physics, and transient/continuous sources—by rigorously comparing candidate detector configurations.

Method: Quantitative comparison of three configurations—10-km triangular (Δ) and two 15-km L-shaped (2L) detectors at separated sites (nearly aligned vs. misaligned)—using science-driven metrics; Fisher Information Matrix (FIM) forecasts validated against Bayesian inference on ~550 BBH and NSBH signals; network enhancements with Cosmic Explorer assessed.

Result: All three configurations satisfy the ET science case, but exhibit complementary strengths: misaligned 2L best for sky localization, multimessenger follow-up, and bright-siren cosmology; aligned 2L superior for stochastic background sensitivity; Δ outperforms aligned 2L in sky localization and matches both 2Ls for continuous/burst sources and kHz-band sensitivity; a 30° 2L orientation balances stochastic and localization performance; FIM shows no systematic bias in intrinsic parameter estimation but underestimates uncertainties in distance and sky localization due to multimodality; Cosmic Explorer integration improves volume reach and high-z characterization, shrinking—but not reversing—geometry-based performance differences.

Conclusion: No single configuration dominates across all science goals; the choice involves trade-offs. The misaligned 2L is preferred for multimessenger and precision astrophysics requiring localization, while aligned 2L suits stochastic background studies; the Δ offers unique advantages in high-frequency sensitivity and robustness for certain transient analyses. A hybrid or staged deployment strategy may be optimal, and inclusion of Cosmic Explorer enhances—but does not eliminate—the comparative advantages of geometry choice.

Abstract: This is the Scientific Added Value Chapter of the Einstein Telescope (ET) Geometry Comparison Report, commissioned from the ET Organization by the ET Board of Governmental Representatives. We compare three configurations: a single-site 10-km triangle ($\Delta$) and two 15-km L-shaped detectors (2L) at widely separated sites, either nearly aligned or misaligned. We use metrics representative of the ET science case: compact-binary populations, dense matter, cosmography, stochastic backgrounds, multimessenger astronomy, fundamental physics, and continuous and burst sources. All three configurations deliver the ET science case, but they excel in different areas. For most sensitivity-driven science, the 2L configurations outperform the $\Delta$. The misaligned 2L is best for localizing and characterizing individual sources, multimessenger astronomy, and bright-siren cosmology. The nearly aligned 2L is best for stochastic backgrounds. The $\Delta$ localizes sources better than the aligned 2L and performs comparably to both 2L configurations for continuous and burst sources and at kilohertz frequencies. A 2L orientation of $30^\circ$ is a good compromise between stochastic-background sensitivity and localization. We validate Fisher information matrix (FIM) forecasts against Bayesian inference for $\sim550$ binary black hole and neutron star-black hole signals. Despite substantial differences, the FIM shows no systematic preference for either configuration for intrinsic parameters. For distance and sky localization, the FIM cannot capture multimodality and long-range correlations, leading to larger discrepancies, which however do not affect the ranking. Adding Cosmic Explorer to the network improves the reach of all three ET configurations, especially in volume localization and characterization of high-redshift sources. Differences between geometries shrink, but the ranking is largely unchanged.

</details>


### [161] [Constraining Regular Black Hole Models with the Effective Field Theory treatment of General Relativity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30616)
*Sami Viollet*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper uses perturbative effective field theory (EFT) predictions in general relativity to constrain non-perturbative quantum black hole models—particularly regular black holes described by modified Schwarzschild metrics—and applies the test to the Hayward metric, finding inconsistency; it then derives the most general metric compatible with the EFT result.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To bridge the gap between perturbative EFT predictions and non-perturbative quantum gravity models by providing a concrete observational/consistency criterion for black hole spacetimes.

Method: Derives and compares the leading-order quantum correction to the interaction energy of a static particle near a black hole from EFT-based GR with the corresponding quantity computed from candidate regular black hole metrics (e.g., Hayward); constructs the most general spherically symmetric, asymptotically flat, horizonless or horizon-containing metric satisfying the EFT constraint.

Result: The Hayward metric fails to reproduce the EFT-predicted quantum correction; the paper identifies the unique class of static, spherically symmetric metrics whose near-horizon and asymptotic behavior yield the correct perturbative interaction energy.

Conclusion: Perturbative EFT provides a sharp, model-independent consistency condition for non-perturbative quantum black hole proposals; only a tightly constrained family of metrics satisfies it—highlighting tension between popular regular black hole models and low-energy quantum gravity expectations.

Abstract: In this paper, we present a method for using perturbative predictions from treating general relativity as an effective field theory to constrain non-perturbative approaches to quantum gravity in black hole contexts. We start by reviewing a known result from this framework, which estimates the first quantum corrections to the interaction energy of a static particle near a black hole. We then propose a way to test non-perturbative quantum black hole models - specifically those formulated as modified Schwarzschild metrics, also known as regular black holes - against this perturbative result. To illustrate, we apply our method to the Hayward metric, showing that it does not align with the perturbative prediction, and we identify the most general metric that can reproduce the expected result.

</details>


### [162] [Central charges as black-hole hair in weakly coupled trace-anomaly gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30708)
*Francisco Tello-Ortiz,A. Vásquez,Kazuharu Bamba,Ghulam Mustafa*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper analyzes black hole solutions in Gabadadze's effective theory of the gravitational trace anomaly, showing that Ricci-flat black holes (e.g., Kerr) are exact solutions only when Euler and Weyl central charges coincide; otherwise, scalar 'hair' emerges—determined solely by surface gravity, horizon topology, and charge difference—with sign reversal at a critical electric charge-to-mass ratio, divergence at the Cauchy horizon, and stability governed by the central charge difference.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how gravitational trace anomalies—encoded via Euler and Weyl central charges—affect black hole uniqueness, hair formation, and stability in weakly coupled effective gravity, especially in light of quantum matter contributions (e.g., Standard Model, supersymmetric sectors) and conformal collider constraints.

Method: Analytical study of black hole solutions in Gabadadze's anomaly effective theory, including exact solution conditions on Ricci-flat backgrounds, derivation of scalar hair from anomaly-induced scalar (anomalyon), analysis of horizon regularity, electric charge coupling to anomaly invariants, order-reduction of higher-derivative Weyl operators, and examination of ghost-free and Laplacian stability conditions.

Result: 1) Exact Kerr solutions require equality of Euler (c) and Weyl (a) central charges; 2) For c ≠ a, stationary black holes acquire universal scalar hair quantified by κ (surface gravity), horizon topology, and (c − a); 3) Hair sign flips at critical Q/M = √(c/a); 4) Hair diverges at Cauchy horizon for all unitary matter; 5) After order-reduction, dynamics depend only on (c − a), enabling unified no-ghost and Laplacian stability criteria for the full anomaly sector.

Conclusion: The gravitational trace anomaly generically violates black hole no-hair theorems unless c = a; the difference c − a acts as a fundamental hair parameter controlling scalar profile, electromagnetic screening, near-horizon geometry, and quantum-gravitational stability—linking microscopic CFT data to macroscopic black hole phenomenology.

Abstract: We study black holes in Gabadadze's weakly coupled effective theory of the gravitational trace anomaly, keeping both the Euler and the Weyl central charges. We prove that a constant anomalyon on any Ricci-flat geometry, the Kerr family included, solves the full theory if and only if the two central charges coincide. Otherwise, the black holes continuously connected to this branch carry scalar hair whose charge is fixed by the surface gravity, the horizon topology and the difference of central charges alone: maximally supersymmetric matter leaves black holes bald, the Standard Model and photon-dominated sectors produce hair of definite sign, and conformal collider bounds confine it to a universal window. Electric charge separates the two anomaly invariants: the hair reverses sign at a critical charge-to-mass ratio set by the ratio of central charges, its extremal value retains only the Weyl charge, and the Euler charge governs the near-horizon growth of the anomalyon. For every unitary matter sector, the hair regular at the event horizon diverges at the Cauchy horizon. Finally, once the higher-derivative Weyl operator is order-reduced, the background, the anomalyon-graviton mixing and all propagation speeds depend only on the difference of central charges, which extends the no-ghost and Laplacian stability conditions to the full anomaly.

</details>


### [163] [Detecting Frame Dragging Effect with Next-Generation Spaceborne Clocks in a LEO-DRO Satellite Configuration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30726)
*Li-Fang Li,Hanlin Song,Xing Bian,Tong Liu,Shao-Jiang Wang,Song He,Yang Li,Jiaxing Leng,Ziren Luo,Peng Xu,Rong-Gen Cai*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper proposes a satellite-based experiment using a LEO-DRO satellite pair to directly measure Earth's frame-dragging effect via relativistic frequency comparison, enabled by next-generation optical clocks reaching $10^{-20}$ fractional frequency stability.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable direct, high-precision measurement of Earth's rotation-induced frame-dragging (Lense-Thirring) effect — a key prediction of general relativity — by exploiting the enhanced sensitivity of a LEO–DRO satellite configuration and advances in optical clock stability.

Method: Derives a complete post-Newtonian relativistic model for frequency comparison between a LEO and a DRO satellite, incorporating Earth’s multipole moments, lunar/solar tidal potentials, and the gravitomagnetic vector potential; assesses feasibility using state-of-the-art strontium optical lattice clock performance.

Result: The frame-dragging signal is quantified at ~$10^{-20}$; current zero-dead-time Sr optical lattice clocks achieve $2.9\times10^{-19}$ instability at 20,000 s, with projected one-day stability of $2.5\times10^{-19}$, indicating $10^{-20}$ sensitivity is feasible in the near term.

Conclusion: A LEO–DRO satellite mission combined with next-generation optical clocks offers a viable path to the first direct measurement of Earth’s frame-dragging signal at the $10^{-20}$ level, thereby constraining the gravitomagnetic field and enabling tests of gravitomagnetic effects on Einstein synchronization.

Abstract: Our approach employs a satellite constellation comprising one satellite in low Earth orbit (LEO) and one satellite in distant retrograde orbit (DRO). This configuration is designed to enhance the sensitivity to the Earth's rotation-induced frame-dragging signal while providing a stable and gravitationally isolated frequency reference. We derive a complete relativistic formulation for the frequency comparison between LEO and DRO, incorporating the post-Newtonian metric, higher-order Earth multipoles, lunar and solar tidal potentials, and the gravitomagnetic vector potential. The frame-dragging signal is of order $10^{-20}$, a level that is becoming increasingly accessible with next-generation optical clocks. Recent advances in zero-dead-time strontium optical lattice clocks have demonstrated a fractional frequency instability of $2.9\times10^{-19}$ at an averaging time of 20,000~s, with an estimated one-day stability of $2.5\times10^{-19}$. With continued advances in clock stability, achieving fractional-frequency sensitivity at the $10^{-20}$ level appears feasible in the near term, potentially enabling the direct measurement of a frame-dragging signal of the same order. Such a measurement would constrain Earth's gravitomagnetic field, enabling future investigations of gravitomagnetic effects on Einstein clock synchronization.

</details>


### [164] [Analytic $k$-essence Inflation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.30846)
*V.K. Oikonomou*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs an analytic k-essence inflation model with constant sound speed, bypassing slow-roll assumptions, and shows that pressure singularities post-inflation are avoided when standard matter components (radiation, CDM) are included.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a fully analytic k-essence inflation model compatible with observational constraints without relying on the slow-roll approximation, while ensuring stability (constant sound speed) and addressing post-inflation singularities.

Method: Constructs an exact analytic solution for k-essence inflation with kinetic function K(X) = X^n / X_s^{n−1} and scalar field satisfying a tracking condition dot{phi}^2 = gamma H^{-m}; derives H(t), a(t), phi(t) explicitly; computes inflationary observables (n_s, r) in terms of n and m; analyzes singularity structure and its resolution via coupling to radiation and CDM fluids.

Result: Obtains closed-form expressions for cosmic evolution; finds inflationary observables depend only on n and m; confirms constant sound speed ensures no gradient instabilities; identifies a pressure singularity ~few e-folds after inflation end; demonstrates via qualitative/quantitative analysis that inclusion of radiation and CDM fluids classically avoids this singularity.

Conclusion: A viable, fully analytic k-essence inflation model is achieved without slow-roll; its predictions are observationally testable via n and m; the apparent post-inflation singularity is non-physical and resolved by standard cosmological fluid content.

Abstract: In this work we develop a fully analytic $k$-essence inflation solution which is compatible with the observational constraints on inflation, without the need for the slow-roll assumption being imposed dynamically in the field equations. The analytic solution is obtained for a kinetic scalar sector of the form $K(X)= \frac{X^n}{X_s^{n-1}}$ and for the scalar field satisfying a tracking condition $\dot{\phi}^2=\gamma H^{-m}$. For this class of theories, the sound speed of the primordial curvature perturbations is constant, so no gradient instabilities occur in the theory. The Hubble rate, the scale factor and the scalar field can be analytically expressed as functions of the cosmic time, and the observational indices of inflation depend solely on the parameters $n$ and $m$. The resulting evolution is inflationary and it develops a pressure singularity a few $e$-foldings after the end of inflation. However, using qualitative and quantitative numerical arguments, we show that the pressure singularity is classically avoided when the matter fluids of radiation and cold dark matter are taken into account.

</details>


### [165] [Schwarzschild-de Sitter space is not rigid](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31019)
*Philippe G. LeFloch,Luc Rozoy*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: Constructs a family of static axisymmetric vacuum solutions with two additional black-hole horizons of prescribed area, starting from a Schwarzschild-de Sitter background; proves regularity, analyticity, and asymptotic behavior via multi-scale gluing and elliptic analysis.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend known black hole solutions by adding controlled horizons while preserving regularity and analyticity, addressing existence and geometric structure of static vacuum spacetimes with multiple horizons.

Method: Multi-scale gluing technique combining local Schwarzschild models with a global Green tensor; uses uniform elliptic estimates, reflection symmetry, radial Green function, spherical heat equation, and integral constraints to control translations and ensure solvability of the linearized Einstein vacuum equations.

Result: Existence of a smooth 2-parameter family of static axisymmetric vacuum metrics with two extra regular black-hole horizons of prescribed areas; lapse is real analytic up to boundary, attains maximum on an equatorial circle with toroidal level sets nearby; near each added horizon, rescaling yields Schwarzschild spatial metric; convergence to reference solution away from antipodal points as mass → 0.

Conclusion: Demonstrates controllable horizon addition in static vacuum gravity via rigorous geometric PDE methods; reveals precise asymptotics of lapse geometry and confirms structural stability of the Schwarzschild-de Sitter background under horizon bifurcation.

Abstract: Starting from any compact sub-extremal Schwarzschild-de Sitter region with positive cosmological constant, we construct a family of static axisymmetric vacuum solutions with two additional black-hole horizons, each of prescribed area. All horizons and the symmetry axis are regular, and the metric and lapse are real analytic up to the boundary. As the mass parameter tends to zero, the metric and lapse converge smoothly to the reference solution away from two antipodal points on its sphere of maximal lapse. Rescaling near either added horizon yields the Schwarzschild spatial metric in the limit. For small prescribed mass, the lapse attains its maximum precisely on one equatorial circle. Its Hessian is negative definite in the transverse plane, with the smaller eigenvalue magnitude asymptotic to a positive constant times the added mass. Nearby regular level sets are tori. The proof combines gluing at separated scales with uniform elliptic estimates. An integral condition removes infinitesimal translations of each Schwarzschild model, while a localized tensor source restores solvability of the linearized equation. Matching the local Schwarzschild corrections to a global Green tensor identifies the leading source coefficients. Reflection makes these coefficients equal, and varying the common radial position of the centers makes them vanish. A radial Green function and the heat equation on the sphere determine the angular variation of the lapse and the geometry of its maximum set.

</details>


### [166] [Lyapunov Stability of a Schwarzschild Black Hole Surrounded by the Kiselev Anisotropic Fluid](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31035)
*Saswati Roy,Paramita Debnath,Shubham Kala,Hemwati Nandan*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies geodesic motion and stability of circular orbits around a Schwarzschild black hole immersed in Kiselev anisotropic fluid, analyzing photon spheres, ISCO/OSCO, and stability via Lyapunov exponents across six equation-of-state parameters ω and normalisation N; it derives a universal closed-form expression for the squared coordinate-time Lyapunov exponent and recovers several known spacetimes as limits.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how surrounding anisotropic dark energy-like fluid (Kiselev type) modifies black hole spacetime structure—particularly photon sphere, ISCO, OSCO, and orbital stability—and to assess implications for astrophysical observables such as black hole shadows, accretion disks, and QPOs.

Method: Analytical derivation and analysis of effective potentials for null and timelike geodesics in the Kiselev–Schwarzschild metric; computation of photon sphere radius, ISCO and OSCO radii; evaluation of coordinate-time Lyapunov exponents to assess orbital stability; systematic variation over six values of ω and full admissible N range including extremal limits.

Result: Photon sphere is always unstable but its instability weakens with N for ω ≤ 0 and vanishes at extremality for ω = −1, −2/3; stable circular orbits exist between ISCO and OSCO only for ω = −1, −2/3 (merging at critical N), while for ω ≥ −1/3 they exist for all r > ISCO; a closed-form expression for (λₜᵀ)² is derived, whose sign change precisely marks ISCO/OSCO boundaries; Schwarzschild–de Sitter, mass-shifted Schwarzschild, and Reissner–Nordström emerge as special cases.

Conclusion: The presence and nature of the Kiselev fluid profoundly alter orbital stability structure: existence of OSCO and bounded stable orbit regions are exclusive to negative ω < −1/3, while positive ω yields unbounded stability akin to Schwarzschild; the Lyapunov exponent criterion fully captures stability boundaries, reinforcing its utility in modified gravity contexts.

Abstract: We investigate the geodesic structure and the stability of circular orbits around a Schwarzschild black hole surrounded by a Kiselev anisotropic fluid, characterised by the equation-of-state parameter $\omega$ and the normalisation parameter $N$. We consider six representative values, $\omega=-1,-2/3,-1/3,0,1/3$ and $1$, over the admissible range of $N$ determined by the horizon structure, including the corresponding extremal limits. Using the effective potentials for null and timelike geodesics, we determine the photon sphere, the innermost stable circular orbit (ISCO) and the outermost stable circular orbit (OSCO), and examine their stability through coordinate-time Lyapunov exponents. The photon sphere is unstable for all values of $\omega$ and $N$ considered. Its instability weakens with increasing $N$ for $\omega\leq0$ and vanishes at extremality for $\omega=-1$ and $-2/3$, whereas it varies non-monotonically with $N$ for $\omega=1/3$ and strengthens for $\omega=1$. For $\omega=-1$ and $-2/3$, stable circular orbits are confined between the ISCO and the OSCO, which merge at a critical value of $N$ beyond which no stable circular orbits exist. In contrast, for $\omega\geq-1/3$ stable circular orbits exist at all radii beyond the ISCO, whose location varies with $N$. We also derive a closed-form expression for the squared coordinate-time Lyapunov exponent $(\lambda_t^T)^2$, valid for arbitrary $\omega$, whose sign changes exactly at the ISCO and OSCO, so that the Lyapunov criterion reproduces the boundaries of stable circular motion. The Schwarzschild--de Sitter, mass-shifted Schwarzschild and Reissner--Nordström geometries are recovered as special cases, and the results may have implications for black-hole shadows, thin accretion discs and quasi-periodic oscillations.

</details>


### [167] [Relativistic dynamical friction and accretion for Kerr black holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.31061)
*Nicholas Speeney,Soumodeep Mitra,Emanuele Berti,Andrea Maselli,Sumanta Chakraborty*

Main category: gr-qc
